- **Śiva commands Nandi to shift at Tiruppuṅkūr** — with the great stone bull blocking Nandanar's distant view of the liṅga, Śiva orders Nandi to move aside; the Tiruppuṅkūr Nandi stands off-axis to this day _(actors: Śiva, Nandi, Nandanar)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/nandanar-tirunalaippovar.md) — (Periya Purāṇam, Tirunāḷaippōvār Nāyaṉār Purāṇam)
- **Śiva crowns the boy Caṇḍeśa** — Śiva and Umā manifest, restore the father's leg, adopt the child as their own, set Śiva's own head-garland on him, and name him Caṇḍeśa, chief of the gaṇas and eternal steward of Śiva's wealth _(actors: Śiva, Umā, Caṇḍeśa, Yajñadatta)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/chandesvara-chandikeshvara.md) — (Periya Purāṇam, Caṇṭēcura Nāyaṉār Purāṇam)
- **Śiva Orders Rājasiṃha to Postpone the Kāñci Consecration** — in a dream Śiva tells the Pallava king Rājasiṃha (Narasiṃhavarman II) to delay dedicating his great stone temple, for the Lord must first attend Pūcalār's mind-temple consecration on the same day _(actors: Śiva, Rājasiṃha / Narasiṃhavarman II)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/pusalar-nayanar-mind-temple.md) — (Periya Purāṇam, Pūcalār Purāṇam)
- **Śiva Purāṇa's Nārada-moha frame compiled** — the surviving Rudra-saṃhitā text containing the monkey-face story takes shape c. 10th–14th century CE [scholarly] _(actors: anonymous Śaiva compilers, Vyāsa (trad.))_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/narada-moha-svayamvara.md) — (Śiva Purāṇa, Rudra-saṃhitā, Sṛṣṭi-khaṇḍa 2–4)
- **Śiva reveals himself and takes the household to Kailāsa** — Śiva appears with Umā on Nandi, declares the test passed, and carries Ciṟuttoṇṭar, his wife, son, and servant to Śivaloka _(actors: Śiva, Umā, Ciṟuttoṇṭar, Cīrāḷaṉ)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/siruthondar-nayanar.md) — (Periya Purāṇam, Ciṟuttoṇṭa Purāṇam)
- **Śiva takes service as the servant Ugnā** — enchanted by Vidyāpati's nācārī songs, Mahādeva disguises himself as the destitute boy Ugnā and enters the poor poet's household as a servant, binding him to secrecy _(actors: Śiva/Ugnā, Vidyāpati, Pārvatī)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ugna-mahadev-vidyapati.md) — (Ugnā legend, Maithili oral tradition) [folk]
- **Śiva's dream-command to the Tillai Dīkṣitars** — arriving at Chidambaram and barred from the sanctum, Nandanar is admitted after Śiva appears in the dream of the Tillai Brahmin priesthood ordering them to kindle a sacred fire for his devotee _(actors: Śiva, the Tillaivāḻ Andaṇar / Dīkṣitars, Nandanar)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/nandanar-tirunalaippovar.md) — (Periya Purāṇam, Tirunāḷaippōvār Nāyaṉār Purāṇam)
- **Śiva-Sūtras revealed on the Śaṅkarpāl rock** — Śiva directs Vasugupta in a dream to a boulder on Mahādeva mountain that turns over to reveal 77 engraved aphorisms in three unmeṣas (c. 850–900 CE [scholarly]) _(actors: Śiva, Vasugupta)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/kashmir-shaiva-texts.md) — (Kṣemarāja, Śiva-sūtra-vimarśinī, opening)
- **Śivopādhyāya completes the Vijñāna Bhairava Vivṛti** — the full standard gloss on the 112 dhāraṇās, proof of living Kashmiri exegesis into the 18th century [scholarly] _(actors: Śivopādhyāya)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/kashmir-shaiva-texts.md) — (Vijñāna Bhairava Vivṛti)
- **Slandered as unchaste and cast out** — the pundits declare that only a non-virgin could know such bodily truth, break her betrothal, and expel her from house and caste _(actors: the Brahmin maiden, the Brahmin scholars)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/muchilottu-bhagavathy.md) — (Muchilottu Bhagavathy thoṭṭam-pāṭṭu)
- **Slaying of the demon Bhairava** — Ramdev destroys (or banishes) the rakshasa Bhairava who was devouring the people around Pokaran _(actors: Ramdev, Bhairava rakshasa)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/ramdev-pir.md) — (Ramdev hagiographic tradition, caubis paracha) [folk]
- **Smṛticandrikā compiled** — Devaṇṇa-bhaṭṭa produces the authoritative digest of the South, a chief source of the Dravida sub-school of Hindu law _(actors: Devaṇṇa-bhaṭṭa)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/dharma-nibandhas-and-digests.md) — ([scholarly] c. 1150–1225 CE)
- **Somadeva completes the Kathāsaritsāgara (c. 1070 CE)** — the Kashmiri brahmin retells Guṇāḍhya's tale in 18 lambhakas and 124 taraṅgas for queen Sūryavatī, wife of King Ananta of Kashmir, to divert her mind amid court troubles _(actors: Somadeva, Sūryavatī, Ananta of Kashmir)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/brihatkatha-kathasaritsagara-and-vikramaditya-cycle.md) — (Kathāsaritsāgara, colophon) [scholarly]
- **Somadeva composes the Kathāsaritsāgara** — a Kashmiri Brahmin condenses Guṇāḍhya's lost Paiśācī *Bṛhatkathā* into 18 lambhakas and 124 taraṅgas of Sanskrit verse, the anthology that fixes these stand-alone tales _(actors: Somadeva, queen Sūryavatī, Guṇāḍhya)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/kathasaritsagara-famous-tales.md) — (Kathāsaritsāgara, colophon; [scholarly] c. 1063–1081 CE)
- **Somadeva fixes the romance in the Ocean of Story** — the Kashmiri poet Somadeva sets the Udayana–Vāsavadattā saga into Books II–III of the Kathāsaritsāgara (c. 1063–1081 CE), the surviving Sanskrit form of Guṇāḍhya's lost Bṛhatkathā _(actors: Somadeva, Guṇāḍhya)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/udayana-vasavadatta.md) — (Kathāsaritsāgara II–III, Tarangas 9–19)
- **Somānanda founds Pratyabhijñā with the Śivadṛṣṭi** — seven āhnikas arguing everything is Śiva, tracing his line to Tryambaka (c. 900–950 CE [scholarly]) _(actors: Somānanda, Utpaladeva)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/kashmir-shaiva-texts.md) — (Śivadṛṣṭi, chs. 1–7)
- **Sontheimer maps the pastoral substrate** — G.-D. Sontheimer's fieldwork among Dhangar singers (1960s–70s) yields Biroba, Mhaskoba und Khandoba (1976), arguing the Sanskritized Khaṇḍobā sits atop a living shepherd-god stratum _(actors: G.-D. Sontheimer, Dhangar singers)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/dhangar-oral-epics-biroba-mhaskoba-dhuloba.md) — (Sontheimer 1976 / Pastoral Deities 1989)
- **Spanda-Kārikās composed** — the vibration doctrine unpacked in ~52 verses, attributed by rival commentators to Vasugupta or his disciple Kallaṭa (9th c. CE [scholarly] [disputed]) _(actors: Vasugupta, Kallaṭa)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/kashmir-shaiva-texts.md) — (Spanda-Kārikās; Kṣemarāja, Spanda-nirṇaya)
- **Śrī Śāradā Maṭha founded for women's sannyāsa** — the Ramakrishna tradition establishes an independent women's monastic order conferring full sannyāsa at Kolkata in 1954 _(actors: Ramakrishna Order, Śāradā Devī's lineage)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/sannyasa-and-the-renunciate-tradition.md) — (Sri Sarada Math history)
- **Śrīharṣa composes the Naiṣadhīyacarita** — 22-canto Nala–Damayantī mahākāvya at the Gāhaḍavāla court of Kannauj, 12th c. CE [scholarly] _(actors: Śrīharṣa)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/classical-kavya-and-sanskrit-drama.md) — (Naiṣadhīyacarita 1–22)
- **Stambheśvar Mahādev, the 'disappearing temple' of Kavi Kamboi** — the modern coastal liṅga-shrine on the Gulf of Khambhāt is submerged by the sea at high tide and revealed at low tide, its darśana timed to the ocean rather than the bell _(actors: pilgrims, the tide)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/mahisagara-sangama-kumarika-kshetra.md) — (Gujarat temple tradition)
- **Subak water-temple landscape inscribed by UNESCO (2012)** — Bali's irrigation-cooperative religion of Dewi Danu's water temples is listed as World Heritage as a manifestation of Tri Hita Karana [scholarly] _(actors: subak cooperatives, Pura Ulun Danu Batur, UNESCO)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/balinese-hinduism-agama-hindu-dharma.md) — (UNESCO listing, 2012)
- **Subba Rao collects the longest Telugu ballad-cycle** — T.V. Subba Rao's 1963–68 fieldwork and Sahitya Akademi edition recover some 20 of the reckoned 32 palm-leaf ballads, establishing Katamaraju as the longest Telugu oral epic beside Palnadu _(actors: T.V. Subba Rao)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/katamaraju-katha.md) — (Katamaraju Kathalu, ed. Subba Rao, A.P. Sahitya Akademi 1976–78)
- **Sultanate sack of Śrīraṅgam; Lokācārya's last journey** — Piḷḷai Lokācārya escorts the Namperumāḷ image south and dies at Jyotiṣkuḍi (1311 or 1323 raid [disputed]) _(actors: Piḷḷai Lokācārya, Namperumāḷ, Ulugh Khan)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/vedanta-desika-and-later-srivaishnava-acharyas.md) — (Wikipedia, Pillai Lokacharya)
- **Sundara digs the suraṅga to Vidyā** — Sundara, Kālī-devotee prince of Kāñcī, courts the learned princess Vidyā of Burdwan through the flower-woman Hīrā and tunnels secretly into her palace-chamber to wed her by gāndharva rite _(actors: Sundara, Vidyā, Hīrā the mālinī, Rāja Vīrasiṃha)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/annadamangal-bharatchandra.md) — (Annadāmaṅgal, Vidyā-Sundar khaṇḍa)
- **Sundarar and Cheramān Perumāḷ ascend to Kailāsa (hagiographic close, c. 8th c. CE)** — Sundarar returns on a white elephant, the Chera king riding after him on a horse charged with the pañcākṣara _(actors: Sundarar, Cheramān Perumāḷ, Śiva)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/periya-puranam-63-nayanmar-legend-map.md) — (Periya Purāṇam, Vellāṉai carukkam)
- **Sundarar and the padikāsu** — the Nāyanār saint calls himself "servant of all born in Tiruvārūr" and receives Śiva's daily gold coin, marking the town as the heart of his Tēvāram corpus _(actors: Sundaramūrti Nāyanār, Śiva)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/tiruvarur-thyagaraja-kamalalaya.md) — (Tēvāram, Tirumurai 7; Periya Purāṇam)
- **Sundarar sings the Tiruttoṇṭattokai (c. 8th c. CE)** — at Tiruvārūr Śiva's command and Viranmiṇḍar's rebuke draw out the 11-verse hymn naming the servants, seed-list of the 63 _(actors: Sundarar, Śiva, Viranmiṇḍar)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/periya-puranam-63-nayanmar-legend-map.md) — (Tēvāram 7.39; Periya Purāṇam)
- **Sung in the Tēvāram** — Tiru-jñāna-Sambandar and Appar glorify Sirappaḷḷi in the Tamil Śaiva hymns, marking Tiruchirāppaḷḷi one of the 275 Pāḍal Petṟa Sthalams _(actors: Sambandar, Appar)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/tiruchirappalli-rockfort-thayumanavar.md) — (Tēvāram, Tirumuṟai 1–7) [scholarly]
- **Sureśvara the Vārttikakāra** — Śaṅkara's direct disciple (hagiographically the converted Maṇḍana Miśra) writes the Bṛhadāraṇyaka and Taittirīya vārttikas and the Naiṣkarmya-siddhi, locating avidyā in Brahman and rejecting jñāna-karma-samuccaya _(actors: Sureśvara, Śaṅkara)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/later-advaita-acharyas.md) — (Naiṣkarmya-siddhi; Wikipedia, Sureshvara) [scholarly]
- **Svapneśvara composes the bhāṣya on Śāṇḍilya** — the principal surviving commentary reads the sūtra through the Gītā, medieval date variously placed c. 13th–15th c. CE [scholarly] [disputed] _(actors: Svapneśvara)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/bhakti-sutras-narada-shandilya.md) — (Svapneśvara's bhāṣya)
- **Swami Vivekananda meditates at Karnaprayag (1890s CE)** — the wandering monk's recorded stay during his Garhwal travels [scholarly] _(actors: Swami Vivekananda)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/panch-prayag-alaknanda-confluences.md) — (Wikipedia, Karnaprayag)
- **T. Gaṇapati Śāstrī publishes the Trivandrum plays** — thirteen anonymous Kerala manuscript dramas identified as Bhāsa's lost works, 1912–15 CE [scholarly] _(actors: T. Gaṇapati Śāstrī)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/classical-kavya-and-sanskrit-drama.md) — (Trivandrum Sanskrit Series nos. 15–42)
- **Tacōḷi Ōtēnan worships at Lōkanārkāvu** — the Nāyar kaḷari-master of Tacōḷi Maṇikkōth, hero of the second cycle, propitiates his tutelary goddess Lōkanārkāvu Bhagavatī before his combats, his valour underwritten by the Devī _(actors: Tacōḷi Ōtēnan, Lōkanārkāvu Bhagavatī)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/vadakkan-pattukal-northern-ballads.md) — (Vaṭakkan Pāṭṭukaḷ, Tacōḷi cycle)
- **Takṣaśilā and Puṣkalāvatī as historical capitals of Gāndhāra** — the eponymous epic cities correspond to Taxila (Bhir Mound/Sirkap) and Charsadda (Bala Hisar), joint capitals of the Gāndhāra mahājanapada named in Darius I's inscriptions c. 518 BCE _(actors: Takṣa, Puṣkala, historical Gāndhāra)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/bharata-gandharva-conquest-takshashila.md) — (archaeology; Achaemenid inscriptions) [scholarly]
- **Taleju brought from Simraungadh to the Kathmandu Valley** — with the fall of Simraungadh to the Tughlaq forces c. 1324–26 CE, the goddess travels north with Harisiṃhadeva's refugee dynasty and becomes the Malla kings' lineage goddess _(actors: Taleju Bhavānī, Harisiṃhadeva, the Malla kings)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/nepal-kumari-taleju-machhindranath.md) — (Nepalese vaṃśāvalī tradition) [scholarly dating; disputed]
- **Taleju temple of Kathmandu Durbar Square built** — king Mahendra Malla raises the towering Taleju temple at Hanuman Dhoka, 1564 CE, opened to the public only at Mahānavamī of Dasain _(actors: Mahendra Malla, Taleju Bhavānī)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/nepal-kumari-taleju-machhindranath.md) — (Newar temple tradition; Nepalese chronicles) [scholarly]
- **Taleju's dice-game withdrawal and the Kumārī decree** — offended by the king's desiring gaze (or the spying queen), the goddess ends her nightly dice sessions and decrees she will henceforth dwell only in a virgin Śākya girl — the charter of the living Kumārī (Malla-era legend, variously attached to Jaya Prakāśa Malla or Trailokya Malla) _(actors: Taleju Bhavānī, Jaya Prakāśa Malla)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/nepal-kumari-taleju-machhindranath.md) — (Kathmandu Valley legend; vaṃśāvalī tradition) [folk]
- **Taṇḍiyaḍigaḷ deepens the Kamalālayam** — the blind Nāyanār of Tiruvārūr enlarges the great temple tank against the mockery of Jains, one of the town's saints catalogued by Sēkkiḻār _(actors: Taṇḍiyaḍigaḷ Nāyanār, Kaḻaṟciṅga Nāyanār)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/tiruvarur-thyagaraja-kamalalaya.md) — (Periya Purāṇam)
- **Tantric flowering of the Vaṭuka cult** — the developed boy-Bhairava worship — dhyāna, stotra, kavaca, and kṣetrapāla role — crystallizes in the Bhairava/Kaula/Śrī Vidyā stream across the medieval Tantric period _(actors: Kaula and Śrī Vidyā lineages)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/vatuka-bhairava-origin.md) — ([scholarly] c. 7th–12th c. CE)
- **Tapas codified as a yogic niyama** — Patañjali's Yoga Sūtras (c. 350–450 CE [scholarly]) list tapas among the niyamas and as the first limb of kriyā-yoga _(actors: Patañjali)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/tapas-boons-and-curses.md) — (Yoga Sūtras 2.1, 2.32)
- **Tāpoi born to the sādhaba house** — A wealthy seafaring merchant of coastal Odisha, father of seven sons, gains a long-wished-for only daughter, Tāpoi, raised in luxury — even a golden moon commissioned as her toy _(actors: Tāpoi, the sādhaba merchant, the seven brothers)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/tapoi-khudurukuni-osha.md) — (Tāpoi kathā, Odia oral tradition)
- **Tāpoi worships Maṅgaḷā with broken rice** — Weeping over a lost goat, the persecuted girl offers the goddess Maṅgaḷā her only possession, a handful of khuda; the goddess restores the goat, takes her under protection, and promises the brothers' return _(actors: Tāpoi, Maṅgaḷā)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/tapoi-khudurukuni-osha.md) — (Tāpoi kathā, Khudurukuṇī Oṣā tradition)
- **Teja Dashami and the Parbatsar cattle fair** — Bhadrapada shukla dashami becomes Tejaji's festival, when tanti-vows are untied at his shrines and one of Rajasthan's largest cattle fairs gathers at Parbatsar [regional] _(actors: Tejaji devotees, ghorla possession-mediums)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/tejaji.md) — (Teja Dashami / Parbatsar fair observance)
- **Tejaji's vow to the burning serpent** — riding to Paner for his bride Pemal, Teja saves a naga from a fire and pledges to return and receive its bite once his errand is done [folk] _(actors: Tejaji, the naga, Pemal)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/tejaji.md) — (Tejaji katha)
- **Temple entry opened to all castes (1947)** — after the campaign associated with Sane Guruji, Pandharpur's Viṭṭhala temple admits 'untouchable' devotees, vindicating the ethic dramatized by Cokhāmeḷā's samādhi at the Mahādvāra _(actors: Sane Guruji, Vārkarī community)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/pandharpur-vitthala-wari.md) — (documented history) [scholarly]
- **The 1772 Raṇchhoḍrāī temple** — the Maratha noble Goplarāv Jagannāth Tāmbvekar builds the present temple on the Gomatī tank at Ḍākor _(actors: Goplarāv Jagannāth Tāmbvekar)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/dakor-ranchhodrai-bodana.md) — (Wikipedia, Ranchhodrai Temple)
- **The 2004 Mahāmaham stampede** — the modern twelve-year cycle (1992, 2004, 2016, next 2028) is marked by a fatal crush at the 24 Feb 2004 bathing that killed some fifty pilgrims _(actors: the Mahāmaham pilgrims)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/kumbakonam-adi-kumbheshwara-mahamaham.md) — (news record) [scholarly]
- **The 30-muhūrta day codified in the Brāhmaṇas** — the ahorātra is fixed at 30 muhūrtas and the year at 10,800, with deity-named muhūrta rosters, c. 900–700 BCE [scholarly] _(actors: Vedic ritualists)_ → [details](../00-time-and-cosmology/muhurta-and-auspicious-timing.md) — (Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa X.4.2; Taittirīya Brāhmaṇa III.10.1)
- **The 32 statuettes halt Bhoja and free themselves** — each apsara-statuette blocks Bhoja's ascent with a tale of Vikrama's magnanimity until, after the thirty-second, he concedes his unworthiness, and the nymphs' curse lifts and they return to heaven _(actors: Bhoja, the 32 apsara statuettes, Vikramāditya)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/simhasana-dvatrimshika-vikramaditya-cycle.md) — (Siṃhāsana-dvātriṃśikā I–XXXII)
- **The abduction of the Brahmin maiden** — Kāttavarāyaṉ carries Āriyamālā off and takes her as his own, at once a love-match and the gravest defilement the caste order can name, provoking the Brahmins' outrage _(actors: Kāttavarāyaṉ, Āriyamālā, the Brahmins)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/kattavarayan-katai.md) — (Kaṭṭavarāyacuvāmi Kataippāṭal) [oral tradition]
- **The Adhyātma Rāmāyaṇa canonizes the robber-conversion legend** — the family-refusal and marā-marā story enters Sanskrit literature c. 14th–15th c. CE, two millennia after the epic's core [scholarly] _(actors: Adhyātma Rāmāyaṇa redactors)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/valmiki-ratnakara-conversion.md) — (Adhyātma Rāmāyaṇa, Ayodhyā-kāṇḍa 6)
- **The Advaitasiddhi** — Madhusūdana Sarasvatī at Kāśī writes the point-by-point refutation of Vyāsatīrtha's Nyāyāmṛta, the high-water mark of the Advaita–Dvaita polemic _(actors: Madhusūdana Sarasvatī, Vyāsatīrtha)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/later-advaita-acharyas.md) — (Advaitasiddhi; Wikipedia) [scholarly]
- **The Āḻvār hagiographies compiled** — the Śrīvaiṣṇava ācāryas fix the saints' miracle-lives in Garuḍavāhana Paṇḍita's Sanskrit Divya Sūri Caritam (c. 12th c.) and Pinbaḻagiya Perumāḷ Jīyar's Tamil Guruparampara Prabhāvam 6000-paḍi (c. 13th–14th c.) _(actors: Garuḍavāhana Paṇḍita, Pinbaḻagiya Perumāḷ Jīyar)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/alvar-hagiographies-guruparampara.md) — (Divya Sūri Caritam; Guruparampara Prabhāvam)
- **The Alvars Sing the 108 Divya Desams** — The twelve Alvar poet-saints (c. 5th-9th c. CE) sing Vishnu shrines in the Naalayira Divya Prabandham, the act of mangalasasanam canonizing the 108 Divya Desams _(actors: Twelve Alvars, Tirumangai Alvar)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/temple-networks-and-circuits.md) — (Naalayira Divya Prabandham)
- **The aṅkam of Ārōmal Cēkavar** — hired to fight a chieftain's judicial duel on the aṅkattaṭṭu, the peerless Cēkava swordsman Ārōmal kills the veteran champion Aṟiṅṅōṭar but is treacherously stabbed at the moment of victory by his own cousin Cāntu _(actors: Ārōmal Cēkavar, Aṟiṅṅōṭar, Cāntu)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/vadakkan-pattukal-northern-ballads.md) — (Vaṭakkan Pāṭṭukaḷ, Ārōmal Cēkavar pāṭṭu)
- **The anklet trial and the death of the Pāṇḍya king** — Kaṇṇaki proves her anklet holds rubies against the queen's pearls; a ruby flies from the broken anklet, and Neṭuñceḻiyaṉ dies declaring himself the thief, his queen dying of grief _(actors: Kaṇṇaki, Neṭuñceḻiyaṉ, Kōpperuntēvi)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/silappatikaram-kannagi-pattini.md) — (Cilappatikāram, Vaḻakkurai kātai)
- **The annual Bhadwa mela at Ramdevra** — lakhs of Hindu and Muslim pilgrims walk to Runicha for the fair running from Bhadrapada shukla 2 (Baba ri Bij) to the ekadashi samadhi day each monsoon _(actors: Ramdev devotees, Kamad bards)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/ramdev-pir.md) — (Ramdevra mela tradition) [regional]
- **The armed Ālvār who sang the most divya deśams** — the only Ālvār depicted with sword and consort, Tirumaṅgai dedicates decads to more of the 108 divya deśams than any other saint _(actors: Tirumaṅgai Āḻvār, Kumudavallī)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/tirumangai-alvar.md) — (Thirumangai Alvar — Wikipedia)
- **The Ashtākṣara from the bridegroom's lips** — the Lord whispers the eight-syllable Oṃ Namo Nārāyaṇāya into the robber's ear, initiating him and transforming Kaliyan into Tirumaṅgai Āḻvār _(actors: Viṣṇu, Tirumaṅgai Āḻvār)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/tirumangai-alvar.md) — (Guruparampara Prabhāvam, Parakāla chapters)
- **The bearded Bālājī ploughed up at Āsotā (1754 CE)** — a Jāṭ cultivator's plough strikes a buried Hanumān image at Āsotā and his wife cleans it, revealing the unique daaḍhī-mūñch (bearded) icon of Sālāsar _(actors: the Āsotā cultivator and his wife, Bālājī)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/mehandipur-salasar-balaji-hanuman-shrines.md) — (Sālāsar Bālājī tradition, Śrāvaṇa V.S. 1811 / 1754 CE) [folk] [regional]
- **The Bhairava's child-curry demand** — Śiva arrives disguised as a fierce Bhairava/Kāpālika ascetic and demands to be fed a curry of a five-year-old only son, killed and cooked by the parents smiling and without grief _(actors: Śiva as Bhairava, Ciṟuttoṇṭar, Veṇkāṭṭu Nangai)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/siruthondar-nayanar.md) — (Periya Purāṇam, Ciṟuttoṇṭa Purāṇam)
- **The Bhāmatī school named** — the polymath Vācaspati Miśra (c. 9th–10th c. CE) writes the Bhāmatī on the Brahmasūtra-bhāṣya, holding the jīva the locus of many avidyās and requiring prasaṃkhyāna-meditation alongside knowledge _(actors: Vācaspati Miśra)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/later-advaita-acharyas.md) — (Bhāmatī; Wikipedia, Vachaspati Mishra) [scholarly]
- **The Bhavānī sword of Shivaji** — Bhavānī gives (or is invoked over) Shivaji's celebrated blade, named for his kuladevī, charter of the Hindavī svarājya _(actors: Bhavānī, Chhatrapati Shivaji)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/tuljapur-tulja-bhavani.md) — (Maratha povāḍā and bakhar tradition) [folk] [disputed]
- **The Bhavānī-sword legend of Shivaji (17th c. CE)** — tradition holds the goddess appeared to Shivaji and gave him the Bhavānī talvār; historians treat the divine gift as legend, the kernel being a fine European blade named 'Bhavānī' for his kuladevī _(actors: Shivaji, Tuljā Bhavānī)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/tulja-bhavani.md) — (Maratha povāḍā and bakhar tradition) [folk] [disputed]
- **The bhoṭo entrusted to Machhindranāth** — the jewelled vest given by the nāga king to a Jyāpu farmer, stolen and disputed, is left in the god's keeping and displayed yearly at Bhoṭo Jātrā until a rightful owner proves his claim _(actors: Karkoṭaka nāga, the Jyāpu farmer, Rāto Machhindranāth)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/nepal-kumari-taleju-machhindranath.md) — (Patan legend) [folk]
- **The blind king variant** — in one strand the Pārijāta errand is set to restore sight to a blinded king, an impossible task designed to make Aruṇagiri fail _(actors: Aruṇagirinātar, Sambandāṇḍāṉ, the blind king)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/arunagirinathar-tiruppugazh.md) — (hagiographic tradition) [disputed]
- **The boita returns and Tāpoi is recognized** — The seven brothers come home laden from the sea, find their sister a ragged goatherd at the landing-place, and hear from her everything the sisters-in-law have done _(actors: the seven brothers, Tāpoi)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/tapoi-khudurukuni-osha.md) — (Tāpoi kathā, Odia oral tradition)
- **The brother slain a second time** — woodcutters felling the acacia kill Nīlaṉ, leaving Nīli doubly bereaved and fixed wholly on vengeance as a shape-shifting yakṣi _(actors: Nīli, Nīlaṉ, villagers)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/icakki-amman-nili-katai.md) — (Nīli Katai)
- **The Buddha-birthday ordinance — an early Buddha-image rite in a Hindu text** — The Nīlamata's festival catalog directs that the Buddha's image be bathed and śramaṇas honored on his birthday, the Buddha counted an avatāra of Viṣṇu — among the earliest brahmanical prescriptions of Buddha-worship [scholarly] _(actors: the Buddha as Viṣṇu-avatāra, Kashmiri devotees, śramaṇas)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/nilamata-purana.md) — (Nīlamata Purāṇa, festival ordinances, vv. c. 684 ff.)
- **The buried guru Hāḍipā** — the still-resisting king abuses his destined outcaste guru Hāḍipā, burying him in a dry well of filth, from which the siddha emerges unharmed by his yoga _(actors: Gopīcandra, Hāḍipā)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/gopichandra-maynamati-nath-epic.md) — (Gopīcandrer Gān) [folk]
- **The burning of six-sevenths of the Great Tale** — spurned by King Sātavāhana, Guṇāḍhya reads the tale aloud and burns it leaf by leaf as the forest animals weep, destroying six of seven books before the king arrives to save the seventh, the Naravāhanadatta saga _(actors: Guṇāḍhya, Sātavāhana, Guṇadeva, Nandideva)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/brihatkatha-kathasaritsagara-and-vikramaditya-cycle.md) — (Kathāsaritsāgara, I.8)
- **The Caitra Pūrṇimā yātrā of Wadi Ratnagiri** — the living cult of Jyotibā, with its pink gulāl, sāsan-kāṭhī processions and cry of "Cāṅg bhalā!", is documented medieval-to-modern regional practice on the Kolhapur hill _(actors: Jyotibā devotees, Kolhapur state patrons)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/jyotiba-kedarling-kolhapur.md) — (regional Marathi tradition)
- **The calf-tribute repudiated** — in the alternate cause, Katamaraju grazes Nellore's cornfields in breach of the pact, the queen destroys his herds unbeknown to the king, and he cancels the tribute as equal to his loss, triggering war _(actors: Katamaraju, Kundamadevi, Nallasiddhi)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/katamaraju-katha.md) — (Narayanappa 2021, variant version)
- **The Caṇḍikeśvara sub-shrine and the temple clap** — every South-Indian Śiva temple sets an axe-bearing Caṇḍikeśvara shrine northeast of the sanctum, where devotees clap or snap fingers to show the steward they leave empty-handed _(actors: Caṇḍikeśvara, temple worshippers)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/chandesvara-chandikeshvara.md) — (Śaiva Āgamas; Chola temple practice)
- **The Cāraṇ caravan attacked by Bāpiyā** — A travelling Cāraṇ party including the woman Bahucharā is set upon by the marauder Bāpiyā, who threatens the women with abduction and dishonour _(actors: Bahucharā (Cāraṇ woman), Bāpiyā (bandit chief), Cāraṇ kinswomen)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/bahuchara-mata.md) — (Cāraṇ folk tradition)
- **The Carnatic Trinity born at Tiruvārūr** — Tyāgarāja (named for the deity), Muttusvāmi Dīkṣitar, and Śyāma Śāstri are all born in the town, carrying its shrine into the Carnatic canon _(actors: Tyāgarāja, Muttusvāmi Dīkṣitar, Śyāma Śāstri)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/tiruvarur-thyagaraja-kamalalaya.md) — ([scholarly] 18th–19th c. CE)
- **The Champakulam idol and the boat race** — the Krishna image is fetched by boat along the Pampa backwaters and halts at Champakulam, an arrival commemorated by Kerala's oldest snake-boat race, the Champakulam Moolam Vallamkali _(actors: Chempakasseri king, boatmen)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/ambalappuzha-krishna-palpayasam.md) — (Ambalappuzha–Champakulam tradition) [regional]
- **The chariot halts for Sālabega** — fearing he will miss the Ratha Yātrā, the ailing poet prays the Lord to wait, and Nandighoṣa stops dead on the Baḍa Daṇḍa, immovable by men or elephants, until Sālabega arrives for darśana _(actors: Sālabega, Jagannātha, the chariot pullers)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/jagannatha-bhakta-legends.md) — (Odia bhakta-caritra) [oral tradition] [folk]
- **The Chola temple and the Āḻi Tēr** — the standing Tyāgarāja temple is raised as a Chola foundation from the 9th c. (Rājendra I inscription, c. 1032), its ~300-tonne festival chariot the largest in Tamil Nadu _(actors: Rājendra Chola I)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/tiruvarur-thyagaraja-kamalalaya.md) — (Chola inscriptions)
- **The Churning of the Sea of Milk carved at Angkor** — the east gallery renders the Samudra Manthana ~50 m long: devas and asuras haul Vāsuki round Mount Mandara while Viṣṇu and his Kūrma avatāra bear the pivot _(actors: devas, asuras, Vāsuki, Viṣṇu, Kūrma)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/greater-india-sacred-geography.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 8.6–8; Mahābhārata Ādi-parva)
- **The Citrasūtra codifies Indian painting** — Book III chs. 35–43, the earliest surviving Sanskrit treatise on painting, sets out the types of citra, iconometry, the rasas, the faults of a picture, and the preparation of pigments _(actors: Viṣṇudharmottara redactors)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/vishnudharmottara-purana.md) — (Viṣṇudharmottara Purāṇa III.35–43; Kramrisch, Part III)
- **The cooking of Cīrāḷaṉ** — the parents fetch their only son from school and, by the ascetic's terms, slay and cook him into a curry with unwavering serenity, then cook even the head at the guest's order _(actors: Ciṟuttoṇṭar, Veṇkāṭṭu Nangai, Cīrāḷaṉ)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/siruthondar-nayanar.md) — (Periya Purāṇam, Ciṟuttoṇṭa Purāṇam)
- **The couplet enters Vaiṣṇava liturgy** — the "Vaiṣṇavas excepted" verse becomes a stock stotra citation across Śrī-Vaiṣṇava and Gauḍīya sampradāyas, paired with Ajāmila as proof the bhakta is beyond Yama _(actors: Vaiṣṇava ācāryas)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/yama-gita.md) — (Viṣṇu Purāṇa III.7; later stotra tradition)
- **The darbār of Bālājī, Pret-rāj and Bhairav** — Mehandīpur's spirit-tribunal is constituted, with Bālājī as judge, Pret-rāj Sarkār (lord of ghosts) as sentencing magistrate, and Bhairav the Kotwāl as executing constable _(actors: Bālājī, Pret-rāj Sarkār, Bhairav Kotwāl)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/mehandipur-salasar-balaji-hanuman-shrines.md) — (Mehandīpur temple tradition) [folk]
- **The Deity carried to Odisha** — Puruṣottama Deva brings the Sākṣi-Gopāla Deity from Vidyānagara to Kaṭaka (Cuttack), and by later temple tradition the image is moved to its present seat at Sākṣigopāl/Satyabāḍī near Purī _(actors: Puruṣottama Deva, Sākṣi-Gopāla)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/sakshi-gopal-witness-krishna.md) — (Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya 5; Satyabāḍī temple tradition)
- **The disputed Viṭhobā substratum** — Sontheimer reads even Viṭṭhal of Paṇḍharpūr as carrying a pastoral hero-stone origin beneath his Vaiṣṇava identity, an influential but contested claim _(actors: Sontheimer, Viṭhobā)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/dhangar-oral-epics-biroba-mhaskoba-dhuloba.md) — (Sontheimer, Pastoral Deities) [disputed]
- **The double wedding among the leather-workers** — having become a Chakkiliyar in work and life, Muttuppaṭṭaṉ marries both sisters by their community's own rites _(actors: Muttuppaṭṭaṉ, Bommakkā, Timmakkā)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/muttuppattan-katai.md) — (Muttuppaṭṭaṉ Katai, vil pāṭṭu)
- **The Dṛg-Dṛśya-Viveka emerges from the Śṛṅgerī circle** — a 46-verse discrimination of seer from seen with māyā's āvaraṇa/vikṣepa powers, ascribed variously to Śaṅkara, Bhāratītīrtha, or Vidyāraṇya, c. 14th c. CE [disputed] _(actors: Bhāratītīrtha, Vidyāraṇya, Adi Shankara (attrib.))_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/darshanas/vedanta-prakarana-granthas.md) — (Dṛg-Dṛśya-Viveka 1–15)
- **The Dutch raid on Guruvāyūr (1716 CE)** — Dutch forces plunder and burn the temple, which is subsequently rebuilt _(actors: Dutch East India Company forces, Guruvāyūr temple)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/guruvayur-krishna-kshetra.md) — (Temple chronicles) [scholarly]
- **The dyūta-krīḍā carved in stone** — the Umā-Maheśvara dice-play, Pārvatī turning away as she wins with Nandin looking on, is sculpted at Elephanta and Ellora from c. 8th c. CE _(actors: Śiva, Pārvatī, Nandin)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/shiva-parvati-dice-game.md) — ([scholarly] Rāṣṭrakūṭa/post-Gupta Śaiva sculpture)
- **The Eran pillar, first satī inscription (510 CE)** — The wife of the chieftain Goparāja, fallen in battle under Bhānugupta, follows him onto the pyre — the earliest dated epigraphic record of satī [scholarly] _(actors: Goparāja's wife, Goparāja, Bhānugupta)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/sati-jauhar-and-the-immolation-rites.md) — (Eran stone pillar inscription)
- **The escaped elder and the seventy-two tally** — in one telling sixty-nine burn and the fleeing elder is hunted down as the seventy-first, the phantom child making seventy-two dead _(actors: Nīli, the last elder)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/icakki-amman-nili-katai.md) — (Nīli Katai) [disputed]
- **The false wife and phantom child at Palaiyanur** — Nīli takes the exact guise of the merchant's wife and, weeping with a phantom infant, publicly claims him as her deserting husband before the village _(actors: Nīli, the merchant, Palaiyanur villagers)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/icakki-amman-nili-katai.md) — (Nīli Katai; source of the proverb 'Nīli's tears')
- **The father's kick and the axe** — Yajñadatta (Echadattan), told his son is wasting the village milk, spies on him and kicks over the sand-liṅga; the rapt boy strikes at the offending leg with his staff, which turns to an axe and severs it _(actors: Vicāra Śarman, Yajñadatta)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/chandesvara-chandikeshvara.md) — (Periya Purāṇam, Caṇṭēcura Nāyaṉār Purāṇam)
- **The fire-ordeal and merger with Naṭarāja** — Nandanar enters the flames before the shrine and emerges a radiant sage with matted locks and sacred thread, walks into the Cit Sabhā, and merges into the jyoti of the dancing Śiva _(actors: Nandanar, Naṭarāja, the Dīkṣitars)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/nandanar-tirunalaippovar.md) — (Periya Purāṇam, Tirunāḷaippōvār Nāyaṉār Purāṇam)
- **The first campaign fails** — insulted, Puruṣottama Deva marches on Kāñcī but is beaten back, and returns to Purī to reproach Jagannātha for abandoning his servant-king to defeat _(actors: Puruṣottama Deva, Jagannātha)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/kanchi-kaveri-jagannatha-legend.md) — (Kāñcī-Kāverī) [regional]
- **The five pirs of Mecca and the flying bowls** — five pirs test Ramdev, whose miracle brings their own bowls flying from Mecca; they bow and hail him Ramsa Pir, chartering his Muslim veneration _(actors: Ramdev, the five pirs of Mecca)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/ramdev-pir.md) — (Ramdev hagiographic tradition, caubis paracha) [folk] [regional]
- **The flying cloth horse parcha** — the child Ramdev mounts a tailor's toy horse of cloth and rags and flies into the sky, vindicating the accused tailor; the toy horse becomes his signature votive _(actors: Ramdev, the tailor)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/ramdev-pir.md) — (Ramdev hagiographic tradition, caubis paracha) [folk]
- **The forced, flawed second Bāhubali** — pressed to raise another colossus, the crippled Kalkuḍa cannot equal his first, and the second statue (the paddana's echo of the Vēṇūr Bāhubali) comes out smaller and flawed, sealing his ruin _(actors: Kalkuḍa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/kalkuda-kallurti.md) — (Kalkuḍa paddana; Vēṇūr statue, 1604 CE) [folk]
- **The forgotten betel pouch** — riding out against a Kodagu cattle-raid, Māndappan turns back home for his forgotten betel/pan pouch, the ill-omened return that hinges the tragedy _(actors: Māndappan, Chemmarathi)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/kathivanur-veeran.md) — (Kathivanur Veeran thottam-pāṭṭu)
- **The forty-veli field ploughed overnight** — set the impossible condition of tilling his master's vast field before leaving, Nandanar finds the forty-veli field ploughed by divine agency at dawn, and the astonished landlord releases him to make the pilgrimage _(actors: Nandanar, the landlord Vēdiyar, Śiva's gaṇas)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/nandanar-tirunalaippovar.md) — (Periya Purāṇam, Tirunāḷaippōvār Nāyaṉār Purāṇam)
- **The Gaṅgā comes to the dying Vidyāpati** — in the Maithili death-legend the aged poet halts short of the river and the Gaṅgā changes course to receive his last breath, the site remembered at Vidyapatinagar (Samastipur) _(actors: Vidyāpati, Gaṅgā)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ugna-mahadev-vidyapati.md) — (Maithili oral tradition; Vidyapatinagar local tradition) [folk]
- **The Gaṅgā-water revelation** — on a waterless road to Śivasiṃha's court the thirsty Vidyāpati tastes Gaṅgā-jala that Ugnā has secretly poured from his matted locks, and the servant is unmasked as Mahādeva _(actors: Vidyāpati, Śiva/Ugnā, Gaṅgā)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ugna-mahadev-vidyapati.md) — (Ugnā legend, Maithili oral tradition) [folk]
- **The garbo lamp-pot as cosmic womb** — A perforated earthen pot holding a lit lamp (garbha + dipa) is danced around in concentric circles as the cosmic womb holding the Devi's indwelling shakti _(actors: Devotees (esp. women), the Devi (Amba/Mataji))_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/garba-and-dandiya-raas.md) — (Gujarati garba tradition; etymology garbha-dipa)
- **The Garuda Purana's ratna-pariksha chapters are redacted** — The gemology section retelling Bala's sacrifice is placed c. 800–1000 CE, with the Agni Purana's parallel gem chapter (246) in the same medieval horizon [scholarly] _(actors: Purana redactors)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/bala-asura-navaratna-origin.md) — (Garuda Purana, Purva-khanda chs. ~68–70; Agni Purana 246)
- **The Gautamī-māhātmya compiled into the Brahma Purāṇa** — The ~105-chapter Godāvarī glorification, the Purāṇa's largest block, takes shape within the late compilation of the extant text (c. 10th–13th century CE and after) [scholarly] _(actors: anonymous Deccan redactors)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/gautami-mahatmya-tales-catalog.md) — (Brahma Purāṇa, vulgate adh. 70–175; Hazra and later scholarship)
- **The Gītā-Māhātmya composed as a Purāṇic appendix** — the eighteen chapter-glorification tales are added to the Padma Purāṇa's Uttara-khaṇḍa as a late devotional phala-śruti, long after the Gītā itself _(actors: anonymous Purāṇic redactors)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/bhagavad-gita/gita-mahatmya-tales.md) — (Padma Purāṇa, Uttara-khaṇḍa; [scholarly] late accretion)
- **The god hidden in the Gomatī** — pursued by the Dvārakā priests, Bodāṇā immerses the image in the Gomatī tank at Ḍākor on Kṛṣṇa's instruction _(actors: Bodāṇā, the Gugli Brāhmaṇas)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/dakor-ranchhodrai-bodana.md) — (Ḍākor Māhātmya)
- **The Goddess Fed Second to Her Son** — after Bāmākhepā eats Tārā's bhog and is beaten by the priests, the goddess appears in the patron-queen's dream refusing food until her hungry son is fed, so Bāmākhepā is henceforth fed before the Goddess _(actors: Bāmākhepā, Tārā, the Rāṇī)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/tarapith-tara-vashishtha-bamakhepa.md) — (Tārāpīṭha oral tradition) [folk] [oral tradition]
- **The gopuram leap** — in self-disgust he flings himself from the north gopuram of the Aruṇācaleśvarar temple to end his life _(actors: Aruṇagirinātar)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/arunagirinathar-tiruppugazh.md) — (hagiographic tradition; Tiruppugazh)
- **The great temple fire (30 November 1970 CE)** — fire destroys much of the complex while the sanctum and mūrti survive unharmed, and the temple is rebuilt under the Guruvayur Devaswom _(actors: Guruvayur Devaswom)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/guruvayur-krishna-kshetra.md) — (Documented history) [scholarly]
- **The hunter-kathā fixed as the Śivarātri charter [scholarly]** — the extant Śiva Purāṇa (c. 1000–1400 CE, Koṭirudra-saṁhitā), the medieval Kedāra-khaṇḍa of the Skanda Purāṇa, and the revised Garuḍa Purāṇa standardize the tale as the Mahā-Śivarātri vrata-māhātmya _(actors: Purāṇic compilers)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/shivaratri-hunter-legend.md) — (Śiva P. Koṭirudra 38–40; Skanda P. Kedāra-khaṇḍa 33; Garuḍa P. Ācāra-khaṇḍa)
- **The idol climbs the cart (1156 CE)** — moved by Bodāṇā's frailty, Kṛṣṇa-Raṇchhoḍrāī mounts his bullock-cart and leaves Dvārakā for Ḍākor on the traditional date V.S. 1212 _(actors: Kṛṣṇa-Raṇchhoḍrāī, Bodāṇā)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/dakor-ranchhodrai-bodana.md) — (Ḍākor Māhātmya)
- **The idol-flights across the Zuari** — as the Portuguese Old Conquests and the 1560 Goa Inquisition demolish the coastal shrines, mahājans secret the mūrtis of Mangeś (from Cortalim) and Śāntā-Durgā (from Quelossim) east into Ponda to rebuild worship _(actors: the mahājan/gāonkār communities, the Portuguese)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/goa-shantadurga-mangeshi-kuladevatas.md) — (documented history; temple tradition)
- **The Īḍu 36,000-paḍi is written down** — Nampiḷḷai's Śrīraṅgam lectures on the Tiruvāymoḻi are recorded by Vaḍakku Tiruvīdhi Piḷḷai, 13th c. [scholarly] _(actors: Nampiḷḷai, Vaḍakku Tiruvīdhi Piḷḷai)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/vedanta-desika-and-later-srivaishnava-acharyas.md) — (Wikipedia, Nampillai)
- **The immolation of the seventy** — true to their oath the seventy Veḷāḷa elders build a pyre and burn themselves, making 'the seventy of Palaiyanur' proverbial for a word kept unto death _(actors: the seventy Veḷāḷa elders)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/icakki-amman-nili-katai.md) — (Nīli Katai; Aṅkāḷaparamēcuvari tradition)
- **The impossible tests are set** — to prove his love the brahmin must cast off the sacred thread, eat beef, drink liquor, handle carcasses and tan hides — become a Chakkiliyar in full _(actors: Muttuppaṭṭaṉ, Bommakkā, Timmakkā, the sisters' father)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/muttuppattan-katai.md) — (Muttuppaṭṭaṉ Katai, vil pāṭṭu)
- **The Jagdambā sword passes to Britain (1875–76 CE)** — the Bhosale sword 'Jagdambā', popularly identified with Shivaji's Bhavānī sword, is presented to the Prince of Wales and remains in the British Royal Collection; the identification itself is contested _(actors: Kolhapur Chhatrapatis, Prince of Wales/Edward VII)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/tulja-bhavani.md) — (Wikipedia: Bhavani sword) [scholarly] [disputed]
- **The Jagdambā sword to Britain (1875–76)** — the Bhosale sword named Jagdambā, popularly identified with the Bhavānī sword, is presented to the Prince of Wales and enters the British Royal Collection _(actors: the Kolhapur Chhatrapatis, Prince of Wales)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/tuljapur-tulja-bhavani.md) — (Wikipedia, Bhavani sword) [scholarly] [disputed]
- **The kaḻu-tiruviḻā cult** — worshippers of the northern Tamil low castes plant a stake, sing the katai and dance the god down into a possessed medium, the gibbet transfigured into the sacred post at his stake festival _(actors: Kāttavarāyaṉ, the possessed medium, Paṟaiyar/Pallar devotees)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/kattavarayan-katai.md) — (Masilamani-Meyer; Tamil folk-cult tradition) [folk] [regional]
- **The Kaumārikā-khaṇḍa composed** — the Nandabhadra theodicy is set down as a sthala-māhātmya glorifying Mahīsāgara-Saṅgama near Cambay, a later medieval accretion to the composite Skanda Purāṇa _(actors: Kaumārikā-khaṇḍa redactors)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/nandabhadra-tested-faith.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa, Kaumārikā-khaṇḍa; [scholarly])
- **The Kaumārika-khaṇḍa retells the tale at Mahīsāgara-saṅgama** — the Skanda Purāṇa localizes Indradyumna's redemption to the Kumārikā-kṣetra confluence, re-using the epic upākhyāna as tīrtha-māhātmya _(actors: Indradyumna, Akūpāra)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/indradyumna-akupara-oldest-beings.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa, Kaumārika-khaṇḍa 7–11)
- **The killer reborn as the guarded merchant** — the murderer of the first birth returns as the trader Darśana Ceṭṭi (Āṉantaṉ of Kāverippūmpaṭṭiṉam), warded by a protective talisman against the pēy _(actors: Darśana Ceṭṭi/Āṉantaṉ, Nīli)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/icakki-amman-nili-katai.md) — (Nīli Katai)
- **The King Finds No Temple, Only the Saint** — Rājasiṃha rides to Tiruniṉṟavūr to see the rival shrine and finds no building at all, only the poor brahmin, and bows on learning the temple was built within the heart _(actors: Rājasiṃha, Pūcalār)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/pusalar-nayanar-mind-temple.md) — (Periya Purāṇam, Pūcalār Purāṇam)
- **The Kommu bards and the Ganga Jatara** — the cattle-war saga is sung across three months by Madiga Kommula-varu horn-bards and Golla minstrels to kommu, titti and talam at the Yadava goddess Ganga's village festivals, its fallen heroes worshipped as village deities _(actors: Kommula-varu bards, Golla Yadavas)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/katamaraju-katha.md) — (Narayanappa 2021)
- **The Lalitopākhyāna and Sahasranāma reach written form** — the Śākta appendix to the Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa carrying the thousand names is composed in the medieval Śrī-Vidyā efflorescence, commonly dated c. 13th–16th c. CE (some argue 9th–12th c.) [scholarly] [disputed] _(actors: anonymous Śrī-Vidyā redactors)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/lalita-sahasranama.md) — (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa, Uttara-bhāga appendix)
- **The lament of Aduna-Paduna** — Gopīcandra's two young queens break into the epic's most famous bilāp, mourning the living husband who abandons them for the jogī's road _(actors: Aduna, Paduna, Gopīcandra)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/gopichandra-maynamati-nath-epic.md) — (Gopīcandrer Gān) [folk] [oral tradition]
- **The last darśana at Bhawanipur — origin of the Ugna Mahādev temple** — Śiva consoles the grief-mad poet with a final appearance and remains present in the liṅga at Bhawanipur (Madhubani), the Ugnāsthān shrine thronged ever since at Śivarātri and Śrāvaṇa _(actors: Śiva, Vidyāpati)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ugna-mahadev-vidyapati.md) — (Ugnā legend; Bhawanipur temple tradition) [regional]
- **The learned maiden of Perinchellūr** — a prodigiously gifted Brahmin girl of the Maṇiyōṭṭu Mana near Taliparamba masters the Vedas and śāstra before her betrothal _(actors: the Brahmin maiden)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/muchilottu-bhagavathy.md) — (Muchilottu Bhagavathy thoṭṭam-pāṭṭu)
- **The Liṅga Purāṇa redacts the Dadhīci legend under Śiva** — the Śaiva Mahāpurāṇa (c. 5th–10th c. CE) reframes the pan-Hindu bone-giver's adamantine spine as a boon of Śiva and stages Viṣṇu's defeat, a sectarian charter for Sthāneśvara _(actors: Liṅga Purāṇa redactors, Sūta)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/kshupa-dadhicha-dispute.md) — (Liṅga Purāṇa, Pūrva-bhāga; [scholarly])
- **The Liṅga Purāṇa's Śveta-upākhyāna composed** — The Śveta chapter takes shape within the Pūrva-bhāga of the Liṅga Purāṇa, academically dated c. 5th–10th century CE with the doctrinal Pūrva-bhāga as the older stratum [scholarly] _(actors: Liṅga Purāṇa compilers)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/sage-shveta-and-yama.md) — (Liṅga Purāṇa, Pūrva-bhāga ch. 30; academic dating [scholarly])
- **The love of Āriyamālā** — Kāttavarāyaṉ sees the Brahmin virgin Āriyamālā and is seized by an impossible love, the untouchable-associated god of the burning-ground desiring the purest of the pure-born _(actors: Kāttavarāyaṉ, Āriyamālā)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/kattavarayan-katai.md) — (Kaṭṭavarāyacuvāmi Kataippāṭal) [oral tradition]
- **The low-caste performer worshipped as god** — once a year the Pulaya/Malayan performer, clad in areca-spathe and coconut fronds, is venerated by high-caste patrons as living Poṭṭan Daivam _(actors: theyyam performer, patrons)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/pottan-theyyam.md) — (North Malabar theyyam tradition)
- **The Madras Devadasis Act abolishes temple dedication** — devadāsī dedication outlawed after the anti-nautch decades (1947 CE) [scholarly] _(actors: Madras legislature, the devadāsī communities)_ → [details](../09-artifacts-symbols-and-arts/classical-dance-forms-and-ritual-theatre.md) — (Madras Devadasis Prevention of Dedication Act, 1947)
- **The maṅgal-kāvya literature of the goddess cluster** — Manasāmaṅgal, Caṇḍīmaṅgal (Mukundarāma c. 1590s), Śītalāmaṅgal and the Ṣaṣṭhī bratakathās, composed 15th–18th c. CE by named poets [scholarly] _(actors: Manasā, Maṅgala Caṇḍī, Śītalā, Ṣaṣṭhī, the maṅgal poets)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/gramadevata-shashthi-cross-links.md) — (Bengali maṅgal-kāvya corpus)
- **The mature muhūrta manuals composed** — Muhūrta-Mārtaṇḍa (16th c.) and Rāma Daivajña's Muhūrta-Cintāmaṇi (1600 CE) codify marriage/saṃskāra election, gaṇḍānta and Bhadrā rules [scholarly] _(actors: Rāma Daivajña, Nārāyaṇa Daivajña)_ → [details](../00-time-and-cosmology/muhurta-and-auspicious-timing.md) — (Muhūrta-Cintāmaṇi; Muhūrta-Mārtaṇḍa)
- **The mendicant turns the sisters-in-law against Tāpoi** — A wandering mendicant woman poisons the wives' minds; six strip Tāpoi to rags, feed her khuda (broken rice), and send her to herd goats, while the youngest sister-in-law alone stays kind _(actors: the mendicant woman, the six sisters-in-law, the kind youngest sister-in-law, Tāpoi)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/tapoi-khudurukuni-osha.md) — (Tāpoi kathā, Odia oral tradition)
- **The Mental Kumbhābhiṣeka and Pūcalār's Ascent** — Pūcalār performs the consecration of his mind-temple, Śiva attends true to His dream-word, and the saint at last reaches the feet of the Lord _(actors: Pūcalār, Śiva)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/pusalar-nayanar-mind-temple.md) — (Periya Purāṇam, Pūcalār Purāṇam)
- **The modern Nandā Devī Rāj Jāts (1905–2014)** — the c. 280-km twelve-yearly royal pilgrimage from Nauti to Homkund, led by the four-horned ram, is held in 1905, 1925, 1951, 1968, 1987, 2000 and 2014 (postponed from 2013 after the Kedarnath floods) _(actors: Kunwars of Kansuwa, Nauti Rājpurohits, the chausiṅgyā khāḍū)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/nanda-devi-and-uttarakhand-deities.md) — (Rāj Jāt ritual tradition [regional] [scholarly])
- **The monolithic Hanumān of Suchindram** — a ~18-foot Hanumān carved from a single granite block, once buried for protection and later re-consecrated, stands among India's tallest monolithic images of the god _(actors: Hanumān)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/suchindram-sthanumalayan.md) — (temple record) [regional]
- **The month-long Māgh recitation at Śālinadī** — Nepali devotees, chiefly women, keep the Svasthānī vow through Māgh with dawn bathing at Sankhu and a chapter read each night for marital welfare _(actors: Nepali vrata-keepers, Svasthānī)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/swasthani-brata-katha.md) — (Śālinadī vrata tradition; Birkenholtz 2018)
- **The Mughal illustrated parrot-books** — Akbar's atelier illustrates the Ṭūṭī-nāma under Mīr Sayyid ʿAlī and ʿAbd al-Ṣamad after 1556, the manuscript now largely in the Cleveland Museum of Art, with a second copy c. 1580 at Dublin _(actors: Akbar, Mīr Sayyid ʿAlī, ʿAbd al-Ṣamad)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/shuka-saptati.md) — (Ṭūṭī-nāma, Mughal recension)
- **The murder for gold (first birth)** — a merchant who weds a woman for her jewels sends her brother off for water and kills her for the treasure, and the brother hangs himself in grief _(actors: the merchant, Navaňjani/Alli, her brother)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/icakki-amman-nili-katai.md) — (Nīli Katai, vil pāṭṭu)
- **The Nāgapaṭṭinam golden Buddha funds Srirangam's third wall** — short of money for Raṅganātha's temple, Tirumaṅgai carries off a Buddhist vihāra's golden Buddha and melts its gold to build Srirangam's third enclosure-wall _(actors: Tirumaṅgai Āḻvār, Raṅganātha)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/tirumangai-alvar.md) — (Guruparampara Prabhāvam, Parakāla chapters)
- **The Nāgara-khaṇḍa compiled (13th–17th c. CE)** — the ~279-chapter Hāṭakeśvara-kṣetra-māhātmya is composed in strata and grafted onto the Skanda Purāṇa as a quasi-caste-purāṇa for the Vaḍnagar Nāgaras _(actors: the Nāgara-khaṇḍa compilers)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/vadnagar-hatakeshvara-kshetra.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa, Nāgara-khaṇḍa; AITM 11–12)
- **The Nārada Purāṇa's Uttarabhāga māhātmya stratum is compiled** — The Gaṅgā-tīrtha and vrata tale-cycle takes shape in the late, stratified Nārada Purāṇa — Hazra's 9th-century core with pre-11th-century accretions, Wilson dating the extant text as late as the 16th–17th century [scholarly] _(actors: anonymous Vaiṣṇava compilers)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/narada-purana-ganga-tales.md) — (R. C. Hazra; H. H. Wilson)
- **The navaratna is welded to the nine grahas** — Medieval jyotisha systematizes Bala's nine relic-gems into the planetary amulet (ruby–Surya, pearl–Chandra, … cat's-eye–Ketu), a later layer than the origin myth itself [scholarly] [late text] _(actors: jyotisha tradition)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/bala-asura-navaratna-origin.md) — (ratna-shastra and jyotisha tradition)
- **The Nawab's covered plate and the grant of Mantrālayam** — the Nawab of Adoni's test-platter of meat turns to fruits and flowers, and the saint receives the village of Manchāle on the Tuṅgabhadrā, mid-17th c. CE [traditional] [folk] _(actors: Rāghavendra Tīrtha, Siddi Masud Khan, Dīvān Venkaṇṇa)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/raghavendra-tirtha-and-the-dvaita-line.md) — (Mantrālayam maṭha tradition)
- **The Nellore grazing pact** — Katamaraju covenants with Nallasiddhi, Telugu-Choda king of Nellore, for pasture in the Nallamala forest, agreeing (one version) to render every male calf born as tribute _(actors: Katamaraju, Nallasiddhi)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/katamaraju-katha.md) — (Katamaraju Kathalu; Narayanappa 2021)
- **The night boundary-patrol of Ayyaṉār** — Ayyaṉār rides out on horseback by night around the village limits to drive off demons, epidemic, drought and thieves _(actors: Ayyaṉār (kāval deyvam); the village; pēy/picācu spirits)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/ayyanar-sastha.md) — (Tamil grāma-devatā / folk tradition)
- **The once-a-year orchid garland of the Salhesh phulbārī** — At the annual Salhesh melā of Mahisautha (Jur Sital, Maithili new year), a rare orchid in the mālin's flower-garden is said to bloom for a single day, read by devotees as Kusumā Mālin's garland still offered to Rājā Salhesh _(actors: Kusumā Mālin, Rājā Salhesh, Dusādh pilgrims)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/maithili-oral-epics-salhesh-dina-bhadri.md) — (Salhesh melā of Mahisautha, Siraha) [folk]
- **The oxen halt and Sālāsar is founded** — the image is carried from Āsotā by bullock-cart and the shrine is raised where the oxen stop, Mohandās installing and serving the god as founder-saint _(actors: Mohandās, the bullock-cart oxen, Bālājī)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/mehandipur-salasar-balaji-hanuman-shrines.md) — (Sālāsar Bālājī tradition) [folk] [regional]
- **The paddanas recorded and the Bhūta cult studied** — Tuḷu daiva-paddanas are transcribed from the 19th–20th c. and systematically analysed in Peter J. Claus's fieldwork and *Oral Epics of India* (1989), framing bhūta-worship as a pre-Vedic Dravidian substratum _(actors: Peter J. Claus, Tuḷuva mediums)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/panjurli-guliga-tulu-daivas.md) — (Oral Epics of India, 1989) [scholarly]
- **The Pañcasakhā circle flourishes at Purī** — five Odia poet-friends develop jñāna-miśra bhakti and the Śūnya Puruṣa doctrine, overlapping Caitanya's Purī years 1510–1533 [regional] [scholarly] _(actors: Balarāma Dāsa, Jagannātha Dāsa, Acyutānanda Dāsa, Yaśovanta Dāsa, Ananta Dāsa)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/odia-literature-sarala-and-panchasakha.md) — (Odia literary tradition)
- **The panch-pir couplet canonizes the five pirs** — Rajasthani oral tradition fixes the roster Pabuji, Harbhuji, Ramdev, Mangaliya Meha and Gogaji as the pancho pir of Marwar [folk] [regional] _(actors: Pabuji, Harbhuji, Ramdev Pir, Mehaji Mangaliya, Gogaji)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/tejaji.md) — (Rajasthani panch-pir couplet, oral tradition)
- **The parrot and the broken pact** — Kundamadevi's tame parrot is shot in the grazing-forest, she sends hunters to slaughter the Yadava cattle, and Katamaraju kills the hunters, refuses the grazing-tax and fortifies at Yerragaddapadu _(actors: Kundamadevi, Padma Raghava, Katamaraju)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/katamaraju-katha.md) — (Katamaraju Kathalu, parrot episode)
- **The parrot errand for the Pārijāta** — set to fetch the celestial Pārijāta flower, Aruṇagiri projects his soul into a parrot's body to fly to Devaloka _(actors: Aruṇagirinātar, Sambandāṇḍāṉ)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/arunagirinathar-tiruppugazh.md) — (hagiographic tradition)
- **The Pātāla-khaṇḍa's Puruṣottama māhātmya is composed** — the Rāma-aśvamedha stratum of the Padma Purāṇa carrying the Ratnagrīva cycle takes shape as a late devotional layer, within the Purāṇa's c. 4th–15th c. CE compilation [scholarly] _(actors: Padma Purāṇa redactors)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/ratnagriva-purushottama-kshetra.md) — (Padma Purāṇa, Pātāla-khaṇḍa; scholarly dating)
- **The perpetual pal-payasam** — the temple's milk-rice pudding is offered to the deity and ladled free to all pilgrims daily, the never-exhausted repayment of the chessboard debt _(actors: Ambalappuzha temple, pilgrims)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/ambalappuzha-krishna-palpayasam.md) — (Ambalappuzha temple tradition) [regional]
- **The peśī of the possessed** — at Mehandīpur the possessing _pret_ speaking through a sufferer is compelled before the court to name itself, confess, and depart, the visible core of the exorcism cult _(actors: the possessed, the pret, Bālājī's court)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/mehandipur-salasar-balaji-hanuman-shrines.md) — (Mehandīpur temple tradition) [folk] [disputed]
- **The petition-and-bell court at Chitai** — the Golu temple near Almora institutionalizes divine justice: written petitions (traditionally on stamped judicial paper) are tied to the railings and granted prayers repaid with brass bells _(actors: Golu Devtā, Kumaoni petitioners)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/nanda-devi-and-uttarakhand-deities.md) — (Chitai sthala tradition [regional])
- **The poet who took the deity's name** — the Śaiva-Siddhānta poet-philosopher Tāyumāṉavar (c. 1705–1742 CE), a court official at Nāyaka-era Trichy, composes his Tamil verse and takes his name from the Rock Fort Lord (not to be confused with the god) _(actors: Tāyumāṉavar)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/tiruchirappalli-rockfort-thayumanavar.md) — (Tamil literary history) [scholarly]
- **The Ponda temples rebuilt** — the Śāntā-Durgā temple rises at Kavlem under Maratha patronage of Chhatrapati Śāhū (c. 1730s) and the Mangeśī complex at Priol through the 18th century, fixing the pantheon's living topography _(actors: the mahājans, Chhatrapati Śāhū)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/goa-shantadurga-mangeshi-kuladevatas.md) — (temple administration; documented history)
- **The Pūrṇimā padayātrā** — Ḍākor becomes a mass full-moon foot-pilgrimage centre, its Śarad and Phālgunī Pūrṇimā throngs re-enacting Bodāṇā's own journeys _(actors: the pilgrims of Ḍākor)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/dakor-ranchhodrai-bodana.md) — (temple tradition)
- **The Puṣṭimārga aesthetic of *mādhurya*** — The hymn enshrines Vallabha's choice of Kṛṣṇa's intimate sweetness over his majesty (*aiśvarya*), the devotional keynote of the Path of Grace _(actors: Vallabhācārya, Śrīnāthjī devotees)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/madhurashtakam.md) — (Śuddhādvaita / Puṣṭimārga theology)
- **The question of pain and pleasure** — trapped by the scholars, she answers that the greatest pain is childbirth and the greatest pleasure is the union of love — correctly, and fatally _(actors: the Brahmin maiden, the Brahmin scholars)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/muchilottu-bhagavathy.md) — (Muchilottu Bhagavathy thoṭṭam-pāṭṭu)
- **The Ramnagar Rāmlīlā is instituted** — the Maharajas of Banaras establish the 31-day town-wide Mānas enactment across the Gaṅgā from Varanasi (early 19th c. CE) [scholarly] _(actors: Maharaja Udit Narayan Singh, the svarūpa boy-actors)_ → [details](../09-artifacts-symbols-and-arts/classical-dance-forms-and-ritual-theatre.md) — (Rāmcaritmānas; Banaras court tradition)
- **The replacement image at Dvārakā** — Kṛṣṇa consoles the departing priests with a second idol found in the Gomatī creek at Dvārakā, fixing Ḍākor's claim to the original _(actors: Kṛṣṇa, the Gugli Brāhmaṇas)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/dakor-ranchhodrai-bodana.md) — (Ḍākor Māhātmya) [disputed]
- **The republic keeps the living goddess** — after the abolition of the Nepali monarchy in 2008 CE, the President of Nepal attends Indra Jātrā and receives the Kumārī's tikā, the institution outliving the kingship it sanctified _(actors: the Royal Kumārī, the President of Nepal)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/nepal-kumari-taleju-machhindranath.md) — (modern record) [scholarly]
- **The ring recognized** — Māṇikā shows the ring to Puruṣottama Deva, who knows it for Jagannātha's own and understands the Lords have gone before him to fight his war _(actors: Puruṣottama Deva, Māṇikā)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/kanchi-kaveri-jagannatha-legend.md) — (Kāñcī-Kāverī; Mādaḷā Pāñji) [regional]
- **The Roopkund catastrophe (c. 9th century CE [scholarly])** — legend holds King Jasdhaval of Kanauj and Queen Balampā perished with their party in Nandā's hailstorm for violating the pilgrimage's austerity; radiocarbon dating places many skeletons c. 850 CE, while 2019 aDNA found multiple groups including a c. 17th–19th-c. Mediterranean cohort [disputed] _(actors: Jasdhaval, Balampā, Nandā Devī)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/nanda-devi-and-uttarakhand-deities.md) — (Rāj Jāt legend [folk]; Harney et al. 2019 [scholarly])
- **The samādhi the Ratha still pauses at** — Sālabega is buried on the Grand Road, and to this day the Jagannātha chariot halts each year at the Muslim-born devotee's tomb, the one such grave on the sacred processional route _(actors: Sālabega, Jagannātha)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/jagannatha-bhakta-legends.md) — (Purī temple observance; Mādaḷā Pāñji lore) [regional]
- **The sand-liṅga bathed in milk** — the boy heaps a liṅga from river-sand and pours the cows' surplus milk over it in daily abhiṣeka, worshipping in total absorption _(actors: Vicāra Śarman, Śiva as sand-liṅga)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/chandesvara-chandikeshvara.md) — (Periya Purāṇam, Caṇṭēcura Nāyaṉār Purāṇam)
- **The Sanskrit kathā enters the Skanda Purāṇa's Revā-khaṇḍa** — the five-chapter Satyanārāyaṇa Vrata Kathā, absent from older Skanda manuscripts, is interpolated under the Purāṇa's name in the early-modern period, with its precedence relative to Satya Pīr unresolved _(actors: anonymous redactors)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/satyanarayana-vrata-katha.md) — (Skanda P., Revā-khaṇḍa; manuscript evidence) [scholarly] [late text] [interpolation] [disputed]
- **The Satya Pīr pāñcālī corpus is composed (16th–18th c.)** — over a hundred Hindu and Muslim poets, among them Phakir Rām, Rāmeśvar Bhaṭṭācārya and Bhāratcandra Rāy, compose Bengali pāñcālīs of Satya Pīr carrying the woodcutter and merchant tale-blocks _(actors: Phakir Rām, Rāmeśvar Bhaṭṭācārya, Bhāratcandra Rāy, Kṛṣṇahari Dās)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/satyanarayana-vrata-katha.md) — (Bengali Satya Pīr pāñcālīs, 16th–18th c.) [scholarly] [regional]
- **The sculptor mutilated to prevent duplication** — after the colossus is raised, Vīra Pāṇḍya has Kalkuḍa's right hand (and in fuller tellings a leg and an eye) severed so his hands can never make an equal Gommaṭa for any rival patron _(actors: Kalkuḍa, Vīra Pāṇḍya)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/kalkuda-kallurti.md) — (Kalkuḍa paddana) [folk]
- **The secret spoken — Ugnā vanishes** — Vidyāpati's wife beats the servant in a household rage; unable to watch Śiva struck, the poet cries out that Ugnā is Mahādeva, and the god vanishes the instant the vow of secrecy breaks _(actors: Vidyāpati's wife, Śiva/Ugnā, Vidyāpati)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ugna-mahadev-vidyapati.md) — (Ugnā legend, Maithili oral tradition) [folk]
- **The seven brothers sail on the boita** — After the parents' deaths, Tāpoi's seven brothers depart on a long trading voyage across the sea, entrusting her to their seven wives _(actors: the seven sādhaba brothers, Tāpoi, the seven sisters-in-law)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/tapoi-khudurukuni-osha.md) — (Tāpoi kathā, Khudurukuṇī Oṣā tradition)
- **The seven-kanda Battle of Yerragaddapadu** — Nallasiddhi invades with a huge army for a week-long war in which even the Yadava cows and oxen fight, led by the single-horned Vishnu-cow Bollavu _(actors: Katamaraju, Nallasiddhi, Bollavu, Kariyavula Raju, Aithama Raju)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/katamaraju-katha.md) — (Katamaraju Kathalu, Battle of Yerragaddapadu, 7 kandas)
- **The seventy Veḷāḷar stand surety** — Palaiyanur's council of seventy Vellala elders, moved by her tears, disarm the merchant of his talisman and swear that all seventy will burn if harm befalls him _(actors: the seventy Veḷāḷa elders, the merchant, Nīli)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/icakki-amman-nili-katai.md) — (Nīli Katai)
- **The short manuals Ātmabodha, Tattvabodha and Aparokṣānubhūti spread under Śaṅkara's name** — a 68-verse primer, a prose catechism, and a 144-verse re-reading of yoga's fifteen limbs as Advaitic inquiry form the entry rungs of the Vedānta curriculum [late text] [disputed] _(actors: Adi Shankara (attrib.))_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/darshanas/vedanta-prakarana-granthas.md) — (Ātmabodha; Tattvabodha; Aparokṣānubhūti)
- **The Sinhala gammaḍuva ritual cycle for Pattini** — Sinhala Buddhist villages honor Pattini, one of the guardian deities, in the post-harvest gammaḍuva, singing the pantis kōlmura and enacting the mango-birth and Palaṅga's resurrection _(actors: Pattini, Palaṅga, gammaḍuva ritualists)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/silappatikaram-kannagi-pattini.md) — (Sinhala Pattini texts, per Obeyesekere) [oral tradition] [regional]
- **The sisters-in-law punished before the veiled 'goddess'** — Tāpoi is seated veiled as Maṅgaḷā and each wife summoned to worship her; the six cruel sisters-in-law have their noses cut off while the kind youngest is spared and honored _(actors: Tāpoi, the seven brothers, the six sisters-in-law, the kind youngest sister-in-law)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/tapoi-khudurukuni-osha.md) — (Tāpoi kathā, Odia oral tradition [folk])
- **The Skanda Purāṇa's Vaiśākha version is composed** — the medieval Vaiśākhamāsa-māhātmya records the alternative conversion of the hunter by Śaṅkha and the rebirth as Vālmīki [scholarly] [late text] _(actors: Skanda Purāṇa compilers)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/valmiki-ratnakara-conversion.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa, Vaiṣṇava-khaṇḍa, Vaiśākhamāsa-māhātmya 17–21)
- **The soul trapped in the parrot** — Sambandāṇḍāṉ has the abandoned body cremated, so the returning saint remains in the parrot form — why his Tiruvaṇṇāmalai icon is a green parrot _(actors: Aruṇagirinātar, Sambandāṇḍāṉ)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/arunagirinathar-tiruppugazh.md) — (hagiographic tradition; temple cult)
- **The Suchindram Kaimukku ordeal** — under Travancore rule the temple was the seat of a trial by ordeal in which the accused dipped a hand in boiling ghee to prove innocence, abolished in the modern reform era _(actors: Travancore administration)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/suchindram-sthanumalayan.md) — (temple/administrative record) [scholarly]
- **The Suchindram Thanumalayan temple built** — the standing Trimūrti temple near Kanyākumārī is founded c. 9th c. CE (Āy/Cēra era) and expanded under Vijayanagara-Nāyaka and Travancore patronage in the 16th–17th c. _(actors: Āy/Cēra patrons, Nāyaka and Travancore rulers)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/suchindram-sthanumalayan.md) — (temple epigraphy) [scholarly]
- **The Śuka corpus takes textual shape** — the Mokṣadharma Śuka-carita is fixed within the Mahābhārata (c. 200 BCE–300 CE), the Bhāgavata Purāṇa making Śuka its narrator is composed c. 8th–10th century CE, and the Devī Bhāgavata and Yoga Vāsiṣṭha retellings follow c. 9th–14th century CE [scholarly] _(actors: anonymous redactors, Purāṇa composers)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/shuka-shukadeva.md) — (Mahābhārata XII; Bhāgavata Purāṇa; Devī Bhāgavata; Yoga Vāsiṣṭha)
- **The Śukasaptati reaches its recensions** — the Sanskrit parrot-cycle assumes its surviving shape by the 12th century CE in two parallel recensions, the plainer simplicior and the ornamented ornatior, descended from a lost common archetype _(actors: anonymous redactors, Cintāmaṇi Bhaṭṭa [disputed])_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/shuka-saptati.md) — (Śukasaptati, simplicior & ornatior)
- **The svayambhu rock of Mehandīpur Bālājī** — a self-manifest relief of child-form Hanumān emerges from the Arāvallī rock on the Dausa–Karauli border and becomes North India's foremost exorcism-court for the afflicted (_saṅkaṭ-wāle_) _(actors: Bālājī / Hanumān, the founding Mahant)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/mehandipur-salasar-balaji-hanuman-shrines.md) — (Mehandīpur Bālājī temple tradition) [folk]
- **The sweeper-king refused a bride** — the Kāñcī king, seeing the Gajapati emperor Puruṣottama Deva sweep the chariots at the Chera Paṅharā, scorns him as a menial and breaks the betrothal of his daughter Padmāvatī _(actors: Puruṣottama Deva, the Kāñcī king, Padmāvatī)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/kanchi-kaveri-jagannatha-legend.md) — (Mādaḷā Pāñji; Kāñcī-Kāverī) [regional]
- **The Tanjore Quartet codifies the Bharatanāṭyam mārgam** — Chinnayya, Ponnayya, Sivanandam and Vadivelu fix the concert format at Serfoji II's court (early 19th c. CE) [scholarly] _(actors: the Tanjore Quartet, Serfoji II)_ → [details](../09-artifacts-symbols-and-arts/classical-dance-forms-and-ritual-theatre.md) — (Tanjore court tradition)
- **The temple tank dug overnight** — by Śiva's grace Nandanar single-handedly excavates the Tiruppuṅkūr teppakkuḷam in one night so the god may have ablution water _(actors: Nandanar, Śiva)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/nandanar-tirunalaippovar.md) — (Periya Purāṇam, Tirunāḷaippōvār Nāyaṉār Purāṇam)
- **The Teṅkalai–Vaḍakalai split hardens into rival kalais** — doctrinal positions of the 14th c. crystallize into temple-controlling sects through the 15th–18th c., climaxing in colonial-era lawsuits [scholarly] _(actors: Teṅkalai, Vaḍakalai)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/vedanta-desika-and-later-srivaishnava-acharyas.md) — (Appadurai, Worship and Conflict under Colonial Rule)
- **The Thomas Munro apparition at Mantrālayam** — legend holds the entombed saint emerged to converse (visibly to Munro alone) with the Collector of Bellary, who confirmed the maṭha's endowment in 1800 CE [folk] [disputed] _(actors: Rāghavendra Tīrtha, Sir Thomas Munro)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/raghavendra-tirtha-and-the-dvaita-line.md) — (Mantrālayam maṭha tradition; popular accounts)
- **The Three Sangams convene at Madurai** — By legend three successive literary academies meet at Madurai under Pāṇḍya patronage over ~9,990 years, the first two lost to floods (Kumari Kandam), only the third surviving _(actors: Pāṇḍya kings, Tamil poets)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/tamil-canon-sangam-and-bhakti.md) — (Iṟaiyaṉār Akapporuḷ commentary)
- **The Tiruppugazh composed** — Aruṇagiri sings thousands of chandam-metered hymns to Murugan across his shrines, of which some 1,300+ survive of a claimed 16,000 _(actors: Aruṇagirinātar, Murugan)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/arunagirinathar-tiruppugazh.md) — (Tiruppugazh)
- **The toe-ring that could not be lifted** — Kaliyan strips the bridegroom's jewels but his hand freezes on the Lord's toe-ring (metti), which will not yield, revealing the groom as Viṣṇu himself _(actors: Kaliyan, Viṣṇu)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/tirumangai-alvar.md) — (Guruparampara Prabhāvam, Parakāla chapters)
- **The tongue-bite and death of Tejaji at Sursura** — finding no unwounded spot, the serpent bites Teja's offered tongue; he dies on Bhadrapada shukla dashami (trad. 1103 CE), Pemal becomes sati, and the naga's boon founds the tanti snakebite cult [folk] [disputed] _(actors: Tejaji, the naga, Pemal, Lilan)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/tejaji.md) — (Tejaji katha, Sursura sthala tradition)
- **The twenty-day Brahmotsavam** — Kāṇipākam observes one of the longest Gaṇeśa festivals, a roughly twenty/twenty-one-day Vināyaka Brahmotsavam from Gaṇeśa Caturthī with processional vāhanas and rathotsava _(actors: Varasiddhi Vināyaka, pilgrims)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/kanipakam-varasiddhi-vinayaka.md) — (temple tradition) [regional]
- **The twin dreams and Mohandās** — the Āsotā Ṭhākur is commanded in a dream to send the image to Sālāsar while the saint Mohandās Mahārāj sees Hanumān the same night with the icon's exact marks, doubly sanctioning the transfer _(actors: the Āsotā Ṭhākur, Mohandās Mahārāj, Hanumān)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/mehandipur-salasar-balaji-hanuman-shrines.md) — (Sālāsar Bālājī tradition, Mohandās legend) [folk]
- **The two-birds verse enters the Upanishads** — RV 1.164.20 is taken over verbatim by the Mundaka (3.1.1-2, non-dual reading) and Shvetashvatara (4.6-7, theistic reading) Upanishads [scholarly] _(actors: Dirghatamas (verse-author), Upanishadic redactors)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/dirghatamas.md) — (Mundaka Upanishad 3.1.1-2; Shvetashvatara Upanishad 4.6-7)
- **The Upamanyu frame enters the written canon** — The Anuśāsana Upamanyu–Sahasranāma block is a late Śaiva stratum of the epic (c. 200 BCE–300 CE), and the Śiva Purāṇa saṃhitās that enthrone him as frame-sage date c. 10th–14th c. CE [scholarly] _(actors: epic and Purāṇic redactors)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/upamanyu.md) — (Mahābhārata, Anuśāsana Parva; Śiva Purāṇa)
- **The Vajranābha romance enters the Harivaṃśa vulgate** — the episode, absent from the BORI Critical Edition's constituted text (relegated to Appendix I), is judged a late accretion of roughly the mid-first millennium CE, yet preserves the earliest named play, cast-list and vidūṣaka in Sanskrit narrative [scholarly] [late text] _(actors: Harivaṃśa redactors)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/pradyumna-prabhavati-vajranabha.md) — (Harivaṃśa, BORI CE Appendix I)
- **The varṇa-verse interpolation debate** — Indologists from Colebrooke and Max Müller onward flag RV 10.90.12 (or the whole hymn) as late, while reformist and critical readers from Dayānanda to Ambedkar contest its meaning [scholarly] [disputed] _(actors: Colebrooke, Max Müller, Dayānanda Sarasvatī, B. R. Ambedkar)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/purusha-sukta.md) — (Ṛgveda, 10.90.12)
- **The Vel writes 'Muttai taru'** — Murugan inscribes the seed of song on Aruṇagiri's tongue with the tip of his Vēl and gives the opening words 'Muttait taru', from which the first Tiruppugazh pours out _(actors: Murugan, Aruṇagirinātar)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/arunagirinathar-tiruppugazh.md) — (Tiruppugazh, opening hymn)
- **The Vetāla cycle fixed in Kashmiri Sanskrit** — Kṣemendra's Bṛhatkathāmañjarī (c. 1037 CE) and Somadeva's Kathāsaritsāgara (c. 1070 CE) preserve the oldest surviving texts of the Vetāla-pañcaviṃśati, from Guṇāḍhya's lost Bṛhatkathā [scholarly] _(actors: Somadeva, Kṣemendra, Guṇāḍhya)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/vetala-panchavimshati-vikram-and-betal.md) — (Kathāsaritsāgara XII, Taraṅgas 75–99)
- **The village as bounded sacred enclosure** — Each village is conceived as a protected body with boundary/guardian gods at the edge and mother goddesses as its life, fertility and fever _(actors: Boundary guardian gods; village mother goddesses)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/gramadevatas-village-deities.md) — (Henry Whitehead, The Village Gods of South India (1916); grāmadevatā studies)
- **The Vīraṭṭāṉam shrines sung in the Tēvāram** — the eight feat-temples enter the record as a set through the hymns of Appar, Sambandar and Sundarar, all counted among the 275 Paadal Petra Sthalams _(actors: Appar, Sambandar, Sundarar)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/ashta-virattanam-eight-feat-shrines.md) — (Tēvāram; Periya Purāṇam) [scholarly c. 6th–9th c. CE]
- **The Viṣṇu Purāṇa reaches its received form, carrying the parable [scholarly]** — Academic dating places the Viṣṇu Purāṇa's composition between c. 400 BCE and c. 450 CE, making the Ṛbhu–Nidāgha chapters a key pre-Śaṅkara witness to Purāṇic non-dual teaching _(actors: Purāṇic compilers, Parāśara–Maitreya frame)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ribhu-nidagha-vishnu-purana.md) — (Viṣṇu Purāṇa, II.15–16; scholarly dating)
- **The Viṣṇudharmottara redacted** — [scholarly] c. 400–600 CE the encyclopedic khila of the Viṣṇu Purāṇa takes shape, likely in Kashmir / the north-Indian northwest per Priyabala Shah, its arts book c. 5th–7th c. CE _(actors: anonymous compilers, Priyabala Shah ed.)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/vishnudharmottara-purana.md) — (Priyabala Shah, critical edition)
- **The Viśvanātha temple vindication of the Mānas** — Kāśī's paṇḍits, hostile to scripture in Awadhi, have the Mānas laid beneath the Sanskrit scriptures in the Viśvanātha sanctum overnight; it is found on top, endorsed "satyaṃ śivaṃ sundaram" by Śiva _(actors: Tulsīdās, Kāśī paṇḍits, Śiva)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/tulsidas.md) — (Mūla Gosāīṃ Carita; Priyādās on Bhaktamāl [folk])
- **The Vivaraṇa school named** — Padmapāda's Pañcapādikā, elaborated by Prakāśātman's Pañcapādikā-Vivaraṇa, founds the line holding Brahman itself the locus of one avidyā, the jīva a reflection, and mahāvākya-knowledge alone liberating _(actors: Padmapāda, Prakāśātman)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/later-advaita-acharyas.md) — (Pañcapādikā-Vivaraṇa) [scholarly]
- **The Vivekacūḍāmaṇi enters circulation under Śaṅkara's name** — the ~580-verse guru–disciple 'Crest-Jewel of Discrimination' (brahma satyaṃ jagan mithyā) becomes the most famous Advaita manual; date and authorship post-Śaṅkara and unsettled [disputed] [scholarly] _(actors: Adi Shankara (attrib.))_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/darshanas/vedanta-prakarana-granthas.md) — (Vivekacūḍāmaṇi 3, 20)
- **The widows' lament and deaths** — Bommakkā and Timmakkā find the body, sing the ballad's set-piece lament and follow their husband in death, joining him in the cult _(actors: Bommakkā, Timmakkā, Muttuppaṭṭaṉ)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/muttuppattan-katai.md) — (Muttuppaṭṭaṉ Katai, vil pāṭṭu)
- **The wives of Keteus contend to burn (316 BCE)** — Greek historians record the two wives of the Indian general Keteus competing for the honor of the pyre, the younger prevailing — the earliest dated report of the practice [scholarly] _(actors: Keteus's wives, Diodorus Siculus, Hieronymus of Cardia)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/sati-jauhar-and-the-immolation-rites.md) — (Diodorus Siculus, Bibliotheca Historica 19)
- **The Yādava-era temple** — the standing shrine on the Yamunācalā, entered by descending flights to a sunken court, is assigned to the Yādava period (c. 12th–13th c.
