CE) with later Maratha rebuilding _(actors: the Yādava and Maratha patrons)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/tuljapur-tulja-bhavani.md) — (Wikipedia, Tulja Bhavani Temple) [scholarly]
- **Third jauhar of Chittor (1568 CE)** — During Akbar's siege the women of Chittorgarh perform the last of the fort's three great jauhars before the men's final śāka ride and the fort's fall [scholarly] _(actors: Akbar, the Rajputs of Chittor)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/sati-jauhar-and-the-immolation-rites.md) — (Mughal chronicles, Abu'l-Faẓl)
- **Tipu Sultan's invasion and the idol's evacuation (1789–1792 CE)** — during the Mysorean thrust into Malabar the utsava image is moved to Ambalappuzha and the main mūrti concealed, with worship restored after 1792 _(actors: Tipu Sultan, Guruvāyūr priests)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/guruvayur-krishna-kshetra.md) — (Temple chronicles) [scholarly]
- **Tirtha Empul holy spring consecrated** — the Warmadewa court founds Bali's great purification-spring temple, whose legend has Indra pierce the earth for amṛta against the tyrant Mayadanawa _(actors: Indra, Mayadanawa)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/greater-india-sacred-geography.md) — ([scholarly] 962 CE; [regional] legend)
- **Tīrtha-yātrā Parva stratum composed** — the oldest systematic gazetteer of Hindu fords takes textual shape within the epic, c. 400 BCE–400 CE [scholarly] _(actors: epic redactors)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/tirtha-and-tirtha-yatra.md) — (Mahābhārata, Vana Parva 80–156)
- **Tirupati laḍḍu — prasāda as protected heritage** — The Śrīvāri laḍḍu of Veṅkaṭeśvara, cooked in the Poṭu kitchen, receives a Geographical Indication tag in 2009, formalizing a mass annadāna economy _(actors: Tirumala temple)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/anna-naivedya-and-prasada.md) — (temple record; GI Registry 2009)
- **Tiruppāṇ carried on Lōka Sāraṅga's shoulders** — struck by the priest's stone, the bard-saint is borne into the Śrīraṅgam sanctum on the archaka's shoulders, sings the Amalanādipirāṉ foot-to-face, and merges into Raṅganātha _(actors: Tiruppāṇ Āḻvār, Lōka Sāraṅga Muni, Raṅganātha)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/alvar-hagiographies-guruparampara.md) — (Divya Sūri Caritam, Tiruppāṇ account)
- **Traditional immolation of Nārāyaṇī Devī, Rānī Satī (c. 1295 CE)** — Rajasthani tradition dates the merchant's wife whose immolation founded the Rānī Satī Dādī-jī cult of Jhunjhunu, the largest living satī-mātā worship [folk] [regional] _(actors: Nārāyaṇī Devī, the Marwari Agrawal community)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/sati-jauhar-and-the-immolation-rites.md) — (Rānī Satī temple tradition, Jhunjhunu)
- **Tribhuvaneśvara worshipped as Harihara** — The Lingarāja swayambhū liṅga is worshipped simultaneously as Śiva and Viṣṇu (Harihara), receiving both bilva and tulasī _(actors: Tribhuvaneśvara (Śiva-Viṣṇu/Harihara), Vaiṣṇava and Śaiva devotees)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/lingaraja-bhubaneswar-ekamra-kshetra.md) — (Ekāmra Purāṇa; Brahma Purāṇa)
- **Trivikramasena outlasts the vetāla's riddles** — carrying the corpse-spirit from the śiṃśapā tree twenty-four times, the king answers each riddle-tale, falls rightly silent at the last, and on the vetāla's warning beheads the treacherous mendicant Kṣāntiśīla _(actors: Trivikramasena/Vikramāditya, the vetāla, Kṣāntiśīla)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/brihatkatha-kathasaritsagara-and-vikramaditya-cycle.md) — (Kathāsaritsāgara XII, Vetāla-pañcaviṃśati) [late text]
- **Tukārām born into Vārkarī devotion** — Tukārām, a Śūdra grocer-cultivator of Dehu, is born to Bolhobā and Kanakāī into a Viṭṭhal-devotee family, inheriting Vārkarī devotion _(actors: Tukārām, Bolhobā, Kanakāī)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/tukaram.md) — (Wikipedia, Tukaram; New World Encyclopedia)
- **Tulsīdās begins the Rāmcaritmānas** — in 1574 CE at Ayodhyā, Tulsīdās starts the Avadhi retelling that anchors saguṇa Rāma-bhakti across North India [scholarly] _(actors: Tulsīdās)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/rama.md) — (Rāmcaritmānas)
- **Tulsīdās frames the Rāmcaritmānas on Satī's doubt (1574 CE)** — Composing the Mānas, Tulsīdās makes the Satī-test the motivating frame: Pārvatī, recalling her former birth's doubt, asks Śiva for the Rāma-story that becomes the poem [scholarly] _(actors: Tulsīdās, Śiva, Pārvatī)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/sati-tests-rama.md) — (Rāmcaritmānas, Bāl-kāṇḍa)
- **Tulsīdās retells the Nārada-moha in the Rāmcaritmānas** — from 1574 CE the Bāl-Kāṇḍ recasts the tale (princess Viśvamohinī) as a cause of Rāma's descent, narrated by Śiva to Pārvatī _(actors: Tulsīdās, Nārada, Viṣṇu, Śiva)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/narada-moha-svayamvara.md) — (Rāmcaritmānas, Bāl-Kāṇḍ)
- **Uḍupi Kṛṣṇa Maṭha — the free meal and Kanakadāsa's window** — Madhva's foundation institutionalizes perpetual caste-blind feeding; the kanakana-kiṇḍi window memorializes the low-caste saint for whom the image is said to have turned _(actors: Madhva, Kanakadāsa)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/anna-naivedya-and-prasada.md) — (Mādhva tradition, c. 13th c. CE)
- **UNESCO inscribes Durga Puja in Kolkata (2021 CE)** — Durga Puja in Kolkata is inscribed on UNESCO's Representative List of the Intangible Cultural Heritage of Humanity, cited for its fusion of religion, craft and public art _(actors: UNESCO, Kolkata pūjā committees)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/durga-puja-bengal.md) — (UNESCO ICH listing, 2021) [scholarly]
- **Uṇṇiyārcca routs the Nāṭāpuram rowdies** — the warrior-sister of the Puttūram house, barred on the road to the Aḷḷimalarkāvu festival by a gang of Jōnaka toughs, cuts her own way through when her husband hesitates _(actors: Uṇṇiyārcca, Kuññirāman)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/vadakkan-pattukal-northern-ballads.md) — (Vaṭakkan Pāṭṭukaḷ, Uṇṇiyārcca pāṭṭu)
- **Upaniṣadic and Purāṇic uptake of the Puruṣa theology** — Śvetāśvatara quotes the "great Puruṣa beyond darkness" verse and the Bhāgavata elaborates the limb-cosmology into the virāṭ-puruṣa [scholarly] _(actors: Upaniṣadic teachers, Purāṇic compilers)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/purusha-sukta.md) — (Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad, 3.8; Bhāgavata Purāṇa, 2.5–2.6)
- **Utpaladeva composes the Īśvara-pratyabhijñā-kārikā** — ~190 verses in four books teaching liberation as recognition of oneself as Maheśvara, duelling Dharmakīrti's Buddhists (c. 925–975 CE [scholarly]) _(actors: Utpaladeva)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/kashmir-shaiva-texts.md) — (Īśvara-pratyabhijñā-kārikā with Vṛtti)
- **Vallabha composes the Madhurāṣṭakam** — Vallabhācārya pours out the eight-verse 'Octet of Sweetness,' traditionally during his Braj *parikramā*, seeing all of Kṛṣṇa and his world as *madhura* _(actors: Vallabhācārya, Kṛṣṇa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/madhurashtakam.md) — (Madhurāṣṭakam; Puṣṭimārga tradition)
- **Varāhamihira systematizes electional astrology** — the Bṛhat Saṃhitā's electional chapters anchor classical muhūrta-śāstra, c. 550 CE [scholarly] _(actors: Varāhamihira)_ → [details](../00-time-and-cosmology/muhurta-and-auspicious-timing.md) — (Bṛhat Saṃhitā)
- **Vasishtha's frog hymn composed** — monsoon frogs croaking 'like brahmins around the Soma bowl' are praised as rain-bringers (or satirized priests), in the family-book stratum c. 1500–1200 BCE [scholarly] _(actors: Vasishtha, the frogs Gomayu and Ajamayu)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/vedas/rigveda-samvada-and-secular-hymns.md) — (Rigveda 7.103)
- **Vengeance consummated** — that night Nīli reveals her pēy form and kills the merchant, closing across two births the debt of the first-birth murder _(actors: Nīli, Darśana Ceṭṭi/Āṉantaṉ)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/icakki-amman-nili-katai.md) — (Nīli Katai)
- **Vicāra Śarman rescues the cows of Ceynalūr** — a Vedic brāhmaṇa boy of Tiru-Ceynalūr on the Manni river, moved by pity for the village's abused cattle, takes over herding them himself _(actors: Vicāra Śarman/Caṇḍeśa, the cows)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/chandesvara-chandikeshvara.md) — (Periya Purāṇam, Caṇṭēcura Nāyaṉār Purāṇam)
- **Videgha Māthava crosses the Sadānīrā — historical Videha emerges** — The Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa's charter-legend of Videgha Māthava led east by Agni Vaiśvānara is read as memory of the eastward Vedic expansion that founded historical Videha (c. 8th–6th c. BCE) [scholarly] _(actors: Videgha Māthava, Agni Vaiśvānara, Gotama Rāhūgaṇa)_ → [details](../03-lineages-vamsha/videha-mithila-line-janaka.md) — (Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa 1.4.1)
- **Vidyāpati Ṭhākura of Bisfī, poet of the Oinwar court** — the Maithili poet-saint (c. 1352–1448 CE [scholarly]) serves the Tirhut court of Kīrtisiṃha and Śivasiṃha and composes his trilingual corpus, including the Śaiva nācārī and maheśvānī songs _(actors: Vidyāpati, Kīrtisiṃha, Śivasiṃha, Lakhimā Devī)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ugna-mahadev-vidyapati.md) — (Kīrtilatā; Puruṣa-Parīkṣā; Oinwar court record) [scholarly]
- **Vidyāraṇya at Śṛṅgeri** — the 14th-c. Jagadguru, traditionally identified with the minister Mādhava who guided Harihara and Bukka in founding Vijayanagara (1336 CE), composes the Pañcadaśī and Jīvanmukti-viveka _(actors: Vidyāraṇya, Bhāratītīrtha, Harihara I, Bukka Rāya)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/later-advaita-acharyas.md) — (Pañcadaśī; Wikipedia, Vidyaranya) [disputed]
- **Vidyāraṇya composes the Pañcadaśī at Śṛṅgerī** — the 15-chapter medieval summa of Advaita in three pentads (viveka, dīpa, ānanda), with a co-authorship tradition naming his teacher Bhāratītīrtha, 14th c. CE [scholarly] _(actors: Vidyāraṇya, Bhāratītīrtha)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/darshanas/vedanta-prakarana-granthas.md) — (Pañcadaśī, chs. 1–15)
- **Vidyāraṇya formalizes vidvat- vs vividiṣā-sannyāsa** — the 14th-c. Jīvanmuktiviveka systematizes renunciation-for-knowledge versus renunciation-of-the-knower and the jīvanmukta ideal _(actors: Vidyāraṇya)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/sannyasa-and-the-renunciate-tradition.md) — (Jīvanmuktiviveka)
- **Vihari Lala Mitra's complete English translation (1891–1899 CE)** — The four-volume Calcutta translation makes the full Yoga-Vāsiṣṭha available in English, still the only complete rendering [scholarly] _(actors: Vihari Lala Mitra)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/yoga-vasishtha.md) — (Mitra, The Yoga-Vasishtha-Maharamayana of Valmiki)
- **Vijayanagara enlargement** — the Kāṇipākam complex is extended from the 14th century CE onward under Vijayanagara patronage, giving it much of its present scale _(actors: Vijayanagara patrons)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/kanipakam-varasiddhi-vinayaka.md) — (temple tradition) [scholarly] 14th c. CE
- **Vijayīndra Tīrtha's debates with Appayya Dīkṣita** — the prolific Kumbakonam pontiff (c. 1514–1595 CE [scholarly]), credited with 104 works, contests the Śaiva-Advaita polymath in the Tanjore country _(actors: Vijayīndra Tīrtha, Appayya Dīkṣita)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/raghavendra-tirtha-and-the-dvaita-line.md) — (Sharma, History of the Dvaita School)
- **Vijñāna Bhairava Tantra composed** — Bhairavī questions Bhairava, who dismisses external forms and teaches the 112 dhāraṇās into undivided awareness (c. 7th–9th c. CE [scholarly]) _(actors: Bhairava, Bhairavī/Devī)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/kashmir-shaiva-texts.md) — (Vijñāna Bhairava, opening dialogue)
- **Vikramāditya's Śaka victory and the Vikrama Saṃvat epoch (57 BCE)** — tradition counts the era from the legendary Ujjain king's victory over the Śakas, though epigraphy shows the era first named Kṛta/Mālava and attached to Vikrama only from c. the 9th c. CE _(actors: Vikramāditya)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/brihatkatha-kathasaritsagara-and-vikramaditya-cycle.md) — (Jyotirvidābharaṇa / era tradition) [disputed] [scholarly]
- **Vīramitrodaya written** — Mitra Miśra of Banaras compiles the multi-part digest named for his patron Bīr Singh Deo of Orchhā, a leading authority of the Banaras sub-school _(actors: Mitra Miśra, Vīrasiṃha Bundela)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/dharma-nibandhas-and-digests.md) — ([scholarly] c. 1610–1620 CE)
- **Vīsaladeva grades the Nāgara sub-groups (13th c. CE)** — a great sacrifice convened by the Vāghela king Vīsaladeva is credited with splitting the Nāgaras into their endogamous sub-divisions, dating the community's received shape _(actors: King Vīsaladeva Vāghela, the Nāgara Brāhmaṇas)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/vadnagar-hatakeshvara-kshetra.md) — (Nāgara-khaṇḍa frame; Nāgara tradition)
- **Vishnuprayag temple built (1889 CE)** — the octagonal Vishnu temple over the confluence is credited to the Maharani of Indore [scholarly] _(actors: Maharani of Indore)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/panch-prayag-alaknanda-confluences.md) — (Wikipedia, Panch Prayag)
- **Viṣṇu and Lakṣmī waylaid as a wedding party** — the Lord and his consort descend disguised as bridegroom and bride, leading a marriage procession through the robber's forest at Tirumaṇaṅkollai _(actors: Viṣṇu, Lakṣmī, Kaliyan)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/tirumangai-alvar.md) — (Divya Sūri Charitam, Kaliyan chapters)
- **Viśvanātha Cakravartī elaborates the prema ladder** — the Madhurya-kadambinī walks the nine stages from śraddhā to prema and the Rāga-vartma-candrikā systematizes rāgānugā practice (c. 1700 CE [late text]) _(actors: Viśvanātha Cakravartī)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/bhakti-rasa-and-the-bhavas.md) — (Madhurya-kadambinī; Rāga-vartma-candrikā)
- **Viṭṭhala shrine inscriptionally attested (1189 CE)** — the earliest secure epigraphic reference to the god's shrine at Pandharpur, followed by 13th-century Yādava-era temple expansion _(actors: Pandharpur temple, Yādava patrons)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/pandharpur-vitthala-wari.md) — (epigraphic scholarship; Dhere, Rise of a Folk God) [scholarly]
- **Viṭṭhalapant's renunciation and return to householder life** — Jñāneśvar's Brāhmaṇa father takes sannyāsa, then on his guru's order returns to marriage with Rukmiṇībāī, a reversal orthodoxy held impossible _(actors: Viṭṭhalapant Kulkarṇī, Rukmiṇībāī, his guru)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/jnaneshwar.md) — (Wikipedia (Dnyaneshwar); Prepp; Vārkarī hagiography)
- **Vivekananda performs Kumārī Pūjā at Belur Maṭh (1901 CE)** — at Belur Maṭh's first Durgā Pūjā, Swami Vivekananda worships young girls as the living Goddess, establishing the Ramakrishna Mission's famous Mahāṣṭamī kumārī-pūjā _(actors: Swami Vivekananda, Ramakrishna Mission)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/durga-puja-bengal.md) — (Belur Maṭh accounts) [scholarly]
- **Vivekananda preaches the Nārada sūtra's parā-bhakti (1890s)** — the Bhakti-Yoga lectures carry the text into the modern revival, followed by Tyagisananda's standard edition [scholarly] _(actors: Swami Vivekananda, Swami Tyagisananda)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/bhakti-sutras-narada-shandilya.md) — (Vivekananda, Bhakti-Yoga)
- **Vopadeva's Muktāphala maps bhakti onto the rasas** — at the Devagiri court, Bhāgavata devotees are sorted under nava-rasa headings, a forerunner of bhakti-rasa theory (13th c. CE [scholarly]) _(actors: Vopadeva, Hemādri)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/bhakti-rasa-and-the-bhavas.md) — (Muktāphala with Hemādri's commentary)
- **Vulgate Harivaṃśa's Nikumbha–Ṣaṭpura cycle takes shape** — [scholarly] the late Viṣṇu-Parva war cycles, judged appendix accretions by the BORI Critical Edition, reach vulgate form in the early-to-mid first millennium CE _(actors: Harivaṃśa redactors)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/nikumbha-bhanumati-shatpura.md) — (Vaidya, Harivaṃśa Critical Edition, BORI 1969–71)
- **Vyāsakāśī temple at Ramnagar** — the c. 1750 CE shrine on Vārāṇasī's lone east-bank ghāṭ anchors the banishment legend, its sanctum holding three copper-cased liṅgas of Vyāsa, Śuka, and Viśvanātha _(actors: the Kāśī Nareś of Ramnagar, Vyāsa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/vyasa-banished-from-kashi.md) — (regional temple tradition; Kāśī-khaṇḍa)
- **Vyāsatīrtha as Vijayanagara rāja-guru and the Nyāyāmṛta** — the Dvaita dialectician (c. 1460–1539 CE [scholarly]) serves Kṛṣṇadevarāya's court, writes the Nyāyāmṛta that provokes Madhusūdana Sarasvatī's Advaitasiddhi, and mentors the Haridāsas _(actors: Vyāsatīrtha, Kṛṣṇadevarāya, Madhusūdana Sarasvatī)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/raghavendra-tirtha-and-the-dvaita-line.md) — (Sharma, History of the Dvaita School)
- **Wedded to the sweeping king** — ordered to marry Padmāvatī to a sweeper in vengeance, the minister waits for the Ratha Yātrā and gives her to the emperor himself as he performs the Chera Paṅharā, honoring the order's letter while voiding its cruelty _(actors: Puruṣottama Deva, Padmāvatī, the minister)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/kanchi-kaveri-jagannatha-legend.md) — (Kāñcī-Kāverī; Mādaḷā Pāñji) [regional]
- **Weighed against the nose-ring** — the Gugli priests demand the idol's weight in gold; Gaṅgābāī's tiny gold nose-ring balances the black-stone god, and they must leave him at Ḍākor _(actors: Gaṅgābāī, Bodāṇā, the Gugli Brāhmaṇas)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/dakor-ranchhodrai-bodana.md) — (Ḍākor Māhātmya)
- **Wilkins prints the first Sanskrit book in English** — Charles Wilkins publishes his Hitopadeśa translation (1787), among the earliest renderings of any Sanskrit work into a European language _(actors: Charles Wilkins, William Jones)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/hitopadesha.md) — (Hitopadeśa, transmission)
- **William Jones translates the Gīta-Govinda (1792 CE)** — the first English rendering carries the poem to Europe among the earliest Sanskrit works known in the West [scholarly] _(actors: William Jones, Jayadeva's text)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/jayadeva-and-the-gita-govinda.md) — (Jones translation, 1792)
- **Wooing by disguise** — using his shape-shifting trickster powers he approaches Āriyamālā in disguise and works charms to reach her across an impassable caste line _(actors: Kāttavarāyaṉ, Āriyamālā)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/kattavarayan-katai.md) — (Kaṭṭavarāyacuvāmi Kataippāṭal) [oral tradition]
- **Xuanzang documents the Prayāga bathing assembly (644 CE)** — the Chinese pilgrim records the great riverside gathering under king Harṣa, the earliest outside eyewitness of mass tīrtha-snāna [scholarly] _(actors: Xuanzang, Harṣa)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/tirtha-and-tirtha-yatra.md) — (Records of the Western Regions)
- **Xuanzang visits Ānandapura (640 CE)** — the Chinese pilgrim describes Vaḍnagar as a walled, populous town, the earliest firm external date for the settlement the Nāgara-khaṇḍa mythologizes _(actors: Xuanzang / Hiuen Tsang)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/vadnagar-hatakeshvara-kshetra.md) — (Xuanzang, Records of the Western Regions)
- **Yādava Prakāśa compiles the Yatidharmasamuccaya** — the classic juridical digest of renouncer-law (rite, staff, begging, rains-retreat) is composed in the 11th–12th c. CE [scholarly] _(actors: Yādava Prakāśa)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/sannyasa-and-the-renunciate-tradition.md) — (Yatidharmasamuccaya, trans. Olivelle 1995)
- **Yādava-era Tuljapur temple built** — the standing Tuljā Bhavānī temple fabric on the Bālā-ghāṭ is assigned to c. 12th–13th c. CE with Maratha-period and modern additions _(actors: Yādava-era builders, Maratha patrons)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/tulja-bhavani.md) — (Wikipedia: Tulja Bhavani Temple) [scholarly]
- **Yājñavalkya's going-forth recorded in the Bṛhadāraṇyaka** — the Upaniṣad (composed c. 8th–6th c. BCE [scholarly]) preserves the archetypal renunciation scene of Yājñavalkya leaving the household after teaching Maitreyī _(actors: Yājñavalkya, Maitreyī)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/sannyasa-and-the-renunciate-tradition.md) — (Bṛhadāraṇyaka Upaniṣad, 2.4 & 4.5)
- **Yajurveda redaction with the Uttaranārāyaṇa appendix** — The hymn enters the Vājasaneyi Saṃhitā as adhyāya 31 with six appended verses naming Hrī and Lakṣmī as the Puruṣa's consorts, c. 1200–800 BCE [scholarly] [late text] _(actors: Yajurveda redactors)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/purusha-sukta.md) — (Vājasaneyi Saṃhitā, 31.17–22)
- **Yamuna dies as Ramanuja arrives at Srirangam** — Ramanuja is designated successor of Yamunacharya, the theological forerunner of Srivaishnavism, but the two never meet — Yamuna dies just as Ramanuja arrives at Srirangam _(actors: Yamunacharya, Ramanuja)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/ramanuja-and-madhva.md) — (Ramanuja hagiography)
- **Abolition of sati (1829)** — Ram Mohan Roy's campaign secures the 1829 abolition of sati, a landmark of rational reform under colonial modernity _(actors: Ram Mohan Roy)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/modern-teachers-and-reform.md) — (Bengal Renaissance reform history)
- **Adi Shankara Founds the Four Amnaya Mathas** — Adi Shankara founds four cardinal monasteries (Jyotirmath, Sringeri, Govardhana at Puri, Dwarka Sharada Pitha), each guarding one Veda and one quarter of India _(actors: Adi Shankara)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/char-dham-and-chota-char-dham.md) — (Wikipedia, Char Dham)
- **Śaṅkara composes the refrain and rebuke** — Moved to pity, Śaṅkara breaks into spontaneous song — 'bhaja govindaṃ … grammar will not save you at the hour of death' — coining the hymn's refrain and its message that only devotion rescues the soul _(actors: Ādi Śaṅkara)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/bhaja-govindam.md) — (Bhaja Govindam, opening verse (Mohamudgara))
- **Śaṅkaradeva's pan-Indian pilgrimage** — Orphaned and schooled under Mahendra Kandalī, Śaṅkaradeva undertakes a ~12-year pilgrimage to the tīrthas, then returns to propagate single-refuge Bhāgavata devotion _(actors: Śaṅkaradeva, Mahendra Kandalī)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/sankaradeva-and-assamese-vaishnavism.md) — (vajiramandravi.com; testbook.com)
- **Akbar-imprisonment frame-legend** — A folk hagiography holds Tulsidas composed the Chalisa while imprisoned by a Mughal emperor and was freed when monkeys besieged the court _(actors: Tulsidas; Akbar/Jahangir (legendary))_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/hanuman-chalisa.md) — (Tulsidas hagiographical folklore)
- **Aniconic open-air shrine and non-Brahmin priesthood** — Grāmadevatās are worshipped at open-air stones, termite-mounds, tridents and neem trees by non-Brahmin pūjārīs, not in Agamic temples by Vedic priests _(actors: Village pūjārīs (pambala, asadi, potha-raju lineages); oracles)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/gramadevatas-village-deities.md) — (Whitehead, Village Gods of South India; village-shrine ethnography)
- **Bahucharā's tragu (self-mutilation)** — Rather than submit, Bahucharā invokes the Cāraṇ right of tragu and cuts off her own breast, turning her shed blood into a sacred death-curse _(actors: Bahucharā, Bāpiyā)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/bahuchara-mata.md) — (Cāraṇ folk tradition)
- **Barred from entering the Vithoba temple** — As a Mahar, Chokhamela is forbidden entry to the Vithoba temple and conducts his devotion from outside the threshold _(actors: Chokhamela; Pandharpur temple priests)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/chokhamela.md) — (Wikipedia (Chokhamela); Zelliot)
- **Basava becomes chief minister to Bijjala II** — Basava rises to treasurer and then chief minister of the Kalachuri king Bijjala II at Kalyana, using his state office to institutionalize the Sharana gatherings and fund the dasoha kitchens _(actors: Basava, Bijjala II)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/lingayat-vachana-poets.md) — (Kalachuri political chronology)
- **Bengal Dol Pūrṇimā / Gaura-Pūrṇimā** — In Bengal the swing of Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa coincides with and doubles as the appearance day (1486 CE) of Caitanya Mahāprabhu, kept by Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇavas as Gaura-pūrṇimā _(actors: Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa, Caitanya Mahāprabhu, Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇavas)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/swing-and-float-temple-festivals.md) — (Gauḍīya tradition; Wikipedia (Dol Jatra); Krishna.com)
- **Bharata codifies svara, śruti, grāma, mūrcchanā and jāti** — The Nāṭya Śāstra (chs. 28–33) fixes the seven svaras with animal-cry associations, the 22 śrutis (proven by the two experimental vīṇās), the grāmas, the 14 mūrcchanās and the 18 jātis _(actors: Bharata Muni)_ → [details](../09-artifacts-symbols-and-arts/sangita-classical-music-svara-raga-tala.md) — (Nāṭya Śāstra chs. 28–33; Dattilam)
- **Bhāskararāya systematizes Śrīvidyā** — Bhāskararāya Makhin (1690–1785) reconciles the diverse vidyās into one coherent Śrīvidyā philosophy, authoring 40+ works and founding a living lineage _(actors: Bhāskararāya Makhin)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/shakta-and-tantra-lineages.md) — (Wikipedia, Shri Vidya)
- **Caṇḍī blinds Manasā in one eye** — Pārvatī (Caṇḍī), hating the rival daughter, strikes and destroys one of Manasā's eyes, leaving her one-eyed (Kāṇī) _(actors: Pārvatī/Caṇḍī, Manasā)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/manasa.md) — (Manasāmaṅgal (Ketakādāsa Kṣemānanda))
- **Childhood Viṭṭhal-bhakti and the milk miracle** — As a child Nāmdev is said to have made the Viṭhobā image physically accept his milk/food offering, taking childlike love as true darśana _(actors: Nāmdev, Viṭṭhal/Viṭhobā)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/namdev.md) — (Vārkarī hagiography [folk])
- **Child marriage to Gaṅgādhar Paṭhak** — Married very young to a much older, orthodox, irascible Brahmin widower who bitterly opposes her devotion _(actors: Bahiṇābāī; Gaṅgādhar Paṭhak (husband))_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/bahinabai.md) — (Wikipedia 'Bahina Bai'; Feldhaus)
- **Composition of the Dharma-maṅgal kāvya** — Named Bengali poets compose the Dharma-maṅgal maṅgal-kāvya cycle (c. 14th–18th c.) to propagate Dharma-pūjā _(actors: Ghanarām Cakravartī, Rūpram Cakravartī, Mānikrām Gāṅgulī, Mayūrabhaṭṭa and others)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/dharma-thakur.md) — (Dharma-maṅgal recensions)
- **Composition of the Mukunda-mala garland** — Kulasekhara composes the Sanskrit Mukunda-mala, a garland of surrender-prayers to Mukunda (Vishnu/Krishna) _(actors: Kulasekhara Alvar; addressed to Mukunda/Vishnu)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/mukunda-mala.md) — (Mukunda-mala Stotra)
- **Ādi Kailāsh / Om Parvat darśana, Kumaon** — The Chhota Kailāsh peak with its Pārvatī Sarovar/Gaurī Kuṇḍ lake and the neighbouring Om Parvat whose snow forms the syllable ॐ, pilgrimaged as a substitute Mount Kailāsa _(actors: Śiva and Pārvatī (Gaurī); Kumaoni pilgrims)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/panch-kailash.md) — (Wikipedia (Adi Kailash, Om Parvat); Uttarakhand sthala tradition)
- **Discipleship under Janārdana-svāmī** — Eknāth studies under the Datta-devotee and fort administrator Janārdana-svāmī at Devagiri/Daulatābād and adopts the signature 'Eka-Janārdana'; tradition adds a vision of Dattātreya _(actors: Eknāth, Janārdana-svāmī, Dattātreya)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/eknath.md) — (Wikipedia Eknath; Britannica; Datta hagiography)
- **Divine commission and arrival in the Sundarbans** — The twins are blessed at Medina and commissioned by Allah to make the land of eighteen tides habitable; they arrive and claim the delta with the azan _(actors: Bonbibi, Shah Jangali)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/bonbibi.md) — (Bonbibi Jahuranama)
- **Emergence of the Nāgara order and its sub-styles (Latina, Śekharī, Bhūmija, Kaliṅga)** — The North Indian curvilinear-śikhara temple with āmalaka-and-kalaśa finials develops Gupta-onward, branching into single- and multi-spired forms and the regional Bhūmija and Kaliṅga (Odisha) sub-orders _(actors: North Indian dynasties; Odia śilpins; exemplars at Khajurāho, Bhubaneswar, Modhera, Puri, Koṇārka)_ → [details](../09-artifacts-symbols-and-arts/temple-architecture-nagara-dravida-vesara.md) — (Aparājitapṛcchā; Śilpa-prakāśa; Bhuvana-pradīpa; Wikipedia (Nagara architecture, Bhumija, Kalinga architecture, Shikhara))
- **Founding of the Kānphaṭa split-ear order** — Gorakṣanāth organizes the Kānphaṭa ('split-ear') yogīs around the Gorakhpur maṭha, with the twelve panths and the Aughaṛ/Darśanī grades _(actors: Gorakṣanāth, Kānphaṭa yogīs)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/nath-sampradaya-and-the-siddhas.md) — (Wikipedia (Gorakhnath); Britannica)
- **Gangamma hunts the Pālegāḍu through successive disguises** — Unable to confront him openly, Gangamma assumes a succession of veṣams (guises) to lure and corner the chieftain _(actors: Gangamma, the Pālegāḍu)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/gangamma.md) — (Tirupati oral tradition; Handelman/Krishnamurthy/Dawson and Flueckiger ethnography [folk/scholarly])
- **Gathering of the sants at Goroba's workshop** — A company of Vārkarī sants assembles at Gorā Kumbhār's potter's yard at Ter, where as the eldest he is asked to judge their spiritual ripeness _(actors: Gorā Kumbhār, Jñāneśvar, Nāmdev, Muktābāī, other sants)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/gora-kumbhar.md) — (Mahīpati, Bhaktavijaya; Vārkarī hagiography)
- **Gauḍapāda composes the Māṇḍūkya-Kārikā** — Gauḍapāda, the parama-guru of Śaṅkara, composes the 215-verse Māṇḍūkya-Kārikā, the earliest systematic treatise of Advaita Vedānta with its ajāta-vāda doctrine _(actors: Gauḍapāda)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/upanishads/mandukya-upanishad.md) — (Gauḍapāda, Māṇḍūkya-kārikā)
- **Indo-Aryan Migrations** — Indo-Aryan speakers migrate into the subcontinent c. 1900-1500 BCE bringing the Indo-European language that becomes Vedic Sanskrit (disputed against an Out-of-India model) _(actors: Indo-Aryans)_ → [details](../01-manvantaras/manvantara-07-vaivasvata/mahayuga-28-current/04-kali-yuga/recorded-history.md) — (History of India (Wikipedia); genetic studies (Reich et al.))
- **Kaṇṇaki burns Madurai (Cilappatikāram)** — After her husband Kōvalaṉ is wrongly executed for theft, Kaṇṇaki proves his innocence and curses the city of Madurai, which burns down _(actors: Kaṇṇaki, Kōvalaṉ)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/tamil-canon-sangam-and-bhakti.md) — (Cilappatikāram)
- **Kabir descends from Satlok as a body of light** — In the Kabir Panth's own account, Kabir descends from Satlok as a body of light and incarnates on a lotus in Lahartara Lake, witnessed by the rishi Ashtanand, marking him a divine avatara rather than a born human _(actors: Kabir, sage Ashtanand)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/kabir.md) — (Kabir Panth tradition (folk))
- **Kaundinya's Pañcārtha-bhāṣya** — The commentator Kaundinya (4th–5th c.) writes the Pañcārtha-bhāṣya on the Pāśupata-sūtra, fixing the pati–paśu–pāśa triad inherited by all later Śaiva schools _(actors: Kaundinya)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/shaiva-schools.md) — (Wikipedia, Pashupata Shaivism)
- **Life as dāsī and the household-labor devotional idiom** — Janābāī serves as maidservant in Nāmdev's home, turning menial chores (grinding, sweeping, gathering dung, fetching water) into continuous devotion and signing her verse 'Dāsī Janī' _(actors: Janābāī, Nāmdev and his family)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/janabai.md) — (Marathi abhaṅgas; Wikipedia (Janabai))
- **Lorik and Candā love-and-war saga** — The cowherd-hero Lorik and his beloved Candā/Mañjarī elope, go into exile and fight cattle-feuds and single combats, the great martial-romantic oral epic of the Ahīr/Yādav (and Gond) herding communities, sung as Lorikāyan in the Bhojpurī/Awadhī belt and as Canainī/Candainī in Chhattisgarh _(actors: Lorik; Candā / Mañjarī)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/regional-and-oral-epics.md) — (Lorikāyan / Canainī-Candainī (oral epic); Lorik-Chanda — Wikipedia)
- **Madhva calms the storm and claims the gopīcandana ballast** — Madhva stills a storm off the Malpe coast and asks the rescued Dvārakā ship only for two lumps of sacred gopīcandana clay used as ballast _(actors: Madhvācārya, ship's merchant-crew)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/udupi-krishna-matha.md) — (Sumadhva Vijaya)
- **Manasollāsa treats metal images among royal arts** — Someśvara III's Abhilaṣitārtha-cintāmaṇi (Mānasollāsa), a dated 12th-c. royal encyclopedia, sets out dhātu-pratimā proportions and ornament _(actors: Cālukya king Someśvara III)_ → [details](../09-artifacts-symbols-and-arts/pancha-loha-bronze-casting.md) — (Mānasollāsa (Abhilaṣitārtha-cintāmaṇi))
- **Marriage of Mirabai to Bhojraj of Mewar** — In 1516 Mira weds Bhojraj, crown prince of Mewar and son of Rana Sanga, marrying into the Sisodiya dynasty of Chittor _(actors: Mirabai, Bhojraj, Rana Sanga)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/mirabai.md) — (Rajput chronicle)
- **Nammalvar's sixteen years of silence under the tamarind** — Nammalvar sits in padmasana in a hollow of a tamarind tree at the Adinatha temple for sixteen years, eyes closed in absorption, neither speaking nor eating _(actors: Nammalvar)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/nammalvar.md) — (Nammalvar hagiography (traditional))
- **Nimbarka founds the Nimbarka Sampradaya** — Nimbarka founds the Kumara/Nimbarka Sampradaya, propounds Dvaitadvaita, elevates Radha to full divine status worshipped inseparably with Krishna as the Yugala, and authors the Vedanta-parijata-saurabha _(actors: Nimbarka)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/nimbarka-vallabha-chaitanya.md) — (Vedanta-parijata-saurabha; Dashashloki)
- **Nivṛttināth initiated by Gahinīnāth** — The eldest sibling, separated from family near Brahmagiri/Tryambakeśvar, receives Nāth dīkṣā from Gahinīnāth, entering the line Gorakṣanātha → Gahinīnāth → Nivṛttināth _(actors: Nivṛttināth, Gahinīnāth)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/nivritti-sopan-muktabai.md) — (Wikipedia (Nivruttinath, Natha Sampradaya); Vārkarī hagiography)
- **Pey Alvar First Sees Lakshmi Beside Vishnu** — Pey Alvar, third of the Mudhal Alvars, is said to have first 'seen' Lakshmi beside Vishnu, recorded in his Munram Tiruvandhadhi _(actors: Pey Alvar)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/alvars-and-nayanars.md) — (Naalayira Divya Prabandham)
- **Pox-as-grace and neem cooling** — Smallpox/chickenpox/cholera treated as the goddess's presence in the body, cooled with neem, turmeric and buttermilk rather than only medicated _(actors: Māriyammaṉ; the afflicted; village healers)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/mariamman.md) — (Tamil folk medical-religious tradition)
- **Raising of the ~66 m vimāna and monolithic capstone** — The ~216 ft Drāviḍa śrī-vimāna (Dakṣiṇa Meru) is built and crowned by a single-block capstone, by tradition hauled up a 6 km ramp from Sārapallam _(actors: Chola masons and engineers under Rājarāja I)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/brihadeeswarar-thanjavur.md) — (Chola inscriptions; Tamil Śaiva sthala folk tradition)
- **Ramanuja breaks with Yadava Prakasha** — Ramanuja first studies Advaita under Yadava Prakasha at Kanchi but breaks with him over the kapyasam scriptural-interpretation dispute, then aligns with the Yamuna line _(actors: Ramanuja, Yadava Prakasha)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/ramanuja-and-madhva.md) — (Ramanuja biography)
- **Ravidas envisions Begampura** — The Chamar sant-poet Ravidas of Varanasi sings his nirguna devotion and articulates the utopia of Begampura, an egalitarian city 'without sorrow or tax,' a great social image of the bhakti movement _(actors: Ravidas)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/north-bhakti-sants.md) — (Guru Granth Sahib (Ravidas hymns))
- **Re-composition of the Ramakien under Rama I** — After Ayutthaya-era versions were lost in the 1767 Burmese sack, King Rama I re-composes the standard Thai national Rama epic (1782-1809) _(actors: King Rama I of the Chakri dynasty)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ramayana/recensions/southeast-asian-ramayanas.md) — (Ramakien)
- **Śrīpādarāja systematizes the Dāsa-kūṭa** — Mulbāgal pontiff (c. 1422–1480) shapes the suḷādi form and composes under aṅkita 'Raṅga Viṭṭhala', first major systematizer of Dāsa-kūṭa song _(actors: Śrīpādarāja (Lakṣmīnārāyaṇatīrtha))_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/haridasa-and-carnatic-bhakti.md) — (Haridāsa-sāhitya; Wikipedia, Sripadaraja)
- **Rukmiṇī withdraws to Diṇḍīrvan; Viṭṭhal follows** — Angered, Rukmiṇī (Rakhumāī) withdraws to nearby Diṇḍīrvan and Viṭṭhal follows her to Pandharpur, explaining the two separate shrines in the temple complex _(actors: Viṭṭhal (Kṛṣṇa), Rukmiṇī/Rakhumāī)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/vithoba-vitthal.md) — (Pandharpur temple tradition; abhaṅga corpus [regional/folk])
- **Sambandar's Pandya reconversion contest** — Summoned by queen Maṅgaiyarkkaracī, Sambandar cures the Pandya king's fever, defeats the Jains in contests of fire and water, and reconverts the king and realm to Śaivism _(actors: Sambandar, Kūṉ Pāṇḍiyaṉ, Maṅgaiyarkkaracī, Kulacciṟai)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/the-nalvar-tamil-shaiva-saints.md) — (Wikipedia, Sambandar)
- **Sangam Avvaiyār contributes to the anthologies** — The earliest Avvaiyār, a Sangam-age court poet (c. 1st–3rd c. CE), composed akam/puṟam verses surviving in Puṟanāṉūṟu and other Eṭṭuttokai anthologies _(actors: Sangam Avvaiyār)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/avvaiyar.md) — (Puṟanāṉūṟu; Eṭṭuttokai; Avvaiyar (Sangam poet) — Wikipedia; Sangam literature — Wikipedia)
- **Sangili Karuppaṉ, the chained subform** — Karuppu fans into named local subforms including Sangili Karuppaṉ, shown bound in chains because his power must be restrained _(actors: Sangili Karuppaṉ (Karuppasāmi subform))_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/karuppasamy-madurai-veeran-and-tamil-kaval-deities.md) — (Tamil kāval-deyvam tradition)
- **Shankara Initiated by Govinda Bhagavatpada** — Journeying north, Shankara meets and is initiated by his guru Govinda Bhagavatpada on the banks of the Narmada, inheriting the proto-Advaita teaching of Gaudapada _(actors: Adi Shankara, Govinda Bhagavatpada)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/adi-shankara.md) — (Wikipedia, Adi Shankara)
- **Sharad Navaratri Shakta substrate** — Gujarat's garba/dandiya sits on the pan-Hindu nine-night autumn worship of Durga/Amba and her victory over Mahishasura _(actors: Durga/Amba, Mahishasura)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/garba-and-dandiya-raas.md) — (Devi Mahatmya (Durga Saptashati, Markandeya Purana))
- **Snana Yatra / Snana Purnima and the Anavasara seclusion** — On Jyeshtha Purnima, Jagannath, Balabhadra and Subhadra are bathed on the Snana Bedi with 108 pots of scented water (Mahasnana) and given the elephant-form Gajavesha (Ganesha) darshan; afterward the deities ritually fall ill with fever (jvara) and enter the Anavasara/Anasara seclusion, treated with herbal remedies, reappearing at the Nava-yauvana/Netrotsava darshan just before the Rath Yatra _(actors: Jagannath, Balabhadra, Subhadra, daita servitors)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/regional-temple-festivals.md) — (Wikipedia (Snana Yatra, Anavasara); Jagannath Temple Puri [traditional])
- **Sāriputta and Moggallāna leave Sañjaya for the Buddha** — The two future chief disciples Sāriputta and Moggallāna, originally pupils of the skeptic teacher Sañjaya Belaṭṭhaputta, abandon his 'eel-wriggling' equivocation and join the Buddhist saṅgha _(actors: Sāriputta, Moggallāna, Sañjaya Belaṭṭhaputta, the Buddha)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/nastika-darshanas-and-the-astika-nastika-frame.md) — (Pali Vinaya / Dīgha Nikāya 2 (tradition))
- **Svami Haridasa's life at Nidhivana** — The rasika singer-saint Svami Haridasa (c.1480-1573, dates disputed), reckoned an incarnation of the sakhi Lalita, lives as an ascetic in Nidhivana and founds the Haridasi Sampradaya _(actors: Svami Haridasa)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/banke-bihari-vrindavan-nidhivan.md) — (Haridasi hagiography)
- **The cruel mother-in-law and the stone-in-the-plate** — Her harsh mother-in-law starves her, hiding a stone under a thin layer of rice, giving rise to a Kashmiri proverb of endurance _(actors: Lallā; her mother-in-law)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/lalleshwari-lal-ded.md) — (Wikipedia, Lalleshwari; Kashmiri oral tradition)
- **The destitute woman's single āmalaka** — A desperately poor Brahmin woman, having nothing else, gives Śaṅkara her one possession — a single gooseberry (āmalaka) _(actors: Ādi Śaṅkara, the poor Brahmin woman)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/kanakadhara-stotram.md) — (Kanakadhārā-stotra frame; Śaṅkara-vijaya hagiographies)
- **The divine child found** — Padikutty Amma finds a baby boy by the river and the couple raise the divine foundling as their son _(actors: Padikutty Amma, Ayyankara Vazhunnavar, the divine child (Muthappan))_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/muthappan.md) — (Parassinikkadavu Muthappan origin legend)
- **The donkey-broom-winnowing-fan-water-pot dhyāna** — The Śītalāṣṭaka fixes her iconography: she rides a donkey and carries a broom, winnowing-fan and cooling water-pot, often worshipped aniconically as a pot or stone under a neem tree _(actors: Śītalā; Śiva (hymn-speaker))_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/shitala.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa — Śītalāṣṭaka; village-shrine ethnography)
- **The famine and Tukārām's turn to devotion** — A c. 1629 famine kills Tukārām's first wife Rakhumābāī and a son and ruins his trade, the collapse that turns the failing grocer toward total Viṭṭhal-devotion _(actors: Tukārām, Rakhumābāī)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/tukaram.md) — (New World Encyclopedia; siddhayoga.org)
- **The Five Siddhāntas** — Five early-CE astronomical schools (Sūrya, Paitāmaha, Vāsiṣṭha, Romaka, Pauliśa) absorbing Greco-Babylonian geometry and the 12-sign zodiac into India _(actors: Anonymous Siddhānta authors)_ → [details](../00-time-and-cosmology/siddhantic-astronomy-and-astronomers.md) — (Pañcasiddhāntikā; histories of Indian astronomy)
- **The forty-day prayer on the Indus** — The despairing Sindhi Hindus fast and pray for forty days on the bank of the Indus to Varuna / the water-god for deliverance _(actors: The Sindhi Hindu community, Varuna (the water-god))_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/jhulelal.md) — (Sindhi folk tradition; prototype of the Chaliho observance)
- **The four-part dawn liturgy fixed** — Annan arranges the morning service as four sequential texts — Suprabhatam (awakening), Stotram (praise), Prapatti (surrender), Mangalasasanam (benediction) _(actors: Prativadi Bhayankaram Annan; Lord Venkateshvara/Shrinivasa)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/venkatesha-suprabhatam.md) — (Venkatesha Suprabhatam (4 parts))
- **The Garland Legend (Sudikkodutta Nacciyar)** — Kodai secretly wears the temple garlands as the Lord's bride; when Periyalvar withholds the 'polluted' offering, Vishnu appears in his dream declaring he prefers the worn garlands, earning her the epithet Sudikkodutta Nacciyar _(actors: Andal, Periyalvar, Vishnu)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/andal.md) — (Andal — Wikipedia; Grokipedia)
- **The Kailasa author-legend** — Legend that Shankara found the first 41 verses (Ananda-lahari) inscribed at Shiva's abode, had them snatched away by Nandin/Ganesha, and supplied the remaining 59 verses himself _(actors: Adi Shankara, Nandin, Ganesha, Shiva)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/soundarya-lahari.md) — (Sri Vidya oral/hagiographic tradition)
- **The miracle of the two mangoes** — Puṉitavati gives a mango to a Śiva-devotee, then miraculously produces a second mango by prayer to serve her husband _(actors: Puṉitavati, Paramatattaṉ, Śiva, a mendicant devotee)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/karaikkal-ammaiyar.md) — (Periya Purāṇam (Cēkkiḻār))
- **The Moovar Sing the 275 Paadal Petra Sthalams** — The Nayanar saint-poets Appar, Sambandar and Sundarar (6th-9th c. CE) compose the Tevaram, qualifying 275/276 Shiva temples as Paadal Petra Sthalams _(actors: Appar, Sambandar, Sundarar)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/temple-networks-and-circuits.md) — (Tevaram / first seven Tirumurai)
- **The ṣoḍaśa-śṛṅgāra sixteen-adornment complex** — Tradition fixes a sixteen-item maṅgala-śṛṅgāra of bridal/married adornment (bindī, sindūr, mehndi, āltā, ornaments) as the complete auspicious adorned state of a wife _(actors: married women; goddess-brides Pārvatī, Lakṣmī, Sītā)_ → [details](../09-artifacts-symbols-and-arts/mehndi-and-ritual-body-adornment.md) — (ṣoḍaśa-śṛṅgāra tradition; vrata/Tīj custom)
- **The periphery guardian-shrine** — Ayyaṉār is enshrined at the village edge (tank-bund, field-boundary, grove, cremation-fringe) facing outward, served by non-Brahman priests with animal offering _(actors: Ayyaṉār; potter/Vēḷḷāḷa community priests; villagers)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/ayyanar-sastha.md) — (Tamil grāma-devatā tradition)
- **The rebuke and composition of the refrain** — Śaṅkara sings 'bhaja govindam... nahi nahi rakṣati ḍukṛñ-karaṇe', warning that grammar cannot save one at death — only devotion to Govinda can _(actors: Ādi Śaṅkara, the grammarian)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/bhaja-govindam.md) — (Bhaja Govindam, opening (refrain) verse)
- **The rival twins Arjan and Surjan** — The boon-sweet is shared with or seized by Bāchhal's co-sister Kāchhal, who bears the twins Arjan and Surjan, Gugga's cousins and lifelong rivals _(actors: Kāchhal, Arjan, Surjan, Gugga)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/gugga-gogaji.md) — (Gugga-gāthā)
- **The runaway bridegroom** — At c. age 12, on hearing the priest's 'sāvadhān', he fled the wedding mandap and renounced household life _(actors: young Nārāyaṇ)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/samarth-ramdas.md) — (Wikipedia; Hindupedia [folk])
- **Traditional blindness of Sūrdās** — Sūr remembered as blind (often from birth), the sightless poet who 'sees' Kṛṣṇa inwardly; scholars debate literal congenital blindness given his visual detail _(actors: Sūrdās)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/surdas.md) — (Wikipedia Surdas; Britannica)
- **Traditional discipleship under Rāmānanda** — Ravidās is remembered, like Kabīr, as one of the disciples of Svāmī Rāmānanda of Vārāṇasī, a link doubted by scholars _(actors: Ravidās, Rāmānanda)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/ravidas.md) — (Wikipedia Ravidas, Ramananda; Bhaktamāl tradition)
- **Turn to Viṭṭhal and refusal of suitors** — Renowned for her beauty, she refuses all men and fixes her heart on Viṭhobā of Pandharpur as her only lord _(actors: Kānhopātrā, Viṭṭhal (Viṭhobā))_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/kanhopatra.md) — (Vārkarī hagiography)
- **Vidyāraṇya replaces the Śāradā image with gold** — Vidyāraṇya (Mādhava, 14th c. Śṛṅgerī pontiff) replaces Śaṅkara's original sandalwood Śāradā mūrti with the gold/pañcaloha image and is credited with rebuilding the Śāradāmbā temple in stone, alongside the Vidyāśaṅkara temple _(actors: Vidyāraṇya (Mādhava), Śāradāmbā)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/saraswati.md) — (Śṛṅgerī Maṭha tradition)
- **Śaṅkara composes the Dvādaśa-mañjarikā** — Śaṅkara expands the song into the twelve-verse 'twelve-blossom' core (Dvādaśa-mañjarikā Stotra) on renunciation, impermanence, satsaṅga, and surrender to Govinda _(actors: Ādi Śaṅkara)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/bhaja-govindam.md) — (Bhaja Govindam / Dvādaśa-mañjarikā Stotra)
- **Śaṅkara composes the hymn to Lakṣmī** — Moved by the woman's self-giving in poverty, Śaṅkara spontaneously praises Lakṣmī in ~18-21 verses, begging her mercy _(actors: Ādi Śaṅkara, Lakṣmī (invoked))_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/kanakadhara-stotram.md) — (Kanakadhārā-stotra; Śaṅkara-vijaya hagiographies)
- **Śaṅkara composes the Saundarya-laharī proper (vv. 42–100)** — Śaṅkara completes the hymn with the head-to-foot (keśādi-pādānta) poetic adoration of the goddess's body, forming the 100-verse Saundarya-laharī _(actors: Ādi Śaṅkara, Tripurasundarī / Lalitā)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/soundarya-lahari.md) — (Saundarya-laharī 42–100)
- **Śaṅkara mounts the Sarvajña-pīṭha at the Kashmir Śāradā shrine** — In the dig-vijaya hagiography Śaṅkara ascends the Sarvajña-pīṭha ('seat of omniscience') at the Kashmir Śāradā Pīṭha, linking the northern learning-seat of the goddess to his southern Śṛṅgerī installation as the two poles of one Śāradā cult of letters _(actors: Ādi Śaṅkara, Śāradā of Kashmir)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/saraswati.md) — (Śṛṅgerī / Śaṅkara hagiography (disputed))
- **Andal Composes the Tiruppavai** — Andal composes the 30-verse Tiruppavai, dramatizing the Margali pavai vow with herself as a gopi waking companions and the household of Krishna, the most-recited portion of the Prabandham _(actors: Andal)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/andal.md) — (Tiruppavai — Wikipedia)
- **Aniconic and fierce-head iconography** — Māri worshipped as plain stone, anthill, trident, pot under a neem tree, or as a fierce ground-level severed head, versus the seated red four-armed temple form _(actors: Māriyammaṉ; village shrines and temples)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/mariamman.md) — (Village-shrine ethnography; Tamil temple iconography)
- **Ayyaṉār as lord of the bhūta-host** — Ayyaṉār leads a retinue of attendant spirits and lieutenant guardians (Karuppu, Muṉīśvaraṉ, Maturai-Vīraṉ) at the village edge, mirroring the Purāṇic Bhūtanātha _(actors: Ayyaṉār; Karuppasāmi, Muṉīśvaraṉ, Maturai-Vīraṉ and other kāval deities)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/ayyanar-sastha.md) — (Tamil folk tradition; Bhūtanātha overlay)
- **Bhadrabāhu–Sthūlabhadra famine migration and the Digambara–Śvetāmbara split** — Facing a twelve-year famine in Magadha, the elder Bhadrabāhu leads monks south to Karnataka (Śravaṇabeḷagoḷa); the monks who stayed north under Sthūlabhadra relaxed the nudity-rule and convened a council at Pāṭaliputra to fix the canon. The returning southern monks rejected both the clothed practice and the northern recension, the legendary seed of the Digambara/Śvetāmbara cleavage _(actors: Bhadrabāhu, Sthūlabhadra, the Magadhan saṅgha)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/nastika-darshanas-and-the-astika-nastika-frame.md) — (Jain tradition; Digambara accounts)
- **Blood sacrifice and the buffalo-bali** — The defining grāmadevatā offering is animal blood, above all the buffalo, re-enacting the goddess's slaying of a buffalo-demon _(actors: Village mother goddess; sacrificed buffalo/goat)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/gramadevatas-village-deities.md) — (Whitehead; grāmadevatā festival ethnography)
- **Bond with the chieftain Atiyamāṉ and the gooseberry boon** — Avvaiyār served the chieftain Atiyamāṉ Neṭumāṉ Añci as poet, friend, and peace-envoy; he gave her a life-prolonging nelli (gooseberry) fruit, seeding her 'immortal old age' legend, and she mourned him in puṟam elegies _(actors: Avvaiyār; Atiyamāṉ Neṭumāṉ Añci; Toṇṭaimāṉ)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/avvaiyar.md) — (Puṟanāṉūṟu; Avvaiyar (Sangam poet) — Wikipedia)
- **Bridal and festival mehndi (henna) custom** — Henna paste is painted on hands and feet at weddings and festivals as an auspicious sign of saubhāgya, with folk beliefs on colour-depth and the hidden groom's name _(actors: brides, women; devotional figures Sītā, Rādhā/gopīs)_ → [details](../09-artifacts-symbols-and-arts/mehndi-and-ritual-body-adornment.md) — (Wikipedia Mehndi/Henna; wedding and festival custom)
- **Composes the abhangas of caste-protest and surrender** — Chokhamela composes his canonical abhangas including 'Us donga pari rasa nohe donga' (the cane is crooked but the juice is not), fusing protest at exclusion with total surrender to Vitthal _(actors: Chokhamela; Vitthal/Vithoba)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/chokhamela.md) — (Chokhamela abhanga corpus; Zelliot)
- **Composition of the Dvādaśa-mañjarikā core** — Śaṅkara composes the twelve-blossom core verses on vairāgya, impermanence of wealth/body/kin, and satsaṅga leading to liberation _(actors: Ādi Śaṅkara)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/bhaja-govindam.md) — (Dvādaśa-mañjarikā Stotra (Bhaja Govindam))
- **Composition of the Pāṇḍuraṅga-māhātmya** — The Sanskrit sthala-purāṇa glorifying the Pandharpur kṣetra, the Candrabhāgā river, and the Puṇḍalīka legend is transmitted in Skanda and Padma Purāṇa recensions _(actors: Purāṇic compilers)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/vithoba-vitthal.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa; Padma Purāṇa [late text])
- **Composition of the Rigveda** — The Rigveda, oldest layer of the four Vedas with hymns to Agni, Indra, Soma, Varuna and Ushas, is composed c. 1500-1200 BCE (traditionally held eternal and authorless) _(actors: Vedic rishis)_ → [details](../01-manvantaras/manvantara-07-vaivasvata/mahayuga-28-current/04-kali-yuga/recorded-history.md) — (Rigveda; Vedic period (Wikipedia))
- **Composition of the Tirumantiram** — Tirumūlar sings the ~3,000-verse Tirumantiram, canonized as the Tenth Tirumuṟai and the foundational Tamil scripture of Yoga and Tantra (scholarly c. 6th–10th c. CE) _(actors: Tirumūlar)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/tamil-siddhars.md) — (Tirumantiram; Wikipedia (Tirumantiram))
- **Coomaraswamy's reading of the Face of Glory as Death-Time the All-Devourer** — A. K. Coomaraswamy interprets the kīrtimukha mask as the iconic All-devourer, Death-and-Time (kāla), consumed-and-transcended at the deity's threshold, paralleling the Upaniṣadic Death-as-devourer theme _(actors: A. K. Coomaraswamy)_ → [details](../09-artifacts-symbols-and-arts/temple-iconographic-motifs.md) — (Coomaraswamy, 'The Face of Glory' (1939))
- **Dakshin Ray's claim over the forest** — The tiger-demon Dakshin Ray claims the jungle as his domain and demands human flesh, rivaling Bonbibi's authority _(actors: Dakshin Ray, Bonbibi)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/bonbibi.md) — (Bonbibi Jahuranama; Bengali folk tradition)
- **Dattila composes the Dattilam** — Dattila's concise gāndharva compendium restates śruti, svara, grāma, mūrcchanā, jāti and tāna alongside the Nāṭya Śāstra _(actors: Dattila)_ → [details](../09-artifacts-symbols-and-arts/sangita-classical-music-svara-raga-tala.md) — (Dattilam)
- **Dayananda Saraswati founds the Arya Samaj** — Swami Dayananda founds the Arya Samaj at Bombay on 10 April 1875, preaching return to the sole authority of the Vedas and pioneering the shuddhi reconversion rite, and authors the Satyarth Prakash _(actors: Dayananda Saraswati)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/modern-teachers-and-reform.md) — (Satyarth Prakash; Arya Samaj history)
- **Deification of Kaṇṇaki as Paṭṭiṉi** — Kaṇṇaki is deified as the goddess of chastity Paṭṭiṉi/Kaṇṇaki Amman, and King Ceṅkuṭṭuvaṉ builds her temple, founding the Paṭṭiṉi cult _(actors: Kaṇṇaki, Ceṅkuṭṭuvaṉ)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/tamil-canon-sangam-and-bhakti.md) — (Cilappatikāram (Vañcikkāṇṭam))
- **Emergence of the Drāviḍa order with storeyed vimāna and towering gopurams** — The South Indian pyramidal storeyed vimāna within concentric walled enclosures develops under Pallava, Cōḻa, Pāṇḍya and later dynasties, with gateway-gopurams eventually out-scaling the central shrine _(actors: Pallavas, Cōḻas, Pāṇḍyas; exemplars at Mahābalipuram, Tañjāvūr (Bṛhadīśvara), Madurai (Mīnākṣī))_ → [details](../09-artifacts-symbols-and-arts/temple-architecture-nagara-dravida-vesara.md) — (Mānasāra; Mayamata; Wikipedia (Dravidian architecture, Vimana, Gopuram))
- **Flight to Pandharpur** — She leaves Mangalveḍhā for Pandharpur, joins the Vārkarī devotees, and gives herself to kīrtana and dance before Viṭṭhal-Rukmiṇī _(actors: Kānhopātrā, Vārkarī devotees)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/kanhopatra.md) — (Vārkarī hagiography)
- **Founding of the Anubhava Mantapa** — Basava founds the Anubhava Mantapa at Kalyana, a casteless 'first religious parliament' open to all and to women, presided over spiritually by Allama Prabhu on the Shunya-simhasana, becoming the crucible of the vachana corpus _(actors: Basava, Allama Prabhu, the Sharanas)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/lingayat-vachana-poets.md) — (Vachana sahitya)
- **Gangamma reveals her fierce form and slays the Pālegāḍu** — Gangamma manifests her terrifying true form and kills the Pālegāḍu, the climactic act commemorated by the great clay form _(actors: Gangamma, the Pālegāḍu)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/gangamma.md) — (Tirupati Gangamma Jātara oral tradition [folk])
- **Husband's awe and withdrawal** — Awed and fearful at the vanishing divine mango, Paramatattaṉ withdraws, flees on a voyage, remarries, and names his daughter Puṉitavati in reverence _(actors: Paramatattaṉ, Puṉitavati)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/karaikkal-ammaiyar.md) — (Periya Purāṇam (Cēkkiḻār))
- **Initiation by guru Viṣobā Khecara** — The Nāth-influenced teacher Viṣobā Khecara corrects Nāmdev's belief that the divine is confined to the Pandharpur image, teaching all-pervading formless presence _(actors: Nāmdev, Viṣobā Khecara)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/namdev.md) — (Wikipedia Namdev [traditional])
- **Installation of the giant monolithic Nandi** — A single-granite-block Nandi (~25 t), one of India's largest, is set before the sanctum _(actors: Chola sculptors; Nāyaka-era patrons (later maṇḍapa))_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/brihadeeswarar-thanjavur.md) — (Chola inscriptions)
- **Jhūlana Yātrā / Hindolā Utsava** — The monsoon swing festival up to Śrāvaṇa Pūrṇimā, when Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa are placed on flower-decked swings and rocked amid kīrtana, rooted in the Bhāgavata Vṛndāvana swing-līlā _(actors: Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa, priests and devotees, Gauḍīya/ISKCON/Puṣṭimārg communities)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/swing-and-float-temple-festivals.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa Kṛṣṇa-līlā; Wikipedia (Jhulan Yatra/Hindola); ISKCON/Krishna.com; Puṣṭimārg sevā tradition)
- **Kabir's trick-initiation by Ramananda** — Kabir lies across the Panchganga ghat steps before dawn; Ramananda trips over him crying 'Ram! Ram!', and Kabir seizes that involuntary cry as his initiating mantra, proclaiming himself the saint's disciple _(actors: Kabir, Ramananda)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/kabir.md) — (Popular hagiography (folk))
- **Kinnaur Kailāsh ~79-ft natural Śivaliṅga** — A ~79-ft svayambhū rock-Śivaliṅga near the Kinnaur Kailāsh summit, held to change colour through the day and reached by an arduous parikramā, worshipped as Śiva's winter abode _(actors: Śiva (Kinner Kailāsh); Himachali pilgrims)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/panch-kailash.md) — (Wikipedia (Kinnaur Kailash); regional yātrā tradition)
- **Living regional floor-art forms diverge** — The art differentiates into Rangoli, Kōlam (incl. pulli/sikku), Muggu, Ālpana, Maṇḍana and other named regional traditions with distinct media and grammars _(actors: Hindu households across north, south, east and west India)_ → [details](../09-artifacts-symbols-and-arts/rangoli-kolam-floor-art.md) — (Wikipedia: Rangoli, Kolam, Muggu, Alpana, Mandana [regional])
- **Madhūcchiṣṭa-vidhāna lost-wax single-pour casting** — The sthapati models the icon in beeswax, invests it in clay, de-waxes by firing, casts solid in one continuous pour, then breaks the mould and chases the bronze _(actors: Viśvakarma sthapati / śilpin)_ → [details](../09-artifacts-symbols-and-arts/pancha-loha-bronze-casting.md) — (Sivaramamurti, South Indian Bronzes; Dehejia, Chola: Sacred Bronzes)
- **Madhurakavi breaks Nammalvar's silence** — Following a southern light to the tamarind tree, Madhurakavi poses a riddle and the boy's first-ever words answer it, declaring the soul's total dependence on God; Madhurakavi becomes his disciple on the spot _(actors: Madhurakavi Alvar, Nammalvar)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/nammalvar.md) — (Tiruvaymoli tradition)
- **Manifestation (prakatya) of Banke Bihari** — At Haridasa's song the divine pair Radha-Krishna appear and merge into the single dark tribhanga icon of Banke Bihari, self-manifested from the soil of Nidhivana _(actors: Svami Haridasa, Radha-Krishna (Banke Bihari icon))_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/banke-bihari-vrindavan-nidhivan.md) — (Haridasi prakatya legend [folk])
- **Marriage to Jaratkāru and birth of Āstīka** — Manasā marries the sage Jaratkāru, who deserts her on the wedding night; she bears the sage Āstīka _(actors: Manasā, Jaratkāru, Āstīka)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/manasa.md) — (Mahābhārata Ādi Parva; Manasāmaṅgal)
- **Matsyendranāth receives the secret yoga** — 9th–10th c. first human Nāth guru and Kaula founder overhears Śiva's hidden yoga; revered in Nepal as Avalokiteśvara/Karuṇāmaya (Red Matsyendranāth) _(actors: Matsyendranāth (Macchindranāth), Śiva)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/nath-sampradaya-and-the-siddhas.md) — (Wikipedia (Matsyendranath, Gorakhnath); Nāth hagiography)
- **Maturai-Vīraṉ the warrior takes service under the Madurai king** — The warrior of low/outcaste birth enters the service of the Madurai Nāyak king, who appoints him kāval-kāraṉ (watchman) of the city _(actors: Maturai-Vīraṉ, the king of Madurai (Tirumalai Nāyak))_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/karuppasamy-madurai-veeran-and-tamil-kaval-deities.md) — (Madurai Veeran ballad (villuppāṭṭu))
- **Meeting and initiation by Vallabhācārya** — Vallabha hears Sūr's self-pity songs, tells him to sing Kṛṣṇa's līlā instead, and initiates him into the Puṣṭimārga via Brahma-sambandha _(actors: Sūrdās; Vallabhācārya)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/surdas.md) — (Caurāsī Vaiṣṇavan kī Vārtā; Hawley; Wikipedia Surdas)
- **Nabakalebara (renewal of Jagannath's wooden bodies)** — Every 12-19 years, when an Adhika Ashadha falls, the wooden bodies of the Puri deities are ritually replaced and the Brahma-padartha soul-substance transferred in secret _(actors: Jagannath)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/regional-temple-festivals.md) — (traditional)
- **Nivṛttināth initiates and guides Jñāneśvar** — Nivṛttināth becomes his own brother's guru and commands Jñāneśvar to compose the Jñāneśvarī (1290), the founding text of Marathi spirituality _(actors: Nivṛttināth, Jñāneśvar)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/nivritti-sopan-muktabai.md) — (Wikipedia (Nivruttinath, Dnyaneshwari))
- **Ostracism as children of a renunciant** — Orthodox Brāhmaṇas brand the children saṃnyāsī-putra, deny them the sacred thread and excommunicate the family _(actors: The four siblings, Brāhmaṇas of the region)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/jnaneshwar.md) — (Wikipedia (Dnyaneshwar); Prepp)
- **Parshvanatha, 23rd Tirthankara** — Parshvanatha establishes the Jain path as the 23rd Tirthankara, traditionally c. 9th-8th c. BCE, the earliest ford-maker most scholars treat as plausibly historical _(actors: Parshvanatha)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/nastika-darshanas-and-the-astika-nastika-frame.md) — (Kalpa-sutra; Jain tradition)
- **Pratisarga 'prophecy' of Adam, Noah, Moses** — The interpolated Pratisarga Parva narrates a Genesis-derived cycle of Ādhama and Havyavatī, Nyūha (Noah) and the flood, and Mūsā (Moses) — an 18th–19th-c. fabrication _(actors: Ādhama, Havyavatī, Nyūha, Mūsā)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/bhavishya-purana.md) — (Bhaviṣya Purāṇa (Pratisarga Parva) [interpolation])
- **Rañjāvatī's ordeal and the birth of Lausen** — Rañjāvatī, wife of Karṇasena of Maina, wins the boon of a son Lausen from Dharma Ṭhākur through extreme hook-swinging self-offering ordeal _(actors: Rañjāvatī, Karṇasena, Dharma Ṭhākur, Lausen)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/dharma-thakur.md) — (Dharma-maṅgal (Rañjāvatī episode))
- **Ramanuja Heads the Shri Sampradaya** — Ramanuja (trad. 1017-1137) is appointed head acharya of the Lakshmi-founded Shri Sampradaya, crystallizing it into the Vishishtadvaita school of qualified non-dualism _(actors: Ramanuja, Lakshmi)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/four-vaishnava-sampradayas.md) — (Wikipedia, Ramanuja)
- **Ramanuja systematizes Vishishtadvaita and reorganizes Srirangam** — Ramanuja settles at Srirangam, reorganizes its Ranganatha temple worship, propounds prapatti as the supreme means to moksha, and writes the Shri Bhashya refuting Shankara sutra by sutra _(actors: Ramanuja)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/ramanuja-and-madhva.md) — (Shri Bhashya; Vedartha Samgraha; Gadya Trayam)
- **Śrīdhar's bhanḍārā** — The brahmin devotee Śrīdhar holds a kanyā-pūjā feast at which the girl Vaiṣṇavī appears and miraculously feeds the whole assembly without the food running short _(actors: Śrīdhar, Vaiṣṇavī, the assembly)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/vaishno-devi-trikuta-katra.md) — (Vaiṣṇo Devī sthala legend)
- **Recovery of the Kṛṣṇa icon from the gopīcandana** — Washing the clay reveals Rukmiṇī's lost Bālagopāla icon, which Madhva carries to Udupi and installs (a Balarāma image is said to go to Malpe) _(actors: Madhvācārya)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/udupi-krishna-matha.md) — (Sumadhva Vijaya; Mādhva regional tradition)
- **Renunciation and the wandering naked dancer** — In her mid-20s Lallā abandons marriage and home, wandering as an ascetic, going about naked, dancing and singing her vākhs in God-intoxication _(actors: Lallā; (variant: Shāh Hamadān / a baker))_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/lalleshwari-lal-ded.md) — (Wikipedia, Lalleshwari; Hoskote, I, Lalla)
- **Restoration of the Jñāneśvarī text (c. 1584)** — Eknāth collates and purifies the corrupted Jñāneśvarī, producing the standard reliable recension underlying the modern printed text; legend ties this to finding Jñāneśvar's Āḷandī samādhi _(actors: Eknāth, Jñāneśvar (text's author))_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/eknath.md) — (Wikipedia Eknath, Dnyaneshwari; Vārkarī hagiography)
- **Rigveda Composed** — Oldest Veda layer composed c. 1500-1200 BCE (scholarly); tradition holds the Vedas eternal and authorless _(actors: Vedic rishis)_ → [details](../01-manvantaras/manvantara-07-vaivasvata/mahayuga-28-current/04-kali-yuga/recorded-history.md) — (Rigveda)
- **Sambandar raises Pūmpāvai from death** — Sambandar raises the merchant's daughter Pūmpāvai from death by his hymn and is given a pearl palanquin, parasol and cymbals by Śiva _(actors: Sambandar, Pūmpāvai, Śiva)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/the-nalvar-tamil-shaiva-saints.md) — (Wikipedia, Sambandar)
- **Self-manifestation of Devadamana on Govardhana** — The raised arm of the icon emerges from Govardhana hill near Āniyor/Jatipura and reveals itself as the self-manifest Govardhana-dhāraṇa form _(actors: Śrīnāthajī (Devadamana/Gopāla), local devotees)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/nathdwara-shrinathji.md) — (Puṣṭimārga vārtā literature)
- **Self-naming colophon** — The closing verses embed Tulsidas's poetic signature (tulasidasa sada hari cera) naming the author within the text _(actors: Tulsidas)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/hanuman-chalisa.md) — (Hanuman Chalisa, closing verses)
- **Shankara Recovers the Badrinath Image from the Alaknanda** — Adi Shankara is said to have recovered the self-manifest black shaligram image of Badrinarayan from the Alaknanda river, establishing the northern dham _(actors: Adi Shankara)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/char-dham-and-chota-char-dham.md) — (Skanda Purana, Badari-mahatmya)
- **Siri and her four-generation line apotheosised** — The Tuḷu goddess-heroine Siri suffers abandonment and is deified as a daiva, her saga continuing across four generations of women (Siri, Sonne, the twins Abbaga-Daraga); recorded by Lauri Honko as the ~15,000-line Siri epic and re-embodied annually at the Siri jātre mass group-possession of women _(actors: Siri; Sonne; twins Abbaga and Daraga; (recorder Gopala Naika / Lauri Honko))_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/regional-and-oral-epics.md) — (Siri-paddana; Lauri Honko, The Siri Epic as Performed by Gopala Naika)
- **Sūrya Siddhānta** — Cornerstone of Hindu mathematical astronomy revealed by Sūrya to Maya; planetary motions, Earth's sphericity, and the 4,320,000-year Mahāyuga synchronising all planets _(actors: Sūrya, Maya (frame); historical redactors)_ → [details](../00-time-and-cosmology/siddhantic-astronomy-and-astronomers.md) — (Sūrya Siddhānta)
- **Staging of the Cihna Yātrā** — Śaṅkaradeva stages the Cihna Yātrā, the first theatrical/visual-art presentation of the movement and seed of aṅkīyā nāṭ drama _(actors: Śaṅkaradeva)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/sankaradeva-and-assamese-vaishnavism.md) — (Wikipedia, Sankardev)
- **Surdas composes the Sursagar** — The blind Braj-bhasha master Surdas, supreme of the Ashtachhap, composes the Sursagar 'Ocean of Sur,' a vast song-anthology on Krishna's childhood and youth _(actors: Surdas)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/north-bhakti-sants.md) — (Sursagar)
- **Śītalā-aṣṭamī / Basoda cold-food vrata** — On Caitra (or regional Śrāvaṇa) aṣṭamī the hearth-fire is doused and only cold/stale Basoda food eaten and offered, enacting the cooling worship _(actors: Śītalā; women vrata-keepers and households)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/shitala.md) — (vrata-kathā tradition)
- **Testing the saints with the thāpaṭṇe (potter's mallet)** — Goroba taps each saint's head with his potter's mallet as one tests a pot for firing, declaring Nāmdev still 'unbaked' for lacking a living guru _(actors: Gorā Kumbhār, Nāmdev, Jñāneśvar, Muktābāī)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/gora-kumbhar.md) — (Mahīpati, Bhaktavijaya; Wikipedia, Namdev)
- **The cousins' feud** — Gugga and his cousins Arjan and Surjan quarrel over inheritance, cattle, or a slight to his bride, escalating into open war among the kinsmen _(actors: Gugga, Arjan, Surjan)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/gugga-gogaji.md) — (Gugga-gāthā)
- **The curse of impotence on Bāpiyā** — Dying by tragu, Bahucharā curses Bāpiyā with impotence and loss of manhood for assaulting a Cāraṇ woman _(actors: Bahucharā, Bāpiyā)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/bahuchara-mata.md) — (Cāraṇ folk tradition)
- **The Digvijaya (Conquest of the Directions)** — Shankara undertakes a pan-Indian debate-tour, defeating and converting rival schools, reordering monastic life and establishing his pithas _(actors: Adi Shankara)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/adi-shankara.md) — (Madhaviya Shankara-Digvijaya)
- **The Gaṅgā-in-the-cobbler's-tub saying** — Ravidās declines a Ganges pilgrimage with 'Man changā to kaṭhautī meṅ Gaṅgā', asserting inner purity over sacred geography _(actors: Ravidās)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/ravidas.md) — (Wikipedia Ravidas; popular hagiography [folk])
- **The 'Kausalya supraja Rama' opening verse** — The hymn opens by addressing the Lord as Rama, son of Kausalya, transplanting the Ramayana dawn-waking of Rama onto Venkateshvara _(actors: Annan; Venkateshvara identified with Rama; (echoing Vishvamitra and Rama))_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/venkatesha-suprabhatam.md) — (Venkatesha Suprabhatam v.1; Valmiki Ramayana Bala-kanda)
- **The voice from the river** — A voice answers from the waters promising a deliverer will be born to Devaki, wife of Ratanchand Lohano, at Nasarpur _(actors: Varuna / the water-god, Devaki, Ratanchand Lohano)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/jhulelal.md) — (Sindhi folk tradition)
- **The wild hunter boy** — The boy takes to non-Brahmanical ways — forest hunting with bow and arrow, toddy-drinking, and eating fish and meat _(actors: Muthappan (as boy), foster-household)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/muthappan.md) — (Muthappan origin legend (theyyam oral tradition))
- **Tiruppan Alvar Carried into the Srirangam Sanctum** — The low-born Panar bard saint Tiruppan Alvar is carried into the Srirangam sanctum and composes the Amalanadipiran, a head-to-foot vision of Ranganatha, emblem of bhakti transcending caste _(actors: Tiruppan Alvar)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/alvars-and-nayanars.md) — (Naalayira Divya Prabandham)
- **Tukārām's dream-initiation by Bābājī Caitanya** — Having no living guru, Tukārām receives dream-dīkṣā when a teacher named Bābājī Caitanya lays a hand on his head and gives the mantra 'Rām Kṛṣṇa Hari.' _(actors: Tukārām, Bābājī Caitanya)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/tukaram.md) — (VivekaVani; New World Encyclopedia)
- **Twelve years of austerity at Ṭākaḷī** — Performed ~12 years of tapas (c. 1620–1632) on the Godāvarī at Ṭākaḷī near Nāśik, reciting the Rāma mantra 13 crore times _(actors: Rāmdās; (vision of Rāma and Hanumān))_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/samarth-ramdas.md) — (Wikipedia; Hindupedia)
- **Vallabha founds the Pushtimarg** — Vallabha founds the Pushtimarg 'Path of Grace' and systematizes Shuddhadvaita, teaching salvation by divine grace and sevA through Brahma-sambandha self-dedication to Krishna, and authors the Anubhashya and Subodhini _(actors: Vallabhacharya)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/nimbarka-vallabha-chaitanya.md) — (Anubhashya; Subodhini; Shodasha-granthah)
- **Vasugupta receives the Śiva-Sūtras** — Śiva reveals the Śiva-Sūtras (77 aphorisms) to Vasugupta, inscribed on a rock at Mahādeva mountain, seeding Kashmir Śaivism and the Spanda doctrine _(actors: Śiva, Vasugupta)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/shaiva-schools.md) — (New World Encyclopedia, Kasmir Saivism; SUNY)
- **Viṭṭhal helps Janī with her chores** — Legend has Viṭhobā/Viṭṭhal himself descend to help Janī at the quern, sweep, gather cow-dung, comb her hair and bathe her, the Lord lowering himself to the servant-girl's labor _(actors: Janābāī, Viṭhobā (Viṭṭhal))_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/janabai.md) — (Vārkarī hagiography; Marathi grinding-song abhaṅgas)
- **Vyāsatīrtha institutionalizes the Haridāsa movement** — Dvaita dialectician and Vijayanagara rāja-guru (c. 1460–1539), bridges Vyāsa-kūṭa and Dāsa-kūṭa, mentors Purandara and Kanaka; signature 'Sirī Kṛṣṇa' _(actors: Vyāsatīrtha (Vyāsarāja), Kṛṣṇadevarāya)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/haridasa-and-carnatic-bhakti.md) — (Haridāsa-sāhitya; Wikipedia, Vyasatirtha)
- **Wandering household** — Economic hardship forces the family to move repeatedly through Maharashtra towns, framing her spiritual turn _(actors: Bahiṇābāī; her family)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/bahinabai.md) — (Feldhaus; Tharu & Lalita)
- **Widowing of Mirabai** — Bhojraj, wounded in battle c.1518, dies c.1521 before succeeding to the throne, leaving Mira widowed young and never reigning as queen _(actors: Mirabai, Bhojraj)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/mirabai.md) — (Rajput chronicle)
- **Xuanzang Records the Prayag Assembly under Harsha** — The Chinese pilgrim Xuanzang records a great bathing-and-almsgiving assembly at Prayag convened by King Harsha in 644 CE, often cited as the earliest historical witness to the Kumbh _(actors: Xuanzang, King Harsha)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/kumbh-mela-and-pilgrimage-cycles.md) — (Wikipedia; Sahapedia)
- **Abhinavagupta receives the Kaula transmission** — Abhinavagupta (c. 975–1025) receives the Kaula transmission from his guru Śambhunātha and fuses Krama's Kālī-core into a Kaula-Trika synthesis in the Tantrāloka _(actors: Abhinavagupta, Śambhunātha)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/shakta-and-tantra-lineages.md) — (Wikipedia, Kashmir Shaivism)
- **Alaktaka / āltā red lac foot-dye in literature and bridal rite** — Lac-derived red dye paints the feet and hands, a kāvya beauty-convention and a Bengali/Odia bridal item leaving auspicious Lakṣmī-like footprints _(actors: brides; goddess images Durgā, Lakṣmī, Kālī; poets (Kālidāsa))_ → [details](../09-artifacts-symbols-and-arts/mehndi-and-ritual-body-adornment.md) — (Sanskrit kāvya; Mahābhārata Lākṣā-gṛha; Wikipedia Alta (dye))
- **All-India pilgrimage** — Wandered the subcontinent on foot c. 1632–1644, concluding that dharma needed physical strength and organization _(actors: Rāmdās)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/samarth-ramdas.md) — (Wikipedia; Hindupedia)
- **Andal Dreams Her Wedding to Vishnu (Varanam Ayiram)** — In the Varanam Ayiram decad of the Nacciyar Tirumoli, Andal dreams her marriage to Vishnu in vivid ritual detail, the textual core of her dream-wedding identity _(actors: Andal, Vishnu)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/andal.md) — (Nacciyar Tirumoli; Andal — Wikipedia)
- **Annual Gangamma Jātara re-enacts the veṣams** — The week-long Tirupati festival re-performs the goddess's disguises day by day, culminating in the colossal fierce clay form which is then broken _(actors: Gangamma, townspeople of Tirupati, festival servitors)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/gangamma.md) — (Tirupati festival tradition; Handelman/Krishnamurthy/Dawson and Flueckiger ethnography [regional/scholarly])
- **Basava's reform program and the ishtalinga** — Basava abolishes caste in worship and table-fellowship, rejects temple-icon ritual and animal sacrifice, and gives every devotee regardless of birth or sex the ishtalinga to wear, making the body the temple _(actors: Basava)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/lingayat-vachana-poets.md) — (Vachana sahitya; Lingayatism)
- **Battle of the 1760 Haridwar Kumbh** — At the 1760 Haridwar Kumbh, competition over bathing primacy erupts into a deadly battle between Shaiva Gosains and Vaishnava Bairagis, leaving hundreds dead _(actors: Shaiva Gosains, Vaishnava Bairagis)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/kumbh-mela-and-pilgrimage-cycles.md) — (Wikipedia, Kumbh Mela)
- **Bhairavnāth's pursuit** — The tantric yogī Bhairavnāth lusts after Vaiṣṇavī and demands marriage; she refuses and flees up the Trikūṭa mountain with him in pursuit _(actors: Bhairavnāth, Vaiṣṇavī)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/vaishno-devi-trikuta-katra.md) — (Vaiṣṇo Devī sthala legend)
- **Bhasmāsura–Mohinī legend at Shrīkhaṇḍ Mahādev** — The demon Bhasmāsura, granted a head-touch incineration boon, is tricked by Viṣṇu-as-Mohinī into laying his own hand on his head and burning to ash — localized at the ~5,155–5,227 m Shrīkhaṇḍ massif with its ~75-ft rock Śivaliṅga _(actors: Bhasmāsura, Śiva, Viṣṇu as Mohinī)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/panch-kailash.md) — (Bhāgavata / Śiva-purāṇic Bhasmāsura cycle; Shrīkhaṇḍ sthala-purāṇa; Wikipedia (Shrikhand Mahadev))
- **Bhogar casts the Palani navapāṣāṇa Murugan image** — The alchemist-Siddhar Bhogar is traditionally credited with casting the Palani Murugan mūrti from nine minerals/poisons (navapāṣāṇa) _(actors: Bhogar; Murugan (Palani))_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/tamil-siddhars.md) — (Wikipedia (Bhogar, Palani Murugan Temple))
- **Birth of Uderolal at Nasarpur** — The child Uderolal is born at Nasarpur on the Indus amid marvels and hailed as the avatara of Varuna _(actors: Uderolal (Jhulelal), Devaki, Ratanchand Lohano)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/jhulelal.md) — (Sindhi folk tradition; Varuna-avatara lore)
- **Bāṅke Bihārī Jhūlana (Vṛndāvana)** — At the Bāṅke Bihārī temple the veiled deity is brought to a golden hindolā (svarṇa-hindolā) for the jhūlana days, one of Braj's most thronged darśanas _(actors: Bāṅke Bihārī (Kṛṣṇa), Vṛndāvana devotees)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/swing-and-float-temple-festivals.md) — (Wikipedia (Banke Bihari Temple); Braj temple tradition)
- **Brāhmaṇa-challenge and floating-stone miracles** — Challenged by Brāhmaṇas who scorn an untouchable's worship, Ravidās produces the sacred thread from his chest and makes a stone float _(actors: Ravidās, Brāhmaṇa challengers)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/ravidas.md) — (Grokipedia; popular hagiography [folk])
