- **Caste-transgressing acts of compassion** — Eknāth defies caste norms by feeding untouchables at śrāddha, bathing a sick outcaste, and (in legend) pouring sacred Rāmeśvaram water down a dying donkey's throat, drawing orthodox censure _(actors: Eknāth, Mahār/Mātaṅga community, Paithan Brāhmaṇas)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/eknath.md) — (Wikipedia Eknath; Vārkarī hagiography)
- **Codification of the temple threshold-ornament canon in the Śilpa/Āgama corpus** — The Mānasāra, Mayamata and Śaiva/Vaikhānasa-Pāñcarātra Āgamas systematize the doorway, pillar and gateway ornament — kīrtimukha, makara-toraṇa, vyāla/yāḷi pillars, dvārapālas and gajalakṣmī lintels — as protective alaṅkāra distinct from cult-image iconometry, codifying older guild practice c. 5th–13th c. CE _(actors: śilpins/sthapatis; redactors of the Mānasāra, Mayamata and Āgama kriyāpādas)_ → [details](../09-artifacts-symbols-and-arts/temple-iconographic-motifs.md) — (Mānasāra; Mayamata; Śaiva/Vaikhānasa/Pāñcarātra Āgama dvāra-lakṣaṇa; T. A. Gopinatha Rao)
- **Co-founding of Vārkarī kīrtana with Jñāneśvar** — Nāmdev, contemporary and companion of Jñāneśvar, helps establish communal nāma-saṅkīrtana as the devotional engine of the Vārkarī movement _(actors: Nāmdev, Jñāneśvar)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/namdev.md) — (Wikipedia Warkari, Namdev; Vārkarī hagiography)
- **Composition of Later Vedic Corpus and Upanishads** — The Sama, Yajur and Atharva Vedas plus Brahmanas, Aranyakas and principal Upanishads are composed c. 1200-500 BCE, shifting from sacrificial ritualism to Atman-Brahman metaphysics _(actors: Vedic sages)_ → [details](../01-manvantaras/manvantara-07-vaivasvata/mahayuga-28-current/04-kali-yuga/recorded-history.md) — (Vedic corpus; Brihadaranyaka and Chandogya Upanishads)
- **Composition of the Mahishasura Mardini Stotra** — A medieval Devi hymn versifying the buffalo-demon slaying is composed, traditionally ascribed to Adi Shankara _(actors: Adi Shankara (traditional attribution); disputed later medieval poet)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/mahishasura-mardini-stotra.md) — (Mahiṣāsura-mardinī stotra)
- **Composition of the Saundarya-laharī as a Śrīvidyā work (text-critical)** — By the text-critical clock the developed Tantric Saundarya-laharī, presupposing the mature Śrī Cakra system, is composed in the medieval Śrīvidyā efflorescence by a later author under Śaṅkara's name _(actors: later Śrīvidyā author (disputed); attributed to Ādi Śaṅkara)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/soundarya-lahari.md) — (Saundarya-laharī; W. Norman Brown (HOS 43))
- **Council of Valabhī redacts the Śvetāmbara Āgama canon** — The Śvetāmbara Jain canon of Āgamas (the Aṅgas and allied texts) is redacted at the Council of Valabhī (c. 5th c. CE) as the surviving word descending from Mahāvīra's gaṇadharas; the Digambaras reject this, holding the original canon lost and revering instead the Ṣaṭkhaṇḍāgama _(actors: Śvetāmbara saṅgha; (against) the Digambaras; Puṣpadanta & Bhūtabali (Ṣaṭkhaṇḍāgama authors))_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/nastika-darshanas-and-the-astika-nastika-frame.md) — (Jain tradition; Ṣaṭkhaṇḍāgama)
- **Counted among the Aṣṭachāp under Viṭṭhalanātha** — Sūr is reckoned greatest of the eight Aṣṭachāp poet-singers, organized into a singing collegium by Vallabha's son Viṭṭhalanātha _(actors: Sūrdās; Viṭṭhalanātha (Gusāīñjī); the Aṣṭachāp)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/surdas.md) — (Wikipedia Aṣṭachāp; Vraj Dwar)
- **Daily dawn threshold-drawing as Lakṣmī-invitation** — Women renew the rice-flour kōlam/muggu at dawn on the swept doorstep to invite Śrī-Lakṣmī, feed small creatures, and seal the home's boundary _(actors: Hindu women / householders; Lakṣmī (invoked))_ → [details](../09-artifacts-symbols-and-arts/rangoli-kolam-floor-art.md) — (Wikipedia, Kolam; Tamil/Telugu household custom [folk])
- **Dandiya Rasa as Krishna's rasa-lila echo** — The paired stick-dance dandiya is read as an echo of Krishna's rasa-lila with the gopis, tying the Goddess and Krishna threads into one festival _(actors: Dancers, Krishna, the gopis)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/garba-and-dandiya-raas.md) — (Bhagavata Purana 10.29-33; classical Tala/Danda rasa repertoire)
- **Development of the Vesara hybrid order in the Deccan** — The intermediate Deccan order fuses Nāgara and Drāviḍa — storeyed articulation pulled into a rounded, often stellate profile — under the Cālukyas, Rāṣṭrakūṭas and Hoysaḷas; its very name and definition remain disputed (geographic hybrid vs. apsidal/round-plan) _(actors: Bādāmi Cālukyas, Rāṣṭrakūṭas, Hoysaḷas; exemplars at Aihoḷe, Paṭṭadakal, Belūr, Halebīḍu, Somanāthapura)_ → [details](../09-artifacts-symbols-and-arts/temple-architecture-nagara-dravida-vesara.md) — (Wikipedia (Vesara, Badami Chalukya architecture); Vāstu Śāstra; Mānasāra)
- **Dhona's bargain abandons Dukhe** — Merchant Dhona, unable to gather honey, is promised wealth by Dakshin Ray in exchange for abandoning the poor boy Dukhe on the Kedokhali flats as a human offering _(actors: Dhona Mauley, Dukhe, Dakshin Ray)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/bonbibi.md) — (Bonbibi Jahuranama)
- **Disciples add the Caturdaśa-mañjarikā verses** — Fourteen of Śaṅkara's disciples (trad. Padmapāda, Toṭaka, Hastāmalaka, Sureśvara, etc.) each contribute a verse, expanding the hymn _(actors: Śaṅkara's disciples (Padmapāda, Toṭaka, Hastāmalaka, Sureśvara and others))_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/bhaja-govindam.md) — (Caturdaśa-mañjarikā Stotra; Cinmayānanda edition tradition)
- **Disciples contribute the Caturdaśa-mañjarikā verses** — Inspired by the master, fourteen disciples (traditionally Padmapāda, Toṭakācārya, Hastāmalaka, Sureśvara, et al.) each add a verse, forming the 'fourteen-blossom' set and the composite ~31-verse hymn _(actors: Padmapāda, Toṭakācārya, Hastāmalaka, Sureśvara (Maṇḍana Miśra), and other disciples of Śaṅkara)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/bhaja-govindam.md) — (Bhaja Govindam / Caturdaśa-mañjarikā Stotra (traditional ascription))
- **Establishment as flagship of the suprabhata genre** — The hymn becomes the most famous and most-imitated awakening-hymn, the template for later suprabhatam compositions for other deities _(actors: Sri Vaishnava tradition; later suprabhata composers)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/venkatesha-suprabhatam.md) — (Venkatesha Suprabhatam; suprabhata-genre tradition)
- **Founding of Ekaśaraṇa Dharma** — Śaṅkaradeva founds the Ekaśaraṇa ('single refuge') monotheistic Kṛṣṇa/Nārāyaṇa devotion, structuring it around the cāri vastu (Nāma, Deva, Guru, Bhakat) _(actors: Śaṅkaradeva)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/sankaradeva-and-assamese-vaishnavism.md) — (Wikipedia, Ekasarana Dharma)
- **Gorakṣa rouses Matsyendra at Kadalī** — Disciple Gorakṣa enters the enchanted women's kingdom of Kadalī in disguise and wakes his bewitched guru with the cry 'Cala Macchindra, Gorakh āyā!' _(actors: Gorakṣanāth, Matsyendranāth)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/nath-sampradaya-and-the-siddhas.md) — (Nāth hagiography; Navnāth Bhaktisār)
- **Hirapur Chausaṭh Yoginī Temple Built** — The Bhauma-Kara queen Hīrādevī builds the small circular hypaethral Chausaṭh Yoginī temple at Hirapur c. 864 CE with 64 black-stone yoginī images and a central Kālī/Mahāmāyā shrine _(actors: Hīrādevī)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/matrikas-and-64-yoginis.md) — (ASI; Wikipedia: Chausath Yogini Temple, Hirapur)
- **Initiation by Sed Bāyu / Siddha Śrīkaṇṭha** — Lallā is initiated into Trika non-dualism by her guru Sed Bāyu (Siddha Śrīkaṇṭha), and in some vākhs surpasses her teacher _(actors: Lallā; Sed Bāyu / Siddha Śrīkaṇṭha)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/lalleshwari-lal-ded.md) — (Grierson & Barnett, Lallā-vākyāni; Wikipedia, Lalleshwari)
- **Jñāneśvar and the Jñāneśvarī** — Jñāneśvar (c.1275–1296 CE) founds the Vārkarī poetic stream with the Marathi Jñāneśvarī Gītā-commentary, Haripāṭh and abhaṅgas, taking samādhi at Āḷandī _(actors: Jñāneśvar/Jñāndev)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/vithoba-vitthal.md) — (Jñāneśvarī (Bhāvārtha-dīpikā); Haripāṭh)
- **Kabir's double critique of Hindu and Islamic orthodoxy** — Kabir delivers his iconoclastic teaching attacking idol-worship, pilgrimage, caste and the sacred thread on the Hindu side and the muezzin's call, namaz, hajj and circumcision on the Muslim side, declaring independence from both religions _(actors: Kabir)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/kabir.md) — (Bijak; Kabir verse)
- **Kaumudi Mahotsava — classical moonlight festival** — The classical-period autumn full-moon festival of moonlight and revelry (kaumudi), the courtly observance-layer beneath the Puranic charters, surviving in the name Kaumudi Purnima _(actors: Classical courts/cities; literary tradition (Kaumudi-mahotsava nataka attributed to Vijjika))_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/sharad-purnima-kojagari-lakshmi-puja.md) — (Kaumudi Mahotsava in classical Sanskrit sources [scholarly/disputed])
- **Koṭi and Cennaya martyred and deified as daivas** — The Billava twin warrior-brothers Koṭi and Cennaya of medieval Tuḷu Nāḍu die fighting injustice and are deified as daivas/bhūtas, worshipped in Bhūta-Kōla possession ritual across coastal Karnataka with their own garaḍi shrines _(actors: Koṭi and Cennaya (Billava twin brothers))_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/regional-and-oral-epics.md) — (Koṭi-Cennaya paddana; Koti and Chennaya — Wikipedia)
- **Lakṣmī's reluctance over merit** — In an expansion, Lakṣmī demurs that the woman lacks accumulated puṇya; Śaṅkara argues her last-gift act is supreme merit, moving the goddess to relent _(actors: Lakṣmī, Ādi Śaṅkara, the poor woman)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/kanakadhara-stotram.md) — (Śaṅkara-vijaya tradition (variant))
- **Lausen makes the sun rise in the west** — To prove Dharma's power, Lausen offers his own body and worships until the god makes the sun rise in the west, then restores him _(actors: Lausen, Dharma Ṭhākur)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/dharma-thakur.md) — (Dharma-maṅgal (Lausen cycle))
- **Śālivāhana meets Īśā (Jesus)** — King Śālivāhana is said to encounter in the Himalayas a teacher calling himself Īśā-putra, born of a virgin — a late Christian interpolation _(actors: Śālivāhana, Īśā)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/bhavishya-purana.md) — (Bhaviṣya Purāṇa (Pratisarga Parva) [interpolation])
- **Madhva founds the Krishna Matha and appoints eight disciples** — Madhva enshrines Kṛṣṇa, digs the Madhva-sarovara, founds the Dvaita headquarters c. 1285 CE, and appoints eight sannyāsī disciples to worship in turn _(actors: Madhvācārya, the eight disciples)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/udupi-krishna-matha.md) — (Sumadhva Vijaya; Mādhva tradition)
- **Madhva Founds the Udupi Seat and Dvaita School** — Madhva (1238-1317), held to be the third avatara of Vayu, heads the Brahma Sampradaya, founds the Udupi seat-cluster, and systematizes the realist Dvaita / Tattvavada doctrine _(actors: Madhva)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/four-vaishnava-sampradayas.md) — (Wikipedia, Madhvacharya)
- **Maṇimēkalai renounces the world and gains the alms-bowl** — Maṇimēkalai, daughter of Kōvalaṉ and Mādhavi, renounces the world for the Buddhist path and obtains the never-empty magic alms-bowl Amuta-curapi _(actors: Maṇimēkalai)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/tamil-canon-sangam-and-bhakti.md) — (Maṇimēkalai)
- **Makkhali Gosala founds the Ajivika order (niyati-vada)** — Makkhali Gosala leads the Ajivika ascetic movement teaching strict fatalism (niyati), and breaks with his former associate Mahavira _(actors: Makkhali Gosala, Mahavira)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/nastika-darshanas-and-the-astika-nastika-frame.md) — (Digha Nikaya 2 (Samannaphala Sutta); Bhagavati Sutra)
- **Mataṅga defines and names rāga in the Bṛhaddeśī** — The Bṛhaddeśī gives the first surviving definition of rāga ('raṅjayati iti rāgaḥ'), shifting theory from jāti to rāga and formalizing the deśī melodic types and the mārga/deśī divide _(actors: Mataṅga Muni)_ → [details](../09-artifacts-symbols-and-arts/sangita-classical-music-svara-raga-tala.md) — (Bṛhaddeśī of Mataṅga)
- **Maturai-Vīraṉ's loves with Bommi and Vellaiyammāḷ** — Maturai-Vīraṉ weds Bommi, a palace dancing-girl, and is bound to Vellaiyammāḷ; the scandalous affair turns the king against him _(actors: Maturai-Vīraṉ, Bommi, Vellaiyammāḷ, the king of Madurai)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/karuppasamy-madurai-veeran-and-tamil-kaval-deities.md) — (Madurai Veeran ballad (villuppāṭṭu))
- **Milarepa Flies to the Kailāsa Summit** — In the Bön/Buddhist legend the yogi Milarepa flies to the summit of Kailāsa on a sunbeam, winning the mountain for Buddhism through devotion rather than climbing _(actors: Milarepa)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/manasarovar-kailash-yatra.md) — (Bon/Buddhist tradition)
- **Mirabai defies widow-propriety and caste** — Rejecting Rajput widow seclusion, Mira sings and dances publicly before Krishna's image and consorts with low-caste sadhus, the core scandal driving the persecution legends _(actors: Mirabai, sadhus)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/mirabai.md) — (Mirabai hagiography)
- **Mucalinda Shelters the Buddha** — The nāga Mucalinda coils seven times and spreads his hood to shelter the meditating Buddha from a seven-day storm _(actors: Mucalinda, Buddha)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/nagas-and-serpents.md) — (Buddhist canon)
- **Muktābāī composes the Tāṭīce Abhaṅga** — When the hurt Jñāneśvar shuts the door (tāṭī), the young Muktābāī coaxes him out with a celebrated abhaṅga-cycle teaching the bhakti ethic of forbearance _(actors: Muktābāī, Jñāneśvar)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/nivritti-sopan-muktabai.md) — (Tāṭīce Abhaṅga; Wikipedia (Muktabai); Tharu & Lalita)
- **Nammalvar composes the Tiruvaymoli and his four works** — Nammalvar composes his four Tamil works totaling 1,296 verses — the Tiruvaymoli, Tiruviruttam, Tiruvaciriyam and Periya Tiruvandhadhi — revered as the Tamil Veda equated with the four Vedas _(actors: Nammalvar)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/nammalvar.md) — (Tiruvaymoli; Nalayira Divya Prabandham)
- **Narsinh Mehta composes Vaishnava Jana To** — The Adi Kavi of Gujarati, Narsinh Mehta, sings Krishna's rasa-lila and composes 'Vaishnava Jana To,' which became Gandhi's favorite bhajan and a near-anthem of the freedom movement _(actors: Narsinh Mehta)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/north-bhakti-sants.md) — (Narsinh Mehta padas)
- **Nāmdev sent to complete his realization under Viṣobā Khecara** — Stung by the 'unbaked' verdict, Nāmdev appeals to Viṭhobā, who confirms it and directs him to the living guru Viṣobā Khecara to be 'fired to completion' _(actors: Nāmdev, Viṭhobā, Viṣobā Khecara, Gorā Kumbhār)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/gora-kumbhar.md) — (Mahīpati, Bhaktavijaya; Vārkarī hagiography)
- **Parents' self-sacrifice and the Paithan purification quest** — Viṭṭhalapant and Rukmiṇībāī end their lives to lift the curse; the boys seek a śuddhi-patra from the pundits of Paithan _(actors: Viṭṭhalapant, Rukmiṇībāī, the siblings, Paithan Brāhmaṇas)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/jnaneshwar.md) — (Wikipedia (Dnyaneshwar); Vārkarī hagiography [folk])
- **Possession and oracle cult** — The goddess mounts chosen devotees who shake, dance and prophesy at the jātara, centred on possession and pot-carrying _(actors: Possessed mediums; village goddess)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/gramadevatas-village-deities.md) — (Grāmadevatā possession-cult studies)
- **Purandara Dāsa codifies Carnatic pedagogy** — 'Father of Carnatic music' (c. 1484–1564) creates the graded piḷḷāri gītes and Māyāmāḷavagauḷa exercises and vast devaranāma under 'Purandara Viṭṭhala' _(actors: Purandara Dāsa)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/haridasa-and-carnatic-bhakti.md) — (Haridāsa-sāhitya; biographies of Purandara Dāsa)
- **Raja Jagat Singh installs Raghunath as Kullu's sovereign** — As penance for a curse, King Jagat Singh installed an idol of Raghunath (Rama) brought from Ayodhya as Kullu valley's true king and ruled as his regent, founding Kullu Dussehra _(actors: Jagat Singh, Raghunath/Rama)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/regional-temple-festivals.md) — (Wikipedia, Kullu Dussehra)
- **Ramakrishna's multi-path realization at Dakshineswar** — The Dakshineswar Kali priest Ramakrishna attains experiential realization through multiple paths — Tantra, Vaishnava bhakti, Advaita, Islam and Christianity — grounding the doctrine that all religions lead to the same God _(actors: Ramakrishna Paramahamsa)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/modern-teachers-and-reform.md) — (Ramakrishna Mission history)
- **Ramanuja's exile to Melkote** — Fleeing Chola Shaiva-aligned persecution, Ramanuja takes refuge at Melkote in the Hoysala Karnataka country, founding institutions there before returning to Srirangam _(actors: Ramanuja)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/ramanuja-and-madhva.md) — (Ramanuja hagiography (traditional))
- **Rebuke and self-revelation as god** — Rebuked for his caste-breaking habits, the boy reveals his true divine (cosmic) form, showing he is god, and the couple worship him _(actors: Muthappan, Ayyankara Vazhunnavar, Padikutty Amma)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/muthappan.md) — (Muthappan origin legend)
- **Reception as the foremost short Vedantic stotra** — The Atma Shatkam becomes the most-quoted short Advaita hymn, chanted across mathas, Yoga schools and Vedanta movements with its Shivo'ham refrain set to music _(actors: Advaita mathas, Yoga and Vedanta teachers, devotees)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/nirvana-shatkam.md) — (Nirvana Shatkam recitation and commentary tradition)
- **Release only through worship in female guise** — The curse can be lifted only by worshipping Bahucharā in female guise; this clause becomes the charter for those who renounce or transcend male gender _(actors: Bahucharā, Bāpiyā)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/bahuchara-mata.md) — (Cāraṇ folk tradition)
- **Rāmānuja's flight to the Hoysaḷa country** — Fleeing Cōḻa Śaiva persecution at Śrīraṅgam, Rāmānuja crosses into Karnataka for a ~12-year exile centered on Melkote (early–mid 12th c. CE) _(actors: Rāmānuja)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/melkote-tirunarayanapuram.md) — (Śrī Vaiṣṇava guruparamparā hagiography)
- **Āryabhaṭa and the Āryabhaṭīya (499 CE)** — Founding dated astronomical text giving π≈3.1416, sine tables, and the radical doctrine of Earth's axial rotation; midnight vs sunrise reckoning schools _(actors: Āryabhaṭa)_ → [details](../00-time-and-cosmology/siddhantic-astronomy-and-astronomers.md) — (Āryabhaṭīya)
- **Samayapuram temple cult** — The largest Māriyammaṉ temple near Tiruchirappalli, with an unanointed clay-and-sand mūla-vigraha and mass Cittirai festivals _(actors: Māriyammaṉ; Samayapuram devotees)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/mariamman.md) — (Samayapuram sthala tradition)
- **Sambandar merges into the jyoti at his wedding** — At his own marriage at Nallūr-Perumaṇam, Sambandar prays and merges into the divine effulgence, taking the entire wedding party into Śiva _(actors: Sambandar)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/the-nalvar-tamil-shaiva-saints.md) — (Wikipedia, Sambandar)
- **Sambandar Turns the Pandya King Back to Shaivism** — The child-prodigy saint Tirugnana Sambandar, fed divine milk by Parvati, composes hymns that lead the Pandya king back to Shaivism from Jainism _(actors: Sambandar, Pandya king)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/alvars-and-nayanars.md) — (Tevaram)
- **Shankara Debates Mandana Mishra** — Shankara challenges the Mimamsa ritualist Mandana Mishra at Mahishmati with his wife Ubhaya Bharati as umpire; Shankara prevails and Mandana takes sannyasa as Sureshvaracharya _(actors: Adi Shankara, Mandana Mishra, Ubhaya Bharati)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/adi-shankara.md) — (esamskriti; Wikipedia)
- **Somānanda founds the Pratyabhijñā school** — Early 10th c., Somānanda founds the Pratyabhijñā ('recognition') school with his Śiva-dṛṣṭi and teaches Utpaladeva _(actors: Somānanda, Utpaladeva)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/shaiva-schools.md) — (IEP, Kashmiri)
- **Svāmimalai/Thañjāvūr Viśvakarma sthapati lineages** — Hereditary bronze-caster guilds of the Kāveri delta, tracing craft to Viśvakarman, transmit and still practise the pañca-loha lost-wax rite _(actors: Svāmimalai/Thañjāvūr Viśvakarma sthapatis)_ → [details](../09-artifacts-symbols-and-arts/pancha-loha-bronze-casting.md) — (Svāmimalai sthapati tradition; Dehejia)
- **Tansen, Akbar and Haridasa** — Disputed tradition makes the Mughal court-musician Tansen a disciple of Haridasa, with Akbar coming incognito to hear him sing _(actors: Svami Haridasa, Tansen, Akbar)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/banke-bihari-vrindavan-nidhivan.md) — (Folk/biographical tradition [disputed])
- **The Bidar/Bedar sultan's summons to his harem** — The Sultan of Bidar, hearing of her beauty, sends soldiers to bring her to his harem; she refuses the royal patronage _(actors: Kānhopātrā, Sultan of Bidar/Bedar, his soldiers)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/kanhopatra.md) — (Vārkarī hagiography)
- **The calf legend** — Bahiṇā's beloved calf collapses and dies in devotional rapture at a kīrtan and she falls near death in grief, a turning point into bhakti _(actors: Bahiṇābāī; the calf)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/bahinabai.md) — (Wikipedia 'Bahina Bai'; Feldhaus)
- **The composing household sings abhangas** — Chokhamela's Mahar household produces a roster of poet-saints: wife Soyarabai on bodily impurity and caste-pollution, son Karmamela's bitter abhangas questioning his untouchable birth, sister Nirmala, and brother-in-law Banka _(actors: Soyarabai; Karmamela; Nirmala; Banka)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/chokhamela.md) — (Soyarabai/Karmamela/Banka/Nirmala abhanga corpus; Tharu & Lalita, Women Writing in India; Zelliot)
- **The drowned manuscripts miracle** — Ordered by Rāmeśvar Bhaṭṭ to destroy his poems, Tukārām sinks his manuscripts in the Indrāyaṇī and fasts 13 days, after which they float up intact, vindicating a Śūdra's right to scripture _(actors: Tukārām, Rāmeśvar Bhaṭṭ)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/tukaram.md) — (mkvyoga.com; New World Encyclopedia)
- **The giant terracotta-horse votive offering** — Potters (Kuyavar/Vēlar) make and consecrate avenues of enormous fired-clay horses (and elephants, bulls) as mounts pledged to Ayyaṉār in fulfilment of vows _(actors: Ayyaṉār; Kuyavar/Vēlar potter-caste; vow-makers)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/ayyanar-sastha.md) — (Terracotta-horse cult lore; Tamil folk tradition)
- **The great endowment inscriptions** — Rājarāja I endows the temple with lands, gold, and ~400 devadāsīs and staff, recorded in one of India's most detailed economic inscriptions _(actors: Rājarāja Chola I; temple personnel and devadāsīs)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/brihadeeswarar-thanjavur.md) — (Rājarāja I's Thanjavur prākāra inscriptions)
- **The slaying of the cousins** — Gugga kills his rival cousins Arjan and Surjan in battle, carrying off their heads in the grimmest recensions _(actors: Gugga, Arjan, Surjan)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/gugga-gogaji.md) — (Gugga-gāthā)
- **The stolen-necklace false accusation and deliverance** — Janī is falsely accused of stealing the temple necklace and condemned to death, but Viṭṭhal intervenes to save his devotee and the charge dissolves _(actors: Janābāī, Viṭṭhal, temple accusers)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/janabai.md) — (Vārkarī hagiography)
- **The vrata-kathā of the cold-food woman** — In the standard tale a woman who kept Basoda is spared the fire/pox while a neighbour who cooked fresh and offended Śītalā is stricken until she submits _(actors: Śītalā; the pious cold-food woman; the offending cooking woman)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/shitala.md) — (Śītalā vrata-kathā tradition)
- **Twelve-year drought caused by the elder being passed over** — Śaṃtanu's kingdom suffers a twelve-year drought because the rightful elder Devāpi was passed over for the throne, offending dharma so the gods withhold rain _(actors: Śaṃtanu, Devāpi, the gods/Parjanya)_ → [details](../03-lineages-vamsha/devapi.md) — (Nirukta II.10; Bṛhaddevatā)
- **Upanishads & Later Vedic Corpus** — Brahmanas, Aranyakas and principal Upanishads c. 1200-500 BCE shift focus to Atman-Brahman metaphysics _(actors: Vedic sages)_ → [details](../01-manvantaras/manvantara-07-vaivasvata/mahayuga-28-current/04-kali-yuga/recorded-history.md) — (Brihadaranyaka, Chandogya Upanishads)
- **Vallabha discovers and serves Shrinathji** — Vallabha discovers and serves the self-manifest Shrinathji image — Krishna as a child lifting Mount Govardhana — on Govardhana hill in Braj, the central icon of the Pushtimarg _(actors: Vallabha, Shrinathji)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/nimbarka-vallabha-chaitanya.md) — (Pushtimarg tradition)
- **Vallabhācārya recognises and establishes Śrīnāthajī** — c. 1493 CE Vallabhācārya encounters the deity on Govardhana, names him Śrī Nāthajī, builds the first shrine and institutes formal sevā _(actors: Vallabhācārya, the deity, the early 84 Vaiṣṇavas)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/nathdwara-shrinathji.md) — (Puṣṭimārga vārtā; Caurāsī Baiṭhak tradition)
- **Vināyaka (Gaṇeśa) devotion and the Vināyagar Agaval** — Avvaiyār composed the Vināyagar Agaval, an akaval-metre adoration of Vināyaka fusing bhakti with kuṇḍalinī-yoga imagery, central to Tamil Gaṇeśa worship _(actors: Avvaiyār; Vināyaka (Gaṇeśa))_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/avvaiyar.md) — (Vināyagar Agaval; Auvaiyar — Wikipedia)
- **Worshipped as a goddess by her husband** — Brought to her husband, Paramatattaṉ and his new family fall at Puṉitavati's feet, confirming he regards her as divine, not as wife _(actors: Puṉitavati, Paramatattaṉ)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/karaikkal-ammaiyar.md) — (Periya Purāṇam (Cēkkiḻār))
- **Śaṅkara composes the Dakṣiṇāmūrti Stotra** — Ādi Śaṅkara hymns Dakṣiṇāmūrti as nirguṇa Brahman, with the mirror/world-as-reflection verse, making him the iṣṭa-deva of Advaita _(actors: Ādi Śaṅkara)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/dakshinamurti.md) — (Dakṣiṇāmūrti Stotra)
- **Ambaji temple and the major centres** — Ambaji (aniconic Sri Yantra shrine of Amba Mata) is the spiritual heart while Ahmedabad and Vadodara host stadium-scale garba/dandiya grounds _(actors: Amba/Ambaji/Mataji, Gujarati communities)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/garba-and-dandiya-raas.md) — (Ambaji/Mataji temple tradition)
- **Apotheosis and the seat at Becharājī** — Bahucharā is recognized as an āvataraṇa of the great Devī and her sthāna at Becharājī in Mehsāṇā becomes her great pilgrimage temple _(actors: Bahucharā Mātā, devotees, the great Devī / Ādi-Śakti)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/bahuchara-mata.md) — (Becharājī sthala-mahatmya / Cāraṇ tradition)
- **Appar's healing and reconversion to Śaivism** — After a Jain phase as Dharmasena, Appar is struck by incurable colic, returns to his sister Tilakavatiyār who applies sacred ash, is healed, and embraces Śaivism for life _(actors: Appar, Tilakavatiyār)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/the-nalvar-tamil-shaiva-saints.md) — (Wikipedia, Appar)
- **Appar Survives the Shaiva Ordeals** — Appar, converted to Jainism then back to Shaivism, survives ordeals of poison, an elephant and the sea, and establishes the sacred-ash (Tiruniru) devotion _(actors: Appar)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/alvars-and-nayanars.md) — (Appar — Wikipedia)
- **Ayyaṉār flanked by Pūrṇā and Puṣkalā** — In his married village form Ayyaṉār-Śāstā stands with twin consorts Pūrṇā (fullness) and Puṣkalā (abundance), the married iconography distinguishing him from the celibate Ayyappa _(actors: Ayyaṉār-Śāstā; Pūrṇā; Puṣkalā)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/ayyanar-sastha.md) — (Tamil grāma-devatā tradition; Śāstā temple iconography)
- **Bāṇagaṅgā arrow-river** — Thirsty during the flight, Vaiṣṇavī shoots an arrow into the earth releasing a stream and washes her hair, creating the Bāṇagaṅgā waypoint _(actors: Vaiṣṇavī)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/vaishno-devi-trikuta-katra.md) — (Vaiṣṇo Devī sthala tradition)
- **Bastar Dussehra instituted by the Bastar house** — The Bastar ruling house instituted a ~75-day Dussehra honouring the guardian-goddess Danteshwari and clan goddess Maoli as a tribal-royal compact, with no Ravana-burning _(actors: Danteshwari, Bastar rulers)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/regional-temple-festivals.md) — (Outlook; Deccan Chronicle; Village Square)
- **Bhedaghat 81-Yoginī Temple Built** — The Kalachuri king Yuvarāja II builds the largest Yoginī temple at Bhedaghat in the early 11th century with 81 cells following the Mūla-cakra scheme of 9 Mātṛkās each leading 8 yoginīs _(actors: Yuvarāja II)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/matrikas-and-64-yoginis.md) — (Wikipedia: Chausath Yogini Temple, Bhedaghat)
- **Building of the medieval Daityasūdana temple and rim shrines** — The visible Daityasūdana temple and rim-cluster of shrines (Kamalajā Devī, Rāma, Śiva, Hanumān) are built in the Yādava era and later _(actors: Yādava (Seuna) patrons, Deccan temple builders)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/lonar-sarovar-daityasudana.md) — (Architectural attribution; Lonar site tradition)
- **Caitanya and ISKCON propagation of the mahāmantra** — Caitanya Mahāprabhu propagates the mantra as yuga-dharma saṅkīrtana and ISKCON later carries it worldwide as the most globally recognized Hindu chant _(actors: Caitanya Mahāprabhu, Six Gosvāmīs, A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, ISKCON)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/upanishads/kalisantarana-upanishad.md) — (Gauḍīya tradition; ISKCON propagation history)
- **Caitanya Grafts the Gaudiya Line onto Madhva** — Caitanya Mahaprabhu (1486-1534) and his Acintya-bheda-abheda theology are affiliated to the Brahma-Madhva line through Madhavendra Puri and Ishvara Puri, forming the Brahma-Madhva-Gaudiya Sampradaya _(actors: Caitanya Mahaprabhu, Madhavendra Puri, Ishvara Puri)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/four-vaishnava-sampradayas.md) — (iskconcommunications.org)
- **Chand Sadagar refuses Manasā's worship** — The Śaiva merchant Chand Sadagar, protected by the Mahājñāna spell, refuses to worship the upstart goddess Manasā _(actors: Chand Sadagar, Manasā, Śiva)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/manasa.md) — (Manasāmaṅgal)
- **Composition of Bhogar 7000** — The seven-thousand-verse alchemical-yogic compendium Pōkar Ēḻāyiram on rasavāda, kāya-kalpa and Palani lore is ascribed to Bhogar _(actors: Bhogar)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/tamil-siddhars.md) — (Bhogar 7000; Wikipedia (Bhogar))
- **Composition of the Begampura hymn** — Ravidās articulates Begampura, a utopian city without sorrow, rank, fear or taxation, the great anti-caste social vision of bhakti _(actors: Ravidās)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/ravidas.md) — (Begampura hymn in Gurū Granth Sāhib; Gail Omvedt Seeking Begumpura)
- **Composition of the Eknāthī Bhāgavata** — Eknāth composes a Marathi ovī exposition of Skandha 11 (Uddhava-gītā) of the Bhāgavata Purāṇa, partly at Vārāṇasī where pandits first oppose then honour it _(actors: Eknāth, Vārāṇasī pandits)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/eknath.md) — (Eknāthī Bhāgavata; Wikipedia Eknath)
- **Composition of the Kelimala and Siddhanta-ke-pad** — Haridasa composes the Kelimala (Garland of Play, ~110 padas) and the Siddhanta-ke-pad (~18 doctrinal padas), the songbooks anchoring Haridasi dhrupad temple-music _(actors: Svami Haridasa)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/banke-bihari-vrindavan-nidhivan.md) — (Svami Haridasa, Kelimala; Siddhanta-ke-pad)
- **Composition of the Kīrtana-ghoṣā** — Śaṅkaradeva composes the Kīrtana-ghoṣā, his masterpiece of Assamese narrative Kṛṣṇa-verse for congregational singing and central scripture of Ekaśaraṇa _(actors: Śaṅkaradeva)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/sankaradeva-and-assamese-vaishnavism.md) — (Wikipedia, Sankardev)
- **Composition of the Śītalāṣṭaka (Skanda Purāṇa)** — The Eight Verses to Śītalā, framed as spoken by Śiva, become the core Sanskrit hymn fixing her form and promising protection from pox and fever _(actors: Śiva (speaker); Śītalā)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/shitala.md) — (Skanda Purāṇa — Śītalāṣṭaka)
- **Composition of the Tukārām Gāthā** — Tukārām composes the Tukārām Gāthā (c. 1632–1650), several thousand plain-Marathi abhaṅgas that become the culminating scripture of the Vārkarī canon _(actors: Tukārām)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/tukaram.md) — (Wikipedia, Tukaram)
- **Composition of the vākhs (Lallā-vākyāni)** — Lallā composes terse four-line vākhs teaching Trika Śiva-consciousness, kuṇḍalinī, the futility of ritual and the dissolving of Hindu-Muslim duality, founding the Kashmiri vernacular literary tradition _(actors: Lallā / Lalleśvarī)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/lalleshwari-lal-ded.md) — (Grierson & Barnett, Lallā-vākyāni)
- **Confrontation with Mirkhshah** — Jhulelal confronts Mirkhshah, manifests water and fire, and compels the tyrant to grant the Hindus freedom of worship _(actors: Jhulelal (Uderolal), Mirkhshah)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/jhulelal.md) — (Sindhi folk tradition)
- **Construction of the Konārk stone-chariot temple** — The Eastern Gaṅga king Narasiṃhadeva I builds the colossal chariot-temple of the Sun with twelve pairs of wheels and seven horses, c. 1241–1258 CE _(actors: Narasiṃhadeva I, Eastern Gaṅga dynasty)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/konark-sun-temple-arka-kshetra.md) — (Academic/historical; epigraphy)
- **Daily suprabhata-seva at Tirumala-Tirupati** — The hymn becomes the first ritual service at brahma-muhurta in the Tirumala shrine, ceremonially waking the standing Lord each dawn _(actors: Tirumala-Tirupati temple priests; pilgrims)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/venkatesha-suprabhatam.md) — (Tirumala temple liturgical tradition (suprabhata-seva))
- **Death in the wall-collapse during corvee labour** — Conscripted into corvee labour repairing a wall (told at Mangalvedhe), Chokhamela is killed when the wall collapses on the Mahar workers _(actors: Chokhamela; Mahar labourers)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/chokhamela.md) — (Wikipedia (Chokhamela); Mahipati, Bhaktavijaya)
- **Death of Kabir at Maghar and the body of flowers** — Kabir deliberately dies at the inauspicious town of Maghar rather than Kashi; his quarreling Hindu and Muslim disciples lift the shroud to find the body vanished and only flowers, which they divide for cremation and burial _(actors: Kabir, Hindu and Muslim disciples)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/kabir.md) — (Kabir hagiography (folk))
- **Death of Vallabha at Varanasi** — Vallabha attains tirobhava at Varanasi in 1531, traditionally by entering the Ganga at Hanuman-ghat and vanishing in a flame _(actors: Vallabhacharya)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/nimbarka-vallabha-chaitanya.md) — (Pushtimarg tradition)
- **Departure and settling at Parassinikkadavu** — Muthappan leaves the household and, by his arrow or wandering (and a sacred dog motif), comes to settle at Parassinikkadavu on the Valapattanam river where his madappura stands _(actors: Muthappan)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/muthappan.md) — (Parassinikkadavu temple lore)
- **Devāpi as purohita invokes Bṛhaspati and ends the drought** — Serving as purohita for Śaṃtanu, the seer Devāpi Ārṣṭiṣeṇa propitiates Bṛhaspati and obtains rain, ending the twelve-year drought _(actors: Devāpi Ārṣṭiṣeṇa, Śaṃtanu, Bṛhaspati)_ → [details](../03-lineages-vamsha/devapi.md) — (Ṛgveda X.98; Nirukta II.10)
- **Doddācārya installs Yoga Anjaneya on the Chinna-malai** — The Śrī Vaiṣṇava ācārya Doddācārya consecrates/propitiates the meditative Yoga Anjaneya (Hanumān) on the small hill to guard the cooled Narasiṃha and the kṣetra _(actors: Doddācārya (Doddayācārya), Yoga Anjaneya/Hanumān)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/sholingur-tirukkadigai-yoga-narasimha.md) — (Śrī Vaiṣṇava sampradāya tradition; sthala tradition)
- **Dukhe's cry and Bonbibi's rescue** — As the tiger closes in, Dukhe cries out to Bonbibi, who hears him and sends Shah Jangali to defeat Dakshin Ray _(actors: Dukhe, Bonbibi, Shah Jangali, Dakshin Ray)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/bonbibi.md) — (Bonbibi Jahuranama)
- **Establishment of daily and weekly recitation tradition** — The Chalisa enters living North-Indian practice as a daily, Tuesday/Saturday, and protective apotropaic recitation across the Hindu diaspora _(actors: North-Indian Hindu devotees)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/hanuman-chalisa.md) — (Living recitation practice (folk/regional))
- **Execution and apotheosis of Maturai-Vīraṉ** — Condemned over the affair, Maturai-Vīraṉ is mutilated and executed; Bommi and Vellaiyammāḷ die with him, and the wronged hero is deified into a guardian-god whose worship the remorseful king institutes _(actors: Maturai-Vīraṉ, Bommi, Vellaiyammāḷ, the king of Madurai, the goddess)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/karuppasamy-madurai-veeran-and-tamil-kaval-deities.md) — (Madurai Veeran ballad (villuppāṭṭu))
- **Festival and vrata floor-art calendar** — Elaborate designs mark Diwali, Mārgaḻi/Mārgaśīrṣa kōlam, Pongal/Saṅkrānti, Onam pookkalam, and Lakṣmī-pūjā/brata vows _(actors: Households; festival celebrants)_ → [details](../09-artifacts-symbols-and-arts/rangoli-kolam-floor-art.md) — (Wikipedia, Rangoli/Kolam/Alpana/Mandana; festival custom [regional][folk])
- **First National Ayurveda Day** — The Government of India designated Dhanteras as National Ayurveda Day, first observed 28 October 2016 _(actors: Government of India)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/dhanvantari.md) — (Ministry of AYUSH)
- **Founding of the Baranagore Math** — After Ramakrishna's death in 1886 his monastic disciples form the first Math at Baranagore, the seed of the Ramakrishna order _(actors: Ramakrishna's monastic disciples)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/modern-teachers-and-reform.md) — (Ramakrishna Mission history)
- **Founding of the Jānakī Mandir and the modern Sītā-cult** — A Sītā image found at Janakpur in 1657 CE becomes the founding relic of the cult, enshrined in the Nau Lakhā Jānakī Mandir built 1910–11 CE, anchoring living Maithilī Janakī-bhakti _(actors: Śūrkiśordās (finder), Queen Vrishabhanu Kunwari of Tikamgarh, Rāmānandī order)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/janakpur-mithila-sita-janmabhumi.md) — (Temple history; Wikipedia (Janaki Mandir); regional Maithilī tradition)
- **Gali Gopuram and hill-foot Lakṣmī-Narasiṃha temple built** — The lower Lakṣmī-Narasiṃha temple and its towering open 'wind tower' (Gali Gopuram) are built and endowed under Vijayanagara-era and later patronage _(actors: Vijayanagara rulers, Rāja of Challapalli, devotees)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/mangalagiri-panakala-narasimha.md) — (Local temple history; Wikipedia (Mangalagiri))
- **Gorakṣanāth systematizes haṭha-yoga** — Chief organizer of the order codifies haṭha-yoga, reputed author of the Gorakṣa-śataka, dating disputed across 9th–13th c _(actors: Gorakṣanāth)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/nath-sampradaya-and-the-siddhas.md) — (Wikipedia (Gorakhnath); Gorakṣa-śataka)
- **Gorakhnāth's penance and the Nāth seat at Girnar** — Guru Gorakhnāth and the Kānphaṭa Nāth yogīs establish a primal seat on Girnar's peaks, co-opting Dattātreya into the lineage _(actors: Gorakhnāth (Gorakṣanātha), Kānphaṭa Nāth yogīs, Dattātreya)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/girnar-raivataka-datta-tirtha.md) — (Nāth hagiography; Wikipedia (Gorakhnath); Mallinson)
- **Jayadeva's compassion reinterpretation** — The Gita Govinda's Dashavatara-stotra recasts Buddha as the compassionate avatara who censures Vedic ritual and ends animal sacrifice out of mercy _(actors: Jayadeva, Keshava-as-Buddha)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/buddha-avatara.md) — (Jayadeva, Gita Govinda, Dashavatara-stotra (12th c.))
- **Kanaka Dāsa, anti-caste poet-saint** — Kuruba warrior-poet-saint (c. 1509–1609), author of Mohanataraṅgiṇī and Haribhaktisāra, the Uḍupi Kanakana Kiṇḍi legend; signature 'Kāginele Ādikeśava' _(actors: Kanaka Dāsa (Ṯhimmappa Nāyaka))_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/haridasa-and-carnatic-bhakti.md) — (Haridāsa-sāhitya; Wikipedia, Kanaka Dasa)
- **Kanakadāsa barred and the deity turns west** — The low-caste saint Kanakadāsa, refused entry, sings from outside until Kṛṣṇa turns west and the back wall splits open so he can have darśana _(actors: Kanakadāsa, Kṛṣṇa)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/udupi-krishna-matha.md) — (Kanakadāsa / Haridāsa tradition)
- **Lakṣmīdhara writes the Saubhāgyavardhinī commentary** — Lakṣmīdhara composes the influential Saubhāgyavardhinī commentary, reading the hymn through the Samaya (internal, right-hand) school against the Kaula reading and aligning it with orthodox Śaṅkara Śrīvidyā _(actors: Lakṣmīdhara (Lakṣmīdharācārya))_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/soundarya-lahari.md) — (Lakṣmīdhara, Saubhāgyavardhinī commentary on the Saundarya-laharī)
- **Lausen's wars and the villain Mahāmada** — Lausen, aided by the Ḍom hero Kālu, survives the plots of the minister Mahāmada and campaigns at Kāmrūp/Hākanda, establishing Dharma-pūjā _(actors: Lausen, Mahāmada (Mahudyā), King Gauṛeśvar, Kālu Ḍom)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/dharma-thakur.md) — (Dharma-maṅgal (Lausen cycle))
- **Madhvācārya, third descent of Vāyu** — In Dvaita doctrine Vāyu descends a third time as Madhvācārya in the Kali Yuga to restore true dualist Vaiṣṇava Vedānta, completing the threefold avatāra after Hanumān and Bhīma _(actors: Madhvācārya)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/vayu.md) — (Madhva-vijaya of Nārāyaṇa Paṇḍitācārya; Mādhva exegesis of Ṛgveda 1.141)
- **Mahavira, 24th Tirthankara, founds/reforms Jainism** — Vardhamana Mahavira, the 24th and last Tirthankara of the cycle, establishes the historical Jain order (c. 599-527 BCE by tradition), contemporary of the Buddha _(actors: Mahavira (Vardhamana))_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/nastika-darshanas-and-the-astika-nastika-frame.md) — (Kalpa-sutra; Bhagavati Sutra)
- **Maṇimahesh Kailāsh and the unclimbed jewel-summit** — The ~5,653 m Maṇimaheśvara peak above Maṇimahesh Lake in Chamba, never summited, bearing the 'jewel on Śiva's forehead', revered as Śiva's western-Himalayan abode _(actors: Śiva as Maṇimaheśvara and Pārvatī as Maṇimaheśvarī)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/panch-kailash.md) — (Wikipedia (Manimahesh Lake); Markaṇḍeya/Chamba Śaiva tradition; Skanda Purāṇa Himalayan khaṇḍa)
- **Maruti (Hanumān) temple campaign** — Installed Hanumān images and founded maṭhs (the eleven Akrā Mārutī and a wider network) as gymnasium-shrines across Maharashtra _(actors: Rāmdās; Rāmdāsī disciples)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/samarth-ramdas.md) — (Wikipedia; Hindupedia)
- **Mass cross-dressing: men take the goddess's female veṣam** — Men of Tirupati dress as women to take Gangamma's veṣam as a vow, ritually becoming the goddess and inverting everyday gender _(actors: Men of Tirupati, Gangamma)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/gangamma.md) — (Handelman/Krishnamurthy/Dawson and Flueckiger ethnography [scholarly])
- **Modern exposition and recitation tradition** — Swāmī Cinmayānanda's 20th-c. commentary and M. S. Subbulakshmi's musical setting establish Bhaja Govindam as a staple of daily and congregational devotion _(actors: Swāmī Cinmayānanda, M. S. Subbulakshmi)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/bhaja-govindam.md) — (Cinmayānanda, Bhaja Govindam (Chinmaya Mission))
- **Modern revival through Cinmayānanda and musical settings** — In the 20th century Swāmī Cinmayānanda's discourse-commentary and M. S. Subbulakshmi's sung rendition popularize Bhaja Govindam as a centerpiece of modern Vedānta and one of the most-quoted Advaita devotional poems _(actors: Swāmī Cinmayānanda (Chinmaya Mission), M. S. Subbulakshmi)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/bhaja-govindam.md) — (Swāmī Cinmayānanda, Bhaja Govindam (Central Chinmaya Mission Trust); recorded musical tradition)
- **Muktābāī as guru to Cāṅgadeva** — The aged occult-yogī Cāṅgadeva, humbled in the wall-riding contest and the blank 65-line letter, becomes disciple with the child Muktābāī as his guru; addressee of the Cāṅgadeva Pāsaṣṭī _(actors: Muktābāī, Cāṅgadeva, Jñāneśvar)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/nivritti-sopan-muktabai.md) — (Wikipedia (Muktabai, Changdev))
- **Murugan and the hot-fruit/cold-fruit riddle** — Murugan, disguised as a boy, humbles the proud poet Avvaiyār with the sutta-paḻam/sudāta-paḻam ('hot fruit or cold fruit') riddle, dramatizing wisdom bowing to grace _(actors: Avvaiyār; Murugan (Subrahmaṇya))_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/avvaiyar.md) — (Murugan bhakti lore; Auvaiyar — Wikipedia)
- **Nammalvar's ascent to Vaikuntha** — After a brief life of about 35 years Nammalvar attains paramapada-prapti, ascending to Vaikuntha, commemorated annually as his apotheosis _(actors: Nammalvar)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/nammalvar.md) — (Nammalvar hagiography (traditional))
- **Narahari Tīrtha reorganizes Śrīkūrmam as a Vaiṣṇava centre** — The Madhva ascetic Narahari Tīrtha, regent of the Kaliṅga Gaṅga court, is tied by inscriptions to the temple's conversion from Śaiva to Vaiṣṇava worship in the late 13th c _(actors: Narahari Tīrtha (Madhva/Dvaita ascetic), Eastern Ganga court)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/avatara-kshetras-srikurmam-srimushnam.md) — (Epigraphia Indica; South Indian Inscriptions; Dvaita tradition)
- **Navaratri recitation tradition** — The hymn enters living practice as a Navaratri/Durga Puja staple and a widely sung devotional piece _(actors: Devotees, musicians, temple liturgy)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/mahishasura-mardini-stotra.md) — (devotional tradition)
- **Nāmdev spreads Viṭṭhal-bhakti into the Punjab** — The tailor-saint Nāmdev (c.1270–1350 CE) propagates the Pandharpur cult through abhaṅgas, 61 of which enter the Sikh Guru Granth Sāhib _(actors: Nāmdev/Nāmadeva)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/vithoba-vitthal.md) — (Nāmdev abhaṅgas; Guru Granth Sāhib)
- **Panini's Ashtadhyayi** — Panini composes his Sanskrit grammar c. 5th-4th century BCE _(actors: Panini)_ → [details](../01-manvantaras/manvantara-07-vaivasvata/mahayuga-28-current/04-kali-yuga/recorded-history.md) — (Ashtadhyayi)
- **Panini's Ashtadhyayi Composed** — Panini composes the Ashtadhyayi, the foundational Sanskrit grammar, c. 5th-4th century BCE _(actors: Panini)_ → [details](../01-manvantaras/manvantara-07-vaivasvata/mahayuga-28-current/04-kali-yuga/recorded-history.md) — (Ashtadhyayi)
- **Prayagwal Priests Rebrand the Magh Mela as Kumbh** — In the mid-1800s the Prayagwal priests rebrand Prayag's annual Magh Mela as 'Kumbh' for socio-economic advantage, the name previously reserved for Haridwar _(actors: Prayagwal priests)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/kumbh-mela-and-pilgrimage-cycles.md) — (Maclean; Wikipedia, Prayag Kumbh Mela)
- **Prayer to shed beauty and become a pēy** — Released from marriage, Puṉitavati prays to Śiva to strip her beauty and grant her the emaciated skeletal pēy form of a ghoul-attendant _(actors: Puṉitavati, Śiva)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/karaikkal-ammaiyar.md) — (Periya Purāṇam (Cēkkiḻār))
- **'Prophecy' of Mahāmada (Muhammad)** — A polemical late insertion casts Muhammad as a piśāca/demon-incarnation whose followers are called musalmān _(actors: Mahāmada)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/bhavishya-purana.md) — (Bhaviṣya Purāṇa (Pratisarga Parva) [interpolation])
- **Punnainallur anthill foundation** — The Thanjavur-area shrine said to arise when the goddess directed a devotee to an anthill image, a Sanskritized Māri seat _(actors: Māriyammaṉ; Thanjavur-era devotee/ruler)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/mariamman.md) — (Punnainallur sthala-purāṇa)
- **Queen Māyā Conceives the Buddha at Anavatapta** — In Buddhist legend Queen Māyā conceives the Buddha at Mānasarovara (Anavatapta/Anotatta), the lake at the centre of the world _(actors: Queen Māyā, the Buddha)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/manasarovar-kailash-yatra.md) — (Buddhist tradition)
- **Ramaṇa Mahaṛṣi prizes and recites the Ribhu-Gītā** — Bhagavān Śrī Ramaṇa Mahaṛṣi (1879–1950) singles out the Ribhu-Gītā as a supreme expression of Self-knowledge and has portions chanted daily at Sri Ramaṇāśramam, Tiruvaṇṇāmalai _(actors: Ramaṇa Mahaṛṣi, Sri Ramaṇāśramam devotees)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ribhu-gita.md) — (Talks with Sri Ramana Maharshi; Ramaṇāśramam records)
- **Recovery and re-consecration of the buried mūla-mūrti** — Rāmānuja recovers the lost Celuvanārāyaṇa image from an anthill/thicket and re-founds regular worship at the ruined hill-temple _(actors: Rāmānuja, Celuvanārāyaṇa/Tirunārāyaṇa)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/melkote-tirunarayanapuram.md) — (Śrī Vaiṣṇava hagiography)
- **Śārṅgadeva composes the Saṅgīta-ratnākara** — At the Yādava court of Devagiri the seven-chapter 'Ocean of Music' synthesizes svara, rāga, tāla, gamaka (nāda-brahman doctrine) authoritatively for both later Hindustani and Carnatic traditions, the last text common to both _(actors: Śārṅgadeva)_ → [details](../09-artifacts-symbols-and-arts/sangita-classical-music-svara-raga-tala.md) — (Saṅgīta-ratnākara of Śārṅgadeva)
- **Rājarāja I recovers and Nampi canonizes the Tirumurai** — The lost early Śaiva hymns are rediscovered by Chola king Rājarāja I in a sealed Chidambaram chamber and arranged into the canon by Nampi Āṇṭār Nampi _(actors: Rājarāja I, Nampi Āṇṭār Nampi)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/tamil-canon-sangam-and-bhakti.md) — (Tirumurai tradition)
- **Rāmprasād Sen opens Śākta-bhakti** — Rāmprasād Sen (c. 1718–1775), Bengal's founding Śākta poet-saint, first addresses Kālī with tender intimacy as Mother in his Rāmprasādī Kālī-songs, reframing the fierce Goddess _(actors: Rāmprasād Sen)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/shakta-and-tantra-lineages.md) — (Wikipedia, Ramprasad Sen)
- **Saundatti Yellammā cult and pan-Deccan identification** — The Yellammā cult centers on the Saundatti hill shrine (Belagavi, Karnataka) with mass pilgrim jātre festivals; Reṇukā is identified with Yellammā, Ekvīrā, Māriyammaṉ, Padmākṣī and Aai as one pan-Deccan mother, and is the goddess of the contested jogtī/devadāsī dedication _(actors: Yellammā (Reṇukā), jogtī/jogamma devotees)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/devi-forms-and-navadurga.md) — (Saundatti sthala tradition; grāmadevatā and ethnographic studies)
- **Service as liturgical singer of Śrīnāthjī at Govardhan** — Sūr serves as lead kīrtaniyā before Śrīnāthjī on Govardhan hill, his padas becoming the standing liturgical music of the Puṣṭimārga _(actors: Sūrdās; Śrīnāthjī (child-Kṛṣṇa))_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/surdas.md) — (Wikipedia Surdas; Grokipedia Shrinathji; Vraj Dwar)
- **Shankara Enters King Amaruka's Corpse to Learn Kama** — When Ubhaya Bharati questions the celibate Shankara on the art of love, he enters the corpse of the dead King Amaruka by parakaya-pravesha to learn the subject and win the debate _(actors: Adi Shankara, Ubhaya Bharati, King Amaruka)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/adi-shankara.md) — (Madhaviya Shankara-Digvijaya, Part 3)
- **Siddhpur established as Matr-gaya, the mother-shraddha tirtha** — Siddhpur's Bindu-sarovara becomes the foremost site for matr-shraddha (rites to the mother/maternal line), paired with Gaya for the father, with the medieval town developed under the Solankis _(actors: pilgrims, Siddharaja Jayasimha (tradition))_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/bindu-sarovar-siddhpur.md) — (Skanda Purana; Siddhpur sthala-purana)
- **Standardization of the Burmese Yama Zatdaw** — Myanmar's Rama epic is standardized under Konbaung-dynasty patronage in the 18th century, shaped by court contact with Thai Ayutthaya after 1767 _(actors: Konbaung dynasty patrons)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ramayana/recensions/southeast-asian-ramayanas.md) — (Yama Zatdaw)
- **Surrender and cry to Viṭṭhal as sole refuge** — Pressed by the king's men, she comes before the image of Viṭṭhal and cries that the god alone is her refuge and protector _(actors: Kānhopātrā, Viṭṭhal, sultan's soldiers)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/kanhopatra.md) — (Kānhopātrā's abhaṅgas; Vārkarī hagiography)
- **Teppotsavam / Float Festival (genre)** — The South-Indian Āgama tank-procession in which the utsava-mūrti is rowed on an illuminated raft around the temple tank over several evenings, spanning both Vaiṣṇava and Śaiva temples _(actors: processional deities (utsava-mūrti), temple priests, devotees)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/swing-and-float-temple-festivals.md) — (Vaikhānasa/Pāñcarātra Āgama utsava-repertoire; Wikipedia (Float Festival, Teppakulam))
- **Textual systematization of the tripartite classification in the śilpa/vāstu and Purāṇa corpus** — Treatises and Purāṇa building-chapters codify the threefold Nāgara/Drāviḍa/Vesara scheme as a divinely-ordained typology, with the Bṛhat Saṃhitā giving an early pre-classification witness and the Agni and Matsya Purāṇas preserving prāsāda-types and the mythic architect-lineage _(actors: Varāhamihira; Bhuvanadeva; Purāṇic compilers; Viśvakarmā and the eighteen vāstu-teachers (mythic))_ → [details](../09-artifacts-symbols-and-arts/temple-architecture-nagara-dravida-vesara.md) — (Bṛhat Saṃhitā; Mānasāra; Mayamata; Aparājitapṛcchā; Sthāpatyaveda; Agni Purāṇa; Matsya Purāṇa)
- **The buffalo reciting the Veda** — Challenged at Paithan, the boy Jñāneśvar makes a water-buffalo recite the Ṛgveda, refuting Brahmin monopoly over scripture _(actors: Jñāneśvar, the buffalo Cakhdyā, Paithan Brāhmaṇas)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/jnaneshwar.md) — (Wikipedia (Dnyaneshwar); Vārkarī hagiography [folk])
- **The child trampled in the clay-pit during kīrtana** — Absorbed in kīrtana while treading clay, Gorā fails to notice his infant child in the pit and tramples it, the measure of his total God-absorption _(actors: Gorā Kumbhār, his child)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/gora-kumbhar.md) — (Mahīpati, Bhaktalīlāmṛta/Bhaktavijaya; Vārkarī hagiography)
- **The cup of poison turned to nectar** — The Rana sends Mira a cup of poison disguised as prasad; she drinks it as Krishna's gift and is unharmed, the poison turning to nectar _(actors: Mirabai, Rana Vikramaditya, Krishna)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/mirabai.md) — (Mirabai hagiography (folk))
- **The Haralayya-Madhuvarasa inter-caste marriage crisis** — A marriage between the son of the untouchable Sharana Haralayya and the daughter of the Brahmin Sharana Madhuvarasa enrages the orthodox; Bijjala orders the fathers blinded and executed, triggering Sharana unrest _(actors: Haralayya, Madhuvarasa, Bijjala II)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/lingayat-vachana-poets.md) — (Basava hagiography)
- **The mother's repudiation** — Grief-stricken Bāchhal rejects the kinslayer Gugga, bars him from the city, and wishes the earth would swallow him _(actors: Bāchhal, Gugga)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/gugga-gogaji.md) — (Gugga-gāthā)
- **The rain of golden āmalakas (kanaka-dhārā)** — Pleased by the hymn, Lakṣmī showers golden gooseberry fruit — a stream of gold — into the woman's home, ending her poverty _(actors: Lakṣmī, the poor Brahmin woman)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/kanakadhara-stotram.md) — (Kanakadhārā-stotra frame; Śaṅkara-vijaya hagiographies)
- **The revolving-temple miracle** — Barred as a low-caste man, Nāmdev sings from behind a shrine (often Auṇḍhā Nāgnāth) and the temple revolves to face him, dramatizing bhakti overriding caste _(actors: Nāmdev, temple priests)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/namdev.md) — (Wikipedia Namdev; Vārkarī hagiography [folk])
- **The Seven Sisters pattern** — Village mothers cluster as seven sister-goddesses (saptakannimār / yedugurakkalu), later equated with the Purāṇic Sapta-Mātṛkā _(actors: Seven village sister-goddesses; Sapta-Mātṛkā (Brāhmī, Vaiṣṇavī, Cāmuṇḍā, etc.))_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/gramadevatas-village-deities.md) — (Whitehead; Sapta-Mātṛkā village-mother studies; Devī-Māhātmya overlay)
- **The Tenkalai-Vadakalai split** — The Shri Sampradaya fractures by the late 14th c. into the Vadakalai (Vedanta Deshika, monkey-analogy effortful grace) and Tenkalai (Manavala Mamunigal, cat-analogy causeless grace) sub-sects over how prapatti and grace operate _(actors: Vedanta Deshika, Manavala Mamunigal)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/ramanuja-and-madhva.md) — (Rahasya-traya-sara; srivaishnava polemics)
- **The tilaka/sindūr painted-mark complex** — Forehead tilaka/bindī, parting-sindūr and patra-lekhā tracery form the kindred painted marks of Viśeṣaka-cchedya, linked to Pārvatī, Sītā and Sāvitrī _(actors: married women; Vaiṣṇava devotees; Pārvatī, Sītā, Sāvitrī)_ → [details](../09-artifacts-symbols-and-arts/mehndi-and-ritual-body-adornment.md) — (Kāmasūtra 1.3; bridal and sectarian custom)
- **The Twin Dream-Command to Bring Andal to Srirangam** — Ranganatha appears in dreams to both Periyalvar and the Srirangam priests, commanding that Andal be brought in bridal attire to be wed to him _(actors: Ranganatha, Periyalvar, Srirangam priests)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/andal.md) — (Andal — Grokipedia)
- **Tulsidas composes the Ramcharitmanas** — Tulsidas composes the Ramcharitmanas in seven kandas in 1574-77, begun at Ayodhya on Rama-Navami, a devotional Awadhi retelling of the Ramayana that becomes the basis of the Ramlila and the 'Bible of north India.' _(actors: Tulsidas)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/north-bhakti-sants.md) — (Ramcharitmanas)
- **Tēvāram recovery and Tirumurai codification** — Rājarāja I with Nambiyāṇḍār Nambi is said to recover the lost Tēvāram hymns from Chidambaram and codify them into the Tirumurai _(actors: Rājarāja Chola I; Nambiyāṇḍār Nambi)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/brihadeeswarar-thanjavur.md) — (Tamil Śaiva tradition; Tēvāram)
- **Utpaladeva systematizes Pratyabhijñā** — Utpaladeva (c. 925–975) systematizes the recognition philosophy in the Īśvara-pratyabhijñā-kārikā _(actors: Utpaladeva)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/shaiva-schools.md) — (Wikipedia, Kashmir Shaivism)
- **Utsava-mūrti vs mūla-bera distinction** — Portable festival bronze (utsava/kautuka-bera) is cast in metal to leave the sanctum and process, distinct from the fixed stone mūla/dhruva icon, by Vaikhānasa measure _(actors: temple priests; Vaikhānasa Āgama tradition)_ → [details](../09-artifacts-symbols-and-arts/pancha-loha-bronze-casting.md) — (Vaikhānasa Āgama; Dehejia)
- **Vaikuntha Ekadashi / Adhyayana Utsava (Paramapada Vasal opening)** — On Mokshada/Vaikuntha Ekadashi in Margazhi, the Paramapada Vasal (gate of Vaikuntha) is opened at Srirangam, Tirupati and other Divya Desams within the 20-day Adhyayana Utsava — Pagal Pathu (ten days) and Ra Pathu (ten nights) of reciting the Nalayira Divya Prabandham, climaxing in the Iyarpa Sattrumurai and Nammalvar Moksham, the temple enactment of the heaven's-gate liberation myth _(actors: Ranganatha/Venkateshwara (Vishnu), Nammalvar, the Alvars, Sri Vaishnava servitors)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/regional-temple-festivals.md) — (Wikipedia (Vaikuntha Ekadashi, Adhyayana Utsavam); Srirangam Temple [traditional/scholarly])
- **Varāhamihira** — Sixth-century synthesiser whose Pañcasiddhāntikā summarises the five Siddhāntas and whose Bṛhat Saṃhitā and Bṛhat Jātaka anchor the saṃhitā and horā branches _(actors: Varāhamihira)_ → [details](../00-time-and-cosmology/siddhantic-astronomy-and-astronomers.md) — (Pañcasiddhāntikā; Bṛhat Saṃhitā; Bṛhat Jātaka)
- **Viṭṭhalanātha systematises the sevā** — Gusāīṃjī (1515–1585) codifies the eightfold aṣṭa-yāma darśana, the Aṣṭachāp kīrtana and divides the deities among his seven sons (Sāt Gharānā) _(actors: Viṭṭhalanātha (Gusāīṃjī), his seven sons, the Aṣṭachāp poets)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/nathdwara-shrinathji.md) — (Puṣṭimārga sevā-paddhati; hagiography)
- **Vijayanagara–Nāyaka building of the standing temple** — The monumental Bhū-Varāha-svāmī temple with its carved ratha-maṇḍapa is founded and endowed under Vijayanagara and Tanjore/Senji Nāyaka patronage in the 16th c. CE _(actors: Vijayanagara rulers, Acyutarāya, Kṛṣṇappa Nāyaka)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/avatara-kshetras-srikurmam-srimushnam.md) — (South Indian Inscriptions; ASI records; Wikipedia, Bhuvarahaswamy Temple Srimushnam)
- **Vision and dream-dīkṣā from Tukārām** — She receives a vision of the Śūdra sant Tukārām, who gives the mantra and accepts her as disciple, mirroring his own dream-initiation _(actors: Bahiṇābāī; Tukārām)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/bahinabai.md) — (Wikipedia 'Bahina Bai'; Feldhaus)
- **Witness to the founding Vārkarī circle and Jñāneśvar's samādhi** — Hagiographic strands place Janī within the circle of Nāmdev and Jñāneśvar, connecting her to foundational events such as Jñāneśvar's sañjīvana samādhi at Āḷandī (1296) _(actors: Janābāī, Nāmdev, Jñāneśvar)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/janabai.md) — (Vārkarī hagiography)
- **Worship in the guardian-cluster** — Sudalaimāḍaṉ is worshipped alongside Karuppasāmi, Muṉiyāṇṭi and others as a kāval-deyvam attending a presiding goddess such as Māriyammaṉ _(actors: Sudalaimāḍaṉ, Karuppasāmi, Muṉiyāṇṭi, Māriyammaṉ)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/sudalaimadan.md) — (Tamil kāval-deyvam tradition)
- **Abhinavagupta unifies the Kashmiri streams** — Abhinavagupta (c. 950–1016) reconciles the Āgama, Spanda and Pratyabhijñā streams into a unified non-dualism in the Tantrāloka and other works _(actors: Abhinavagupta)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/shaiva-schools.md) — (Wikipedia, Kashmir Shaivism)
- **Akbar/Tānsen legend** — Popular but disputed legend has Emperor Akbar or Tānsen seek out the blind singer; treated as later hagiography, not documented _(actors: Sūrdās; Akbar; Tānsen)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/surdas.md) — (Wikipedia Surdas; cf. Mīrā tradition)
- **Andal Merges into Ranganatha (Goda-Kalyanam)** — Borne to Srirangam as a bride, Andal ascends to Ranganatha's couch and merges bodily into the deity in a burst of light, a paradigm of jiva-Brahman union _(actors: Andal, Ranganatha)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/andal.md) — (Andal — Wikipedia; JKYog)
- **Appar survives the ordeals under Mahendravarman I** — Appar survives a lime kiln, poison, a maddened elephant and being cast into the sea bound to a stone, convincing the Pallava king Mahendravarman I to reconvert to Śaivism _(actors: Appar, Mahendravarman I)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/the-nalvar-tamil-shaiva-saints.md) — (Wikipedia, Appar)
- **Assassination of Bijjala and the Kalyana dispersal** — The marriage-crisis repression provokes Bijjala's assassination c.1167 and the violent scattering of the Kalyana Sharanas, who flee to Ulavi carrying the vachana manuscripts that preserve the movement _(actors: Sharanas, Bijjala II, Channabasava)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/lingayat-vachana-poets.md) — (Kalachuri chronology)
- **Aurangzeb's Braj temple destruction** — Aurangzeb's 1670 demolition of the Mathurā Keśavadeva temple endangers the Govardhana deity, prompting its removal _(actors: Aurangzeb, the Tilakāyat priesthood)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/nathdwara-shrinathji.md) — (Nathdwārā temple history; Mughal chronicles)
- **Bhāskara I** — Earliest commentator on Āryabhaṭa, author of Mahābhāskarīya and a rational sine approximation, and an early user of positional decimal notation with zero _(actors: Bhāskara I)_ → [details](../00-time-and-cosmology/siddhantic-astronomy-and-astronomers.md) — (Mahābhāskarīya; Laghubhāskarīya; Āryabhaṭīya-bhāṣya)
- **Blood-sacrifice and possession at the jātara** — Fierce-goddess buffalo-style blood offerings and possession (āveśam) channel Gangamma's presence, contrasting the vegetarian Agamic worship of Veṅkaṭeśvara _(actors: Gangamma, devotees, oracles and veṣam-wearers)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/gangamma.md) — (Grāmadevatā and possession ethnography [folk/scholarly])
- **Bāmākhepā perfects Tantric sādhanā at Tārāpīṭha** — Bāmākhepā (1837–1911) perfects yoga and Tantric sādhanā in the Tārāpīṭha cremation ground, becomes its spiritual head and 'sweetens' the terrifying Tārā into an intimate Mother _(actors: Bāmākhepā, Kailāśpati Bābā)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/shakta-and-tantra-lineages.md) — (Wikipedia, Bamakhepa)
- **Cōḻa Naṭarāja bronze perfected** — The Cōḻa atelier perfects the Ānanda-Tāṇḍava dancing-Śiva bronze, the supreme cast icon of the tradition _(actors: Cōḻa-era śilpin ateliers)_ → [details](../09-artifacts-symbols-and-arts/pancha-loha-bronze-casting.md) — (Sivaramamurti; Dehejia)
- **Caurangī / Pūraṇ Bhagat restored and perfected** — The maimed prince of the Pūraṇ Bhagat legend is healed and perfected by Matsyendra/Gorakṣa, becoming the Nāth/Mahāsiddha Caurangī _(actors: Caurangīnāth (Pūraṇ Bhagat), Gorakṣa, Matsyendra)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/nath-sampradaya-and-the-siddhas.md) — (Nāth hagiography; Caturaśīti-siddha-pravṛtti)
- **Charaṇ Pādukā footprints** — Vaiṣṇavī pauses to look back at Bhairavnāth, leaving her footprints imprinted on the rock at Charaṇ Pādukā _(actors: Vaiṣṇavī, Bhairavnāth)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/vaishno-devi-trikuta-katra.md) — (Vaiṣṇo Devī sthala tradition)
- **Civavākkiyar's iconoclastic songs** — Civavākkiyar composes the Civavākkiyam, attacking caste, idolatry and ritual and insisting on the formless inner Śiva _(actors: Civavākkiyar)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/tamil-siddhars.md) — (Civavākkiyam; Wikipedia (Sivavakkiyar))
- **Codification of the Dharma-gājan, ghaṭ and ordeal rites** — The Dharma-pūjā/gājan festival with ghaṭ-sthāpana, vow-taking bhaktas, hook-swinging and fire-walking ordeals becomes the cult's central rite _(actors: Gājan bhaktas/sannyāsīs, Ḍom paṇḍit/deāsī, Dharma Ṭhākur)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/dharma-thakur.md) — (Bengali folk tradition; Dharma-pūjā-vidhāna)
- **Companionship and grief at Jñāneśvar's samādhi** — Nāmdev tours pilgrimage sites with Jñāneśvar and composes verses memorializing Jñāneśvar's sañjīvana samādhi at Āḷandī (1296) _(actors: Nāmdev, Jñāneśvar)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/namdev.md) — (Wikipedia Namdev, Dnyaneshwar [folk])
- **Compilation of the Bijak** — The eastern Banaras branch compiles the Bijak ('seed/map of hidden treasure') in the 17th century as the holy scripture of the Kabir Panth, organizing Kabir's verse into Ramaini, Shabad and Sakhi _(actors: Kabir Panth, Banaras branch)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/kabir.md) — (Bijak)
- **Composition of ~300+ abhaṅgas preserved in the Sakal Sant Gāthā** — Janābāī composes some 300 or more abhaṅgas—grinding-songs, intimate Viṭhobā verses and verses about the sants—preserved in the Vārkarī Abhaṅga-gāthā / Sakal Sant Gāthā _(actors: Janābāī)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/janabai.md) — (Marathi abhaṅgas; Sakal Sant Gāthā; Wikipedia (Janabai))
- **Composition of the Bhāvārtha Rāmāyaṇa** — Eknāth writes a devotional Marathi Rāmāyaṇa, leaving it incomplete at the Yuddha-kāṇḍa; his disciple Gāvbā completes it _(actors: Eknāth, disciple Gāvbā, Rāma)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/eknath.md) — (Bhāvārtha Rāmāyaṇa; Wikipedia Eknath)
- **Composition of the Jñāneśvarī** — On Nivṛttināth's command Jñāneśvar dictates the ~9,000-ovī Marathi commentary-paraphrase of the Bhagavad Gītā in 1290 _(actors: Jñāneśvar, Nivṛttināth (guru), Saccidānanda Bābā (scribe))_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/jnaneshwar.md) — (Jñāneśvarī (Bhāvārtha-dīpikā) 1290; Wikipedia (Dnyaneshwari))
- **Construction of the Lingarāja temple** — The mature Kaliṅga-architecture temple is built c. mid-11th c. CE under Somavaṃśī (Keśarī) patronage, later elaborated by the Eastern Gaṅgas _(actors: Somavaṃśī/Keśarī kings (Yayāti Keśarī tradition), Eastern Gaṅgas)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/lingaraja-bhubaneswar-ekamra-kshetra.md) — (Madala Pañji; epigraphy and architectural history)
- **Conversion and descent into the earth** — When the ground refuses to open for a Hindu, Gugga takes the kalmā/becomes a pīr so the earth receives him, and he and his blue mare are swallowed alive into pātāla _(actors: Gugga, his blue mare Nīlī)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/gugga-gogaji.md) — (Gugga-gāthā)
- **Conversion of Hoysaḷa king Biṭṭideva to Viṣṇuvardhana** — Rāmānuja converts the Hoysaḷa ruler Biṭṭideva from Jainism, who is restyled Viṣṇuvardhana and patronizes Melkote _(actors: Rāmānuja, Biṭṭideva/Viṣṇuvardhana)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/melkote-tirunarayanapuram.md) — (guruparamparā; Hoysaḷa inscriptions)
- **Cross-border Mithilā pilgrimage circuit** — Janakpur anchors a Nepal–Bihar Rāmāyaṇa circuit linking Dhanushadham, Jaleshwar, Sitamarhi and Punaura-dhām, walked at Vivāha Pañcamī, Rāma Navamī and Sītā Navamī, with the furrow birth-spot disputed between Janakpur and Sitamarhi _(actors: Maithilī pilgrims, Rāmānandī order)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/janakpur-mithila-sita-janmabhumi.md) — (Pilgrimage tradition; Wikipedia (Janakpur, Mithila region); regional Sitamarhi/Punaura-dhām counterclaim)
- **Death at the temple, choosing the god over the king** — At the feet of Viṭṭhal she gives up her life in the temple rather than be carried to the sultan's harem _(actors: Kānhopātrā, Viṭṭhal, Sultan of Bidar)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/kanhopatra.md) — (Vārkarī hagiography)
- **Ādi Śaṅkara institutes the South-Indian Bhaṭṭa priesthood** — Ādi Śaṅkara is held to have reformed worship at Paśupati and decreed that only Dravida (southern) Brahmins serve as mūl-bhaṭṭa _(actors: Ādi Śaṅkara, the Bhaṭṭa priests)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/pashupatinath-kathmandu-nepal-kshetra.md) — (temple tradition; regional history (disputed))
- **Eastern Ganga construction of the Śrīkūrmam temple** — The standing stone complex with its dual dhwaja-stambhas and pillared maṇḍapa is built in Kaliṅga style (13th–14th c.) with later Gajapati, Reddi and Vijayanagara additions _(actors: Eastern Ganga, Gajapati, Reddi and Vijayanagara patrons)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/avatara-kshetras-srikurmam-srimushnam.md) — (Wikipedia Srikurmam Temple; ASI; South Indian Inscriptions)
- **Eastern Ganga construction of the stone temple** — The standing Kaliṅga-style stone complex is built/rebuilt under Eastern Ganga king Narasiṃhadeva I (c. 1238–1264 CE) over an older Cōḷa-period core _(actors: Narasiṃhadeva I (Eastern Ganga dynasty))_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/simhachalam-varaha-narasimha.md) — (Wikipedia, Simhachalam Temple; ASI records)
- **Eknāth restores the Jñāneśvarī** — Eknāth (c.1533–1599 CE) of Paiṭhaṇ produces a critical text of the Jñāneśvarī and composes the Eknāthī Bhāgavata and Rukmiṇī-svayamvara _(actors: Eknāth)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/vithoba-vitthal.md) — (Eknāthī Bhāgavata; Eknāth abhaṅgas)
- **Establishment of the Madurai Veeran temple cult** — Maturai-Vīraṉ gains a firmly established temple following, his image flanked by Bommi and Vellaiyammāḷ and set as guardian at temple gateways across the Tamil south, worshipped alongside Karuppasāmi _(actors: Maturai-Vīraṉ, Bommi, Vellaiyammāḷ, Karuppasāmi)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/karuppasamy-madurai-veeran-and-tamil-kaval-deities.md) — (Madurai Veeran temple tradition)
- **First Organized Prayag Kumbh (1870)** — The recognizably modern, administered Kumbh at Allahabad is held in 1870 under British supervision _(actors: British administration)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/kumbh-mela-and-pilgrimage-cycles.md) — (Wikipedia)
- **Floor-art read as folk-yantra / maṇḍala geometry** — The dot-grid kōlam and cauk are interpreted as the household counterpart of priestly yantra/maṇḍala, sharing bindu-centred bounded symmetric grammar and motifs (svastika, padma, śatkoṇa) _(actors: Practitioners; folk/scholarly interpreters)_ → [details](../09-artifacts-symbols-and-arts/rangoli-kolam-floor-art.md) — (Tradition; cf. sacred-symbols-and-emblems.md §II [scholarly])
- **Foundation of the Banke Bihari temple (1864)** — The Haridasi Goswamis build the present Banke Bihari Mandir in Vrindavana in 1864 CE, moving the icon from Nidhivana; it becomes the most-visited temple of Braj _(actors: Haridasi Goswami custodians)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/banke-bihari-vrindavan-nidhivan.md) — (Banke Bihari Temple history [scholarly])
- **Founding the Rāmdāsī Sampradāya** — Established a non-Vārkarī order centred on Rāma and Hanumān, fusing practical Advaita Vedānta with civic activism and 'Mahārāṣṭra dharma'/swarājya _(actors: Rāmdās; Kalyāṇ Svāmī; Veṇābāī)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/samarth-ramdas.md) — (Wikipedia; Hindupedia)
- **Gautama Buddha founds Buddhism** — Siddhartha Gautama, a Magadhan shramana, teaches anatman and rejects Vedic authority, founding Buddhism (6th-5th c. BCE) _(actors: Siddhartha Gautama (the Buddha))_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/nastika-darshanas-and-the-astika-nastika-frame.md) — (Buddhist canon; Digha Nikaya)
- **Historical incorporation of Buddha into the avatar canon** — By c. 8th c. CE Buddha is added as a Vishnu-avatara across several Puranas as a Brahmanical theological response subordinating Buddhism _(actors: Vaishnava Puranic tradition, Buddha)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/buddha-avatara.md) — (Agni Purana 49, Garuda Purana 1.86, Padma Purana, Varaha Purana; Adivaraha inscription (7th c.))
- **Husband's opposition and the bhakti-vs-strīdharma conflict** — Her discipleship to a Śūdra guru brings caste scandal and her husband's rage and threats; she holds wifely duty and devotion in unresolved tension rather than renouncing the household _(actors: Bahiṇābāī; Gaṅgādhar Paṭhak; Tukārām)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/bahinabai.md) — (Tharu & Lalita; Feldhaus; Wikipedia 'Bahina Bai')
- **Islamization of the Hikayat Seri Rama** — The Malay prose Rama story is reframed within an Islamic cosmological frame (Allah, prophet-hierarchy) roughly 1300-1700 CE, one of the oldest Malay literary works _(actors: Malay hikayat tradition)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ramayana/recensions/southeast-asian-ramayanas.md) — (Hikayat Seri Rama)
- **Jnaneshwar composes the Jnaneshwari and takes living samadhi** — The Varkari founding luminary Jnaneshwar composes the Jnaneshwari Marathi paraphrase of the Bhagavad Gita in 1290, then dies at 21 by living sanjivana samadhi at Alandi _(actors: Jnaneshwar)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/north-bhakti-sants.md) — (Jnaneshwari; Amritanubhava)
- **Karaikkal Ammaiyar Becomes a Ghoul-Attendant at Shiva's Dance** — The earliest Nayanar renounces her beauty to become a skeletal pey (ghoul) attendant at Shiva's dance, composing some 143 verses _(actors: Karaikkal Ammaiyar, Shiva)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/alvars-and-nayanars.md) — (Tirumurai Book 11)
- **Later Dāsa-kūṭa revival** — 18th-c. revival under Vijaya Dāsa, Gopāla Dāsa, and Jagannātha Dāsa (Harikathāmṛtasāra), extending the Haridāsa corpus _(actors: Vijaya Dāsa, Gopāla Dāsa, Jagannātha Dāsa)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/haridasa-and-carnatic-bhakti.md) — (Haridāsa-sāhitya)
- **Madhva rejects Advaita and founds Dvaita** — Initiated young under the Advaitin Acyutapreksha, Madhva rejects Advaita and launches Tattvavada (Dvaita), teaching the pancha-bheda five eternal distinctions and authoring the 37 Sarvamula granthas _(actors: Madhva, Acyutapreksha)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/ramanuja-and-madhva.md) — (Brahma Sutra Bhashya; Anuvyakhyana; Sarvamula granthas)
- **Madurai Mariamman Teppakulam** — The Vandiyur temple tank at Madurai with its float-festival, a Māri tank-cult beside the Mīnākṣī complex _(actors: Māriyammaṉ; Madurai devotees)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/mariamman.md) — (Madurai sthala tradition)
- **Madurai Meenakshi Teppam** — Mīnākṣī and Sundareśwara are floated on a raft around the central island of the Vāṇḍiyūr Teppakkuḷam on the Thai full moon, tied to the birth-anniversary of 17th-c. ruler Tirumalai Nāyak who dug the tank _(actors: Mīnākṣī, Sundareśwara (Śiva), Tirumalai Nāyak, Madurai devotees)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/swing-and-float-temple-festivals.md) — (Wikipedia (Vandiyur Mariamman Teppakulam, Meenakshi Temple); Madurai temple calendar)
- **Mahavira and Buddha** — Mahavira (Jainism) and Gautama Buddha flourish c. 6th-5th c. BCE, challenging Vedic ritual and caste _(actors: Mahavira, Gautama Buddha)_ → [details](../01-manvantaras/manvantara-07-vaivasvata/mahayuga-28-current/04-kali-yuga/recorded-history.md) — (Jain & Buddhist tradition)
- **Maṇimahesh Yātrā (Chhaḍī procession, Janmāṣṭamī–Rādhāṣṭamī)** — The major annual pilgrimage from Hadsar via Dhancho to Maṇimahesh Lake, climaxing in a holy dip when the sacred Chhaḍī mace procession from Bharmour/Chamba arrives during the Janmāṣṭamī-to-Rādhāṣṭamī fortnight _(actors: Chamba/Bharmour pilgrims; Chhaḍī mace-bearers)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/panch-kailash.md) — (Wikipedia (Manimahesh Lake); Chamba Śaiva living tradition)
- **Manasā destroys Chand's fortune and six sons** — Manasā wrecks Chand's garden, fleet and friend and kills his six elder sons to break his resistance _(actors: Manasā, Chand Sadagar)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/manasa.md) — (Manasāmaṅgal)
- **Mediation by Bara Khan Gazi and partition of the forest** — Dakshin Ray takes refuge with the pir Bara Khan Gazi; Bonbibi spares him, accepts him as a son, and partitions the forest's bounty rather than seizing it _(actors: Bonbibi, Dakshin Ray, Bara Khan Gazi (Gazi Pir), Shah Jangali)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/bonbibi.md) — (Bonbibi Jahuranama)
- **Mitaoli Ekattarso Mahadeva Temple Built** — The 64-celled circular Ekattarso Mahadeva yoginī temple is built at Mitaoli c. 1323 CE, often cited as inspiration for the Indian Parliament's circular plan → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/matrikas-and-64-yoginis.md) — (ASI)
- **Mīrābāī remembered as Ravidās's disciple** — The Rajput princess-saint Mīrābāī is traditionally tied to Ravidās as her guru, a boundary-crossing memory scholars treat as later accretion _(actors: Ravidās, Mīrābāī)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/ravidas.md) — (Wikipedia Ravidas, Mirabai [folk/disputed])
- **M. S. Subbulakshmi's 1963 recording** — M. S. Subbulakshmi records the canonical popular rendition for the Tirumala Tirupati Devasthanams, fixing the hymn's cadence in modern practice _(actors: M. S. Subbulakshmi; Tirumala Tirupati Devasthanams)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/venkatesha-suprabhatam.md) — (Living recitation tradition; 1963 TTD recording)
