- **Namdev recovers the chanting bones** — Namdev identifies Chokhamela's remains in the rubble because his bones still chant 'Vitthal, Vitthal', confirming his sanctity past death _(actors: Namdev; Chokhamela (deceased))_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/chokhamela.md) — (Mahipati, Bhaktavijaya / Bhaktalilamrta)
- **Nathamuni recovers the lost Divya Prabandham** — Nathamuni recites Madhurakavi's eleven verses 12,000 times at Alvartirunagari until Nammalvar appears in yogic vision and reveals the entire 4,000 hymns with their music and esoteric meaning, which he institutes as the Dravida Veda _(actors: Nathamuni, Nammalvar)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/nammalvar.md) — (Nammalvar hagiography (traditional))
- **Nāthamuni recovers the Divya Prabandham** — By legend Nāthamuni recovers the lost 4,000 Āḻvār verses through yogic meditation on Nammāḻvār, compiling the Nālāyira Divya Prabandham _(actors: Nāthamuni, Nammāḻvār)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/tamil-canon-sangam-and-bhakti.md) — (Nālāyira Divya Prabandham tradition)
- **Origin of Govardhana Pūjā and the Annakūṭa festival** — The līlā becomes the charter for the Kārttika Śukla Pratipadā festival of Govardhana Pūjā / Annakūṭa, the Govardhana parikramā, and Giri-rāja / Śrī Nāthjī worship _(actors: Vaiṣṇava devotees, Gauḍīya and Puṣṭimārga traditions)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/govardhana-dharana.md) — (Festival and sectarian tradition (Vallabha Puṣṭimārga; Braj practice))
- **Pilgrimage to Kailāsa walking on her head** — She journeys to Mount Kailāsa, climbing on her hands/head downward in humility to avoid treading on the sacred mountain _(actors: Kāraikkāl Ammaiyār)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/karaikkal-ammaiyar.md) — (Periya Purāṇam (Cēkkiḻār))
- **Poturāju the buffalo-king escorts the mothers** — The fierce male guardian-brother Poturāju escorts, controls and executes for the seven mother-goddesses, presiding over the buffalo-sacrifice _(actors: Poturāju (Pothuraju); the seven mother-goddesses)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/gramadevatas-village-deities.md) — (Bonalu/Pothuraju festival ethnography)
- **Prabhupada Founds ISKCON as 32nd Link** — Prabhupada founds ISKCON (1966), reckoning himself the 32nd link in the Brahma-Madhva-Gaudiya parampara counted from Krishna _(actors: A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupada)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/four-vaishnava-sampradayas.md) — (HareKrishnaMandir.org)
- **'Prophecies' of Mughals, Timur, and the British** — The Pratisarga Parva 'foretells' the Mughals, Timur, Akbar, Nādir Shāh, the English, Calcutta, and Queen Victoria — fabricated retrojections dating the layer to the 18th–19th c _(actors: Mughals, Timur, Akbar, Queen Victoria)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/bhavishya-purana.md) — (Bhaviṣya Purāṇa (Pratisarga Parva) [interpolation])
- **Reconciliation of Mirkhshah (variant)** — In a variant Jhulelal converts Mirkhshah's heart rather than destroying him, teaching one Lord under different names _(actors: Jhulelal, Mirkhshah)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/jhulelal.md) — (Sindhi folk tradition (variant))
- **Restoration of the child and the vow of stillness** — In devotional variants Viṭhobā's grace restores the child to life, or Gorā vows never to tread clay again until the Lord intervenes _(actors: Gorā Kumbhār, Viṭhobā, his child, his wife)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/gora-kumbhar.md) — (Vārkarī hagiography)
- **Rise of the Sixteen Mahajanapadas** — Sixteen kingdoms and oligarchies arise c. 600-321 BCE with cities, coinage and the ascendancy of Magadha during the second urbanization _(actors: Mahajanapadas; Magadha)_ → [details](../01-manvantaras/manvantara-07-vaivasvata/mahayuga-28-current/04-kali-yuga/recorded-history.md) — (History of India (Wikipedia))
- **Rāmāmātya introduces the mela in the Svaramelakalānidhi** — At Vijayanagara, Rāmāmātya's Svaramelakalānidhi introduces the mela parent-scale as a classifying device, seeding the melakarta system _(actors: Rāmāmātya)_ → [details](../09-artifacts-symbols-and-arts/sangita-classical-music-svara-raga-tala.md) — (Svaramelakalānidhi of Rāmāmātya (1550))
- **Sealing of the jagamohana (1903)** — After the main deul/śikhara collapsed and worship lapsed, the British fill and seal the surviving porch with sand in 1903 to prevent its fall _(actors: British Archaeological authorities)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/konark-sun-temple-arka-kshetra.md) — (Academic/historical)
- **Sekkizhar Composes the Periya Puranam** — Sekkizhar composes the Periya Puranam (12th c., the 12th Tirumurai), the hagiography of all 63 Nayanars narrating their temple-visits as miracle events _(actors: Sekkizhar)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/275-paadal-petra-sthalams.md) — (Periya Puranam)
- **Shankara Establishes the Four Amnaya Pithas** — Shankara installs four cardinal mathas (Shringeri, Puri, Dvaraka, Jyotirmatha), each with a Veda, mahavakya and first head, and reorganizes renunciants into the Dashanami order _(actors: Adi Shankara, Sureshvara, Padmapada, Hastamalaka, Totaka)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/adi-shankara.md) — (Hindupost; Sringeri Math)
- **Sibling Great Living Chola Temples** — Rājendra Chola I extends the programme with the Gangaikoṇḍa Cholapuram Bṛhadīśvara and Airāvateśvara at Darasuram, forming the UNESCO triad _(actors: Rājendra Chola I)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/brihadeeswarar-thanjavur.md) — (Chola inscriptions; UNESCO Great Living Chola Temples)
- **Singraur excavations — the archaeological site** — The site survives as the excavated mound at Singraur; ASI/B. B. Lal (1977–1986) exposed a brick water-tank/hydraulic complex of the Śuṅga–Kuṣāṇa horizon, part of the Archaeology of the Rāmāyaṇa Sites project _(actors: B. B. Lal, Archaeological Survey of India)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/sringaverapura-guha-ganga-crossing.md) — (B. B. Lal, Excavations at Sringaverapura / Archaeology of the Ramayana Sites)
- **Sopāndev's samādhi at Saswad** — The third sibling, the abhaṅga-poet 'Sopān-kākā', takes samādhi at Saswad shortly after Jñāneśvar in 1296; his shrine anchors a Pandharpur pālkhī _(actors: Sopāndev)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/nivritti-sopan-muktabai.md) — (Wikipedia (Sopan, Pandharpur Wari))
- **Sureśvara's Mānasollāsa vārttika** — Sureśvara (traditionally Maṇḍana Miśra) composes the authoritative Advaita commentary on Śaṅkara's Stotra _(actors: Sureśvara / Maṇḍana Miśra)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/dakshinamurti.md) — (Mānasollāsa of Sureśvara)
- **Systematisation of the Pañcha Sabhai fivefold set** — Medieval Tamil Śaiva-Siddhānta tradition assembles the five long-independent dance-halls into a single canonical set modelled on Chidambaram's golden hall _(actors: Tamil Śaiva-Siddhānta tradition, Nāyaṉmār milieu)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/pancha-sabhai-nataraja-dance-halls.md) — (Tamil Śaiva tradition; sthala-purāṇas of the five temples; Tirumantiram/Tēvāram milieu)
- **Tamil rendering and English condensation circulated** — The text spreads via a Tamil verse rendering and a widely-circulating ~122-verse English condensation (N. R. Krishnamoorthi Aiyer's Essence of Ribhu Gita) plus a complete SAT translation _(actors: Ulaganātha Swāmigaḷ/Bhikṣu Śāstri, N. R. Krishnamoorthi Aiyer, Society of Abidance in Truth)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ribhu-gita.md) — (Tamiḻ Ṛbhu-Gītai; The Essence of Ribhu Gita; SAT complete translation)
- **Textual systematization of vastu** — The science is set down in datable treatises — Varahamihira's Brihat Samhita (~6th c.), Mayamata, Manasara, and Bhoja's Samarangana Sutradhara (11th c.) — plus Puranic vastu chapters _(actors: Varahamihira, King Bhoja of Dhara, the Puranic redactors)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/vastu-shastra-and-the-vastu-purusha-mandala.md) — (Brihat Samhita; Mayamata; Manasara; Samarangana Sutradhara; Matsya, Agni & Garuda Puranas)
- **The basket with the serpent** — Mira is sent a covered basket said to hold a venomous snake but on opening it finds a garland of flowers or a Krishna image, the snake transformed _(actors: Mirabai, the in-laws, Krishna)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/mirabai.md) — (Mirabai hagiography (folk))
- **The Bengali Śītalā-maṅgal force-my-worship cycle** — Maṅgal-kāvya poets (incl. Kṛṣṇarām Dās, 17th c.) narrate Śītalā compelling an unwilling king/merchant to worship her by visiting smallpox on his realm _(actors: Śītalā; the resisting king/merchant; Bengali maṅgal poets)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/shitala.md) — (Bengali Śītalā-maṅgal-kāvya)
- **The borgeet fire and the 34 survivors** — A fire destroys Śaṅkaradeva's 240 borgeet manuscripts; only ~34 are recovered from memory, after which, grieved, he stops composing and asks Madhavadeva to continue the genre _(actors: Śaṅkaradeva, Madhavadeva)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/sankaradeva-and-assamese-vaishnavism.md) — (assaminfo.com; atributetosankaradeva.org)
- **The didactic primers — Ātticcūṭi, Koṉṟai Vēntaṉ, Mūturai, Nalvaḻi** — The medieval (Chola-era) Avvaiyār composed the alphabetic moral primers Ātticcūṭi and Koṉṟai Vēntaṉ and the verse counsels Mūturai (Vākkuṇṭa Ammai) and Nalvaḻi/Naṉneṟi — the foundational moral first-readers of Tamil schooling _(actors: Medieval Avvaiyār)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/avvaiyar.md) — (Ātticcūṭi; Koṉṟai Vēntaṉ; Mūturai; Nalvaḻi; Auvaiyar — Wikipedia)
- **The Girnar (Lili) Parikramā established** — The annual ~36 km forest circumambulation of the massif runs from Kārttika Ekādaśī to Pūrṇimā, gathering pilgrims and sādhus through the Gir forest _(actors: Pilgrims, Nāgā sādhus, Nāths and avadhūtas)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/girnar-raivataka-datta-tirtha.md) — (Regional tradition; Junāgaḍh / Gujarat Tourism)
- **The Kanakana Kindi becomes the principal darśana point** — The small west-wall navagraha window named for Kanaka becomes the main mukha-darśana aperture, so every pilgrim sees Kṛṣṇa as the saint did _(actors: Kanakadāsa, Udupi devotees)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/udupi-krishna-matha.md) — (Udupi / regional tradition)
- **The kodai festival and possession** — The annual kodai festival climaxes with the god descending into a cāmiyāṭi medium who dances, gives oracles and blesses devotees, with blood sacrifice and paḍaiyal offerings _(actors: Sudalaimāḍaṉ, the cāmiyāṭi medium, pūcāri priests, devotees)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/sudalaimadan.md) — (Sudalaimāḍaṉ kodai festival tradition; Tamil possession-worship tradition)
- **The Nund Rishi link** — Tradition holds the infant Nund Rishi, founder of the Kashmiri Rishi Sufi order, was suckled or blessed by Lallā, symbolizing her bridging of both communities _(actors: Lallā; Nūr-ud-Dīn / Nund Rishi)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/lalleshwari-lal-ded.md) — (Wikipedia, Lalleshwari, Nund Rishi)
- **The swapped head-and-body etiology** — In folk variants Reṇukā's head is rejoined to an outcaste woman's body, producing the split high-caste/low-caste goddess linking Reṇukā to Mātaṅgī/Māriyammaṉ _(actors: Paraśurāma, Reṇukā, the outcaste (Mātaṅgī/Cāṇḍāla) woman)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/renuka-yellamma.md) — (South Indian folk Yellamma/Mariyamman variants)
- **The twelve Āḻvārs sing Śrīraṅgam** — All twelve Āḻvārs glorify the shrine in the Nālāyira Divya Prabandham, the only Divya Desam so sung _(actors: The twelve Āḻvārs (Toṇḍaraḍippoḍi, Āṇḍāḷ, Tiruppāṇ, et al.))_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/srirangam-ranganatha.md) — (Nālāyira Divya Prabandham)
- **Thrissur Pooram founded by Sakthan Thampuran** — Around 1796 Raja Rama Varma 'Sakthan Thampuran' of Cochin organized ten temples around Vadakkunnathan into the unified Thrissur Pooram after smaller temples were barred from the Aarattupuzha Pooram _(actors: Sakthan Thampuran)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/regional-temple-festivals.md) — (Wikipedia, Thrissur Pooram)
- **Tukārām's bodily ascent to Vaikuṇṭha** — At his end (1649/1650) Tukārām tells Avalī he is going to Vaikuṇṭha and is taken bodily in a radiant chariot before a crowd, leaving no corpse _(actors: Tukārām, Avalī)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/tukaram.md) — (mkvyoga.com; New World Encyclopedia)
- **UNESCO Intangible Cultural Heritage inscription (2023)** — 'Garba of Gujarat' is inscribed on UNESCO's Representative List of Intangible Cultural Heritage of Humanity in December 2023, recognizing one of the world's largest religious dance festivals _(actors: UNESCO, Gujarat/Gujarati diaspora)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/garba-and-dandiya-raas.md) — (UNESCO ICH listing, 2023)
- **Variant: the tree-shrine revelation** — In a parallel sthala-legend the goddess reveals herself at an aniconic seat under a sacred tree discovered by a devotee, coexisting with the tragu legend _(actors: Bahucharā Mātā, a devotee)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/bahuchara-mata.md) — (Becharājī sthala-mahatmya)
- **Vikramaditya's Reign and the Vikrama Samvat Epoch** — The legendary cakravartin Vikramaditya reigns at Ujjayini with his court of nine gems including Kalidasa, and the Vikrama Samvat era (57 BCE) is founded counting from his reign _(actors: Vikramaditya, Kalidasa)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/ujjain-avantika.md) — (Simhasana-dvatrimshika tradition)
- **Vitthalanatha forms the Ashtachhap** — Vallabha's son Vitthalanatha completes his father's works, organizes temple seva and forms the Ashtachhap, the eight poet-saints (four his, four Vallabha's) whose Braj-bhasha songs become the sect's liturgical music _(actors: Vitthalanatha, Surdas)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/nimbarka-vallabha-chaitanya.md) — (Ashtachhap corpus)
- **Vivekananda's Chicago address** — Vivekananda delivers the 'Sisters and Brothers of America' address at the World's Parliament of Religions in Chicago in September 1893, introducing Vedanta and Yoga to the West _(actors: Swami Vivekananda)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/modern-teachers-and-reform.md) — (Ramakrishna Mission history)
- **Yatōktakāri Obeys Tirumaḻicai Āḻvār** — The reclining Viṣṇu at Tiru-Veḷḷakkuḷam rises and leaves (then returns) at the command of Tirumaḻicai Āḻvār, becoming Yatōktakāri, 'he who did exactly as told' _(actors: Viṣṇu (Yatōktakāri), Tirumaḻicai Āḻvār)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/kanchipuram-city-of-temples.md) — (divyadesam.com)
- **Adi Śaṅkara recovers the image and re-establishes Badrinath** — In the 8th c. CE Adi Śaṅkara recovers the Badrī mūrti from the Nārada-kuṇḍa, revives the temple as the northern pole of his pan-India Char Dhām, and sets the Nambūdiri Rāwal priesthood _(actors: Adi Śaṅkarācārya)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/badrinath-badarikashrama-kshetra.md) — (Char Dhām tradition (Adi Śaṅkara))
- **Ajivikas receive Mauryan patronage (Barabar caves)** — Ashoka and his grandson Dasharatha dedicate the Barabar and Nagarjuni rock-cut caves to Ajivika ascetics, the school's main epigraphic footprint _(actors: Ashoka, Dasharatha, Ajivika ascetics)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/nastika-darshanas-and-the-astika-nastika-frame.md) — (Barabar/Nagarjuni cave inscriptions)
- **Śaṅkara's commentary** — Ādi Śaṅkara composes the standard Viṣṇu-sahasranāma-bhāṣya, reading the names as praise of the one non-dual Brahman _(actors: Ādi Śaṅkarācārya)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/vishnu-sahasranama.md) — (Śaṅkara, Viṣṇu-sahasranāma-bhāṣya)
- **Śaṅkara's Taittirīya-bhāṣya** — Ādi Śaṅkara (c. 8th c. CE) composes a full Advaita commentary on the Taittirīya, fixing the standard reading of the Brahman-definition, pañca-kośa and Bhṛgu inquiry _(actors: Ādi Śaṅkara)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/upanishads/taittiriya-upanishad.md) — (Śaṅkara, Taittirīya-Upaniṣad-bhāṣya)
- **Aśokan and later rock-edicts at Girnar's foot** — A boulder at Girnar's base carries Aśoka's fourteen Major Rock Edicts plus the Rudradāman and Skandagupta Sudarśana-lake inscriptions — the secular historical clock _(actors: Aśoka, Rudradāman, Skandagupta)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/girnar-raivataka-datta-tirtha.md) — (Junagadh rock inscription (Wikipedia); epigraphic record)
- **Bhartṛhari renounces Ujjain** — King Bhartṛhari abandons his throne and queen Piṅgalā to become a Nāth yogī, subject of north-Indian Jogī ballads _(actors: Bhartṛhari (Bhartṛnath), Piṅgalā, Gorakṣa)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/nath-sampradaya-and-the-siddhas.md) — (Bhartṛhari–Gopīcand ballads; Nāth hagiography)
- **Bhartrhari Renounces the Ujjain Throne** — The poet-king Bhartrhari, disillusioned by the tale of the immortality-fruit passed between king, queen and lover, renounces the Ujjain throne to become a yogin and compose the Shatakatraya _(actors: Bhartrhari, Vikramaditya)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/ujjain-avantika.md) — (folk tradition)
- **Bonalu and jātara festival complex** — Telangana women carry decorated pots of cooked rice to the village mothers in Āṣāḍha amid drumming, possession and Poturāju's procession _(actors: Women devotees; Mahākālī, Pochamma, Maisamma; Poturāju)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/gramadevatas-village-deities.md) — (Bonalu festival ethnography; Medaram jātara studies)
- **Brahmagupta and the Brāhmasphuṭasiddhānta (628 CE)** — First text to treat zero and negatives as numbers; carried the decimal system to Baghdad as the Zīj al-Sindhind; rejected Āryabhaṭa's rotating Earth _(actors: Brahmagupta)_ → [details](../00-time-and-cosmology/siddhantic-astronomy-and-astronomers.md) — (Brāhmasphuṭasiddhānta; Khaṇḍakhādyaka)
- **Building of the Jānakī Mandir** — The discovered Sītā image (1657 CE) is enshrined and the great Nau Lakhā Jānakī Mandir is built in 1910-11 CE by Queen Vrishabhanu Kunwari, anchoring the modern cult _(actors: Śūrkiśordās (sādhu), Queen Vrishabhanu Kunwari of Tikamgarh)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/janakpur-mithila-sita-janmabhumi.md) — (Temple history; regional Maithilī tradition)
- **Building of the west-facing temple and Kiraṇotsava alignment** — The medieval Cālukya/Śilāhāra stone temple is built with the west-facing svayambhū mūrti and a solar alignment that admits the setting sun onto the Goddess thrice yearly (Kiraṇotsava) _(actors: Cālukya king Karṇadeva (trad.), Śilāhāra dynasty)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/kolhapur-mahalakshmi-karavira.md) — (Wikipedia: Mahalaxmi Temple, Kolhapur; temple administration)
- **Buta Malik's discovery of the cave** — A shepherd given coals that turned to gold returns to find the cave and ice-liṅga, re-popularizing the shrine _(actors: Buta Malik, a sādhu (held to be Śiva))_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/amarnath-ice-lingam-and-amar-katha.md) — (Living Amarnāth Yātrā discovery legend [folk])
- **Cēkkiḻār composes the Periya Purāṇam** — In 1135 CE Cēkkiḻār composes the Periya Purāṇam, the hagiography of all 63 Nāyaṉārs, under Chola Kulottuṅga II _(actors: Cēkkiḻār, Kulottuṅga II)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/tamil-canon-sangam-and-bhakti.md) — (Periya Purāṇam)
- **Composition of the Amṛtānubhava** — Jñāneśvar writes an independent non-dual philosophical work expounding cidvilāsa and the unity of Śiva and Śakti _(actors: Jñāneśvar)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/jnaneshwar.md) — (Amṛtānubhava; Wikipedia (Amrutanubhav))
- **Composition of the autobiographical Abhaṅga-gāthā / Ātmanivedana** — She composes a first-person abhaṅga cycle narrating her own life and inner struggle, a landmark of pre-modern Indian women's self-writing and a primary witness to Tukārām's circle _(actors: Bahiṇābāī)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/bahinabai.md) — (Feldhaus, Bahina Bai: A Translation of Her Autobiography and Verses; Tharu & Lalita)
- **Composition of the Rukmiṇī Svayaṃvara** — Eknāth composes a Marathi narrative poem on Kṛṣṇa winning and wedding Rukmiṇī, drawn from the Bhāgavata _(actors: Eknāth, Kṛṣṇa, Rukmiṇī)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/eknath.md) — (Rukmiṇī Svayaṃvara; Wikipedia Eknath)
- **Composition of the Sūr Sāgar** — Sūr's vast Braj Kṛṣṇa-song anthology (Ocean of Sūr); small attested early core grew by centuries of accretion under his open signature _(actors: Sūrdās)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/surdas.md) — (Sūr Sāgar; Wikipedia Surdas; Hawley)
- **Death of Basava at Kudalasangama** — Having withdrawn to Kudalasangama amid the crisis, Basava dies there, traditionally described as merging into the linga _(actors: Basava)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/lingayat-vachana-poets.md) — (Basava hagiography)
- **Death of Ravidās** — Ravidās dies c. 1520 CE (traditional) after a life as cobbler-saint of Vārāṇasī _(actors: Ravidās)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/ravidas.md) — (Wikipedia Ravidas [traditional])
- **Dense inscriptional endowments and Vijayanagara patronage** — Hundreds of inscriptions record endowments by Eastern Ganga, Kakatiya, Reddi, Gajapati and Vijayanagara rulers, including Kṛṣṇadevarāya after his Kaliṅga campaign _(actors: Kakatiya, Reddi, Gajapati, Vijayanagara rulers; Kṛṣṇadevarāya)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/simhachalam-varaha-narasimha.md) — (Epigraphia Indica; South Indian Inscriptions)
- **Ādi Śaṅkara's Śrīśaila sojourn and the Śivānanda-laharī** — Tradition holds Ādi Śaṅkara visited Śrīśaila, composed the Śivānanda-laharī before Mallikārjuna, and survived a Kāpālika seeking his head _(actors: Ādi Śaṅkara, a Kāpālika ascetic)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/srisailam-srisaila-mallikarjuna-bhramaramba.md) — (Śaṅkara-vijaya hagiographies; Śivānanda-laharī colophon-lore (disputed))
- **Diaspora spread of the cult** — Tamil emigration carries Sudalaimāḍaṉ shrines, kodai and firewalking observances into Malaysia, Singapore, Sri Lanka and beyond _(actors: Sudalaimāḍaṉ, Tamil diaspora communities)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/sudalaimadan.md) — (Tamil folk tradition [regional])
- **Disputed guru association with Śivājī** — Traditional accounts make Rāmdās the guru/rājguru of Chhatrapati Śivājī; modern historians regard the relationship as late and exaggerated _(actors: Rāmdās; Chhatrapati Śivājī)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/samarth-ramdas.md) — (Hindupedia; Rāmdāsī hagiography; scholarly consensus [disputed])
- **Double veneration as Lalleśvarī and Lal ʿĀrifa** — Lallā is revered simultaneously by Hindus as Lalleśvarī / Lallā Yogīśvarī and by Muslims/Sufis as Lal ʿĀrifa / Lal Ded, becoming the great bridge between Trika Śaivism and Kashmiri Sufism _(actors: Lallā; Kashmiri Hindu and Muslim communities)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/lalleshwari-lal-ded.md) — (Wikipedia, Lalleshwari; Hoskote, I, Lalla)
- **Dukhe enriched and worship established** — Bonbibi sends Dukhe home laden with wealth and restores him to his mother, establishing her worship among the forest people _(actors: Bonbibi, Dukhe)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/bonbibi.md) — (Bonbibi Jahuranama)
- **Enactment of Nammalvar's Vaikuntha-ascent at Srirangam** — During the Adhyayana Utsava at Srirangam the full Tiruvaymoli is recited and Nammalvar's image is taken through the Paramapada Vasal into the sanctum, dramatically staging his liberation before the assembled devotees _(actors: Nammalvar, Srirangam devotees)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/nammalvar.md) — (Tiruvaymoli; Srirangam liturgy)
- **Establishment of the daily all-caste cult** — The non-Brahmanical, all-caste-and-faith Parassinikkadavu cult is established with daily theyyam performance, toddy/fish/meat offerings, and the trademark fish-curry-and-boiled-gram prasāda _(actors: Muthappan, theyyam communities, devotees of all castes)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/muthappan.md) — (Parassinikkadavu Muthappan temple lore; Kerala theyyam folklore)
- **Firewalking, karagam and neem-vow festivals** — Signature Māri rites — coal-bed firewalking (tīmiti), karagam pot-dance, neem-skirt vows and body-piercing austerities that 'cool' the goddess _(actors: Māriyammaṉ; vow-takers and dancers)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/mariamman.md) — (Festival ethnography; Tamil folk-performance tradition)
- **Founding/glorification of the Koṇārka Sun Temple** — The Brahma Purāṇa describes the great Sun Temple of Koṇārka (built 1241 CE) and its pilgrimage merit, a key dating anchor → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/brahma-purana.md) — (Brahma Purāṇa [late text])
- **Founding of the pādukā-pālkhī procession** — In 1685 Tukārām's youngest son Nārāyaṇ Mahārāj begins the pādukā-pālkhī custom, carrying the saint's sandals in a palanquin from Dehu to Pandharpur _(actors: Nārāyaṇ Mahārāj, Tukārām)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/tukaram.md) — (Wikipedia, Pandharpur Wari)
- **Garbha Joon / Ardh Kuwārī womb-cave** — Vaiṣṇavī meditates nine months inside the narrow Garbha Joon cave at Ardh Kuwārī, then tears open its far end to emerge as Bhairavnāth nears _(actors: Vaiṣṇavī, Bhairavnāth)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/vaishno-devi-trikuta-katra.md) — (Vaiṣṇo Devī sthala legend)
- **Gorakhnāth's penance and the Nāth siddha-seat** — Guru Gorakṣanātha does penance on a Girnar summit, establishing it as a primal Nāth/Kānphaṭa yogī seat (9th-13th c. CE historical layer) _(actors: Gorakhnāth (Gorakṣanātha), Kānphaṭa yogīs)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/girnar-raivataka-datta-tirtha.md) — (Nāth tradition; Wikipedia 'Gorakhnath')
- **Household and the poet-maidservant Janābāī** — The maidservant Janābāī lives in Nāmdev's household and becomes a celebrated abhaṅga-poet, repeatedly naming Nāmā in her verse _(actors: Nāmdev, Rājāī (wife), Janābāī)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/namdev.md) — (Wikipedia Namdev, Janabai)
- **Iconography of the dvitiya-avatara** — Kurma is depicted as a plain tortoise bearing Mandara or as a hybrid tortoise-bodied four-armed Vishnu holding shankha, chakra, gada and padma _(actors: Vishnu-Kurma (cult image))_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/kurma-avatara.md) — (Pancharatra Agamas; temple iconography)
- **Interment within the temple and the tarati samādhi-tree** — She is buried within the Viṭṭhal-Rukmiṇī temple precinct—traditionally the only person so interred—and a tarati tree grows from her samādhi _(actors: Kānhopātrā, Śrī Viṭṭhal-Rukmiṇī temple community)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/kanhopatra.md) — (Sri Vitthal-Rukmini temple tradition; Vārkarī hagiography)
- **Iron bridal chamber with a hidden flaw** — Chand builds an impenetrable iron nuptial chamber for Lakhindar and Behulā; Viśvakarmā, coerced by Manasā, leaves a needle-fine hole _(actors: Chand Sadagar, Lakhindar, Behulā, Viśvakarmā, Manasā)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/manasa.md) — (Manasāmaṅgal (Bijay Gupta))
- **Śiva names her 'Ammai' (Mother) and grants boons** — On Kailāsa Śiva addresses her as 'Ammai'; she begs undying love, freedom from rebirth, never to forget Him, and to witness His cosmic dance forever _(actors: Śiva, Kāraikkāl Ammaiyār)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/karaikkal-ammaiyar.md) — (Periya Purāṇam (Cēkkiḻār))
- **Kabir's verses entered into the Adi Granth** — Guru Arjan Dev compiles the Adi Granth in 1604 at Kartarpur, incorporating Kabir's verses and making him the largest non-Sikh bhagat contributor to Sikh scripture _(actors: Guru Arjan Dev, Kabir)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/kabir.md) — (Adi Granth / Guru Granth Sahib)
- **Kailāsanāthar Temple Built by Rājasiṃha** — The Pallava king Rājasiṃha (Narasiṃhavarman II) builds the Kailāsanāthar temple c. 685–705 CE, the oldest standing temple in Kāñcī and foundational to the Dravidian style _(actors: Rājasiṃha (Narasiṃhavarman II), Mahendravarman III)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/kanchipuram-city-of-temples.md) — (Wikipedia, Kailasanathar Temple)
- **Kannappar Offers His Own Eyes to Shiva** — The tribal hunter Nayanar Kannappar offers his own eyes to a Shiva linga, becoming the archetype of devotion over ritual purity _(actors: Kannappar, Shiva)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/alvars-and-nayanars.md) — (Nayanars — Wikipedia)
- **Krishnadevaraya Composes the Amuktamalyada** — The Telugu poet-king Krishnadevaraya retells Andal's life in the Amuktamalyada ('she who gives a worn garland'), an emperor's literary tribute _(actors: Krishnadevaraya)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/andal.md) — (Amuktamalyada — Grokipedia)
- **Lakshmidhara's Saubhagyavardhani commentary** — The most authoritative commentary reads the hymn through the orthodox Samayacara lens, setting the chief interpretive divide against Kaula/prayoga readings _(actors: Lakshmidhara (commentator))_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/soundarya-lahari.md) — (Saubhagyavardhani of Lakshmidhara)
- **List-placement as ninth/twenty-first avatara** — Buddha fixed as the 9th of ten in Krishna-as-8th lists and the 21st/23rd in the long Bhagavata schemas, contested against Balarama in other traditions _(actors: Buddha, Krishna, Balarama, Kalki)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/buddha-avatara.md) — (Agni Purana 49, Garuda Purana 1.86.10-11, Bhagavata Purana 1.3)
- **Madhva obtains the Udupi Krishna icon and founds the Krishna Matha** — Madhva miraculously obtains a Krishna murti encased in gopichandana from a ship off the Karnataka coast and enshrines it at Udupi, establishing the Krishna Matha and the Ashta Matha with their Paryaya worship rotation _(actors: Madhva)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/ramanuja-and-madhva.md) — (Madhva hagiography (traditional))
- **Manasā as Bengali folk-goddess and the Behulā–Lakhindar legend** — In eastern India the nāga-sister becomes the independent serpent-goddess Manasā, whose Manasā-maṅgala poems tell of the merchant Cānd Sadāgar's feud and Behulā's corpse-raft journey to revive Lakhindar _(actors: Manasā; Cānd Sadāgar; Lakhindar; Behulā)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/jaratkaru-and-manasa.md) — (Bengali Manasā-maṅgala / Manasā-vijaya (c. 13th–18th c. CE); Purāṇic strata)
- **Medieval/early-modern temple foundation and 1692 CE rebuild** — The standing pagoda-roofed Paśupatinātha temple, on possibly Licchavi-era foundations, is rebuilt in 1692 CE and becomes the presiding national shrine of Nepal _(actors: Nepali rulers/patrons)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/pashupatinath-kathmandu-nepal-kshetra.md) — (recorded history; temple tradition)
- **Meykaṇḍar composes the Śivajñāna-bodham** — In the 13th c. Meykaṇḍar authors the twelve-sūtra Śivajñāna-bodham, founding systematic Tamil Śaiva Siddhānta and the Meykaṇḍar Sampradāya _(actors: Meykaṇḍar)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/shaiva-schools.md) — (Wikipedia, Meykandar)
- **Muktābāī's disappearance in a storm** — Unlike her brothers' entombed samādhis, Muktābāī is said to vanish into light during a thunderstorm on the Tāpī near Mehūn c. 1297; shrine at Muktainagar _(actors: Muktābāī)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/nivritti-sopan-muktabai.md) — (Wikipedia (Muktabai))
- **Muṉīśvaraṉ's Śaiva pedigree and colour-forms** — Muṉīśvaraṉ/Muṉiyāṇṭi, read as a form of Śiva, splits into Veḷḷai (white, milder) and Karu/Karuppu (black, fierce) Muṉīśvaraṉ forms in the kāval register _(actors: Muṉīśvaraṉ/Muṉiyāṇṭi, Śiva)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/karuppasamy-madurai-veeran-and-tamil-kaval-deities.md) — (Tamil-Śaiva guardian-deity tradition)
- **Namdev co-founds the Varkari kirtana tradition** — The tailor sant Namdev of Pandharpur, Jnaneshwar's contemporary, co-founds the Varkari kirtana tradition and travels to the Punjab, his hymns entering the Guru Granth Sahib _(actors: Namdev)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/north-bhakti-sants.md) — (Namdev abhangas; Guru Granth Sahib)
- **Periya Purāṇam and the Chola bronze/Naṭarāja milieu** — Under continuing Chola patronage the Nāyanār hagiography Periya Purāṇam is composed and the Naṭarāja bronze tradition is perfected as dynastic Śaiva emblem _(actors: Sēkkiḻār; Chola bronze-casters and court)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/brihadeeswarar-thanjavur.md) — (Periya Purāṇam (Tirumurai 12); Chola inscriptions)
- **Pāmbāṭṭi-cittar's kuṇḍalinī songs** — The snake-charmer Siddhar composes verses refraining 'āḍu, pāmbē!' ('dance, O snake!'), coding the raising of kuṇḍalinī _(actors: Pāmbāṭṭi-cittar)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/tamil-siddhars.md) — (Pāmbāṭṭi-cittar pāṭalkaḷ; Wikipedia (Siddhar))
- **Rajarāja I Bṛhadīśvara bronze commissions** — Rājarāja I builds the Thañjāvūr Bṛhadīśvara temple; its inscriptions record numerous donated metal images (tirumēni) with specified metal-weight, among the earliest dated bronzes _(actors: Rājarāja I Cōḻa; temple donors)_ → [details](../09-artifacts-symbols-and-arts/pancha-loha-bronze-casting.md) — (Bṛhadīśvara/Thañjāvūr Cōḻa inscriptions; Dehejia)
- **Recovery of the Rāmapriya/Selvapiḷḷai utsava-mūrti from Delhi** — Rāmānuja journeys north to retrieve the processional image carried to Delhi among temple plunder and found with the Sultan's daughter _(actors: Rāmānuja, Sultan of Delhi, Selvapiḷḷai/Rāmapriya icon)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/melkote-tirunarayanapuram.md) — (Śrī Vaiṣṇava hagiography)
- **Relocation of Śrīnāthajī from Braj to Mewār (1672)** — The icon is carried by bullock-cart out of Braj (c.1669–70); at Siṅhāḍ the cart sinks and will not move, taken as the deity's choice to settle _(actors: The Tilakāyat (Dāmodaradāsa line), bearers, the deity)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/nathdwara-shrinathji.md) — (Nathdwārā Temple Board history)
- **Relocation of Shrinathji to Nathdwara** — In 1672, fleeing Aurangzeb, the Shrinathji image is moved from Govardhana to Nathdwara in Rajasthan, which becomes the chief Pushtimarg shrine _(actors: Pushtimarg followers)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/nimbarka-vallabha-chaitanya.md) — (Pushtimarg tradition)
- **Rise of Mahavira and the Buddha** — Mahavira (Jainism) and Gautama Buddha flourish c. 6th-5th century BCE, their critique of Vedic ritual and caste reshaping the religious field Hinduism later defines itself against _(actors: Mahavira; Gautama Buddha)_ → [details](../01-manvantaras/manvantara-07-vaivasvata/mahayuga-28-current/04-kali-yuga/recorded-history.md) — (History of India (Wikipedia))
- **Rukuṇā Rath Yātrā of Lingarāja** — On Aśokāṣṭamī the deity is carried in chariot to the Rāmeśvara temple, an Odishan parallel to Purī's Ratha Yātrā _(actors: Lingarāja/Rāmeśvara, Bhuvaneśvarī, devotees)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/lingaraja-bhubaneswar-ekamra-kshetra.md) — (regional temple tradition)
- **Samadhi set at the foot of the temple steps** — Namdev builds Chokhamela's samadhi at the foot of the Vithoba temple steps, so the saint barred in life rests at the threshold every pilgrim touches _(actors: Namdev; Chokhamela)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/chokhamela.md) — (Wikipedia (Chokhamela); Pandharpur hagiography)
- **Samādhi shrine at Ter and pālkhī tradition** — Gorā's samādhi at Ter becomes a Vārkarī pilgrimage point, his palanquin joining the wārī to Pandharpur _(actors: Gorā Kumbhār, Vārkarī pilgrims)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/gora-kumbhar.md) — (Wikipedia, Gora Kumbhar; Vārkarī tradition)
- **Scholarly disambiguation from Sabarimala Ayyappa** — Scholars treat the married, horse-riding Tamil Ayyaṉār-Śāstā and the celibate Keralan Ayyappa as distinct cult-figures later conflated under shared names _(actors: Ayyaṉār-Śāstā; Ayyappa (Naiṣṭhika Brahmacāri); modern scholarship)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/ayyanar-sastha.md) — (Scholarly grāma-devatā studies; comparative reading)
- **Shankara Disappears at Kedarnath** — After his works and tour, Shankara dies at age 32, traditionally disappearing at Kedarnath in the Himalayas _(actors: Adi Shankara)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/adi-shankara.md) — (Wikipedia, Adi Shankara)
- **Sundarar and the slave-deed legend** — At his wedding Śiva, disguised as an old Brahmin, produces a deed proving Sundarar's family were his hereditary bond-slaves; Sundarar accepts the call, abandons the wedding and begins his hymns chiding Śiva _(actors: Sundarar, Śiva)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/the-nalvar-tamil-shaiva-saints.md) — (Wikipedia, Sundarar)
- **Śītalā among the disease-mothers with Jvarāsura** — Śītalā heads the vasanta/pox disease-mother set, attended by Jvarāsura the Fever-Demon and grouped as 'seven sisters', structurally twinning the southern Māriyammaṉ _(actors: Śītalā; Jvarāsura; Olāī Caṇḍī; Māriyammaṉ (counterpart))_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/shitala.md) — (North Indian / Bengali folk tradition)
- **The 18th-century Gaekwad-era temple** — The present Becharājī temple is traditionally said to have been built or rebuilt in the 18th century under Gaekwad (Maratha) patronage, consolidating the older folk seat _(actors: Mānājī Rāo Gaekwad (in temple lore), Becharājī temple establishment)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/bahuchara-mata.md) — (Becharājī / Mehsāṇā temple tradition)
- **The bed of iron spikes becomes flowers** — Sent a bed of iron spikes to kill her, Mira lies down and it becomes a bed of flowers _(actors: Mirabai, the Rana, Krishna)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/mirabai.md) — (Mirabai hagiography (folk))
- **The curtain/jhanki darshan and no-bells seva custom** — Distinctive worship arises: intermittent curtain (jhanki) darshan, no bells or conch, and the Mangala/feet darshan opened only once a year on Akshaya Tritiya _(actors: Banke Bihari deity, Haridasi servitors, devotees)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/banke-bihari-vrindavan-nidhivan.md) — (Haridasi seva tradition [regional])
- **The merger at Udero Lal** — Jhulelal directs a shrine to be built and vanishes/merges into the river, founding the joint mandir-dargah tirtha of Udero Lal _(actors: Jhulelal, the Sindhi community)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/jhulelal.md) — (Udero Lal sthala-tradition)
- **The Śūnya-Purāṇa and Buddhist-survival debate** — Rāmāi Paṇḍit's Śūnya-Purāṇa frames Dharma as Śūnya/Niraṅjan, fueling the modern scholarly thesis (Śāstrī, Dasgupta) that the god is a vestige of Pāla-era Buddhism — a still-disputed claim _(actors: Rāmāi Paṇḍit, Haraprasād Śāstrī, Sashibhusan Dasgupta)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/dharma-thakur.md) — (Śūnya-Purāṇa; Buddhist-survival scholarship)
- **The Saundatti-Yellamma hill cult and jātre** — The Saundatti hill shrine becomes the great pan-Deccan Yellammā center with its annual full-moon jātre and bathing tank _(actors: Yellammā, pilgrims)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/renuka-yellamma.md) — (Saundatti sthala tradition; grāmadevatā studies)
- **The Tiruvaḷḷuvar-sister legend and riddle-poet fame** — Folk legend makes Avvaiyār the sister of Tiruvaḷḷuvar (author of the Tirukkuṟaḷ) among seven siblings turned sages; her popular fame also rests on floating riddle-and-retort verse, both legendary rather than historical _(actors: Avvaiyār; Tiruvaḷḷuvar)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/avvaiyar.md) — (Auvaiyar — Wikipedia; Tirukkuṟaḷ — Wikipedia)
- **Tirumaṅgai Ālvār sings Tiru Siṅgavēḷ Kuṇḍram** — The Ālvār's maṅgalāśāsanam canonizes Ahobilam as the sole Narasiṃha Divya Desam _(actors: Tirumaṅgai Ālvār)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/ahobilam-nava-narasimha.md) — (Nālāyira Divya Prabandham)
- **Tirumala/Tirupati Teppotsavam** — The five-day float festival of Venkaṭeśwara (Malayappa Swāmi) with consorts on the Svāmi Puṣkariṇi at Tirumala, a Vaikhānasa-Āgama float-utsava distinct from the nine-day Brahmotsavam _(actors: Venkaṭeśwara/Malayappa Swāmi, Śrī Devī, Bhū Devī, Sītā-Rāma, Kṛṣṇa, TTD)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/swing-and-float-temple-festivals.md) — (TTD (Tirumala Tirupati Devasthanams); Vaikhānasa Āgama; Wikipedia (Tirumala Venkateswara Temple))
- **Tiruppāṇ Āḻvār carried into the sanctum and merges** — The musician-saint is carried into the sanctum by Lokasāraṅga Muni at the Lord's command, sings the Amalanādipirān, and merges into Raṅganātha _(actors: Tiruppāṇ Āḻvār, Lokasāraṅga Muni, Raṅganātha)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/srirangam-ranganatha.md) — (Nālāyira Divya Prabandham; hagiography)
- **Tukārām and the Tukārām Gāthā** — Tukārām (c.1598/1608–1649/1650 CE) of Dehu, the poetic summit of Vārkarī devotion, composes the thousands of raw Marathi abhaṅgas of the Tukārām Gāthā _(actors: Tukārām (Tukobā))_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/vithoba-vitthal.md) — (Tukārām Gāthā)
- **Tēvāram hymnal glorification** — The 7th–8th-c. Nāyanmār (Sambandar, Appar, Sundarar) sing Aruṇācala in the Tamil Tēvāram, fixing it as a Pāḍal Petṟa Sthalam _(actors: Sambandar, Appar, Sundarar (Nāyanmār))_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/arunachala-tiruvannamalai-fire-hill.md) — (Tēvāram (Tirumuṟai 1–7))
- **Tyāgarāja and the kṛti as nāma-saṅkīrtana** — Carnatic Trinity composer (1767–1847), 700+ Telugu kṛtis, Pañcaratna kṛtis, frames music as singing the Name; signature 'Tyāgarāja' _(actors: Tyāgarāja)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/haridasa-and-carnatic-bhakti.md) — (Biographies of the Carnatic composers)
- **Vallabha Founds Pushtimarg** — Vallabhacharya (1479-1531) revives Shuddhadvaita and founds Pushtimarg, Krishna-worship as the child Shrinathji of Govardhana, seated at Nathdwara _(actors: Vallabha)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/four-vaishnava-sampradayas.md) — (Britannica, Vallabha; Wikipedia)
- **Variant: martyr's death against the Ghaznavids** — In alternative recensions Gugga dies fighting the Turkic/Ghaznavid invaders and is deified as a battle-martyr rather than vanishing into the earth _(actors: Gugga, Turkic/Ghaznavid invaders)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/gugga-gogaji.md) — (Gugga-gāthā (variant))
- **Vādirāja Tīrtha reforms the paryāya to a two-year cycle** — The 16th-c. Sōde pontiff Vādirāja regularises the Aṣṭa Maṭha worship-rotation into a two-year paryāya, completing one full eight-maṭha round every 16 years _(actors: Vādirāja Tīrtha, the Aṣṭa Maṭha pontiffs)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/udupi-krishna-matha.md) — (Aṣṭa Maṭha tradition)
- **Veṅkaṭamakhin establishes the 72-melakarta system** — The Caturdaṇḍī-prakāśikā derives exactly 72 exhaustive parent scales with the kaṭapayādi mnemonic, founding modern Carnatic music _(actors: Veṅkaṭamakhin)_ → [details](../09-artifacts-symbols-and-arts/sangita-classical-music-svara-raga-tala.md) — (Caturdaṇḍī-prakāśikā of Veṅkaṭamakhin (1660))
- **Veneration as Sant Janābāī, foremost early woman Vārkarī poet** — Janābāī is venerated as Sant Janābāī at Pandharpur and Gangākheḍ and fixed in the roster of Marathi and pan-Indian women saints alongside Mīrābāī, Āṇḍāḷ and Karaikkāl Ammaiyār _(actors: Janābāī, Vārkarī tradition)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/janabai.md) — (Wikipedia (Janabai); Wikipedia (Warkari))
- **Vijayanagara–Nāyaka building of the Śrīmuṣṇam Bhū-Varāha temple** — The monumental temple with its carved ratha-maṇḍapa and Nityapuṣkariṇī tank is founded and endowed under Vijayanagara authority and the Tanjore/Senji Nāyakas in the 16th c _(actors: Vijayanagara rulers (Acyutarāya), Tanjore/Senji Nāyakas (Kṛṣṇappa Nāyaka))_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/avatara-kshetras-srikurmam-srimushnam.md) — (Wikipedia Bhuvarahaswamy Temple Srimushnam; South Indian Inscriptions; ASI)
- **Vivekananda founds the Ramakrishna Mission** — Vivekananda founds the Ramakrishna Mission on 1 May 1897, fusing renunciation with social service under the maxim of serving the living being as God _(actors: Swami Vivekananda)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/modern-teachers-and-reform.md) — (Ramakrishna Mission history)
- **Weaving of the Bṛndāvanī Vastra** — Around 1554 Śaṅkaradeva has a ~40-yard tapestry woven depicting Kṛṣṇa's Vṛndāvana līlā, a rare material relic of the movement _(actors: Śaṅkaradeva)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/sankaradeva-and-assamese-vaishnavism.md) — (Wikipedia, Sankardev)
- **Śaṅkara's bhāṣya on the Praśna** — Ādi Śaṅkara composes a commentary on the Praśna, framing it as the prose complement to the verse Muṇḍaka among his ten canonical Upaniṣad bhāṣyas _(actors: Ādi Śaṅkara)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/upanishads/prashna-upanishad.md) — (Śaṅkara, Praśna-Upaniṣad-bhāṣya)
- **Allama Prabhu's awakening at the cave temple** — After his dancer wife Kamalate's death plunges him into grief and wandering, the siddha Animishayya gives Allama a linga and instruction in a cave temple, igniting his enlightenment and his signature 'Guheshvara.' _(actors: Allama Prabhu, Kamalate, Animishayya)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/lingayat-vachana-poets.md) — (Allama Prabhu hagiography)
- **Aśokan and later inscriptions on the Girnar boulder** — A boulder at the hill's foot carries Aśoka's Major Rock Edicts (c. 257 BCE) plus Rudradāman and Skandagupta inscriptions on the Sudarśana lake dam _(actors: Aśoka, Rudradāman, Skandagupta)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/girnar-raivataka-datta-tirtha.md) — (Wikipedia 'Junagadh rock inscription')
- **Aruṇagirinātar's Tiruppugaḻ** — The 15th-c. Tiruvaṇṇāmalai-born saint Aruṇagirinātar, redeemed by the hill's grace, composes the Tiruppugaḻ devotional hymns _(actors: Aruṇagirinātar)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/arunachala-tiruvannamalai-fire-hill.md) — (Tiruppugaḻ)
- **Attributed works Sūr Sārāvalī and Sāhitya Laharī** — Two further works attributed to Sūr — the Sūr Sārāvalī (essence-garland) and the riddle-verse Sāhitya Laharī — of debated authenticity _(actors: Sūrdās)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/surdas.md) — (Sūr Sārāvalī; Sāhitya Laharī; Wikipedia Surdas; Encyclopædia of Indian Literature)
- **Aurobindo withdraws to Pondicherry and founds his Ashram** — Aurobindo withdraws from the freedom struggle to Pondicherry in 1910, develops Integral Yoga aiming at the descent of the Supermind, and co-founds the Sri Aurobindo Ashram in 1926 with the Mother _(actors: Sri Aurobindo, Mirra Alfassa)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/modern-teachers-and-reform.md) — (The Life Divine; Savitri)
- **Bābā Kīnārām founds the Aghorī order** — The Vārāṇasī ascetic Bābā Kīnārām founds the named Aghorī order (the sole surviving Kāpālika-descended line) with its seat at Krīṃ-Kuṇḍ, dying in the latter 18th c _(actors: Bābā Kīnārām)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/shaiva-schools.md) — (Wikipedia, Aghori)
- **Beheading of Bhairavnāth** — At the Holy Cave the Goddess takes her Mahākālī/Caṇḍī form and beheads Bhairavnāth at the threshold _(actors: Vaiṣṇavī (Mahākālī), Bhairavnāth)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/vaishno-devi-trikuta-katra.md) — (Vaiṣṇo Devī sthala legend)
- **Beholding the cosmic dance at Tiruvālaṅkāṭu** — Directed to Tiruvālaṅkāṭu, the skeletal Ammaiyār dances at Śiva's feet beating cymbals, eternally watching His cremation-ground tāṇḍava _(actors: Śiva (Naṭarāja), Kāraikkāl Ammaiyār)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/karaikkal-ammaiyar.md) — (Periya Purāṇam (Cēkkiḻār); Mūtta Tiruppatikam)
- **Bhātkhaṇḍe fixes the 10-thāṭ classification** — V. N. Bhātkhaṇḍe organizes Hindustani rāgas under ten parent thāṭs, a practical counterpart to the South's 72 melakartas _(actors: V. N. Bhātkhaṇḍe)_ → [details](../09-artifacts-symbols-and-arts/sangita-classical-music-svara-raga-tala.md) — (Hindustani Sangeet Paddhati of Bhātkhaṇḍe (early 20th c.))
- **Caitanya Mahāprabhu's Years at Purī** — Caitanya (1486–1534 CE) spent his last ~18 years at Purī absorbed in Jagannātha worship, making it a supreme Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇava tīrtha _(actors: Caitanya Mahāprabhu)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/jagannath-puri-purushottama-kshetra.md) — (Caitanya-caritāmṛta; Caitanya-bhāgavata)
- **Chaitanya's initiation and ecstatic turn at Gaya** — Travelling to Gaya c.1508-09 for his father's shraddha, the scholar Nimai is initiated by the ascetic Ishvara Puri into the Gopala mantra and overtaken by ecstatic Krishna-bhava _(actors: Chaitanya Mahaprabhu, Ishvara Puri)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/nimbarka-vallabha-chaitanya.md) — (Chaitanya-charitamrita; Chaitanya-bhagavata)
- **Composition and transmission of her abhaṅgas** — Her Marathi abhaṅgas, cries to Viṭṭhal as sole protector, are preserved in the Vārkarī Sakal Sant Gāthā corpus _(actors: Kānhopātrā, Vārkarī song-tradition)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/kanhopatra.md) — (Kānhopātrā's abhaṅgas in the Sakal Sant Gāthā)
- **Composition of the Bonbibi Jahuranama** — The dobhasi-Bengali puthi codifying Bonbibi's narrative is composed/printed in the 19th century, ascribed to Munshi Mohammad Khater (and earlier Bayanuddin) _(actors: Munshi Mohammad Khater, Bayanuddin)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/bonbibi.md) — (Bonbibi Jahuranama)
- **Composition of the Dāsbodh** — Dictated the Dāsbodh (20 daśakas × 10 samāsas) as a guru-disciple dialogue on practical Vedānta, dharma and statecraft, traditionally at Śivthar Ghaḷ _(actors: Rāmdās; disciple Kalyāṇ)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/samarth-ramdas.md) — (Dāsbodh; Wikipedia (Dasbodh); Hindupedia)
- **Concealment and Maratha restoration of the image** — The image is concealed during medieval invasions and worship restored under Maratha (Kolhapur Chhatrapati) patronage _(actors: Maratha rulers of Kolhapur)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/kolhapur-mahalakshmi-karavira.md) — (Wikipedia: Mahalaxmi Temple, Kolhapur)
- **Creation of the bhāruḍ folk-dramas** — Eknāth originates the bhāruḍ — allegorical, comic Marathi folk-songs/street-dramas using everyday characters to teach spirituality, democratizing scripture for the unlettered, alongside abhaṅgas and gauḷaṇī _(actors: Eknāth)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/eknath.md) — (bhāruḍ corpus; Wikipedia Eknath)
- **Critical edition of the vākhs (Grierson & Barnett)** — After ~400 years of oral transmission the vākhs are collected and edited as Lallā-vākyāni by Grierson and Barnett (1920), establishing the standard scholarly text _(actors: George Grierson; Lionel D. Barnett)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/lalleshwari-lal-ded.md) — (Grierson & Barnett, Lallā-vākyāni (1920))
- **Crystallization of the pālkhī Wārī processions** — In the 18th c. Haibatrāo organizes the pālkhī foot-processions carrying the sants' pādukās (Jñāneśvar from Āḷandī, Tukārām from Dehu) into the modern Pandharpur Wārī drawing up to a million pilgrims _(actors: Haibatrāo (Haibatbābā); Vārkarī pilgrims)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/vithoba-vitthal.md) — (Pandharpur Wārī living tradition)
- **Deification as Jāharvīr nāg-devtā** — Through his death Gugga becomes the manifest hero and serpent-lord who protects devotees from snakebite, venerated at the Gogāmeḍī shrine across Hindu, Muslim and Sikh communities _(actors: Gugga (Jāharvīr / Zāhir Pīr), bhopā mediums and jogīs)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/gugga-gogaji.md) — (Gogāmeḍī sthala tradition; Gugga Naumī observance)
- **Ādi Śaṅkara Consecrates the Śrī-Cakra before Kāmākṣī** — Ādi Śaṅkara pacifies the fierce Kāmākṣī by consecrating a Śrī-Cakra before her in the cavity under the central shrine, seating her in yogic calm and founding a living Śrī-Vidyā seat _(actors: Ādi Śaṅkara, Kāmākṣī)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/kanchipuram-city-of-temples.md) — (kamakoti.org; Kamakshi tradition)
- **Ādi Śaṅkara Installs Chaṇḍī Devī Image** — Ādi Śaṅkarācārya is said to install the main image of Chaṇḍī Devī on Nīl Parvat in the 8th century _(actors: Ādi Śaṅkarācārya)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/haridwar-rishikesh-ganga-dwara.md) — (tourmyindia; Wikipedia)
- **Enshrinement of Bībī/Tuḷukka Nācciyār** — The Sultan's daughter follows the recovered icon to Melkote out of devotion, dies of separation, and is enshrined at the deity's feet _(actors: Bībī/Tuḷukka Nācciyār, Rāmapriya deity, Rāmānuja)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/melkote-tirunarayanapuram.md) — (guruparamparā; local tradition)
- **Founding of the Ahobila Maṭha (c. 1398 CE)** — Narasiṃha grants the Mālōla icon to the first Jīyar, founding the itinerant Śrīvaiṣṇava Ahobila Maṭha _(actors: Narasiṃha, Ādivaṇ Śaṭhakōpa Yatīndra Mahādēśikan (first Jīyar))_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/ahobilam-nava-narasimha.md) — (Ahobila Maṭha tradition)
- **Founding of the Kabir Panth** — After Kabir's death the Sant-mat sect crystallizes in the 17th-18th c. revering him as Satguru and divine incarnation, splitting into the Kabir Chaura and Dharmadasi/Chhattisgarh branches _(actors: Kabir's followers, Dharmadas)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/kabir.md) — (Anurag Sagar; Sukh Nidhan (late Panthi texts))
- **Gopīcand's renunciation** — King Gopīcand is persuaded by his mother Maināvatī to renounce and is initiated by Jālandhara/Hāḍipā; the Bhartṛhari–Gopīcand ballads become the chief Nāth epics _(actors: Gopīcand, Maināvatī, Jālandhara/Hāḍipā)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/nath-sampradaya-and-the-siddhas.md) — (Bhartṛhari–Gopīcand ballads; Nāth hagiography)
- **Heliodorus Pillar Erected** — The Greek devotee Heliodorus erects a Garuḍa-stambha to Vāsudeva at Besnagar c. 113 BCE, the earliest dated physical witness to Garuḍa-Vaiṣṇava cult _(actors: Heliodorus)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/garuda.md) — (Heliodorus pillar inscription)
- **Historical elaboration of the temple complex** — From the Sātavāhana–Ikṣvāku era cult near Nāgārjunakoṇḍa, the standing complex (prākāra wall, gopuras, galleries) is built up under the Reḍḍi, Kākatīya, and Vijayanagara dynasties _(actors: Sātavāhana/Ikṣvāku, Reḍḍi, Kākatīya, Vijayanagara dynasties; Vīraśaiva-Liṅgāyata devotees)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/srisailam-srisaila-mallikarjuna-bhramaramba.md) — (Epigraphy and temple history; Vīraśaiva maṭha tradition)
- **Hymns enshrined in the Bhaktamāl and Gurū Granth Sāhib** — Ravidās features in Nābhādās's Bhaktamāl (c. 1600) and ~40 of his hymns are compiled into the Gurū Granth Sāhib by Gurū Arjan Dev in 1604 _(actors: Ravidās, Nābhādās, Gurū Arjan Dev)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/ravidas.md) — (Bhaktamāl; Gurū Granth Sāhib; Wikipedia Ravidas, Bhaktamal)
- **Installation at Nathdwārā under Rāṇā Rāj Siṃh** — Mahārāṇā Rāj Siṃh I of Mewār grants asylum; the havelī is built at renamed Nāthdvāra and the icon installed in 1672 CE (V.S. 1728) _(actors: Mahārāṇā Rāj Siṃh I, the Tilakāyat, Śrīnāthajī)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/nathdwara-shrinathji.md) — (Mewār–Nathdwārā history)
- **Institution of Cheti Chand** — Jhulelal's appearance is commemorated as Cheti Chand, the Sindhi New Year and his birthday, with the bahirano procession and river-immersion _(actors: The Sindhi Hindu community)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/jhulelal.md) — (Cheti Chand (Chaiti Chand) observance)
- **Jogti/jogappa and contested devadasi dedication** — Dedicated jogti/jogappa orders and the legally banned but persistent devadasi dedication develop around Saundatti-Yellammā _(actors: jogti, jogappa, devadasis, Yellammā)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/renuka-yellamma.md) — (Jogti/jogappa and devadasi ethnography; Karnataka Devadasis Act 1982)
- **Kali-yuga dynastic chronicle (Maurya–Gupta)** — Future-tense king-lists of Bārhadratha, Pradyota, Śiśunāga, Nanda, Maurya, Śuṅga, Kāṇva, Andhra/Sātavāhana and Gupta dynasties — datable historical memory recast as prophecy _(actors: Mauryas, Śuṅgas, Sātavāhanas, Guptas)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/vayu-purana.md) — (Vāyu Purāṇa; Brahmāṇḍa/Matsya/Viṣṇu Purāṇas [scholarly])
- **Karuvūrār and the Bṛhadīśvara consecration** — The Siddhar-poet Karuvūr-tēvar is associated with the Chola Tanjore Bṛhadīśvara temple, his Tiruvicaippā hymns entering Tirumuṟai Book 9 _(actors: Karuvūrār; Chola court)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/tamil-siddhars.md) — (Tirumuṟai tradition; Wikipedia (Siddhar))
- **Koch rebuilding of 1565 CE** — The destroyed temple is rebuilt by Koch king Naranārāyaṇa and Chilarāi in 1565 CE in the nilachala-dome form _(actors: Naranārāyaṇa, Chilarāi)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/kamakhya-nilachala-kamarupa.md) — (Wikipedia, Kamakhya Temple (historical record))
- **Lakhindar dies of snakebite** — Manasā sends the serpent Kālnāgini through the pinhole and Lakhindar is fatally bitten on his wedding night _(actors: Manasā, Kālnāgini, Lakhindar, Behulā)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/manasa.md) — (Manasāmaṅgal)
- **Lalla** — Eighth-century author of the Śiṣyadhīvṛddhida-tantra, a major post-Brahmagupta siddhānta on planetary theory and eclipse correction _(actors: Lalla)_ → [details](../00-time-and-cosmology/siddhantic-astronomy-and-astronomers.md) — (Śiṣyadhīvṛddhida-tantra)
- **Later life, children, and death** — She bears children (traditionally a son Viṭhobā), keeps composing, and dies c. 1700, reputedly foretelling her own death in verse _(actors: Bahiṇābāī)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/bahinabai.md) — (Wikipedia 'Bahina Bai'; Feldhaus)
- **Madhva proclaims himself the third Vayu-avatara** — Madhva proclaims himself the third incarnation of Vayu after Hanuman and Bhima, sent to restore right doctrine, and brands Advaita 'crypto-Buddhism.' _(actors: Madhva)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/ramanuja-and-madhva.md) — (Madhva works)
- **Medieval temple-building and Pañcārāma grouping** — The five shrines are built/expanded under Eastern Cālukya, Cōḻa and Kākatīya patronage (9th–13th c. CE) and later bundled as a single fivefold set; Śrīnātha glorifies Drākṣārāma in 15th-c. Telugu _(actors: Eastern Cālukyas (Bhīma I), Cōḻas, Kākatīyas, the poet Śrīnātha)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/pancharama-kshetras-andhra.md) — (Inscriptions; Bhīmeśvara Purāṇamu (Śrīnātha); scholarly history)
- **Millennium celebration and UNESCO living-temple status** — Continuous worship for ~1000 years culminates in the 2010 millennium festivities; UNESCO inscribes it as a Great Living Chola Temple _(actors: Tamil Nadu state; temple community; UNESCO)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/brihadeeswarar-thanjavur.md) — (UNESCO World Heritage documentation)
- **Mirabai merges into the Krishna image at Dwarka** — In old age Mira travels to Dwarka and, when Mewar emissaries come to summon her home, embraces the Ranchhor-ray image and merges bodily into the Krishna-murti, leaving only her garment _(actors: Mirabai, Dwarkadhish)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/mirabai.md) — (Mirabai hagiography (folk))
- **Modern reclamation as Mahar/Dalit icon** — Chokhamela becomes a central symbol of Mahar self-respect in 19th-20th-c. Maharashtra, standing at the hinge between bhakti-era devotion and Ambedkar-era Dalit assertion _(actors: Mahar community; B. R. Ambedkar movement)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/chokhamela.md) — (Zelliot, From Untouchable to Dalit)
- **Muttusvāmi Dīkṣitar's Sanskrit kṛtis** — Trinity composer (1775–1835), grand slow Sanskrit kṛtis, Navagraha and Kamalāmbā Navāvaraṇa cycles, nottu-svara; signature 'Guruguha' _(actors: Muttusvāmi Dīkṣitar)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/haridasa-and-carnatic-bhakti.md) — (Biographies of the Carnatic composers)
- **Muṉīśvaraṉ in the Malaysia/Singapore diaspora** — Muṉīśvaraṉ temples become major Tamil community shrines in Malaysia and Singapore, the god travelling as protector of estate and urban communities _(actors: Muṉīśvaraṉ/Muṉiyāṇṭi, diaspora Tamil community, pūcāri)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/karuppasamy-madurai-veeran-and-tamil-kaval-deities.md) — (Diaspora (Malaysia/Singapore) temple practice)
- **Mylapore Kapālīśwarar Theppam** — The Chennai Kapālīśwarar (Śiva) temple's float festival, with processional deities floated over three nights on the Mylapore tank in the month of Thai — a metropolitan Śaiva instance of the genre _(actors: Kapālīśwarar (Śiva) processional deities, Mylapore devotees)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/swing-and-float-temple-festivals.md) — (Wikipedia (Kapaleeshwarar Temple); Chennai temple festival calendar)
- **Nandanar Enters the Chidambaram Sanctum** — The untouchable Pulaiyar labourer-saint Nandanar enters the Chidambaram sanctum, becoming an emblem of bhakti dissolving caste barriers _(actors: Nandanar)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/alvars-and-nayanars.md) — (Nayanars — Wikipedia)
- **Nimbarka Pioneers Joint Radha-Krishna Worship** — Nimbarka, head of the Kumara Sampradaya and an amsha of the Sudarshana cakra, settles at Vrindavana and pioneers the joint worship of Radha-Krishna under the Dvaitadvaita school _(actors: Nimbarka)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/four-vaishnava-sampradayas.md) — (Wikipedia, Nimbarka Sampradaya)
- **Nivṛttināth's samādhi at Tryambakeśvar** — After Jñāneśvar's samādhi the eldest brother goes to Tryambakeśvar near Nashik and takes samādhi c. 1297; his shrine there is a Vārkarī pilgrimage site _(actors: Nivṛttināth)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/nivritti-sopan-muktabai.md) — (Wikipedia (Nivruttinath))
- **Northern journey to the Punjab** — Nāmdev travels north and spends years in the Punjab (village Ghuman), bridging Marathi Viṭṭhal-bhakti into the north-Indian Sant-mat world _(actors: Nāmdev)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/namdev.md) — (Wikipedia Namdev [traditional])
- **Patronage of the hijra community and the nirvāṇa rite** — Bahucharā becomes chief patron of the hijra community; the ritual emasculation (nirvāṇa) is performed under her invocation and hijras make pilgrimage to Becharājī as her devotees _(actors: Bahucharā Mātā, the hijra community)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/bahuchara-mata.md) — (Hijra devotional tradition; Serena Nanda, Neither Man nor Woman)
- **Perpetual sandalwood coating ritual** — The fierce ugra-Narasiṃha icon is kept permanently encased in cooling sandalwood paste, appearing as a liṅga-like mound year-round _(actors: Simhachalam Devasthanam priests)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/simhachalam-varaha-narasimha.md) — (Wikipedia, Simhachalam; templepurohit.com)
- **Pochamma the smallpox-and-fertility mother** — The beloved Telugu village mother who sends and cures pox and guards children, sister to Māriyammaṉ and Śītalā _(actors: Pochamma; child devotees)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/gramadevatas-village-deities.md) — (Telugu grāmadevatā ethnography)
- **Redaction of the Mahabharata** — The Mahabharata is redacted c. 400 BCE-400 CE (traditionally composed by Vyasa at the Dvapara/Kali cusp recording events of ~3102 BCE) _(actors: Vyasa)_ → [details](../01-manvantaras/manvantara-07-vaivasvata/mahayuga-28-current/04-kali-yuga/recorded-history.md) — (Mahabharata)
- **Redaction of the Ramayana** — Valmiki Ramayana core composed c. 500 BCE-200 CE (scholarly); tradition places events in Treta Yuga _(actors: Valmiki (traditional))_ → [details](../01-manvantaras/manvantara-07-vaivasvata/mahayuga-28-current/04-kali-yuga/recorded-history.md) — (Ramayana)
- **Rāmānuja makes Śrīraṅgam the Śrī Vaiṣṇava capital** — Rāmānuja reorganizes the temple's worship and administration, establishing it as the headquarters of Viśiṣṭādvaita Śrī Vaiṣṇavism _(actors: Rāmānuja)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/srirangam-ranganatha.md) — (Koil Oḻugu; hagiography)
- **Seasonal winter shift of worship to Pāṇḍukeśwar/Joshimath** — Each winter, when the snow-locked shrine closes, the festival image descends to Yogadhyān Badrī at Pāṇḍukeśwar (and the Joshimath maṭha) so worship is never dark _(actors: Badrī-Nārāyaṇa utsava-mūrti, temple priesthood)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/badrinath-badarikashrama-kshetra.md) — (Wikipedia (Sapta Badri); temple tradition)
- **Seasonal worship and winter idol-shift** — The snow-locked high shrine opens Akṣaya-tṛtīyā to Kārttika; in winter the utsava-mūrti shifts to Omkareśvara Temple at Ukhimaṭh _(actors: Kedarnath temple priests; pilgrims)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/kedarnath-kedara-kshetra.md) — (Wikipedia, Panch Kedar; Chota Char Dham)
- **Sectarian commentaries and daily recitation** — Later Vaiṣṇava ācāryas (Parāśara Bhaṭṭa, Madhva tradition) gloss the names; the hymn becomes the most-recited Vaiṣṇava/Smārta daily stotra _(actors: Parāśara Bhaṭṭa, Vaiṣṇava and Smārta reciters, M. S. Subbulakshmi)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/stotras/vishnu-sahasranama.md) — (Bhagavad-guṇa-darpaṇa; living recitation tradition)
- **Sembiyaṉ Mahādevī's bronze & temple patronage** — The 10th-c. Cōḻa queen donates temples and bronze icons across the Kāveri delta, a key dated horizon in the bronze sequence _(actors: Queen Sembiyaṉ Mahādevī; Tiruvārūr/delta temples)_ → [details](../09-artifacts-symbols-and-arts/pancha-loha-bronze-casting.md) — (Dehejia, Chola: Sacred Bronzes; Cōḻa inscriptions)
- **Smallpox eradication and the cult's survival** — After the WHO declared smallpox eradicated in 1980 the Śītalā cult broadened to cover chickenpox, measles, heat-fevers and child-protection rather than dying out _(actors: Śītalā; modern devotees; (WHO eradication context))_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/shitala.md) — (anthropology of religion [scholarly])
- **Sundarar blinded and his sight restored** — Married to Paravai and Caṅkili, Sundarar breaks his oath and is struck blind, gradually regaining sight through penitent hymns; Śiva acts as go-between to reconcile him with Paravai _(actors: Sundarar, Paravai, Caṅkili, Śiva)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/the-nalvar-tamil-shaiva-saints.md) — (Wikipedia, Sundarar)
- **The 2013 Kedarnath Flash Floods** — Catastrophic June 2013 flash floods devastate the Kedarnath valley while the temple itself survives → [details](../06-sacred-geography/char-dham-and-chota-char-dham.md) — (Wikipedia, Chota Char Dham)
- **The Belaguri debate and conversion of Madhavadeva** — Once a Śākta sacrificer, Madhavadeva is converted to single-refuge bhakti by Śaṅkaradeva at the famous Belaguri debate (maṇi-kāñcana saṃyoga), adding Guru as a cāri-vastu principle _(actors: Śaṅkaradeva, Madhavadeva, Gayāpāṇi/Rāmadāsa)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/sankaradeva-and-assamese-vaishnavism.md) — (Wikipedia, Madhavdev, Ekasarana Dharma)
- **The Cāṅgadeva (wall-riding) episode and Pāsaṣṭī** — The proud yogī Cāṅgadeva arrives on a tiger; the siblings ride a moving mud wall to meet him, and Jñāneśvar answers his blank letter with the 65-verse Cāṅgadeva Pāsaṣṭī, humbling him into discipleship _(actors: Jñāneśvar, Cāṅgadeva, Muktābāī, the siblings)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/jnaneshwar.md) — (Cāṅgadeva Pāsaṣṭī; Wikipedia (Changdev); Vārkarī hagiography [folk])
- **The living Amarnāth Yātrā** — One of India's largest pilgrimages, via Pahalgām and Baltāl routes, culminating on Śrāvaṇa Pūrṇimā with the chaḍī mubarak procession _(actors: Pilgrims (yātrīs); Shri Amarnathji Shrine Board)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/amarnath-ice-lingam-and-amar-katha.md) — (Living Amarnāth Yātrā tradition; Nīlamata Purāṇa & Rājataraṅgiṇī (early footprint))
- **The Paryāya Mahotsava handover** — Every changeover year on Makara Saṅkrānti the incoming maṭha svāmī receives the akṣaya pātra and ritual charge of the kṣetra in Udupi's greatest festival _(actors: Aṣṭa Maṭha pontiffs)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/udupi-krishna-matha.md) — (Udupi calendar; Aṣṭa Maṭha records)
- **The rājagopuram as the Tamil Nadu state emblem** — Srivilliputhur's eleven-tier rājagopuram, raised under medieval Pāṇḍya/Nāyaka patronage, becomes the emblem on the Government of Tamil Nadu state symbol _(actors: Pāṇḍya/Nāyaka patrons, Government of Tamil Nadu)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/srivilliputhur-vatapatrasayi-andal-kshetra.md) — (Temple tradition; Government of Tamil Nadu)
- **The Srikurmam Kurma-kshetra cult** — At Srikurmam in Andhra Pradesh the tortoise form is the presiding deity; theologised as the Kurma-kshetra and tied by tradition to Ramanuja's Vaishnava reform _(actors: Vishnu-Kurma, Sri Vaishnava tradition, Ramanuja)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/kurma-avatara.md) — (Sthala-purana; temple history)
- **Tukaram's manuscripts float back from the Indrayani** — The Shudra grocer-sant Tukaram of Dehu, emotional apex of the Varkari tradition, sees his abhanga manuscripts drowned in the Indrayani river float back intact, and is traditionally taken bodily to Vaikuntha at his death _(actors: Tukaram)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/north-bhakti-sants.md) — (Tukaram Gatha)
- **Ujjaini Mahakali temple founded (Bonalu charter)** — In 1813 a Hyderabad battalion at Ujjain vowed to install Mahakali's idol if she lifted a plague back home; on deliverance they founded the Ujjaini Mahakali temple, origin of the Bonalu cycle _(actors: Mahakali, Hyderabad battalion)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/regional-temple-festivals.md) — (Wikipedia, Bonalu; Pragyata)
- **Vāgbhaṭa's synthesis of the two schools** — Vāgbhaṭa harmonizes the Caraka and Suśruta traditions into the Aṣṭāṅga Saṅgraha and Aṣṭāṅga Hṛdaya, completing the Bṛhat-trayī _(actors: Vāgbhaṭa)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/ayurveda-and-the-science-of-life.md) — (Aṣṭāṅga Hṛdaya; Aṣṭāṅga Saṅgraha)
- **Vishnupad Temple Rebuilt by Ahalyābāī Holkar** — The Maratha queen Ahalyābāī Holkar of Indore rebuilds the octagonal Vishnupad temple enclosing Viṣṇu's footprint in its present form in 1787 CE _(actors: Ahalyābāī Holkar)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/gaya-pitru-tirtha.md) — (Wikipedia, Vishnupad Temple)
- **Worship as Kūttāṇṭavar and the Kūvākam festival** — Aravān is enshrined in Tamil Nadu as Kūttāṇṭavar (a severed-head icon); the eighteen-day Kūvākam (Koovagam) festival re-enacts the marriage, sacrifice, and widowhood, and is the great gathering of the Aravāṇi/Thirunaṅgai community _(actors: Aravān (Kūttāṇṭavar), Aravāṇi/Thirunaṅgai community)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/aravan-iravan.md) — (Kūttāṇṭavar temple tradition (Kūvākam, Villupuram, Tamil Nadu))
- **2013 flood survival** — The temple survives the catastrophic June 2013 Kedarnath flash floods that devastated the valley, a boulder credited with diverting the water _(actors: Kedarnath temple; pilgrims)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/kedarnath-kedara-kshetra.md) — (Wikipedia, Chota Char Dham / Kedarnath Temple)
- **Abhinavagupta canonizes śānta as the ninth rasa** — Abhinavagupta's Abhinavabhāratī expounds rasa as disinterested aesthetic bliss and champions śānta-rasa to complete the nava-rasa _(actors: Abhinavagupta)_ → [details](../09-artifacts-symbols-and-arts/natya-shastra-and-the-classical-arts.md) — (Abhinavabhāratī (c. 1000 CE))
- **Śaṅkaradeva's death at Bheladuar** — Śaṅkaradeva dies (traditionally 1568) at Bheladuar in Cooch Behar under Koch king Naranārāyaṇa's patronage _(actors: Śaṅkaradeva, Naranārāyaṇa)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/sankaradeva-and-assamese-vaishnavism.md) — (Wikipedia, Sankardev)
- **Śaṅkara's bhāṣya on the Muṇḍaka** — Ādi Śaṅkara (c. 8th c. CE) composes a commentary treating the Atharvan trio together, making the parā/aparā distinction central to Advaita epistemology _(actors: Ādi Śaṅkara, Ānandagiri)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/upanishads/mundaka-upanishad.md) — (Śaṅkara, Muṇḍakopaniṣad-bhāṣya)
- **Allama Prabhu merges with the linga at Kadalivana** — Allama Prabhu is said to have become one with the linga at Kadalivana near Srisailam _(actors: Allama Prabhu)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/lingayat-vachana-poets.md) — (Allama Prabhu hagiography (traditional))
- **Ascribed authorship of the Nandikeśvara Kāśikā and Abhinaya-darpaṇa** — Nandikeśvara is the named speaker of the Nandikeśvara Kāśikā (Māheśvara Sūtras) and traditional author of the Abhinaya-darpaṇa, the post-classical dance-gesture grammar _(actors: Nandikeśvara, Upamanyu (commentator))_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/nandi-and-shaiva-mounts.md) — (Nandikeśvara Kāśikā; Abhinaya-darpaṇa; Bharatārṇava)
- **Baladeva Vidyabhushana Wins the Galta Debate** — Baladeva Vidyabhushana composes the Govinda-bhashya and Prameya-ratnavali (18th c.) to win the Gaudiyas a recognized seat at the Galta (Jaipur) debate, bridging them to Madhva _(actors: Baladeva Vidyabhushana)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/four-vaishnava-sampradayas.md) — (iskconcommunications.org)
- **Basava founds the Liṅgāyat tradition** — Basava (c. 1105–1167), treasurer under Kalachuri king Bijjala II, organizes the Liṅgāyat/Vīraśaiva bhakti movement rejecting caste, ritual sacrifice and priestly mediation _(actors: Basava, Bijjala II)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/shaiva-schools.md) — (Wikipedia, Basava)
- **Behulā's raft-journey with the corpse** — Refusing to abandon him, Behulā floats down-river on a raft with Lakhindar's decaying body to reach the gods _(actors: Behulā, Lakhindar)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/manasa.md) — (Manasāmaṅgal)
- **Bhāskara II and the Siddhānta Śiromaṇi (1150 CE)** — Summit of classical astronomy (Līlāvatī, Bījagaṇita, etc.); anticipates differential calculus with instantaneous planetary velocity and the cakravāla solution of Pell's equation _(actors: Bhāskara II (Bhāskarācārya))_ → [details](../00-time-and-cosmology/siddhantic-astronomy-and-astronomers.md) — (Siddhānta Śiromaṇi)
- **Buddha's Enlightenment at Bodh Gayā** — Gautama Buddha attains enlightenment under the Bodhi tree beside the Phalgu at Bodh Gayā, the Buddhist pole of the Gayā district _(actors: Gautama Buddha)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/gaya-pitru-tirtha.md) — (Wikipedia, Bodh Gaya; Britannica)
- **Cekkilar Versifies the Periya Puranam** — Cekkilar (c. 1135 CE) versifies the hagiographies of the 63 Nayanars in the Periya Puranam, added as Book 12 to complete the Tirumurai canon _(actors: Cekkilar)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/alvars-and-nayanars.md) — (Periya Puranam; Tirumurai)
- **Chaitanya launches sankirtana at Navadvipa** — Chaitanya launches nightly congregational sankirtana of the Hare Krishna maha-mantra with Nityananda and Advaita Acharya at Shrivasa's courtyard, inaugurating mass Gaudiya devotion _(actors: Chaitanya, Nityananda, Advaita Acharya)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/nimbarka-vallabha-chaitanya.md) — (Chaitanya-charitamrita)
- **Chandanotsavam — annual Nijarūpa Darśanam** — On Akṣaya Tṛtīyā the paste is removed and the true carved Varāha-Narasiṃha form is revealed for about twelve hours before being re-coated in four annual installments _(actors: Simhachalam Devasthanam; pilgrims)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/simhachalam-varaha-narasimha.md) — (Wikipedia, Simhachalam; Devasthanam tradition)
- **Charter of śālagrāma–tulasī worship and Tulsī-Vivāha** — The episode grounds the joint śālagrāma–tulasī pūjā, the Tulsī-Vivāha festival on Prabodhinī Ekādaśī, and the conch's place in Vaiṣṇava worship _(actors: Viṣṇu/śālagrāma, Tulasī/tulasī plant, devotees)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/shankhachuda-tulasi.md) — (Brahma-Vaivarta Purāṇa; Padma Purāṇa; festival tradition)
- **Composition of Manāche Ślok and Mārutī stotras** — Wrote the 205-verse Manāche Ślok (Manobodh) counselling the mind, and the Bhīm-rūpī Mārutī Stotra and other Hanumān hymns _(actors: Rāmdās)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/samarth-ramdas.md) — (Manāche Ślok (Manobodh); Bhīm-rūpī Mārutī Stotra)
- **Composition of Marathi abhaṅgas forming the Vārkarī canon** — Nāmdev's Marathi abhaṅgas, with the Jñāneśvarī and Tukārām Gāthā, become the core scriptural canon of the Vārkarī tradition _(actors: Nāmdev)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/namdev.md) — (Marathi abhaṅgas; Wikipedia Warkari, Namdev)
- **Composition of the four Tirumuṟai Book 11 works** — She composes the Aṟputat Tiruvantāti (100 vv), Tiru Iraṭṭai Maṇimālai (20 vv), and two Mūtta Tiruppatikams (~22-23 vv), ~143 verses in Tirumuṟai Book 11 _(actors: Kāraikkāl Ammaiyār)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/karaikkal-ammaiyar.md) — (Tirumuṟai Book 11 (Aṟputat Tiruvantāti, Tiru Iraṭṭai Maṇimālai, Mūtta Tiruppatikam I & II))
- **Ādi Śaṅkara Founds the Kāñcī Kāmakoṭi Pīṭha** — By the Kāñcī matha's tradition, Ādi Śaṅkara founds the Kāmakoṭi Pīṭha at Kāñcī and attains his final siddhi there _(actors: Ādi Śaṅkara)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/kanchipuram-city-of-temples.md) — (kamakoti.org)
- **Dispersal of the Seven Houses across Rajasthan** — The same Aurangzeb-era flight scatters the other Puṣṭimārga deities — Dvārkādhīśa to Kankroli, Mathureśa to Kota, etc. — across Rajasthan _(actors: The Seven Houses (Sāt Gharānā) custodians, Rājput patrons)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/nathdwara-shrinathji.md) — (Puṣṭimārga tradition; Rajasthan temple histories)
- **Ekvīrā as Maharashtrian kuladevī** — At Karla and Māhūr Reṇukā is worshipped as Ekvīrā/Reṇukā-Mātā, kuladevī of the CKP, Agri-Koli and Mahar communities _(actors: Ekvīrā/Reṇukā, CKP, Agri-Koli, Mahar communities)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/renuka-yellamma.md) — (Karla Ekvīrā sthala tradition; Maharashtrian Śakti-pīṭha tradition)
- **Flying severed head and redemptive boon** — Bhairavnāth's severed head flies ~3 km up the mountain to the Bhairavnāth shrine; repenting, he is forgiven and granted that no yātrā is complete without visiting his shrine _(actors: Bhairavnāth, Vaiṣṇo Devī)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/vaishno-devi-trikuta-katra.md) — (Vaiṣṇo Devī sthala legend; regional variant)
- **Formation of the Siddha-vaidya medical system** — The Siddhars develop Siddha medicine—mineral/metal-based therapy, varma vital-points and nāḍi pulse diagnosis—as a distinct Tamil medical science _(actors: Agathiyar, Bhogar, Koṅkaṇar, Pulippāṇi, Dhanvantari and other Siddhars)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/tamil-siddhars.md) — (Siddha-vaidya corpus; Wikipedia (Siddha medicine))
- **Founding of the Yatirāja Maṭha** — Rāmānuja, titled Yatirāja, establishes the Yatirāja Maṭha at Melkote as a seat of his order and paramparā transmission _(actors: Rāmānuja)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/melkote-tirunarayanapuram.md) — (Śrī Vaiṣṇava guruparamparā)
- **Gahininath transmits the Nāth current to the Vārkarīs** — Maharashtrian Nāth Gahininath initiates Nivṛttināth, channeling the Nāth lineage into Jñāneśvar's Vārkarī movement _(actors: Gahininath, Nivṛttināth, Jñāneśvar)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/nath-sampradaya-and-the-siddhas.md) — (Navnāth Bhaktisār; Wikipedia (Navnath))
- **Hiḍimbā Devī cult at Manali** — Hiḍimbā is later worshipped as a Kullu-valley goddess with the 1553 CE Dhungri cedar-wood temple, recast from rākṣasī to tutelary mountain deity and royal ancestress _(actors: Hiḍimbā Devī, Himachali devotees and royal line)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/hidimba-hidimbi.md) — (Hidimba Devi Temple tradition (regional/folk))
- **Independent knowledge-cult of Hayagrīva** — Hayagrīva worshipped as patron of learning in Śrī-Vaiṣṇava and Tantric traditions, with shrines at Tirumala and Chettipunyam and Vedānta Deśika's Hayagrīva Stotra _(actors: Hayagrīva (cult deity), students/scholars, Vedānta Deśika)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/hayagriva.md) — (Hayagrīva Upaniṣad; Śrī-Vaiṣṇava temple tradition; Vedānta Deśika's Hayagrīva Stotra)
- **Institution of Chaliho Sahib** — The forty-day Chaliho penance is kept in gratitude, re-enacting the founding forty-day Indus prayer _(actors: Sindhi devotees)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/jhulelal.md) — (Chaliho Sahib observance)
- **Jayarashi composes the Tattvopaplavasimha** — Jayarashi Bhatta writes a radically skeptical Lokayata-linked work undermining the validity of every pramana (c. 8th c.
