CE) _(actors: Jayarashi Bhatta)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/nastika-darshanas-and-the-astika-nastika-frame.md) — (Tattvopaplavasimha)
- **Joint enshrinement of the kāval cluster at the village edge** — Karuppasāmi, Maturai-Vīraṉ, Muṉīśvaraṉ and Sudalaimāḍaṉ are grouped as a hot male guardian-ring around a presiding ammaṉ such as Māriyammaṉ, served by non-Brahman pūcāri with blood-sacrifice and possession-dance _(actors: Karuppasāmi, Maturai-Vīraṉ, Muṉīśvaraṉ, Sudalaimāḍaṉ, Māriyammaṉ, pūcāri, cāmiyāṭi)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/karuppasamy-madurai-veeran-and-tamil-kaval-deities.md) — (Tamil grāmadevatā / kāval-deyvam tradition)
- **Kapila's āśrama and the Makar Saṅkrānti melā at Sāgara-dvīpa** — The Kapil Muni temple marks the hermitage; the annual Makar Saṅkrānti Gaṅgāsāgar Melā draws millions, encoded in the proverb 'Sab tīrtha bār bār, Gaṅgāsāgar ek bār' _(actors: Pilgrims, Kapil Muni cult)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/gangasagar-sagara-dvipa-kapila-ashram.md) — (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa / Kapila tradition; sthala-purāṇa; folk proverb)
- **Later invocation as brahmavādinī exemplars** — Gārgī and Maitreyī become the canonical names cited for women's access to Vedic learning and brahmavidyā, commented on by Ādi Śaṅkara and invoked in modern debates and institutions _(actors: Gārgī Vācaknavī, Maitreyī, Ādi Śaṅkara, later commentators and reformers)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/gargi-and-maitreyi.md) — (Ādi Śaṅkara, Bṛhadāraṇyaka-bhāṣya; Wikipedia (Gargi Vachaknavi, Maitreyi))
- **Maurya Empire & Ashoka** — First pan-Indian empire c. 322-185 BCE; Ashoka patronizes Buddhism and issues rock/pillar edicts _(actors: Chandragupta, Bindusara, Ashoka)_ → [details](../01-manvantaras/manvantara-07-vaivasvata/mahayuga-28-current/04-kali-yuga/recorded-history.md) — (History of India)
- **Mirabai and Jiva Gosvami at Vrindavana** — Told that the ascetic Jiva Gosvami would not meet a woman, Mira retorts that Krishna is the only true male in Vrindavana and all else are gopis, whereupon the abashed scholar comes out to meet her _(actors: Mirabai, Jiva Gosvami)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/mirabai.md) — (Gaudiya transmission (folk))
- **Naraka-caturdaśī / Dīpāvalī aetiology** — The slaying of Naraka becomes the charter-myth for Naraka-caturdaśī before Dīpāvalī, observed with the pre-dawn oil bath and lamps across India _(actors: festival tradition; Kṛṣṇa, Narakāsura)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/narakasura-vadha.md) — (Purāṇic festival tradition (Padma/Skanda Purāṇa))
- **Near-simultaneous end of the prodigy generation** — All four siblings plus Cāṅgadeva take samādhi or depart within roughly a year (1296–1297), closing the founding Vārkarī household _(actors: Jñāneśvar, Sopāndev, Muktābāī, Nivṛttināth, Cāṅgadeva)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/nivritti-sopan-muktabai.md) — (Wikipedia (Dnyaneshwar, Muktabai, Nivruttinath, Sopan))
- **Nāyaka rebuilding of the temple-city** — After Sultanate-era damage, the Madurai-Nāyaka rulers (esp. Tirumalai Nāyaka) rebuild the complex with its gopurams and thousand-pillar hall _(actors: Tirumalai Nāyaka, Ariyanātha Mudaliyār)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/madurai-meenakshi-sundareshwara-kshetra.md) — (academic/historical record; temple tradition)
- **Onam — Mahābali's Annual Return** — Kerala folk-tradition holds that the golden-age king Māveli (Mahābali) returns to visit his people once a year, celebrated as the Onam harvest festival _(actors: Mahābali (Māveli), Viṣṇu)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/vamana-bali.md) — (Kerala Onam festival tradition)
- **Pan-Deccan equation Reṇukā = Yellammā = Māri** — A single Deccan mother-goddess identified across regions as Reṇukā, Yellammā, Ekvīrā, Māriyammaṉ, Padmākṣī and Aai _(actors: Reṇukā/Yellammā/Māriyammaṉ)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/mariamman.md) — (Deccan folk tradition)
- **Poleramma and Maisamma the boundary guardians** — Boundary mothers stationed at the village limit, gate or irrigation-tank who turn back epidemic and demon and receive buffalo-bali _(actors: Pōleramma; Maisamma (Mahiṣamma))_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/gramadevatas-village-deities.md) — (Whitehead; Telangana village-deity studies)
- **Prāṇa-pratiṣṭhā and netra-unmīlana animate the cast icon** — The finished bronze is consecrated by life-installation and the eye-opening rite, at which the image first 'sees' and becomes the living deity fit for pūjā _(actors: śilpin / consecrating priest)_ → [details](../09-artifacts-symbols-and-arts/pancha-loha-bronze-casting.md) — (Āgama kriyāpāda; Śilpa Śāstra; Śukranīti 4)
- **Priyādās commentary elaborates his life** — Priyādās's 1712 commentary on the Bhaktamāl elaborates the miracle-tales and full hagiographic life of Ravidās _(actors: Priyādās)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/ravidas.md) — (Priyādās Bhaktamāl commentary (1712))
- **Ramaṇa Mahaṛṣi's tapas at Aruṇācala** — From 1896 the sage Ramana Maharshi makes Aruṇācala his lifelong seat of tapas, teaching the hill as Śiva and the Heart/Self and composing the Five Hymns to Aruṇācala _(actors: Ramaṇa Mahaṛṣi)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/arunachala-tiruvannamalai-fire-hill.md) — (Ramana Maharshi corpus (Akṣaramaṇamālai / Five Hymns to Aruṇācala))
- **Ramana Maharshi's death-experience and Self-realization** — At sixteen in 1896 Ramana undergoes a spontaneous death-experience resolving into permanent Self-realization, then settles for life at Tiruvannamalai teaching atma-vichara, 'Who am I?' _(actors: Ramana Maharshi)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/modern-teachers-and-reform.md) — (Ramana Maharshi biography)
- **Ramanuja Learns the Charama Sloka at Tirukkoshtiyur** — At Tirukkoshtiyur, Ramanuja learns the secret charama sloka from his teacher _(actors: Ramanuja)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/108-divya-desams.md) — (Sri Vaishnava hagiography)
- **Redaction of the Valmiki Ramayana** — The Ramayana's core text is composed c. 500 BCE-200 CE (traditionally narrating Treta Yuga events far earlier) _(actors: Valmiki)_ → [details](../01-manvantaras/manvantara-07-vaivasvata/mahayuga-28-current/04-kali-yuga/recorded-history.md) — (Ramayana)
- **Rise of the Haridasa movement** — The Kannada bhakti-musical Haridasa wing of Madhva's school carries Dvaita to the common people through devaranamas and dasa-sahitya, with Purandaradasa hailed grandfather of Carnatic music _(actors: Purandaradasa, Vyasatirtha, Kanakadasa)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/ramanuja-and-madhva.md) — (Dasa-sahitya; devaranamas)
- **Rāmānuja's body enshrined as a tomb-shrine** — Rāmānuja's preserved body is enshrined within the temple in the fifth prākāra, a rare relic-shrine in Hinduism _(actors: Rāmānuja, temple priests)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/srirangam-ranganatha.md) — (Koil Oḻugu; temple tradition)
- **Sañjīvana (living) samādhi at Āḷandī** — At about 21 Jñāneśvar voluntarily seals himself in conscious meditation at Āḷandī in 1296; the tradition holds him still present beneath the shrine _(actors: Jñāneśvar, Nāmdev (companion))_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/jnaneshwar.md) — (Wikipedia (Dnyaneshwar); Vārkarī hagiography [folk])
- **Samādhi at Paithan (1599) and Eknāth Ṣaṣṭhī** — Eknāth takes samādhi at Paithan on the Godāvarī in 1599, commemorated by the Eknāth Ṣaṣṭhī and an Eknāth Mahārāj pālkhī within Vārkarī palanquin culture _(actors: Eknāth)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/eknath.md) — (Wikipedia Eknath; Vārkarī tradition)
- **Shankaradeva founds Ekasarana Dharma** — The Assamese polymath Shankaradeva founds Ekasarana Dharma, a single-refuge monotheistic Krishna-devotion, and creates the Kirtana-ghosha, the borgeet song-genre, ankiya nat plays, and the sattra and namghar institutional network _(actors: Shankaradeva, Madhavadeva)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/north-bhakti-sants.md) — (Kirtana-ghosha)
- **Sītā Eliya / Seetha Amman temple identification** — The modern Sri Lankan Rāmāyaṇa trail identifies the Aśoka-vāṭikā with Sītā Eliya near Hakgala, site of the living Seetha Amman kovil and the Sītā Oya stream _(actors: Sri Lankan Rāmāyaṇa-trail pilgrims, Hindu devotees)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/lanka-ramayana-trail-ashoka-vatika.md) — (Sri Lankan Rāmāyaṇa-trail tradition)
- **Sundarar composes the Tiruttoṇḍa Tohai** — Sundarar composes the Tiruttoṇḍa Tohai naming the foremost Śiva-devotees, the seed-list from which the canonical 63 Nāyaṉārs were fixed _(actors: Sundarar)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/the-nalvar-tamil-shaiva-saints.md) — (Wikipedia, Sundarar; Nayanars)
- **Tagore's Songs of Kabir** — Rabindranath Tagore publishes Songs of Kabir in 1915, making Kabir a global figure of universalist mysticism _(actors: Rabindranath Tagore)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/kabir.md) — (Tagore, Songs of Kabir (1915))
- **The annual Girnar (Lili) Parikramā** — Pilgrims and sādhus circumambulate the massif over ~36 km from Kārttika Ekādaśī to Pūrṇimā, with the Bhavnāth Mahāśivarātri fair gathering renunciants _(actors: Pilgrims, Nāgā sādhus, Nāths, avadhūtas)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/girnar-raivataka-datta-tirtha.md) — (Regional tradition; Junāgaḍh / Gujarat Tourism)
- **The garland-link tradition with Śrīraṅgam** — Garlands made at Āṇḍāḷ's natal shrine are sent to be offered to Raṅganātha at Śrīraṅgam, with mutual festival exchange (Āḍi Pūram, mārgaḻi Tiruppāvai) _(actors: Srivilliputhur and Śrīraṅgam temples, Āṇḍāḷ, Raṅganātha)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/srivilliputhur-vatapatrasayi-andal-kshetra.md) — (Temple tradition; hagiography)
- **The Pañcasakhā and Odia Bhakti** — The five Odia poet-devotees (Balarāma, Jagannātha, Yaśovanta, Ananta, Acyutānanda Dāsa) centered Utkala Vaiṣṇavism on Jagannātha; Jagannātha Dāsa's Odia Bhāgavata became household scripture _(actors: Pañcasakhā poets)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/jagannath-puri-purushottama-kshetra.md) — (Odia Vaiṣṇava tradition; scholarship)
- **The Sabarimala sacred-geography identification** — The Ayyappan pilgrimage shrine of Sabarimala in Kerala takes its name from Śabarī's hill, a traditional devotional overlay linking the tribal ascetic's tapas to the living pilgrimage cult _(actors: Śabarī (traditional association), Ayyappan pilgrimage tradition)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/shabari.md) — (Bhakti and regional folk tradition; Kerala sacred geography)
- **Tulsīdās's vision of Rāma at Rāmghāṭ** — Hanumān leads Tulsīdās to a darśana of Rāma and Lakṣmaṇa as young hunters at Citrakūṭa's Rāmghāṭ, the origin of his famous couplet _(actors: Tulsīdās, Hanumān, Rāma, Lakṣmaṇa)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/chitrakoot-kamadgiri.md) — (Tulsī hagiography; Bhaktamāl tradition)
- **Tulsidas Begins the Ramcaritmanas at Ayodhya (1574)** — Gosvami Tulsidas begins composing the Ramcaritmanas at Ayodhya in 1574 CE, recasting Valmiki's Sanskrit epic into Avadhi for the people _(actors: Tulsidas)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/ayodhya.md) — (Wikipedia; tradition)
- **Vijayanagara-era construction of the Ahobilam temples** — Vijayanagara kings and the Maṭha build and endow the standing Upper and Lower Ahobilam shrines (15th–16th c. CE) _(actors: Vijayanagara kings, Ahobila Maṭha)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/ahobilam-nava-narasimha.md) — (Epigraphy; Wikipedia (Ahobilam))
- **Viśvanātha Temple Rebuilt by Ahilyābāī Holkar** — After repeated medieval demolitions the Kāśī Viśvanātha (Gyanvapi) temple is rebuilt by Ahilyābāī Holkar in 1780 _(actors: Ahilyābāī Holkar)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/kashi-varanasi.md) — (Kashi-khanda; Shiva Purana)
- **Āḻvār consecration of Tiruvēṅkaṭam as Divya Deśam** — The Āḻvārs (Nammāḻvār, Tirumaṅgai) hymn Tirumala as Tiruvēṅkaṭam in the Divya Prabandham, making it one of the most-sung 108 Divya Deśams and a horizon of prapatti _(actors: Nammāḻvār, Tirumaṅgai Āḻvār)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/tirumala-tirupati-venkateshwara.md) — (Nālāyira Divya Prabandham)
- **Worship as Khāṭū Śyām Jī / Śyām Bābā** — The self-manifested head is enshrined at Khāṭū, Rajasthan; worshipped as Khāṭū Śyām Jī, Śyām Bābā, Hāre kā Sahārā, with the annual Phālguna fair _(actors: Khāṭū Śyām Jī devotees, Khāṭū shrine (Sīkar, Rajasthan))_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/barbarika-khatu-shyam.md) — (Khāṭū Śyām sthala-tradition [folk] [regional])
- **Śyāma Śāstri, the Devī-upāsaka composer** — Eldest of the Trinity (1762–1827), archaka of Baṅgāru Kāmākṣī, Navaratnamālikā and rhythmically intricate svarajatis; signature 'Śyāmakṛṣṇa' _(actors: Śyāma Śāstri)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/haridasa-and-carnatic-bhakti.md) — (Biographies of the Carnatic composers)
- **Akka Mahadevi renounces clothing for Shiva** — After the Jain king Kaushika breaks his pact never to violate her devotion, Akka Mahadevi leaves him, renounces all possessions and clothing covering herself only with her hair, and walks to Kalyana _(actors: Akka Mahadevi, King Kaushika)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/lingayat-vachana-poets.md) — (Akka Mahadevi hagiography)
- **Ankalamman carries Brahmā's skull** — The Tamil grief-and-cremation mother who carries Brahmā's severed skull / relieves Śiva of the Brahmahatyā sin, a local Kālī of the cremation-ground _(actors: Aṅkālamman; Śiva; Brahmā)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/gramadevatas-village-deities.md) — (Tamil grāmadevatā tradition)
- **Behulā's dance and the revival bargain** — Behulā dances before Śiva and the devas; Manasā agrees to revive Lakhindar and the six sons in exchange for Chand's worship _(actors: Behulā, Śiva, the devas, Manasā)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/manasa.md) — (Manasāmaṅgal)
- **Canonization among the 63 Nāyaṉārs** — Named in Cuntarar's Tiruttoṇḍa Tohai as among the earliest devotees and versified by Cēkkiḻār in the Periya Purāṇam, ranked senior to the Nālvar _(actors: Cuntarar, Cēkkiḻār, Kāraikkāl Ammaiyār)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/karaikkal-ammaiyar.md) — (Tiruttoṇḍa Tohai (Cuntarar); Periya Purāṇam (Cēkkiḻār))
- **Chaitanya takes sannyasa at Katwa** — In 1510 Chaitanya takes sannyasa at Katwa from Keshava Bharati, receiving the name Krishna Chaitanya _(actors: Chaitanya, Keshava Bharati)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/nimbarka-vallabha-chaitanya.md) — (Chaitanya-charitamrita)
- **Compilation of the Navnāth Bhaktisār** — The 18th-c. Marathi hagiographic cycle narrating the nine Nāths' miracles and initiations becomes a household devotional grantha _(actors: Dhuṇḍisut Mālū, the Navnāth)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/nath-sampradaya-and-the-siddhas.md) — (Navnāth Bhaktisār / Navnāth Kathāsār)
- **Composition of nirguṇa hymns enshrined in the Guru Granth Sāhib** — About 61 Hindi/Marwari nirguṇa hymns attributed to Nāmdev enter the Sikh Guru Granth Sāhib, making him a poet straddling both saguṇa and nirguṇa bhakti _(actors: Nāmdev; Sikh tradition (compilers of the Ādi Granth))_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/namdev.md) — (Hindi/Marwari hymns in the Guru Granth Sāhib; Wikipedia Namdev)
- **Discovery of the Varadvinayak Idol from the Lake (1690)** — The Varada-Vinayaka murti is reportedly found immersed in an adjoining lake at Mahad in 1690 CE, leading to the temple's construction in 1725 _(actors: Ramji Mahadev Biwalkar)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/ashtavinayaka.md) — (temple history)
- **Eastern Gaṅga Temple Construction** — The surviving 12th-c. Kaliṅga-style temple was built under Eastern Gaṅga kings Anantavarman Coḍagaṅga and Anaṅgabhīma III (c. 1135–1230 CE) over an older, likely Sabara-origin cult _(actors: Anantavarman Coḍagaṅga, Anaṅgabhīma III)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/jagannath-puri-purushottama-kshetra.md) — (epigraphic / academic history)
- **Establishment of the Āḷandī pālkhī and the four-saint succession** — Jñāneśvar heads the Jñāneśvar→Nāmdev→Eknāth→Tukārām lineage; his pādukā are carried annually from Āḷandī to Pandharpur, the procession reshaped in the 1820s by Haibatravbābā Ārphaḷkar _(actors: Jñāneśvar, Nāmdev, Eknāth, Tukārām, Haibatravbābā Ārphaḷkar)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/jnaneshwar.md) — (Wikipedia (Warkari, Pandharpur Wari))
- **Establishment of the Anubhava Maṇṭapa** — Basava establishes the Anubhava Maṇṭapa at Basavakalyāṇ, the 'first religious parliament,' where śaraṇas of all backgrounds debate and produce the vachana corpus _(actors: Basava, Allama Prabhu, Akka Mahādevī)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/shaiva-schools.md) — (Wikipedia, Anubhava Mantapa)
- **Founding of the Maurya Empire** — The Maurya Empire (c. 322-185 BCE), first pan-Indian empire, is founded by Chandragupta; Ashoka patronizes Buddhism and issues rock and pillar edicts _(actors: Chandragupta; Bindusara; Ashoka)_ → [details](../01-manvantaras/manvantara-07-vaivasvata/mahayuga-28-current/04-kali-yuga/recorded-history.md) — (History of India (Wikipedia))
- **Institution of the Vairamudi diamond-crown festival** — Melkote's signature brahmotsava crowns the processional Celuvarāya with the secret diamond Vairamudi crown kept under treasury seal _(actors: Celuvarāya/Rāmapriya deity, temple priests, royal patrons)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/melkote-tirunarayanapuram.md) — (local tradition; Vijayanagara-era endowment records)
- **Madhavadeva composes the Nāma-ghoṣā** — Madhavadeva composes the Nāma-ghoṣā ('book of the Name'/Hajāri-ghoṣā), consolidating the faith after the founder's death and producing a peak of Assamese literature _(actors: Madhavadeva)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/sankaradeva-and-assamese-vaishnavism.md) — (Wikipedia, Madhavdev)
- **Madhava-Vidyaranya compiles the Sarva-darshana-sangraha** — Madhava-Vidyaranya surveys sixteen darshanas (14th c.), opening with Carvaka as the lowest and ascending to Advaita, providing the standard Carvaka portrait _(actors: Madhava-Vidyaranya)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/nastika-darshanas-and-the-astika-nastika-frame.md) — (Sarva-darshana-sangraha ch. 1)
- **Mādhava of Saṅgamagrāma and the Kerala school** — Founder of the Kerala school, credited with infinite-series expansions for π, sine and cosine anticipating European calculus by ~250 years _(actors: Mādhava of Saṅgamagrāma)_ → [details](../00-time-and-cosmology/siddhantic-astronomy-and-astronomers.md) — (histories of Indian astronomy; Yuktibhāṣā (transmission))
- **Nambiyandar Nambi Recovers the Tevaram Hymns** — The scattered Tevaram hymns, legendarily found walled-up at Chidambaram, are rediscovered and organized into Tirumurai Books 1-7 by Nambiyandar Nambi under Chola king Rajaraja I _(actors: Nambiyandar Nambi, Rajaraja I)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/alvars-and-nayanars.md) — (Tirumurai — Wikipedia)
- **Pañcavaṭī / Nāsik as living tīrtha** — Sītā Guphā, Kālārām Mandir, Tapovan, Rāmkuṇḍ and the Godāvarī ghāṭs constitute the worshipped site _(actors: Pilgrims; Peśvā-era builders; B. R. Ambedkar (1930 satyāgraha))_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/panchavati-nashik-godavari.md) — (Nāsik sthala-mahātmya; historical record)
- **Place in the Vārkarī succession** — Eknāth is the consolidator third link in Jñāneśvar→Nāmdev→Eknāth→Tukārām, recovering the textual heritage and bridging the gap to Tukārām's age _(actors: Eknāth, Jñāneśvar, Nāmdev, Tukārām)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/eknath.md) — (Wikipedia Warkari; north-bhakti-sants.md)
- **Priyadas's narrative elaboration of Mira's life** — Priyadas's 1712 commentary on the Bhaktamal provides the first full narrative of Mira — the poison, snake-basket, persecuting Rana and miracles — building the familiar dramatic life out of Nabhadas's bare notice _(actors: Priyadas)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/mirabai.md) — (Priyadas, Bhaktamal commentary (1712))
- **Ravidāssia movement and 2009 Vienna attack** — The Camār-based Ravidāssia movement venerates Ravidās as founder-Guru; the 2009 murder of Sant Rāmānand in a Vienna dera attack pushes it toward separate identity _(actors: Ravidāssia community, Sant Niranjan Dass, Sant Rāmānand)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/ravidas.md) — (Wikipedia Ravidassia [scholarly])
- **Śārṅgadeva compiles the Saṅgīta-ratnākara** — At the Yādava court of Devagiri, Śārṅgadeva synthesizes the music-and-dance science bridging the Nāṭya Śāstra and the modern rāga–tāla systems _(actors: Śārṅgadeva)_ → [details](../09-artifacts-symbols-and-arts/natya-shastra-and-the-classical-arts.md) — (Saṅgīta-ratnākara (13th c.))
- **Rāmānuja's ordering of the shrine worship** — Rāmānuja organises Tirumala worship, fixes the Vaiṣṇava emblems and establishes the Pāñcarātra liturgy, settling the deity's identity as Veṅkaṭeśvara-Viṣṇu _(actors: Rāmānuja)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/tirumala-tirupati-venkateshwara.md) — (Śrīvaiṣṇava ācārya tradition)
- **Rukmiṇī-kalyāṇam recitation and festival tradition** — The marriage narrative is read as an auspicious maṅgala text and ritually enacted as a kalyāṇotsavam across South India, held to confer marital harmony _(actors: Vaiṣṇava reciters/devotees, temple traditions (Telugu/Tamil/Kannada), Potana)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/rukmini-harana.md) — (Bhāgavata Purāṇa 10.52-54 (liturgical use); Potana Telugu Bhāgavatamu)
- **Sanskritization of the folk goddess** — Temple back-stories reframe Māri as Pārvatī/Durgā/Ādi Parāśakti and replace blood-sacrifice with Agamic vegetarian worship, contested between reform temples and village shrines _(actors: Māriyammaṉ; temple reformers; village priests)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/mariamman.md) — (Grāmadevatā studies)
- **Seat at Sajjangad and mahāsamādhi** — Made Sajjangad fort near Sātārā the order's mother-maṭh with a Rāma temple, and died there in 1681 _(actors: Rāmdās; Maratha grantors)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/samarth-ramdas.md) — (Wikipedia (Samarth Ramdas, Sajjangad); Hindupedia)
- **Sivananda founds the Divine Life Society** — The physician-turned-monk Sivananda founds the Divine Life Society in 1936 at Rishikesh, propagating a synthetic practical yoga and seeding global teachers _(actors: Swami Sivananda)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/modern-teachers-and-reform.md) — (Divine Life Society history)
- **Sundarar's bodily ascent to Kailāsa** — Śiva sends a white elephant to carry Sundarar bodily to Kailāsa, with his friend Cēramāṉ Perumāḷ following on horseback _(actors: Sundarar, Cēramāṉ Perumāḷ, Śiva)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/the-nalvar-tamil-shaiva-saints.md) — (Wikipedia, Sundarar; Tirumurai)
- **Syncretic identification as Zinda Pir / Khwaja Khizr** — At the Indus river-shrines Jhulelal is identified with the immortal water-saint Zinda Pir / Khwaja Khizr, venerated by Hindus and Muslims alike _(actors: Jhulelal, Zinda Pir / Khwaja Khizr, Sindhi Hindus and Muslims)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/jhulelal.md) — (Udero Lal sthala-tradition; floating-shrine (Zindapir) cult; Khidr lore)
- **The 1311 Sultanate raid and the icon's exile** — Malik Kāfūr's army sacks the south; Nam Perumāḷ is hidden and carried into decades of exile, later restored under Vijayanagara _(actors: Malik Kāfūr / Delhi Sultanate, temple priests, Goppaṇa Uḍaiyār, (variant: Tulukka Nācciyār))_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/srirangam-ranganatha.md) — (Koil Oḻugu; folk Tulukka Nācciyār variant)
- **The Avvaiyar Riddle at Pazhamudircholai** — Murugan disguised as a boy poses the roasted-fruit riddle to the aged poet Avvaiyar, humbling her bookish learning before divine grace _(actors: Murugan, Avvaiyar)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/arupadai-veedu-murugan.md) — (Tamil tradition; Vinayaka/Avvaiyar lore)
- **The Nandi maṇḍapa of temple tradition** — The couchant white bull faces the liṅga in a dedicated pavilion (Nandi-maṇḍapa); monumental examples at Lepākṣi, Tanjore, Mysuru, Hampi, and Kāśī _(actors: Nandi, Śaiva temple tradition, sthapatis)_ → [details](../08-beings-and-bestiary/nandi-and-shaiva-mounts.md) — (Śaiva Āgamas; temple tradition; Sthala-purāṇas)
- **The Siṃhastha Kumbh at Nāsik–Tryambak** — The Godāvarī source-region hosts the Siṃhastha (Jupiter-in-Leo) Kumbh every ~12 years, with bathing split between Rāmkuṇḍ (Nāsik) and Kuśāvarta (Tryambak) _(actors: Vaiṣṇava and Śaiva akhāḍās, pilgrims)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/godavari-gautami-dakshina-ganga.md) — (pilgrimage tradition)
- **The three pinḍīs of the Holy Cave** — The Goddess is enshrined aniconically as three natural rock pinḍīs — Mahākālī, Mahālakṣmī, Mahāsarasvatī — the Tridevī fused in the cave _(actors: Mahākālī, Mahālakṣmī, Mahāsarasvatī (Vaiṣṇo Devī))_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/vaishno-devi-trikuta-katra.md) — (Śākta tradition of the three pinḍīs; Devī Māhātmya)
- **Tirumangai Alvar sings Tiru Naimisaranyam (Divya Desam)** — The Alvar poet-saint composes the mangalasasana enshrining Naimisharanya as a Divya Desam with Devaraja Perumal and Harilakshmi Thayar _(actors: Tirumangai Alvar, Devaraja Perumal)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/naimisharanya-chakra-tirtha.md) — (Nalayira Divya Prabandham (Tirumangai Alvar))
- **Varadarāja's Six Words to Rāmānuja** — Through Tirukkacci Nambi, the deity Varadarāja answers young Rāmānuja's doubts with six sentences that become foundational axioms of Viśiṣṭādvaita Śrī Vaiṣṇavism _(actors: Varadarāja, Tirukkacci Nambi, Rāmānuja)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/kanchipuram-city-of-temples.md) — (Sri Vaishnava Guruparampara)
- **2010 proclamation of Ravidāssia religion and Amritbāṇī** — In 2010 at Seer Govardhanpur the Ravidāssia community proclaims a distinct religion with its own scripture, the Amritbani Guru Ravidass Ji (~240 hymns) _(actors: Ravidāssia community, Dera Sachkhand Ballan)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/ravidas.md) — (Wikipedia Ravidassia, Amritbani Guru Ravidass [scholarly])
- **Abhayadatta compiles the lives of the 84 Mahāsiddhas** — 12th-c. Abhayadatta Śrī's Caturaśīti-siddha-pravṛtti fixes the canonical roster of the 84 perfected tantric adepts, transmitted to Tibet as the Grub-thob brgyad-cu _(actors: Abhayadatta Śrī, the 84 Mahāsiddhas)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/nath-sampradaya-and-the-siddhas.md) — (Caturaśīti-siddha-pravṛtti; Grub-thob brgyad-cu)
- **Akka Mahadevi tested at the Anubhava Mantapa** — Akka Mahadevi's nakedness scandalizes the orthodox assembly; Allama Prabhu interrogates her and she wins the debate, establishing herself as a fully realized Sharane _(actors: Akka Mahadevi, Allama Prabhu)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/lingayat-vachana-poets.md) — (Shunyasampadane)
- **Annamācārya's Telugu saṅkīrtana corpus** — The 15th-century padakavi Annamayya composes thousands of saṅkīrtana on Veṅkaṭeśvara, centring the deity in Telugu devotional song; copper-plate songs survive in the temple _(actors: Annamācārya (Annamayya))_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/tirumala-tirupati-venkateshwara.md) — (Telugu Vaiṣṇava tradition)
- **Chaitanya rediscovers the holy sites of Braj** — Making Jagannatha Puri his base, Chaitanya tours South India and then Vrindavana/Braj, rediscovering the lost holy sites of Krishna's lila _(actors: Chaitanya Mahaprabhu)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/nimbarka-vallabha-chaitanya.md) — (Chaitanya-charitamrita)
- **Chand's left-handed worship establishes Manasā's cult** — Chand finally offers the pūjā-flower with his left hand while looking away, grudgingly establishing Manasā's worship among humans _(actors: Chand Sadagar, Manasā, Behulā)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/manasa.md) — (Manasāmaṅgal)
- **Composition of the Major Puranas** — The major Puranas are composed c. 300-1000 CE, encoding the yuga/manvantara/kalpa cosmology, avatar theology and the Kali-Yuga decline narrative _(actors: Puranic compilers)_ → [details](../01-manvantaras/manvantara-07-vaivasvata/mahayuga-28-current/04-kali-yuga/recorded-history.md) — (Vishnu, Bhagavata, Shiva, Markandeya, Devi-Bhagavata Puranas)
- **Deification as Subhadrā of the Jagannātha Purī triad** — Subhadrā is worshipped as a goddess flanked by Jagannātha (Kṛṣṇa) and Balabhadra (Balarāma) at Purī, riding the chariot Darpadalana in the Rath Yātrā _(actors: Subhadrā, Jagannātha, Balabhadra, Sudarśana)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/subhadra.md) — (Jagannātha Purī temple tradition / Sthala-purāṇa)
- **Ādi Śaṅkara Re-consecrates Bharat Mandir** — Ādi Śaṅkarācārya re-consecrates the Bharat Mandir of Rishikesh (dedicated to Hṛṣīkeśa Nārāyaṇa) c. 789 CE on Basant Panchami _(actors: Ādi Śaṅkarācārya)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/haridwar-rishikesh-ganga-dwara.md) — (Skanda Purana, Kedar-Khanda; tirthayatra.org)
- **Diaspora spread and firewalking abroad** — Māri carried by indentured and merchant Tamils becomes a principal diaspora goddess across Singapore, Malaysia, Sri Lanka, South Africa, Mauritius, Réunion and the Caribbean, exporting the tīmiti firewalk _(actors: Māriyammaṉ; diaspora Tamil communities)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/mariamman.md) — (Diaspora temple records and ethnography)
- **Ellamma and Mutyalamma head a kin-group of mothers** — Ellamma the all-mother and Mutyālamma the pearl/pox-mother lead a large kin-group of Telugu/Kannada village mothers, one Mother in many aspects _(actors: Ellamma; Mutyālamma; Gangamma, Peddamma, Nookalamma and kin)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/gramadevatas-village-deities.md) — (Deccan grāmadevatā ethnography)
- **Establishment of Barpetā Sattra** — Madhavadeva establishes and develops the great Barpetā Sattra, a principal seat of the Nika tradition and of post-founder organization _(actors: Madhavadeva)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/sankaradeva-and-assamese-vaishnavism.md) — (Wikipedia, Madhavdev)
- **Godāvarī-māhātmya and the Siṃhastha Kumbh** — Nāsik–Tryambak hosts the Jupiter-in-Leo Kumbh Mela at Rāmkuṇḍ and Kuśāvarta on the Dakṣiṇa Gaṅgā _(actors: Akhāḍās, pilgrims; sage Gautama (māhātmya))_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/panchavati-nashik-godavari.md) — (Brahma Purāṇa Gautamī-māhātmya; Godāvarī-māhātmya; pilgrimage tradition)
- **Gupta Empire & Aryabhata** — Golden Age c. 320-550 CE; Kalidasa's literature, and Aryabhata's Aryabhatiya (499 CE) fixes the 3102 BCE Kali epoch _(actors: Gupta emperors, Kalidasa, Aryabhata)_ → [details](../01-manvantaras/manvantara-07-vaivasvata/mahayuga-28-current/04-kali-yuga/recorded-history.md) — (Aryabhatiya)
- **Hagiography in Mahīpati's Bhaktalīlāmṛta** — The 18th-c. hagiographer Mahīpati recorded Rāmdās's life and miracles in the Bhaktalīlāmṛta, a principal devotional source _(actors: Mahīpati)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/samarth-ramdas.md) — (Mahīpati, Bhaktalīlāmṛta; Wikipedia (Mahipati) [late text])
- **Hoysaḷa–Vijayanagara–Woḍeyar building and endowment** — Successive dynasties endow the temples, raise the gopuras and Rāya-gopura and tank, fixing the site's historical clock (12th–19th c. CE) _(actors: Hoysaḷa, Vijayanagara and Mysore Woḍeyar dynasties)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/melkote-tirunarayanapuram.md) — (Hoysaḷa & Vijayanagara temple inscriptions)
- **Jhulelal as portable patron of the Sindhi diaspora** — After the 1947 Partition displaced Sindhi Hindus from Sindh, Jhulelal became the central portable identity-marker of the scattered community _(actors: Jhulelal, the Sindhi Hindu diaspora)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/jhulelal.md) — (Daryapanthi tradition; scholarly accounts of the Sindhi diaspora)
- **Matsyendranāth founds the Nāth lineage** — Matsyendranāth (9th–10th c.) becomes the foundational human guru of the Nāth Siddha-yogī order and the Kaula yoga stream _(actors: Matsyendranāth)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/shaiva-schools.md) — (Wikipedia, Natha Sampradaya)
- **Medieval consolidation of the eight as one network** — Sri Vaishnava commentators and Pancharatra doctrine crystallise the eight into a single fixed numbered set mirroring the twelve Jyotirlingas _(actors: Sri Vaishnava acharyas; Pancharatra/Agama tradition)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/ashta-svayamvyakta-vishnu-kshetras.md) — (Divya Suri Carita; Pancharatra Agama; medieval Vaishnava commentary)
- **Māṇikkavācakar's initiation and the horse legend** — Sent to buy cavalry horses, Māṇikkavācakar meets Śiva disguised as a guru, is initiated and spends the treasury gold building a temple; Śiva sends jackals turned to horses and floods the Vaigai to vindicate him _(actors: Māṇikkavācakar, Śiva)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/the-nalvar-tamil-shaiva-saints.md) — (Wikipedia, Manikkavacakar)
- **Nakkirar Composes the Tirumurukarruppatai** — The Sangam poet Nakkirar composes the Tirumurukarruppatai, the founding charter enumerating Murugan's six abodes, c. 2nd-4th c. CE _(actors: Nakkirar)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/arupadai-veedu-murugan.md) — (Tirumurukarruppatai (Nakkirar))
- **Nīlakaṇṭha Somayājī and the Tantrasaṅgraha (1501)** — Kerala-school systematiser who proposed a geo-heliocentric model with five planets orbiting the Sun, predating Tycho Brahe's scheme _(actors: Nīlakaṇṭha Somayājī; Jyeṣṭhadeva)_ → [details](../00-time-and-cosmology/siddhantic-astronomy-and-astronomers.md) — (Tantrasaṅgraha; Yuktibhāṣā)
- **Pañcavaṭī as living Rāma-tīrtha and Kumbh city** — Nāsik/Pañcavaṭī becomes a tīrtha-cluster (Sītā Gufā, Tapovan, Kapāleśvara, Rāmkuṇḍ, Kālārām) and one of four Siṃhastha Kumbh cities _(actors: Pilgrims, akhāḍās, Peśvā-era patrons, B. R. Ambedkar)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/panchavati-nashik-godavari.md) — (Gautamī-māhātmya (Brahma Purāṇa); Nāsik sthala-mahātmya; pilgrimage and historical record)
- **Pan-Indian veneration and the two-Nāmdevs question** — Venerated as Sant Nāmdev in Maharashtra and Bhagat Nāmdev in Sikh/Punjabi tradition, his cross-regional dual corpus later spawns the disputed two-Nāmdevs hypothesis _(actors: Nāmdev; later Sant-literature scholars)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/namdev.md) — (Wikipedia Namdev, Sant (religion); academic scholarship [disputed])
- **Swaminarayan codifies the Swaminarayan Sampradaya** — The Gujarati ascetic Swaminarayan forms the Swaminarayan Sampradaya, preaching strict ethics and Krishna-Narayana devotion and codifying conduct in the Shikshapatri and teachings in the Vachanamrut _(actors: Swaminarayan (Sahajanand Swami))_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/modern-teachers-and-reform.md) — (Shikshapatri; Vachanamrut)
- **Vaiṣṇo Devī as major modern Śākta pilgrimage** — The Trikūṭa cave-shrine above Katra becomes one of the most-visited goddess shrines in India, drawing millions yearly under the Shri Mata Vaishno Devi Shrine Board _(actors: Pilgrims, SMVDSB)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/vaishno-devi-trikuta-katra.md) — (Living Vaiṣṇo Devī Yātrā tradition; Wikipedia)
- **Śaṅkara's Two Bhāṣyas** — Ādi Śaṅkara composes two distinct commentaries on the Kena — a pada-bhāṣya (word-gloss) and a vākya-bhāṣya (sentence commentary) _(actors: Ādi Śaṅkara)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/upanishads/kena-upanishad.md) — (Śaṅkara, Kenopaniṣad pada- & vākya-bhāṣya)
- **Akka Mahadevi merges into Chennamallikarjuna at Srisailam** — Akka Mahadevi journeys to the Kadalivana forest at Srisailam seeking final union with Chennamallikarjuna, where she is said to have merged into the divine _(actors: Akka Mahadevi)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/lingayat-vachana-poets.md) — (Akka Mahadevi hagiography (traditional))
- **Arunagirinatar Sings the Thiruppugazh** — The 15th-century saint Arunagirinatar composes the Thiruppugazh and companion hymns, the great medieval bhakti layer over the Arupadai network _(actors: Arunagirinatar)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/arupadai-veedu-murugan.md) — (Thiruppugazh; Kandar Anubhuti)
- **Building of the Konārk Sun Temple** — King Narasiṃhadeva I of the Eastern Gaṅga dynasty builds the colossal Konārk Sun Temple as Sūrya's stone chariot with seven horses and twenty-four wheels around 1250 CE _(actors: Narasiṃhadeva I)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/surya-and-his-family.md)
- **Chaitanya composes the Shikshashtakam and deputes the Six Goswamis** — Chaitanya leaves the eight-verse Shikshashtakam summarizing his mission and deputes the Six Goswamis of Vrindavana to recover Braj and codify Gaudiya teaching in Sanskrit _(actors: Chaitanya, Rupa, Sanatana, Jiva Goswami)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/nimbarka-vallabha-chaitanya.md) — (Shikshashtakam; Shat-sandarbha; Bhakti-rasamrita-sindhu)
- **Gorakhnāth organizes the Nāth order and haṭha-yoga** — Gorakhnāth, disciple of Matsyendranāth, organizes the Nāth Sampradāya and haṭha-yoga and founds the Kānphaṭa yogī order and the Gorakhpur seat _(actors: Gorakhnāth)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/shaiva-schools.md) — (Wikipedia, Gorakhnath)
- **Gupta Empire Golden Age** — The Gupta Empire (c. 320-550 CE) sees classical Sanskrit literature, Aryabhata fixing the 3102 BCE Kali epoch astronomically (Aryabhatiya, 499 CE), and the crystallization of classical Hinduism _(actors: Kalidasa; Aryabhata)_ → [details](../01-manvantaras/manvantara-07-vaivasvata/mahayuga-28-current/04-kali-yuga/recorded-history.md) — (Aryabhatiya (499 CE); History of India (Wikipedia))
- **Jai Singh II's Jantar Mantar observatories** — Rajput ruler who built five masonry observatories (c. 1724–1738) with giant fixed instruments and compiled the Zīj-i Muhammad Shāhī, fusing Sanskrit, Islamic and European astronomy _(actors: Sawai Jai Singh II)_ → [details](../00-time-and-cosmology/siddhantic-astronomy-and-astronomers.md) — (histories of Indian astronomy; Zīj-i Muhammad Shāhī)
- **Kausalyā-suprabhātam veneration** — The Veṅkaṭeśa Suprabhātam opens by invoking Kausalyā, making her name the liturgical first word of dawn in Viṣṇu temples _(actors: Prativādibhayaṅkaram Aṇṇaṅgarācārya, Viśvāmitra (quoted), Rāma)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/characters/kausalya.md) — (Veṅkaṭeśa Suprabhātam; cf. Vālmīki Bāla Kāṇḍa)
- **Māṇikkavācakar merges into Naṭarāja at Chidambaram** — After defeating a Buddhist delegation at Chidambaram and restoring a mute princess's speech, Māṇikkavācakar sings the Accō Patikam and merges into the form of Naṭarāja, attaining liberation _(actors: Māṇikkavācakar)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/the-nalvar-tamil-shaiva-saints.md) — (Wikipedia, Manikkavacakar)
- **Modern Dalit assertion and Jayantī devotion** — Ravidās becomes a principal saint-icon of Dalit self-respect; Guru Ravidāss Jayantī (Māgh Pūrṇimā) is celebrated across Punjab and the diaspora _(actors: Ravidāssia and Dalit communities)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/ravidas.md) — (Omvedt Seeking Begumpura; Grokipedia Guru Ravidass Jayanti [scholarly/folk])
- **Selective Sanskritization into Durgā/Kālī/Pārvatī** — Village mothers are progressively identified with Purāṇic Devī forms and their buffalo-bali read as Mahiṣāsura-mardana, contested by reform that replaces blood-sacrifice with vegetarian worship _(actors: Village mothers; Durgā/Kālī/Pārvatī; reform temple managements)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/gramadevatas-village-deities.md) — (Whitehead; grāmadevatā Sanskritization studies; Devī-Māhātmya)
- **Shastriji Maharaj founds BAPS** — Shastriji Maharaj founds BAPS on the Akshar-Purushottam Darshan, consecrating murtis at Bochasan on 5 June 1907 _(actors: Shastriji Maharaj)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/modern-teachers-and-reform.md) — (BAPS history)
- **Tamil Bhakti — Alvars & Nayanars** — 5th-9th c. CE earliest organized Bhakti movement: 12 Alvars (Vaishnava) and 63 Nayanars (Shaiva) sing Tamil hymns _(actors: Andal, Nammalvar, Appar, Sambandar)_ → [details](../01-manvantaras/manvantara-07-vaivasvata/mahayuga-28-current/04-kali-yuga/recorded-history.md) — (Bhakti movement)
- **The historical Somnath temple (recorded-history clock)** — The Somnath shrine is repeatedly destroyed and rebuilt, sacked by Mahmud of Ghazni in 1025-26 CE and last reconsecrated in 1951 _(actors: Mahmud of Ghazni, later restorers)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/prabhasa-somnath-tirtha.md) — (recorded history; 12-jyotirlingas.md)
- **The Saṃhati schisms into four branches** — After the founders' era the Ekaśaraṇa movement fractures into four Saṃhatis (Brahma, Puruṣa, Nika, Kala) led by distinct successors differing on cāri-vastu emphasis and Brahminism _(actors: Dāmodaradeva, Haridev, Puruṣottama, Gopāladeva, Padma Atā)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/sankaradeva-and-assamese-vaishnavism.md) — (Wikipedia, Ekasarana Dharma)
- **Vijayanagara woven Kiṣkindhā geography** — The Vijayanagara empire (1336–1565 CE) read its capital as Rāma's Kiṣkindhā, building the Virūpākṣa and Hazāra Rāma (Rāmacandra) temples and fixing the Hampi-Anegundi identification of the vānara kingdom _(actors: Vijayanagara kings, (Rāma, Sugrīva, Hanumān as imperial patrons))_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/kishkindha-hampi-anegundi.md) — (Vijayanagara-era Rāma tradition; Hazāra Rāma temple reliefs; epigraphy [scholarly])
- **Adi Shankara Founds Advaita** — Shankara (c. 700-750 CE scholarly) systematizes Advaita Vedanta and founds the four mathas; traditional date 509-477 BCE _(actors: Adi Shankara)_ → [details](../01-manvantaras/manvantara-07-vaivasvata/mahayuga-28-current/04-kali-yuga/recorded-history.md) — (Brahmasutra-bhashya)
- **Agastya Founds Tamil Grammar (the Agattiyam)** — Tamil tradition makes Agastya author of the lost Agattiyam, earliest Tamil grammar, and head of the First Tamil Sangam at Madurai teaching Tolkappiyar _(actors: Agastya, Tolkappiyar)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/agastya.md) — (Tamil tradition; Iraiyanar Akapporul commentary)
- **Anthologization of vachanas under Vijayanagara** — In the 15th-16th c. the scattered vachanas are collected and the Shunyasampadane and other compilations produced, fixing the canon and the dramatized memory of the Anubhava Mantapa _(actors: Vijayanagara compilers)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/lingayat-vachana-poets.md) — (Shunyasampadane)
- **Canonization of the Tirumuṟai** — The rediscovered Tēvāram palm-leaves are organized into Books 1–7 by Nambiyāṇḍār Nambi under Chola Rājarāja I, completed by Cēkkiḻār's Periya Purāṇam (c. 1135) as Book 12 _(actors: Nambiyāṇḍār Nambi, Rājarāja I, Cēkkiḻār)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/the-nalvar-tamil-shaiva-saints.md) — (Wikipedia, Tirumurai)
- **Death of Chaitanya at Puri** — Chaitanya dies at Puri in 1534, traditions holding he merged into the Jagannatha/Tota-Gopinatha deity or drowned in the sea in ecstasy _(actors: Chaitanya Mahaprabhu)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/nimbarka-vallabha-chaitanya.md) — (Chaitanya hagiography (folk))
- **Gangamma and Reṇukā-Yellammā as elaborated instances** — Two village goddesses outgrew the local frame into pan-regional cults with their own pilgrimage centres at Tirupati and Saundatti _(actors: Gangamma of Tirupati; Reṇukā–Yellammā of Saundatti)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/gramadevatas-village-deities.md) — (Regional cult studies; Deccan folk tradition)
- **Rise of the Tamil Bhakti Movement** — The 12 Alvars and 63 Nayanars (5th-9th c. CE) sing Tamil hymns to Vishnu and Shiva, forming the earliest organized Bhakti movement that later spreads north _(actors: Alvars; Nayanars; Andal; Nammalvar)_ → [details](../01-manvantaras/manvantara-07-vaivasvata/mahayuga-28-current/04-kali-yuga/recorded-history.md) — (Bhakti movement (Wikipedia))
- **Two clocks synchronised at the Kali-yuga epoch** — The siddhānta astronomers' adoption of the 3102 BCE Kali-yuga epoch and the 4,320,000-year Mahāyuga welds the historical astronomical clock to the cyclic cosmological clock _(actors: Siddhāntic astronomers collectively)_ → [details](../00-time-and-cosmology/siddhantic-astronomy-and-astronomers.md) — (Sūrya Siddhānta; Āryabhaṭīya)
- **Yijing reports the grammarian Bhartṛhari** — the 7th-c. Chinese pilgrim's notice anchors the Vākyapadīya author, whose identity with the Śatakatraya poet remains unresolved [scholarly] [disputed] _(actors: Bhartṛhari, Yijing)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/classical-kavya-and-sanskrit-drama.md) — (Yijing, travel record; Śatakatraya)
- **Yogananda founds the Self-Realization Fellowship** — Yogananda comes to the U.S. in 1920 and founds the Self-Realization Fellowship to bring Kriya Yoga to the West, later authoring the influential Autobiography of a Yogi (1946) _(actors: Paramahansa Yogananda)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/modern-teachers-and-reform.md) — (Autobiography of a Yogi)
- **Adi Shankara Founds the Four Mathas** — Adi Shankara (c. 700-750 CE) establishes Advaita Vedanta, founds the four mathas, and writes the Brahmasutra-bhashya (traditional Kanchi dating of 509-477 BCE disputed) _(actors: Adi Shankara)_ → [details](../01-manvantaras/manvantara-07-vaivasvata/mahayuga-28-current/04-kali-yuga/recorded-history.md) — (Brahmasutra-bhashya; Adi Shankara (Wikipedia))
- **Idol-Shifting to Bet Dvaraka (1551)** — When raiders threatened Dvaraka, the deity is said to have been moved to Bet Dvaraka for safety, anchoring Bet's claim as Krishna's home → [details](../06-sacred-geography/dwarka-bet-dwarka-saurashtra.md) — (Wikipedia, Dwarkadhish Temple)
- **Prabhupada founds ISKCON** — Arriving in New York in 1965, Prabhupada founds ISKCON in July 1966, spreading Hare Krishna sankirtana worldwide and translating the Bhagavad Gita As It Is and the Srimad Bhagavatam _(actors: A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupada)_ → [details](../07-acharyas-and-sampradayas/modern-teachers-and-reform.md) — (Bhagavad Gita As It Is; Srimad Bhagavatam)
- **Ramanuja and Madhva — Vedanta Acharyas** — Ramanuja (~11th-12th c.) founds Vishishtadvaita; Madhva (1238-1317) founds Dvaita and the Udupi Krishna matha _(actors: Ramanuja, Madhva)_ → [details](../01-manvantaras/manvantara-07-vaivasvata/mahayuga-28-current/04-kali-yuga/recorded-history.md) — (Sri Bhashya; Dvaita texts)
- **Mahmud of Ghazni Sacks Somnath** — The Somnath temple is sacked by Mahmud of Ghazni in 1025–26 CE, beginning its history as the most-destroyed-and-rebuilt temple, last rebuilt in 1951 _(actors: Mahmud of Ghazni)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/12-jyotirlingas.md) — (recorded history)
- **Marine Excavations at Dvaraka (1983-1990)** — The NIO Marine Archaeology Unit under S. R. Rao excavates submerged structures, a jetty and stone anchors off Dvaraka and Bet Dvaraka, dating a port to c. 1500 BCE _(actors: S. R. Rao, NIO)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/dwarka-bet-dwarka-saurashtra.md) — (S. R. Rao, The Lost City of Dvaraka (1999); NIO surveys)
- **North Indian Bhakti & Sant Movement** — 14th-17th c. vernacular devotion: Kabir, Mirabai, Chaitanya, Tulsidas (Ramcharitmanas), Surdas, Tukaram _(actors: Kabir, Mirabai, Chaitanya, Tulsidas)_ → [details](../01-manvantaras/manvantara-07-vaivasvata/mahayuga-28-current/04-kali-yuga/recorded-history.md) — (Bhakti movement)
- **Shalivahana's victory inaugurates the Shaka era** — An alternate Gudi Padwa charter marks Shalivahana's victory and the start of the Shalivahana Shaka era _(actors: Shalivahana)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/regional-temple-festivals.md) — (folk)
- **Aurangzeb Demolishes and Ahilyabai Rebuilds Kashi Vishwanath** — Aurangzeb demolishes the Kashi Vishwanath temple in 1669 CE, and Ahilyabai Holkar rebuilds the present temple in 1780 _(actors: Aurangzeb, Ahilyabai Holkar)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/12-jyotirlingas.md) — (recorded history)
- **Founding of the Khalsa** — On Vaisakhi in 1699 Guru Gobind Singh founded the Khalsa, an event mapped onto the Mesha Sankranti harvest/new-year date _(actors: Guru Gobind Singh)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/regional-temple-festivals.md) — (Sikh tradition; Wikipedia, Vaisakhi)
- **Madhva Founds Dvaita Vedanta** — Madhva (1238-1317 CE) founds Dvaita Vedanta and the Udupi Krishna matha, holding an irreducible difference between jiva and Brahman _(actors: Madhva (Ananda Tirtha))_ → [details](../01-manvantaras/manvantara-07-vaivasvata/mahayuga-28-current/04-kali-yuga/recorded-history.md) — (Dvaita Vedanta works)
- **Tulsidas composes the Hanuman Chalisa** — Goswami Tulsidas composes the 40-verse Awadhi Hanuman Chalisa and the Ramcharitmanas (1574), crystallizing North Indian Hanuman bhakti _(actors: Tulsidas)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/hanuman.md) — (Tulsidas, Hanuman Chalisa, Ramcharitmanas)
- **Vijayanagara & Maratha Hindu Sovereignty** — Vijayanagara (1336-1646) last great southern Hindu empire; Shivaji (1630-1680) frames Hindavi Swarajya _(actors: Vijayanagara rulers, Shivaji)_ → [details](../01-manvantaras/manvantara-07-vaivasvata/mahayuga-28-current/04-kali-yuga/recorded-history.md) — (Late-medieval history)
- **Brahmo Samaj Founded** — Raja Ram Mohan Roy founds the Brahmo Samaj on 20 Aug 1828, Calcutta — monotheist, anti-sati, anti-caste reform _(actors: Raja Ram Mohan Roy)_ → [details](../01-manvantaras/manvantara-07-vaivasvata/mahayuga-28-current/04-kali-yuga/recorded-history.md) — (Brahmo Samaj history)
- **Kabir's Nirguna Bhakti** — Kabir (c. 1440-1518 CE), weaver-poet of the nirguna Sant tradition, composes dohas critiquing both Hindu ritualism and Islamic legalism, later claimed by Hindus, Muslims and Sikhs _(actors: Kabir)_ → [details](../01-manvantaras/manvantara-07-vaivasvata/mahayuga-28-current/04-kali-yuga/recorded-history.md) — (Bijak; Bhakti movement (Wikipedia))
- **Sumitra Defeated by Mahapadma Nanda** — Sumitra, traditionally the last Ikshvaku king of Ayodhya, is defeated by Mahapadma Nanda of Magadha and flees to Rohtas, ending the dynasty _(actors: Sumitra, Mahapadma Nanda)_ → [details](../03-lineages-vamsha/suryavamsha-and-chandravamsha.md) — (Vishnu Purana IV.22; Puranic king-lists)
- **Vasishtha's fire-pit and the Agnikula origin** — At Mt. Abu, Vasishtha's fire-pit (agni-kunda) gives rise to the Agnikula Rajput-origin legend _(actors: Vasishtha)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/puranas/skanda-purana-khanda-map.md) — (Skanda Purana (Prabhasa-khanda, Arbuda-khanda))
- **Arya Samaj Founded** — Swami Dayananda Saraswati founds the Arya Samaj in 1875 with the slogan 'Back to the Vedas' _(actors: Swami Dayananda Saraswati)_ → [details](../01-manvantaras/manvantara-07-vaivasvata/mahayuga-28-current/04-kali-yuga/recorded-history.md) — (Satyarth Prakash (1875))
- **Birth of Santoshi Ma** — Santoshi Ma, a goddess of recent popular origin said to be a daughter of Ganesha, emerges through 1960s vrat literature and is propelled to fame by the 1975 film Jai Santoshi Maa _(actors: Santoshi Ma)_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/devi-forms-and-navadurga.md) — (Jai Santoshi Maa (1975); vrata-katha tradition)
- **Guru Nanak Founds Sikhism** — Guru Nanak (1469-1539 CE) founds Sikhism within the Sant milieu, teaching nirguna devotion _(actors: Guru Nanak)_ → [details](../01-manvantaras/manvantara-07-vaivasvata/mahayuga-28-current/04-kali-yuga/recorded-history.md)
- **Chaitanya Mahaprabhu and Gaudiya Vaishnavism** — Chaitanya Mahaprabhu (1486-1534 CE) leads ecstatic Krishna sankirtana in Bengal, founding the inspiration for Gaudiya Vaishnavism (Achintya Bheda Abheda) _(actors: Chaitanya Mahaprabhu)_ → [details](../01-manvantaras/manvantara-07-vaivasvata/mahayuga-28-current/04-kali-yuga/recorded-history.md) — (Bhakti movement (Wikipedia))
- **Manjunatha Linga Installed at Dharmasthala** — A Shiva linga from Kadri is later installed and named Manjunatha (attributed to Vadiraja Tirtha's 16th-c. influence), fusing folk daiva worship, Jain stewardship and Shaiva temple _(actors: Birmanna Pergade, Vadiraja Tirtha)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/sthala-puranas-major-temples.md) — (Dharmasthala temple tradition)
- **Vivekananda & Ramakrishna Mission** — Vivekananda gives the 1893 Chicago Parliament address and founds the Ramakrishna Mission on 1 May 1897 _(actors: Swami Vivekananda, Ramakrishna)_ → [details](../01-manvantaras/manvantara-07-vaivasvata/mahayuga-28-current/04-kali-yuga/recorded-history.md) — (Ramakrishna Mission history)
- **Composition of the Adhyatma Ramayana** — The Advaita-Vedanta Adhyatma Ramayana, recasting Rama as supreme Brahman, is composed (c. 13th-15th c. CE) and embedded in the Brahmanda Purana _(actors: Vyasa (ascribed))_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ramayana/README.md) — (Adhyatma Ramayana (Brahmanda Purana))
- **Dayananda founds the Arya Samaj condemning murti-puja** — Dayananda Sarasvati founds the Arya Samaj in 1875, condemning image-worship as un-Vedic and calling Hindus back to nirguna theism _(actors: Dayananda Sarasvati)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/mantra-japa-and-murti-puja.md) — (Arya Samaj)
- **Modern Global Hindu Movements** — 20th c. global gurus and movements: Yogananda, ISKCON (Prabhupada, 1966), TM, and yoga's worldwide export _(actors: Yogananda, Prabhupada, Maharishi Mahesh Yogi)_ → [details](../01-manvantaras/manvantara-07-vaivasvata/mahayuga-28-current/04-kali-yuga/recorded-history.md) — (Modern Hinduism)
- **Shankara founds Advaita and the Dashanami orders** — Adi Shankara (c. 8th c. CE) composes the Brahma Sutra Bhashya, organizes renunciate monks into the ten Dashanami orders, and founds four cardinal mathas _(actors: Adi Shankara)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/atman-brahman-and-the-six-darshanas.md) — (Brahma Sutra Bhashya; Advaita matha tradition)
- **Sri Yukteswar's Ascending-Yuga Variant** — Sri Yukteswar (The Holy Science, 1894) claims Kali ended ~1700 CE and we are now in ascending Dvapara Yuga _(actors: Sri Yukteswar)_ → [details](../01-manvantaras/manvantara-07-vaivasvata/mahayuga-28-current/04-kali-yuga/recorded-history.md) — (The Holy Science (1894))
- **Vijayanagara Empire** — The Vijayanagara Empire (1336-1646), the last great Hindu empire of the south, sustains temple Hinduism through the Mughal era _(actors: Vijayanagara rulers)_ → [details](../01-manvantaras/manvantara-07-vaivasvata/mahayuga-28-current/04-kali-yuga/recorded-history.md)
- **Caitanya Mahāprabhu Rediscovers the Lost Tīrthas of Braj** — Visiting Braj c. 1515, Caitanya Mahāprabhu locates lost sites like Rādhā-kuṇḍa and Imlītalā by trance and deputes the Six Goswamis to recover the tīrthas and write the theology of bhakti _(actors: Caitanya Mahāprabhu, the Six Goswamis)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/mathura-vrindavan-braj.md) — (Gaudiya tradition; Six Goswamis (Wikipedia))
- **Lakulisha founds the Pashupata sampradaya** — The last of the 28 Yogeshvara avatars, Lakulisha, descends as the historical founder of the Pashupata sampradaya _(actors: Lakulisha (Shiva))_ → [details](../02-deities-and-cosmic-hierarchy/shiva-avatars-and-forms.md) — (Linga Purana; Kurma Purana)
- **Rūpa Goswami Recovers the Govinda-deva Deity** — Rūpa Goswami recovers the Rādhā-Govinda-deva deity at Gomā-ṭīlā c. 1525; the great red-sandstone temple is built in 1590 under Mān Singh of Amber with Akbar's patronage _(actors: Rūpa Goswami, Mān Singh, Akbar)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/mathura-vrindavan-braj.md) — (Six Goswamis (Wikipedia))
- **Shivaji and the Maratha Resurgence** — Shivaji (1630-1680) frames Hindu sovereignty as Hindavi Swarajya, leading the Maratha resurgence _(actors: Shivaji)_ → [details](../01-manvantaras/manvantara-07-vaivasvata/mahayuga-28-current/04-kali-yuga/recorded-history.md)
- **Founding of the Brahmo Samaj** — Raja Ram Mohan Roy founds the Brahmo Samaj on 20 Aug 1828 in Calcutta, a monotheist, anti-idolatry, anti-sati, anti-caste reform movement _(actors: Raja Ram Mohan Roy; Dwarkanath Tagore)_ → [details](../01-manvantaras/manvantara-07-vaivasvata/mahayuga-28-current/04-kali-yuga/recorded-history.md) — (Brahmo Samaj (Wikipedia/Britannica))
- **Rādhā-Ramaṇa Self-Manifests for Gopāla Bhaṭṭa** — The Rādhā-Ramaṇa deity self-manifests from a śālagrāma-śilā for Gopāla Bhaṭṭa Goswami in 1542 _(actors: Gopāla Bhaṭṭa Goswami)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/mathura-vrindavan-braj.md) — (theharekrishnamovement.org)
- **Founding of the Arya Samaj** — Swami Dayananda Saraswati founds the Arya Samaj in 1875 in Bombay with the slogan 'Back to the Vedas,' rejecting Puranic ritual and idolatry, and publishes Satyarth Prakash _(actors: Swami Dayananda Saraswati)_ → [details](../01-manvantaras/manvantara-07-vaivasvata/mahayuga-28-current/04-kali-yuga/recorded-history.md) — (Satyarth Prakash (1875))
- **Svāmī Haridāsa Manifests Banke Bihārī** — The singer-saint Svāmī Haridāsa manifests the combined Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa deity Banke Bihārī at Nidhivana and founds the Haridāsī Sampradāya of devotional music _(actors: Svāmī Haridāsa)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/mathura-vrindavan-braj.md) — (Banke Bihari Temple (Wikipedia))
- **Adi Shankara Consecrates Mukambika at Kollur** — Adi Shankara is said to have consecrated the Goddess Mukambika at Kollur, strongly associating the svayambhu shrine with him _(actors: Adi Shankara)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/sthala-puranas-major-temples.md) — (Kollur tradition)
- **Vallabhācārya Rediscovers Śrī Nāthajī on Govardhana** — Vallabhācārya, founder of the Puṣṭimārga, rediscovers the self-manifest Śrī Nāthajī (the Govardhana-dhāraṇa form) on Govardhana hill, later moved to Nathdwara _(actors: Vallabhācārya)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/mathura-vrindavan-braj.md) — (Pustimarga tradition)
- **Vivekananda's Chicago Address and Ramakrishna Mission** — Swami Vivekananda delivers his 1893 Parliament of the World's Religions address in Chicago and founds the Ramakrishna Mission on 1 May 1897, carrying Vedanta to the West _(actors: Swami Vivekananda; Ramakrishna Paramahamsa)_ → [details](../01-manvantaras/manvantara-07-vaivasvata/mahayuga-28-current/04-kali-yuga/recorded-history.md)
- **Sri Yukteswar's Ascending-Yuga Reframing** — Sri Yukteswar in The Holy Science (1894) holds Kali Yuga ended ~1700 CE and we are now in an ascending Dvapara Yuga on a 24,000-year precessional cycle, reframing the entire 'we are in Kali' premise _(actors: Sri Yukteswar)_ → [details](../01-manvantaras/manvantara-07-vaivasvata/mahayuga-28-current/04-kali-yuga/recorded-history.md) — (The Holy Science (1894))
- **Founding of ISKCON** — A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupada founds ISKCON in 1966, spreading Gaudiya Vaishnavism worldwide _(actors: A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupada)_ → [details](../01-manvantaras/manvantara-07-vaivasvata/mahayuga-28-current/04-kali-yuga/recorded-history.md)
- **Rise of Hindu Nationalism** — Hindu nationalism crystallizes through Savarkar's Hindutva (1923), the RSS (1925) and the VHP (1964), shaping diaspora temple-building and live heritage debates _(actors: Savarkar; RSS; VHP)_ → [details](../01-manvantaras/manvantara-07-vaivasvata/mahayuga-28-current/04-kali-yuga/recorded-history.md)
- **Composition of Tulsidas's Ramcharitmanas** — Tulsidas begins the Awadhi Ramcharitmanas on Ramanavami 1574 CE in Ayodhya, the dominant North-Indian bhakti retelling and basis of Ramlila _(actors: Tulsidas)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ramayana/recensions/ramcharitmanas-tulsidas.md) — (Ramcharitmanas (Tulsidas))
- **Ramanuja composes the Sri Bhashya** — Ramanuja (1017-1137 CE) establishes Vishishtadvaita, reading souls and matter as the real body of Brahman _(actors: Ramanuja)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/atman-brahman-and-the-six-darshanas.md) — (Sri Bhashya)
- **Ramanuja's Saptavidha-anupapatti against Advaita maya** — Ramanuja launches the seven great untenables against the Advaita avidya/maya in his Sri Bhashya, the most famous demolition of the doctrine _(actors: Ramanuja)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/maya-and-avidya.md) — (Sri Bhashya)
- **Shankara founds the four cardinal mathas** — Adi Shankara organizes renunciates into the Dashanami orders and founds four mathas at Shringeri, Puri, Dvaraka, and Jyotir/Badri, each headed by a Shankaracharya _(actors: Adi Shankara)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/guru-shishya-parampara.md) — (Advaita matha tradition)
- **Woodroffe publishes The Serpent Power** — Sir John Woodroffe (Arthur Avalon) translates the Shat-chakra-nirupana in 1919, fixing the now-dominant seven-chakra image _(actors: John Woodroffe)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/chakras-and-kundalini.md) — (The Serpent Power (1919))
- **Ramcharitmanas Inscribed on UNESCO Memory of the World** — The Ramcharitmanas is inscribed on UNESCO's MOWCAP Regional Register on 7-8 May 2024 in Ulaanbaatar, alongside the Panchatantra and Sahridayaloka-Locana _(actors: UNESCO)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ramayana/recensions/ramcharitmanas-tulsidas.md) — (UNESCO MOWCAP 2024)
- **Basava founds the Virashaiva/Lingayat tradition** — Basava (12th c.) founds the Virashaiva/Lingayat movement rejecting temple icons in favor of the personal ishta-linga _(actors: Basava)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/guru-shishya-parampara.md) — (Virashaiva tradition)
- **Composition of Kamban's Iramavataram** — Kamban composes the Tamil Iramavataram (Kambaramayanam) under Chola patronage c. 12th c. CE, recasting Rama as full incarnate Vishnu and ending at his coronation _(actors: Kamban, Sadaiyappa Vallal)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ramayana/recensions/kamban-iramavataram.md) — (Kamban, Iramavataram)
- **Madhva founds Dvaita Tattvavada** — Madhva (1238-1317 CE) systematizes Dvaita with the five eternal differences, teaching eternal soul-God distinction _(actors: Madhva)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/atman-brahman-and-the-six-darshanas.md) — (Brahma Sutra Bhashya (Madhva))
- **Self-sacrifice of the Elder Brothers (Annanmar Katai)** — The twin Tamil warrior-brothers Ponnar and Cankar of Ponnivala, serving Shiva/Vishnu, willingly sacrifice themselves to the gods, their sister Tankal performing their funeral rites; they become guardian deities _(actors: Ponnar, Cankar, Tankal)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/regional-and-oral-epics.md) — (Annanmar Katai (Tamil oral epic))
- **Martyrdom and deification of Madurai Veeran** — The low-caste warrior-hero Madurai Veeran is executed and dismembered over a forbidden love, then deified as a Tamil guardian deity worshipped with his consorts _(actors: Madurai Veeran, Vellaiyammal, Bommakkal)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/regional-and-oral-epics.md) — (Maturai Viran (Tamil folk tradition))
- **Death and deification of Pabuji** — The Rajput hero-chieftain Pabuji Rathod, a camel-rescuer regarded as an incarnation of Lakshmana, dies in battle and is deified as a grama-devata and camel-protector god _(actors: Pabuji Rathod)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/regional-and-oral-epics.md) — (Pabuji ki Phad (Rajasthani oral epic))
- **Saga of Devnarayan** — Devnarayan, deified hero-king of the Gujar community held to be an incarnation of Vishnu, avenges the Bagaravat brothers and is worshipped via the giant phad scroll _(actors: Devnarayan, Savai Bhoj)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/regional-and-oral-epics.md) — (Devnarayan ki Phad (Rajasthani oral epic))
- **Romance of Dhola and Maru** — Dhola (prince Sal Kunvar) and Marvani, betrothed as children and separated, are reunited across desert and rival queens in the Rajasthani romance-epic _(actors: Dhola, Marvani/Maru)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/regional-and-oral-epics.md) — (Dhola Maru ra Duha (Rajasthani ballad cycle))
- **The 52 battles of Alha and Udal** — The brother-heroes Alha and Udal, Banaphar commanders of King Paramardideva of Mahoba, fight their 52 battles culminating in resistance to Prithviraj Chauhan (assoc. 1182 CE); Alha is held never to have died _(actors: Alha, Udal, Paramardideva, Prithviraj Chauhan)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/regional-and-oral-epics.md) — (Alha-Khand (attrib. Jagnik of Mahoba))
- **Lakulisa founds the Pasupata order** — Lakulisa (c. 2nd c. CE) founds the ash-smeared Lakulisa-Pasupata ascetic order, among the earliest organised Saiva sects _(actors: Lakulisa)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/agamas-tantras-dharmashastras.md) — (Pasupata-sutras)
- **The Battle of Palnadu** — The fratricidal Battle of Palnadu (c. 1178-1182 CE) pits two factions of Palnadu chiefs; statesman-warrior Brahma Nayudu preaches an anti-caste order and the boy-hero Balachandrudu dies heroically, the fallen worshipped as the Palnati Virulu _(actors: Brahma Nayudu, Balachandrudu, Nagamma)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/regional-and-oral-epics.md) — (Palnati Virula Katha; Srinatha's Palnati Vira Caritra)
- **Miracles of Male Madeshvara** — The miracle-working Virashaiva siddha Madeshvara of the Male Madeshvara hills, worshipped as a form of Shiva, is celebrated in the Kannada oral epic _(actors: Madeshvara)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/regional-and-oral-epics.md) — (Male Madeshvara (Kannada oral epic))
- **Behula recovers Lakhindar's life (Manasa-mangal)** — The merchant Chand Sadagar refuses serpent-goddess Manasa's worship, so she kills his son Lakhindar by snakebite on his wedding night, until the widowed bride Behula floats the corpse to the gods, wins his life back, and forces Chand to worship Manasa _(actors: Manasa, Chand Sadagar, Lakhindar, Behula)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/regional-and-oral-epics.md) — (Manasa-mangal (Bengali Mangal-kavya))
- **Vasugupta receives the Siva-Sutras** — Vasugupta (9th c.) receives and composes the Siva-Sutras and Spanda-Karika, founding the textual basis of non-dual Kashmir Saivism _(actors: Vasugupta)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/agamas-tantras-dharmashastras.md) — (Siva-Sutras, Spanda-Karika)
- **Chandi establishes her worship (Chandi-mangal)** — The fierce Durga-form Chandi establishes her worship among hunters and merchants through the Kalaketu hunter and Dhanapati/Srimanta merchant cycles _(actors: Chandi, Kalaketu, Dhanapati, Srimanta)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/regional-and-oral-epics.md) — (Chandi-mangal (Mukundarama, 16th c.))
- **Composition of the Krittivasi Ramayan** — Krittibas Ojha composes the Bengali Srirama Panchali (15th c. CE), the most beloved Bengali retelling and charter-text of Bengali Durga Puja _(actors: Krittibas Ojha)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ramayana/recensions/krittivasi-ramayan.md) — (Krittivasi Ramayan)
- **Maṇḍana Misra converted to Advaita by Sankara** — The Mimamsaka Maṇḍana Misra is, by tradition, converted to Advaita Vedanta by Sankara, a hinge moment between Mimamsa and Vedanta _(actors: Maṇḍana Misra, Sankara)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/darshanas/mimamsa-sutras.md) — (Mimamsa tradition)
- **Vallabha founds Shuddhadvaita and Pushti-marga** — Vallabha (1479-1531 CE) teaches pure non-dualism with the world as real emanation of Krishna and the path of grace _(actors: Vallabha)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/atman-brahman-and-the-six-darshanas.md) — (Shuddhadvaita tradition)
- **Bonbibi protects the Sundarbans against Dakshin Ray** — The forest-goddess Bonbibi protects honey-gatherers and woodcutters of the Sundarbans against the tiger-demon Dakshin Ray, her cult shared by Hindus and Muslims _(actors: Bonbibi, Dakshin Ray, Shah Jongoli)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/regional-and-oral-epics.md) — (Bonbibi Jahuranama)
- **Sankara composes the Brahma-sutra-bhasya** — Adi Sankara (c. 8th c.) composes his Brahma-sutra-bhasya, the oldest surviving complete commentary, founding the Advaita reading of Vedanta _(actors: Adi Sankara)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/darshanas/brahma-sutras.md) — (Brahma-sutra-bhasya)
- **Ramanuja writes the Sri Bhasya** — Ramanuja (11th-12th c.) composes the Sri Bhasya on the Brahma Sutras, founding Visistadvaita and defending Pancaratra as Veda-concordant _(actors: Ramanuja)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/darshanas/brahma-sutras.md) — (Sri Bhasya)
- **Madhva writes the Dvaita commentary** — Madhva (13th c.) composes his Brahma-sutra-bhasya and Aṇu-bhasya, founding the Dvaita dualist school of Vedanta _(actors: Madhva)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/darshanas/brahma-sutras.md) — (Brahma-sutra-bhasya, Aṇu-bhasya)
- **Gaṅgesa founds Navya-Nyaya** — Gaṅgesa Upadhyaya (c. 1325, Mithila) composes the Tattva-cintamaṇi, founding the Navya-Nyaya 'New Logic' school _(actors: Gaṅgesa Upadhyaya)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/darshanas/nyaya-sutras.md) — (Tattva-cintamaṇi)
- **Praśastapada re-founds Vaiseṣika** — Praśastapada (c. 4th-6th c. CE) composes the Padartha-dharma-saṃgraha, re-founding classical Vaiseṣika and introducing a creator-Isvara into the atomist system _(actors: Praśastapada)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/darshanas/vaisheshika-sutras.md) — (Padartha-dharma-saṃgraha)
- **Chaitanya founds Achintya-bhedabheda** — Chaitanya (1486-1534 CE), systematized by the Gosvamis of Vrindavana, teaches inconceivable difference-and-non-difference, the basis of Gaudiya Vaishnavism _(actors: Chaitanya, Gosvamis)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/atman-brahman-and-the-six-darshanas.md) — (Gaudiya Vaishnava tradition)
- **Vivekananda founds the Ramakrishna Math** — Vivekananda, disciple of Ramakrishna, founds the Ramakrishna Math, a modern global lineage _(actors: Ramakrishna, Vivekananda)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/guru-shishya-parampara.md) — (Ramakrishna Math tradition)
- **Composition of Vimalasuri's Paumacariya** — Vimalasuri composes the Paumacariya (c. 3rd-5th c. CE), the earliest extant Jain Ramayana, rationalizing the epic and reframing Rama within Jain shalakapurusha cosmology _(actors: Vimalasuri)_ → [details](../04-deep-dives/ramayana/recensions/jain-and-buddhist-ramayanas.md) — (Paumacariya (Vimalasuri))
- **Gaudapada composes the Mandukya Karika** — Gaudapada (Shankara's parama-guru) develops ajata-vada in the earliest systematic Advaita text, holding nothing is ever truly born _(actors: Gaudapada)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/atman-brahman-and-the-six-darshanas.md) — (Mandukya Karika)
- **Vikramaditya's victory founds the Vikrama era** — The legendary Vikramaditya of Ujjain, after victory over the Shakas, founds the Vikrama Samvat era (epoch 57/58 BCE) _(actors: Vikramaditya)_ → [details](../00-time-and-cosmology/hindu-calendar-and-panchanga.md) — (Samvatsara tradition)
- **Discovery of the Paippalada Manuscripts in Odisha (1957)** — Durgamohan Bhattacharyya discovers a well-preserved set of Paippalada palm-leaf manuscripts in Odisha in 1957, becoming the basis of the modern critical edition of the lost recension _(actors: Durgamohan Bhattacharyya)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/vedas/atharvaveda.md) — (Paippalada Samhita scholarship)
- **Mahavira takes his first disciple Indrabhuti Gautama** — Jains honor the Ashadha full moon as the day Mahavira took his first disciple, Indrabhuti Gautama _(actors: Mahavira, Indrabhuti Gautama)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/guru-shishya-parampara.md) — (Jain tradition)
- **Sayana Composes the Rigveda Bhashya at Vijayanagara** — Sayanacharya, minister-scholar at the Vijayanagara court under Bukka I and Harihara II, composes the authoritative classical commentary on the entire Rigveda with his brother Madhava _(actors: Sayanacharya, Madhava (Vidyaranya))_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/vedas/rigveda.md) — (Sayana's Rigveda Bhashya)
- **Adi Shankara Founds Advaita Vedanta** — Adi Shankara (c. 8th c. CE) systematizes non-dualist Advaita Vedanta in his bhashyas on the prasthana-trayi, teaching that Brahman alone is real and Atman=Brahman _(actors: Adi Shankara)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/upavedas-vedangas-darshanas.md) — (Shankara's bhashyas; Vivekachudamani)
- **Ramanuja Founds Vishishtadvaita** — Ramanuja (c. 11th-12th c. CE) establishes qualified non-dualism, teaching Brahman (Vishnu/Narayana) qualified by real souls and matter, with bhakti and prapatti central, in his Sri Bhashya _(actors: Ramanuja)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/upavedas-vedangas-darshanas.md) — (Sri Bhashya)
- **Madhvacharya Founds Dvaita Vedanta** — Madhvacharya (c. 13th c. CE) establishes dualist Tattvavada with five eternal distinctions, holding God, souls, and matter as distinct realities and liberation by grace and bhakti _(actors: Madhvacharya)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/upavedas-vedangas-darshanas.md) — (Dvaita Vedanta works)
- **Chaitanya Mahaprabhu Founds Achintya Bheda Abheda** — Chaitanya Mahaprabhu (c. 15th-16th c. CE) founds the Gaudiya Vaishnava school of inconceivable simultaneous oneness and difference of soul and God _(actors: Chaitanya Mahaprabhu)_ → [details](../90-literature-corpus/upavedas-vedangas-darshanas.md) — (Gaudiya Vaishnava tradition)
- **Founding of Shree Siddhivinayaka, Prabhadevi** — Contractor Lakṣmaṇ Viṭhu Pāṭīl builds the Siddhivināyaka (Gaṇeśa) temple at Prabhādevī, Mumbai, funded by the devout childless Deubāī Pāṭīl who wished other barren women might obtain children by the deity; later grown into one of India's most-visited and wealthiest temples _(actors: Lakṣmaṇ Viṭhu Pāṭīl; Deubāī Pāṭīl; Gaṇeśa)_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/sthala-puranas-major-temples.md) — (Wikipedia, Siddhivinayak Temple, Mumbai)
- **Samādhi of Shirdi Sāī Bābā and rise of the Shirdi kṣetra** — On 15 October 1918 the composite supra-sectarian saint Sāī Bābā of Shirdi attains samādhi; his body is interred in the Buṭī Wāḍā (built as a Kṛṣṇa temple), which becomes the Samādhi Mandir, while his Dwārakāmāī mosque, ever-burning dhuni and ūdī anchor a modern all-India Sāī pilgrimage network _(actors: Sāī Bābā of Shirdi; Gopālrāo Buṭī; Govind Dabholkar ('Hemādpant'))_ → [details](../06-sacred-geography/sthala-puranas-major-temples.md) — (Śrī Sāī Satcharita; Wikipedia, Sai Baba of Shirdi)
- **Tilak popularizes public Ganesh Chaturthi** — Lokmanya Tilak popularizes the modern mass sarvajanik Ganesh Chaturthi in Maharashtra in 1893, a datable historical layer atop the Puranic myth _(actors: Lokmanya Tilak)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/festivals-and-vratas.md) — (recorded history)
- **Buddha's first sermon at Sarnath** — The same Ashadha full moon marks the Buddha's first sermon at Sarnath, observed as Guru Purnima across Indic traditions _(actors: Buddha)_ → [details](../05-concepts-and-dharma/festivals-and-vratas.md) — (Buddhist tradition)
